# Candidate topics and skeptic verdicts (workflow run wf_041adedb-1ec, 2026-10-01)

Companion to RESEARCH_DIRECTIONS_2026-10-01.md. Nineteen merged candidate topics, each judged by three adversarial reviewers (novelty, feasibility, venue fit) instructed to refute by default when uncertain; scores are 0-10 on that lens. A topic "survives" only if at most one lens refuted it. The memo re-framed the topics into five directions after this review.

## I01: Who holds it now? Causal object-to-arm tracking with a switching SE(3) grasp latent for bimanual handovers, stale-grasp detection and calibrated trust in the object constraint

Direction: Causal 4D human-hand-object tracking from one metric RGB-D view in which the interaction state (which hand holds the object, the grip mode, and the full SE(3) hand-object grasp transform) is an inferred hybrid discrete-continuous latent rather than a slowly updated constant offset; the held object, measured independently of the body detector, is used as a strong 6-DoF prior for the arm chain.

Research question: During bimanual manipulation with handovers, when a holding hand is occluded but the held object's 6-DoF pose is still measured, does a causal fixed-lag estimator that jointly infers a discrete grasp mode (free / left / right / both, with rigid / slip / regrasp / release dynamics) and a per-episode full SE(3) grasp transform, with evidence flowing both ways (visible object places a hidden hand; visible hand places a hidden object), (a) reduce wrist, elbow and object error during multi-second occlusions and in the 0-2 s after a handover, and (b) recover the rotational degrees of freedom one view cannot observe (shoulder twist / elbow swivel, forearm axial rotation), compared with the thesis's constant translation-only grip offset plus two-link IK, hold-last, per-frame foundation-model estimates with temporal smoothing, and one-directional hand-to-object cueing? Can the innovation statistic of the grasp-closure factor and the mode posterior detect a stale or slipped grasp before the wrist error grows past a tolerance, and decide, with a bounded risk of a wrong 'constrained' label, among object-conditioned recovery, hold-last and abstention?

Why novel (as proposed): The thesis uses only the translational part of the grip (wrist = object origin + R*h), so the object's orientation, the one measurement that directly constrains forearm axial rotation and hence the swivel/twist the thesis lists as unobservable, is discarded. Its grip offset is a slowly updated constant (EMA gain 0.02); the thesis measured 7.0 / 2.7 / 6.3 cm per-axis scatter of that offset on the loop recording, and object-conditioned recovery helped on one natural occluded window (5.2 cm vs 11.4-17.6 cm) but failed on another because the offset was stale. Closest work runs in other directions or settings: ComPose (arXiv 2605.23523, 2026) asks 'when to trust hands' only for hand-to-object cueing in RGB, with no body chain or handover modes; VisTracker (CVPR 2023) infills the occluded object from human motion, offline, RGB; IMU-HOI (CVPR 2026) fuses hand forward kinematics and object motion with contact gating human-to-object, using body-worn IMUs and no slip modes; HA-HOI / Real2Sim in HOI (arXiv 2605.14462) is human-first, object-follow; Pfisterer et al. (ICRA 2025) use the tracked hand as a prior for object kinematic structure; AKC (arXiv 2606.19240) corrects occluded arm depth from arm lengths with no object and no grasp; BIGS (CVPR 2025), HOLD (CVPR 2024) and CHOIR (arXiv 2605.20992) reconstruct interaction offline by per-sequence optimisation with no causal operation and no explicit grasp-mode switching; GeneOH Diffusion (ICLR 2024), TOCH (ECCV 2022) and OMOMO / CHOIS generate or denoise offline assuming the hand is observed; physics-based HOI (RePHO CVPR 2026, ScoreHOI ICCV 2025, PhysCap TOG 2020) targets plausibility, not contact attribution between two hands and a support; handover datasets (HO-Cap NeurIPS 2025 D&B, HOH NeurIPS 2023 D&B, DexH2R ICCV 2025, H2O, the Data in Brief 2023 bimanual handover set) are interpersonal or human-robot, or do not define causal grip-state detection as a task or measure its downstream effect. The delta: (a) a hybrid discrete-continuous state where a handover is a mode transition L -> LR -> R with re-estimated grasp transforms; (b) a full 6-DoF grasp-closure factor that makes twist observable; (c) bidirectional hand-object evidence in metric depth; (d) a causal fixed-lag estimator whose honesty labels come from factor activity / covariance and whose 'constrained' decision is risk-controlled; (e) grip-state and possession-transfer event detection evaluated as a task, with attribution of 3D error to grip-state errors. Plainly: the factor-graph machinery is standard and the closest single pieces exist; the novelty is the object-to-arm formulation, the 6-DoF closure for twist, the grasp-mode dynamics and the handover-specific evaluation, so the paper must show measurable gains on twist and on stale-grasp detection.

Method (as proposed): Inputs: aligned RGB-D at 30 fps from one D435, intrinsics, the calibrated desk world frame (desk and wall markers). Front ends (frozen): RTMPose (v3, ONNX Runtime, p99 under about 12 ms; wholebody variant for hand-root keypoints) back-projected with calibrated depth where a depth-ownership test passes (see I02); WiLoR or HaMeR for per-hand MANO orientation and wrist position; object SE(3) from the ArUco cube with a PnP covariance from corner noise, and FoundationPose plus SAM 2 masks for marker-free objects. State: torso SE(3); per arm the v3 unit-quaternion swing-twist chain (shoulder swing, shoulder twist, elbow flexion) extended by a wrist rotation (2-DoF flexion/deviation); object SE(3); discrete mode m in {free, L, R, LR} with per-hand contact mode in {rigid, slip, regrasp, release}; grasp transforms G_L, G_R in SE(3), each tied to a grip episode. Estimator: a fixed-lag smoother (GTSAM incremental fixed-lag or a small Gauss-Newton in PyTorch / Theseus; 2-3 frame lag matching the thesis causal merger, up to 10-15 frames as an ablation) with factors for (1) keypoint reprojection plus metric depth residuals with robust (Cauchy) loss and the existing bone-length / depth-jump gates as switchable factors; (2) bone-length priors with person-specific lengths; (3) joint-limit barriers on the swing-twist parameters; (4) object pose measurements; (5) a 6-DoF grasp-closure factor Log(T_obj^-1 * FK_hand(q) * G_m), active only in the current mode, giving the bidirectional coupling (translation-only closure as the ablation that reproduces the thesis); (6) grasp dynamics: rigid gives G_t = G_{t-1} with tight covariance, slip an SE(3) random walk with large covariance, release-then-grasp resets G from the first co-visible frames; (7) constant-velocity tangent-space priors (v3 M5 Kalman as the zero-coupling ablation). Mode inference: HMM / IMM / max-mixture over modes with emissions from hand-object distance, relative velocity agreement, SAM 2 mask contact and the closure residuals; a handover precursor hazard (object speed, other hand's distance) as in a Rao-Blackwellized particle filter; the thesis 25/35 cm hysteresis state machine is the baseline. Optional emission variants: (i) a learned switching grip-dynamics model (switching LDS or small conditional flow over the hand-object offset) trained on ARCTIC, OakInk2, TACO and GRAB, run forward on object motion only during outages; (ii) a per-frame wrench-feasibility QP (object acceleration under gravity explained by candidate hand and desk/rail contacts within friction cones) whose residuals feed the HMM, to be piloted for one week before committing. Stale-grasp detection: normalized innovation squared of the closure factor on frames where the hand is visible, accumulated by CUSUM, switching to slip mode when it fires. Decision: choose constrained / held / abstain by expected wrist error, with the 'constrained' threshold set by conformal risk control. Outputs keep the honesty labels (measured / held / constrained), now derived from active factors and window marginal covariances. A multi-view variant (2-3 D435, iSAM2, per-view keypoint and marker factors) can reuse the same latent (see I14 for calibration). The v1 two-link IK and object-conditioned recovery are the special case with fixed mode and frozen translation-only G and serve as the initializer.

Evaluation (as proposed): Public: HO-Cap (multi-camera RGB-D with handovers between one person's hands; one camera as input, multi-view annotation as reference; derive grip-state labels from its ground truth), BEHAVE and InterCap (single view as input, multi-view fit as pseudo ground truth; synthetic masking of hand/arm plus natural object occlusions), ARCTIC for bimanual grip dynamics and regrasps (RGB-only setting, depth from Depth Anything as an ablation). Own data: R4-R7 with manual wrist labels; the loop (two handovers) and handover-on-rail recordings; new recordings with scripted regrasps, slips and handovers from 3-10 subjects, where the ArUco cube is kept as free 6-DoF object truth but hidden from the markerless branch, plus a second reference view or borrowed mocap; optional force-sensitive resistors on the cube faces for contact truth. Baselines: per-frame WiLoR + FoundationPose with Kalman smoothing; thesis constant-offset recovery + two-link IK; hold-last; v3 S2 and tangent-space Kalman (D-038); AKC; MotionBERT-lite infill; a ComPose-style one-directional variant; distance-threshold and MS-TCN-style event detectors; HOLD/BIGS offline as accuracy (not latency) upper bounds. Metrics: wrist and elbow error (cm, world frame) restricted to occluded frames and to 0-2 s after a handover; shoulder-twist and swivel error (deg) against SMPL truth; object ADD-S and rotation error; grasp-offset error; NIS consistency; mode-classification F1, grasp/release/transfer event F1 at plus or minus 3, 5 and 10 frames, handover timing error; stale-grasp detection delay and false alarms per minute; rate of wrong 'constrained' labels; v3 false_measured and teleports; latency p99; causal-vs-offline divergence; recovery-error attribution (grip-state error vs geometry) using oracle, estimated and no grip state. Ablations: no mode latent (always both grips), translation-only vs full SE(3) closure, one-directional coupling, slow-update grip (thesis), lag 0 / 3 / 15 frames, no wrist rotation, with and without hand keypoints, ArUco vs FoundationPose object input, with and without wrench and learned-dynamics emissions.

Target venues: ICCV 2027, CVPR 2027 (if public-benchmark gains are strong), CoRL 2027, RSS 2027, IEEE RA-L with ICRA/IROS presentation, 3DV 2027, IEEE T-RO (extended version)

Builds on the thesis: Object-conditioned recovery and its grip-offset failure analysis give the problem statement and main baseline; the v3 swing-twist quaternion core (oracle-identical to v1) gives manifold FK and Jacobians; the causal delay-buffer merger and 29-30 fps replay (p99 about 12 ms) leave about 20 ms per frame for a fixed-lag solver; honesty labels and false_measured become evaluated outputs; the S-type occlusion generator with teleport budgets plus new grip-shift and regrasp scenarios give a stress test; the loop and handover-on-rail recordings, labelled grip episodes and the ArUco cube (free object ground truth, clean object velocity) are pilot data; world frame with gravity and fitted rail line support the optional wrench test.

Feasibility (as proposed): Have: D435, RTX 3080 (enough for frozen WiLoR, SAM 2 small and FoundationPose at reduced rate; the smoother runs on CPU), Unity, 28 recordings. Need: GTSAM/Theseus (free); HO-Cap, BEHAVE, InterCap, ARCTIC downloads (over 1 GB, need user approval and registration); scanned or CAD meshes for 3-5 objects; a second D435 (about USD 300-400) or borrowed mocap for reference; 3-10 additional subjects (ethics amendment); about 300-500 labelled wrist/elbow frames on occluded segments plus a few hours of grip-change labels. Timeline: months 1-2 front-end integration, depth gating and estimator with fixed mode on existing recordings; months 3-4 mode inference and public-data evaluation; month 5 new recordings with regrasps and handovers; months 6-7 ablations and writing. ICCV 2027 (deadline around March 2027) or RA-L is realistic; CVPR 2027 (around November 2026) is too early. FoundationPose and HaMeR at 30 fps on a 3080 are not guaranteed; plan for keyframe-rate foundation models (5-10 Hz) with tracking in between, and report that honestly.

Risks (as proposed): Main rejection risk: 'a well-engineered factor graph combining existing models' with gains confined to frames where a tagged object is held; mitigate with a strong HO-Cap result on occluded and post-handover frames, an ablation showing the mode latent and 6-DoF closure (not the front ends) carry the gain, and marker-free runs with FoundationPose. HO-Cap ground truth is partly built with FoundationPose, so using it as a front end may bias comparisons; disclose and use marker-based object truth on own data. BEHAVE/InterCap pseudo ground truth is only accurate to a few cm, and their objects are mostly large and two-handed. Soft or wrapped grasps (as on the loop recording) may keep the slip mode always on. On slow desk tasks effect sizes may be small and the physics (wrench) signal too weak to beat proximity, so true occlusions must be recorded deliberately; ComPose may be extended to the reverse direction first.

### novelty skeptic: score 4, refuted=True

Search status: the session web-search budget was exhausted (all four attempted queries returned "budget used, 200 of 200"), so this review could not run a fresh search. Every work cited below is either from the idea's own upstream list, whose verified flags this run could not re-check, or from memory. All are therefore marked verified=false. Following the default rule, uncertainty counts against the idea. The judgement below rests on the structure of the claim, not on a newly found paper that does exactly this.

1) Each mechanism already exists in the literature.
- Hybrid discrete-continuous contact-mode estimation with IMM, max-mixture or switching factors is textbook in robotics. Examples: manifold particle filters for contact manipulation (Koval, Pollard and Srinivasa, IJRR 2016); contact-aided invariant EKF for legged robots (Hartley et al., RSS 2018 / IJRR 2020); tactile factor-graph in-hand tracking (Sodhi et al., ICRA 2021; PatchGraph, RA-L 2022; NeuralFeels, Science Robotics 2024).
- CUSUM or NIS stale-constraint detection and conformal risk control (ICLR 2024) are off-the-shelf.
- So the method contribution reduces to applying known switching estimation to a human arm plus a held object. A CVPR/ICCV reviewer will call this "a well-engineered factor graph". The idea's own risk section already concedes this.

2) Using the object to place the body is published.
- OMOMO (SIGGRAPH Asia 2023) and CHOIS (ECCV 2024) generate full-body and hand pose, including wrist orientation, from object motion alone. That is the "object places the hidden hand and arm" direction, though offline and generative.
- VisTracker (CVPR 2023) does joint human-object tracking under occlusion.
- BEHAVE, InterCap and CHORE-style SMPL-H plus object fitting use contact terms that couple wrist orientation to object pose.
- IMU-HOI (CVPR 2026, per the upstream list) already gates hand-object coupling by contact in both the human-to-object and object-to-human directions.
- ComPose (2026) already asks "when to trust hands" with a trust gate.
- The bidirectional coupling claim therefore reduces to "causal, metric depth, and arm chain instead of SMPL". That is a setting change, not a new idea.

3) The 6-DoF closure making twist observable is the most original piece, but it is close to trivial. Any SMPL-H plus object fit with a full-pose hand-object contact or relative-pose term already constrains forearm axial rotation. The thesis dropped object orientation only because its wrist had no orientation. Fixing that is closing a gap in the thesis, not a contribution at venue level.

4) Grasp and handover event detection overlaps existing benchmarks:
- Ego4D's hand-object state-change and point-of-no-return temporal localisation (CVPR 2022)
- HOH (NeurIPS 2023 D&B), HO-Cap (NeurIPS 2025 D&B) and H2O handover phase analysis
- DexH2R (ICCV 2025)

A new "possession transfer" metric is incremental unless it comes with a dataset.

5) The empirical risk weakens the novelty further. The gains are confined to frames where a held, posed object exists. HO-Cap ground truth is partly built with FoundationPose. The pilot evidence is one subject, with object-conditioned recovery helping on 1 of 2 natural windows.

Net assessment:
- Real but narrow delta: a causal, metric RGB-D, full-SE(3), mode-switching object-to-arm constraint, evaluated on handovers.
- Below the bar for CVPR/ICCV/ECCV as stated.
- Plausible for RA-L/IROS/ICRA if reframed with a sharper claim and a dataset or observability result.

Prior work found:
- Object Motion Guided Human Motion Synthesis (OMOMO) (SIGGRAPH Asia 2023 (ACM TOG)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16237; overlap: Infers full-body and hand pose, including wrist orientation, from object motion alone. This is the object-to-body direction the idea claims, but offline and generative rather than causal estimation.; verified=False
- Controllable Human-Object Interaction Synthesis (CHOIS) (ECCV 2024) https://github.com/lijiaman/chois_release; overlap: Object-conditioned human motion with contact constraints that tie the hands to the object pose. Offline.; verified=False
- Visibility Aware Human-Object Interaction Tracking from Single RGB Camera (VisTracker) (CVPR 2023) https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2023/html/Xie_Visibility_Aware_Human-Object_Interaction_Tracking_From_Single_RGB_Camera_CVPR_2023_paper.html; overlap: Joint human-object tracking under occlusion with visibility-aware infilling. It runs in the opposite infill direction (human fills in the object) and is offline RGB.; verified=False
- IMU-HOI: A Symbiotic Framework for Coherent Human-Object Interaction and Motion Capture via Contact-Conscious Inertial Fusion (CVPR 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28604; overlap: Contact-gated fusion of hand forward kinematics and object motion. This is the closest to the idea's contact-gated coupling, but with IMUs and no slip or regrasp modes.; verified=False
- ComPose: When to Trust Hands for Object Pose Tracking (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23523; overlap: Learned trust gate on the hand-object coupling during tracking. Covers the hand-to-object direction only, with no arm chain; the easy extension is the reverse direction.; verified=False
- BEHAVE: Dataset and Method for Tracking Human Object Interactions (CVPR 2022) https://arxiv.org/abs/2204.06950; overlap: SMPL-H plus object fitting with contact correspondences. Its full-pose hand-object coupling already constrains forearm rotation, which undercuts the claim that 6-DoF closure makes twist observable.; verified=False
- Pose Estimation for Contact Manipulation with Manifold Particle Filters (Koval, Pollard, Srinivasa) (IJRR 2016 (from memory)); overlap: Hybrid contact-mode plus continuous-pose estimation for manipulated objects. The standard machinery behind the proposed mode latent.; verified=False
- Contact-aided invariant extended Kalman filtering for legged robot state estimation (Hartley et al.) (RSS 2018 / IJRR 2020 (from memory)); overlap: Contact-switching kinematic constraints that are switched on and off by contact state. The same structure as an active-only-in-mode grasp-closure factor.; verified=False
- NeuralFeels: Neural fields for visuotactile perception (Suresh et al.) (Science Robotics 2024 (from memory)); overlap: Online pose-graph tracking of an in-hand object with hand kinematics and contact. The factor-graph hand-object coupling is established on the robot side.; verified=False
- Ego4D: Around the World in 3,000 Hours of Egocentric Video (hand-object interaction benchmarks: state change, point-of-no-return) (CVPR 2022 (from memory)); overlap: Temporal localisation of grasp and state-change events as a benchmark task. Overlaps the proposed grasp/release/transfer event F1.; verified=False
- HO-Cap: A Capture System and Dataset for 3D Reconstruction and Pose Tracking of Hand-Object Interaction (NeurIPS 2025 Datasets and Benchmarks) https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06843; overlap: Handovers between one person's two hands with multi-view ground truth. The main evaluation set; its ground truth partly comes from FoundationPose.; verified=False
- HOH: Markerless Multimodal Human-Object-Human Handover Dataset (NeurIPS 2023 Datasets and Benchmarks) https://arxiv.org/html/2310.00723v5; overlap: Annotated handover phases and possession transfer (between two people).; verified=False
- Conformal Risk Control (ICLR 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.02814; overlap: Off-the-shelf method for the risk-bounded 'constrained' label. Adds no novelty on its own.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Drop "causal 4D HOI tracking" as the headline, because it invites a CVPR comparison against OMOMO, VisTracker, IMU-HOI and ComPose that the idea cannot win on novelty. Reposition it as a robotics/estimation paper (RA-L with IROS/ICRA, or CoRL if it feeds a teleoperation or demonstration-capture loop) built on two crisp claims.

(1) An observability result. Show analytically, with the Fisher information or rank of the factor-graph Jacobian, and empirically that a full-SE(3) grasp-closure factor makes shoulder twist and elbow swivel observable from one view, while translation-only closure and pose-only baselines leave them unobservable. Report degree errors against mocap or a second view.

(2) Stale-grasp and slip detection as a measured task. Report detection delay, false alarms per minute, and the downstream arm error avoided. Also provide a small public dataset of single-person bimanual handovers, regrasps and slips with free object ground truth (the ArUco cube hidden from the markerless branch) and contact truth (FSRs on the cube faces), because no existing set labels slip and regrasp.

Present the mode-switching machinery explicitly as standard (IMM / switchable factors, citing Koval 2016 and Hartley 2020). Claim novelty only for the object-anchored arm observability and the slip-detection benchmark. Add a teleoperation or human-to-robot demonstration use case where the object-constrained arm under occlusion visibly changes robot outcomes.

### feasibility skeptic: score 3.5, refuted=True

Feasibility verdict on the idea as written: refuted. The core mechanism is buildable, and a smaller version is feasible. The full package is not, because of three problems: there is no ground truth for the headline claims, the public data does not match the claims, and the engineering scope is larger than the research.

1. The headline claims have no ground truth in the stated budget. Claim (b), recovering shoulder twist, elbow swivel and forearm axial rotation, needs rotational truth for each arm segment.
- Own data has none. The thesis's natural-occlusion evaluation rests on 3-4 manually clicked wrist labels per arm (thesis Table 7.8, loop recording), with no elbow or twist reference.
- The proposed "second reference view" gives a second set of keypoints, not segment rotations. Mocap is only "borrowed", with no source named.
- The candidate public datasets each fail in a different way:
  - HO-Cap annotates MANO hands and objects. To my knowledge it has no body or elbow ground truth, so wrist, elbow and twist error cannot be scored there (not re-verified this session).
  - BEHAVE and InterCap use pseudo ground truth with few-cm and several-degree error. That is the same order as the effect the paper must show, and their objects are mostly large two-handed items with few within-person handovers.
  - ARCTIC has mocap SMPL-X body truth, which is the right truth. But it is RGB-only, so the "metric RGB-D" premise turns into monocular-depth ablations. It also has few true left-to-right possession transfers.
- So the two claims a top-venue reviewer would test first, twist recovery and gains in the 0-2 s after a handover, have no convincing reference anywhere in the plan.

2. The premise fails on the author's own data exactly at the handover. On the loop recording, 40 of the 41 frames without a detected cube marker fall in the two-hand transfer on the desk (thesis Section 7.4.2). The case "object measured, holding hand hidden" is therefore missing at the very moment the mode latent matters most. This can be fixed with a cube carrying a marker on every face, or with FoundationPose on a meshed object, but the idea does not say so.

3. Engineering risk dominates. The method stacks:
- five frozen front ends (RTMPose wholebody, WiLoR or HaMeR, SAM 2, FoundationPose, ArUco);
- a custom SE(3) fixed-lag smoother with switchable robust factors and wrist DoFs;
- IMM or max-mixture mode inference with a handover hazard model;
- CUSUM stale-grasp detection;
- conformal risk control;
- an optional wrench-feasibility QP and an optional learned switching-dynamics model trained on four large datasets;
- a multi-view iSAM2 variant;
- plus 3-10 new subjects under an ethics amendment and dataset downloads that each need approval.

For one student over 12-18 months on a single RTX 3080, that is two to three papers' worth of integration. The research signal (does the 6-DoF closure plus the mode latent help?) can be tested with about a fifth of it. The stated risk that "gains are confined to frames where a tagged object is held" is real: the effect is conditional on a narrow event class. On slow desk tasks there may be too few such frames for significance across subjects.

4. Data and resources it does not say how to obtain:
- Twist or rotation truth: no plan.
- A source for borrowed mocap.
- Contact truth: the force-sensing resistors are "optional".
- Grip-mode labels for slip versus regrasp: these are hard to label from video, and the slip mode may stay on permanently for wrapped grasps. The thesis measured 7.0 / 2.7 / 6.3 cm per-axis scatter of the grip offset on the loop recording.
- Training data for the learned emission model: ARCTIC, OakInk2, TACO and GRAB have different embodiments, no D435 noise, and no handover statistics. It is optional and should be dropped.

What is feasible:
- A GTSAM fixed-lag smoother over torso, two 4- or 6-DoF arms, the object SE(3) and two grasp transforms is small and fits well inside the roughly 20 ms per-frame CPU budget.
- The v3 swing-twist quaternion core gives manifold forward kinematics.
- The thesis's object-conditioned recovery, its 25/35 cm hysteresis and the constant-offset two-link IK are a ready baseline and a ready initializer.
- A second D435 costs about USD 300-400.
- Cheap 6-DoF segment truth is possible: ArUco cuffs on the distal forearm and the upper arm, facing a second D435 and kept out of the primary branch's input or masked. This gives forearm axial rotation and elbow truth at roughly 1-2 cm and a few degrees, which is enough to test twist recovery.

Minimum viable experiment (about 3-4 months):
- Hardware: two D435s plus a cube with markers on all six faces, or the ArUco cube plus FoundationPose on a scanned mesh. Wrist and upper-arm ArUco cuffs are seen only by the reference camera.
- Subjects: three (the author and two labmates).
- Script per subject: 20 left-to-right handovers, 10 regrasps and 10 induced slips. Add deliberate occlusions of the holding hand behind a box, and arm-along-optical-axis poses.
- Methods compared on the same RTMPose plus depth front end:
  - (a) the thesis constant translation-only offset with two-link IK;
  - (b) the smoother with translation-only closure;
  - (c) the smoother with full SE(3) closure;
  - (d) (c) plus a 4-mode HMM (free, L, R, LR) using episode-reset grasp transforms;
  - (e) hold-last;
  - (f) a tangent-space Kalman filter.
- Metrics:
  - wrist and elbow error (cm) on occluded frames and in the 0-2 s after each handover;
  - forearm axial rotation and elbow swivel error (deg) against the cuffs;
  - mode F1 and handover event timing against frame labels from the reference view;
  - stale-grasp detection delay and false alarms per minute from the closure innovation (NIS plus CUSUM) on the induced slips.
- Go criteria:
  - (c) beats (b) on twist by a clear margin, for example over 10 deg median on occluded frames;
  - (d) beats (a) on post-handover wrist error by 30 percent or more, consistently across all three subjects;
  - the CUSUM detects induced slips before the wrist error passes 5 cm in most cases.

If (c) does not beat (b) on twist, the most distinctive claim is dead and the remainder is incremental. A secondary check uses a small ARCTIC subset (needs approval, a few sequences only): ground-truth or noisy object pose plus detected 2D body keypoints, scored against mocap twist. This tests whether the 6-DoF closure recovers twist under clean truth.

Venue realism: the minimum experiment supports an RA-L or ICRA/IROS paper. ICCV or CVPR would need marker-free, multi-subject results and a public-benchmark result that the current dataset plan cannot deliver.

Prior work found:
- ComPose: When to Trust Hands for Object Pose Tracking (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23523; overlap: Trust-gated hand-to-object cueing during occlusion in RGB; one-directional, no arm chain and no handover modes. Could be extended to the reverse direction first.; verified=False
- Visibility Aware Human-Object Interaction Tracking from Single RGB Camera (VisTracker) (CVPR 2023) https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2023/html/Xie_Visibility_Aware_Human-Object_Interaction_Tracking_From_Single_RGB_Camera_CVPR_2023_paper.html; overlap: Infills the occluded object from human motion. Offline, RGB, and human-to-object only.; verified=False
- IMU-HOI: A Symbiotic Framework for Coherent Human-Object Interaction and Motion Capture via Contact-Conscious Inertial Fusion (CVPR 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28604; overlap: Contact-gated fusion of hand forward kinematics and object motion. Uses body-worn IMUs and has no slip or handover modes.; verified=False
- HO-Cap: A Capture System and Dataset for 3D Reconstruction and Pose Tracking of Hand-Object Interaction (NeurIPS 2025 Datasets and Benchmarks) https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06843; overlap: Proposed main benchmark, with handovers between one person's hands. Believed to have no body or elbow ground truth, so arm and twist claims cannot be scored on it. Part of its object ground truth comes from FoundationPose.; verified=False
- ARCTIC: A Dataset for Dexterous Bimanual Hand-Object Manipulation (CVPR 2023) https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2023/html/Fan_ARCTIC_A_Dataset_for_Dexterous_Bimanual_Hand-Object_Manipulation_CVPR_2023_paper.html; overlap: The only listed public source with mocap body (SMPL-X) truth for twist. RGB-only, with few true left-to-right possession transfers.; verified=False
- BEHAVE: Dataset and Method for Tracking Human Object Interactions (CVPR 2022) https://arxiv.org/abs/2204.06950; overlap: Single-view RGB-D human-object tracking evaluated against multi-view pseudo ground truth whose error is comparable to the expected effect size. Mostly large two-handed objects.; verified=False
- Seeing Through Occlusion: Deterministic Arm Kinematic Correction for Robot Teleoperation (AKC) (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19240; overlap: Corrects occluded arm depth from arm lengths, with no object constraint. Already re-implemented in the thesis eval track as a baseline.; verified=False
- BIGS: Bimanual Category-agnostic Interaction Reconstruction from Monocular Videos via 3D Gaussian Splatting (CVPR 2025) https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2025/papers/On_BIGS_Bimanual_Category-agnostic_Interaction_Reconstruction_from_Monocular_Videos_via_3D_CVPR_2025_paper.pdf; overlap: Offline per-sequence optimisation of bimanual hand-object interaction. No causal operation and no grasp-mode switching.; verified=False
- FoundationPose: Unified 6D Pose Estimation and Tracking of Novel Objects (CVPR 2024); overlap: The marker-free object front end. On an RTX 3080 it realistically runs at keyframe rate only, and it needs object meshes.; verified=False
- Conformal Risk Control (ICLR 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.02814; overlap: The proposed method for bounding wrong 'constrained' labels. It needs calibration sets the labelling budget does not supply.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Narrow the work to one RA-L or ICRA/IROS paper: 'Object-to-arm causal smoothing with a switching SE(3) grasp latent: recovering forearm twist and post-handover arm pose from one RGB-D view'.

Keep:
- a GTSAM fixed-lag smoother (2-3 frame lag, 15 frames as an ablation) over torso, two swing-twist arms with a wrist axial DoF, the object SE(3) and two episode-reset grasp transforms;
- a 6-DoF grasp-closure factor, with translation-only closure as the ablation that reproduces the thesis;
- a 4-state HMM (free, L, R, LR) whose emissions are distance, velocity agreement and closure residual;
- NIS plus CUSUM stale-grasp detection.

Drop for this paper:
- the wrench QP, learned switching dynamics, conformal risk control, multi-view iSAM2, WiLoR/HaMeR and SAM 2;
- HO-Cap, BEHAVE and InterCap as primary benchmarks.

Ground truth:
- buy a second D435;
- put markers on all faces of the cube so it stays measured through transfers;
- add ArUco cuffs on the distal forearm and the upper arm, seen only by the reference camera, as cheap 6-DoF segment truth for twist, elbow and wrist;
- record 3 subjects with scripted handovers, regrasps, induced slips and deliberate occlusions.

As a secondary check, use a small ARCTIC subset (mocap body truth) to test twist recovery from object pose.

Pre-register two go/no-go tests:
- full SE(3) closure must clearly beat translation-only closure on forearm twist error;
- the mode latent must cut post-handover wrist error by 30 percent or more against the thesis constant-offset baseline across all subjects.

Only if both pass, extend to FoundationPose marker-free objects and more subjects for a CVPR, ICCV or CoRL submission.

### venue skeptic: score 4.5, refuted=True

Area-chair view. (1) No single headline. The idea bundles a hybrid grasp-mode latent, 6-DoF closure, two-way coupling, CUSUM stale-grasp detection, conformal risk control, an optional wrench QP, learned grip dynamics and a multi-view variant. Reviewers at CVPR or ICCV would read this as a well-engineered factor graph around frozen front ends (RTMPose, WiLoR, FoundationPose, SAM 2), which is the risk the idea itself names. Each piece is standard. The '6-DoF closure makes twist observable' point is textbook redundant-arm IK: end-effector orientation fixes the swivel angle. Its novelty is only that the end effector here is a held object, which reviewers will call incremental unless the twist gain is large and measured against real truth. (2) The evaluation does not match the claims. The headline metrics are elbow, shoulder-twist and swivel error during occlusion and after handovers. HO-Cap has no arm or body truth. BEHAVE and InterCap pseudo ground truth is good only to a few cm, and their large two-handed objects rarely show handovers. Own data is one subject, one D435 and no mocap, and the 3-10 subject extension depends on an ethics amendment, a second camera and labels that do not exist yet. ARCTIC is the only listed set with mocap truth for the body, both hands and the object through bimanual regrasps, and the plan uses it only as a side source. (3) The regime may be narrow. The method helps only when the holding hand is hidden but the object's 6-DoF pose is still measured. In marker-free runs, FoundationPose tends to fail exactly when hands occlude the object, and during handovers both hands cover it. The thesis evidence is one helpful natural window and one failure. Without statistics on how often this regime occurs, reviewers will call the problem niche. Relying on ArUco on own data also invites 'markers make it easy'. (4) Venue fit. A causal estimator with honesty labels, latency budgets and grip-state event detection is a robotics or teleoperation systems contribution, not CVPR or ICCV representation learning. CoRL would need a downstream robot-learning or teleoperation result. RA-L (with an ICRA or IROS presentation) fits best and is reachable. ICCV 2027 acceptance is unlikely as framed. Score is for RA-L as the best-fitting venue. At CVPR or ICCV I would put it at about 3.

Prior work found:
- ComPose: When to Trust Hands for Object Pose Tracking (arXiv 2026 (2605.23523)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23523; overlap: Learns when to use hand evidence to track the object during hand-object interaction. This is the reverse direction of I01's object-to-arm idea, and its authors could plausibly add the reverse direction first. My search budget was used up, so I could not re-check it here. It was marked verified upstream.; verified=False
- ARCTIC: A Dataset for Dexterous Bimanual Hand-Object Manipulation (CVPR 2023) https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2023/html/Fan_ARCTIC_A_Dataset_for_Dexterous_Bimanual_Hand-Object_Manipulation_CVPR_2023_paper.html; overlap: Mocap ground truth for the body (SMPL-X), both hands (MANO) and the object during bimanual regrasps. It is the only public set listed that has real truth for elbow, forearm twist and swivel together with object 6-DoF. I01 uses it only for grip dynamics, but it should be the main benchmark for the twist claim (RGB only). Not re-checked in this session.; verified=False
- HO-Cap: A Capture System and Dataset for 3D Reconstruction and Pose Tracking of Hand-Object Interaction (NeurIPS 2025 Datasets and Benchmarks) https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06843; overlap: Multi-view RGB-D of hand-object interaction, including handovers between one person's own hands. Its annotations are hand and object only (MANO plus object pose), with no arm chain, so elbow and swivel claims cannot be tested on it. Its object truth partly uses FoundationPose, which biases a FoundationPose front end. Not re-checked in this session.; verified=False
- Visibility Aware Human-Object Interaction Tracking from Single RGB Camera (VisTracker) (CVPR 2023) https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2023/html/Xie_Visibility_Aware_Human-Object_Interaction_Tracking_From_Single_RGB_Camera_CVPR_2023_paper.html; overlap: Single-view human-object tracking that infills the occluded object from human motion (human to object). I01 claims the reverse direction plus a causal estimator and grasp modes. Reviewers will see it as the closest single-view HOI tracking baseline. Not re-checked in this session.; verified=False
- IMU-HOI: A Symbiotic Framework for Coherent Human-Object Interaction and Motion Capture via Contact-Conscious Inertial Fusion (CVPR 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28604; overlap: Contact-gated fusion of hand forward kinematics and object motion. Contact-gated human-object coupling therefore already appeared at a 2026 vision venue, which weakens the 'coupling' novelty. Not re-checked in this session.; verified=False
- Seeing Through Occlusion: Deterministic Arm Kinematic Correction for Robot Teleoperation (AKC) (arXiv 2026 (2606.19240)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19240; overlap: Corrects occluded arm depth from arm lengths for teleoperation, with no object prior. This is a direct baseline for causal arm recovery, and it shows the teleoperation and robotics framing is where this problem class is being published. Not re-checked in this session.; verified=False
- Conformal Risk Control (ICLR 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.02814; overlap: I01 would apply this off-the-shelf tool to the 'constrained' label. It adds another contribution line rather than novelty. Not re-checked in this session.; verified=False
- BEHAVE: Dataset and Method for Tracking Human Object Interactions (CVPR 2022) https://arxiv.org/abs/2204.06950; overlap: Single-view RGB-D human-object tracking benchmark with SMPL plus object pseudo ground truth. Its objects are mostly large and handled with two hands, it has few handovers, and its pseudo ground truth is accurate to only a few cm. That is weak for cm-level wrist or twist claims. Not re-checked in this session.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Cut it to one claim: 'The held object is a 6-DoF sensor for the occluded arm: causal recovery of wrist, elbow and forearm twist through grasp changes and handovers.' Keep only (a) the full SE(3) grasp-closure factor and (b) a small 4-mode HMM for grasp switching with re-estimation of the grasp transform. Treat stale-grasp detection as the built-in failure test (innovation and NIS), not as a separate contribution. Drop conformal risk control, the wrench QP and learned grip dynamics, or move them to an appendix. Make ARCTIC the main benchmark: its mocap SMPL-X, MANO and object truth give real twist and swivel error. Use a single RGB view plus estimated or rendered depth, mask the arm synthetically, and also evaluate natural occlusions. Back this with a small new RGB-D handover set: 3-5 subjects, second-view or mocap reference, the ArUco cube kept only as truth. Before committing, report how often frames have the hand hidden but the object visible on ARCTIC, HO-Cap and the new set, to show the regime matters. Main baselines: the thesis constant offset, per-frame WiLoR with Kalman smoothing, AKC, and a ComPose-style one-way variant. Target RA-L with ICRA or IROS presentation, or IROS directly. Use a teleoperation or demonstration-capture framing, where causal operation and honesty labels are real requirements. Aim for CoRL only with a downstream result, for example cleaner human demonstrations improving a learned policy, or teleoperation success rate under occlusion.

## I02: Whose depth is this pixel? Depth-ownership and amodal depth for RGB-D body and hand lifting under object and furniture occlusion

Direction: Robust use of metric sensor depth in RGB-D human and hand pose estimation when the depth at a keypoint belongs to an occluder (held object, desk, the other hand).

Research question: Can a per-keypoint depth-ownership estimate (body surface / held object / scene occluder / invalid) plus an amodal depth offset, learned from synthetic RGB-D and enforced through an explicit kinematic chain, remove the systematic failures of naive depth sampling (wrist taking the cube's depth, hips taking the desk's depth, co-shifting hips that fool every gate) and outperform both RGB-only metric lifting and existing RGB-D fusion modules on interaction datasets?

Why novel (as proposed): AnyHand (arXiv 2603.25726, 2026) adds a lightweight depth-fusion module to RGB hand models trained on synthetic RGB-D with occlusions, but fuses depth implicitly and is hand-only. PromptHMR (CVPR 2025) adds a depth token for body placement but uses no sensor depth. The arm kinematic correction of Kwok, Koenig and Hu (arXiv 2606.19240, 2026; apparently the AKC method the thesis re-implemented, match not confirmed against the thesis bibliography) corrects occluded joint depth with fixed bone lengths and the Pythagorean theorem, a deterministic rule that cannot tell which surface the depth came from and fails when hips shift together. Pyramid Deep Fusion (2023) fuses RGB-D for two hands without explicit occlusion reasoning. The delta is an explicit, supervised ownership-and-amodal-depth layer for body and hands in interaction scenes, evaluated on where depth fusion breaks. The v3 track measured that the obvious rule fails: a depth-front rule flagged the right wrist on 889 of 898 frames and the flags anti-correlated with true occlusion, and RTMPose never abstains.

Method (as proposed): Inputs: RGB crop around each 2D keypoint, the aligned depth patch, SAM 2 masks of person and held object, and the 2D keypoint heatmap. Head: a small CNN/transformer per keypoint predicting (a) ownership class over {body, held object, scene occluder, invalid} and (b) an amodal depth offset d_body - d_sensor with heteroscedastic variance. Training data: Unity renders of the thesis avatar rig and desk scene with randomised subject shape, clothing, camera pose and objects, with per-pixel instance IDs so ownership and amodal depth are exact; AnyHand for hands; D435-style depth noise (edge flying pixels, holes, quantisation) learned from the 28 real recordings. Solver: lift each keypoint with d_sensor + offset weighted by ownership probability, then solve the swing-twist chain with bone-length and joint-limit constraints (v3 quaternion core) so impossible depth assignments are rejected geometrically. Torso: when both hips are owned by the desk, solve the root from shoulders plus a seated-pose prior instead of shifted hips, directly targeting the co-shift failure. Self-training on real data: frames where ownership is certain supervise the noise model; v3 S-type synthetic masks provide paired occluded/unoccluded real samples.

Evaluation (as proposed): Public RGB-D interaction data: BEHAVE and InterCap (Kinect RGB-D, SMPL and object ground truth) for body joints under object occlusion; HO-Cap for hands under hand-object occlusion; AnyHand protocol for hands. Own recordings: wrist labels and tape-measured wrist-to-cube distances. Baselines: naive depth sampling (thesis v1), median-in-mask depth, AKC rule (re-implementation exists in the eval track), PromptHMR / WiLoR RGB-only with metric scale from Depth Anything, AnyHand-style implicit fusion. Metrics: world-frame MPJPE per joint, error split by ownership class, root tilt error on desk-occluded hips, ownership AUROC, calibration of predicted depth variance. Ablations: no ownership head, no kinematic solve, synthetic-only vs synthetic plus self-training, effect of the D435 noise model.

Target venues: CVPR 2027, ICCV 2027, 3DV 2027, WACV 2027 (fallback)

Builds on the thesis: The thesis identified and measured the exact failure modes (hip depth from the desk, wrist depth from the cube, co-shifting hips undetectable by any gate, depth-front rule invalid on this task); the Unity project with the avatar rig provides a renderer with perfect instance IDs; the eval track already contains an AKC re-implementation; the v3 kinematic core and RTMPose pipeline give the solver and detector; recordings give the real-noise model.

Feasibility (as proposed): Fully feasible with existing hardware: a small per-keypoint head trains easily on a 3080; Unity rendering of 100k-500k frames takes a few days. Need: BEHAVE and InterCap subsets (full RGB-D is several hundred GB; user approval for large downloads), a few hundred extra wrist/hip labels. Timeline: month 1 synthetic pipeline with instance IDs and noise; month 2 head training; month 3 kinematic integration and BEHAVE/InterCap evaluation; month 4 ablations and writing. The fastest and safest path to a first-author paper per the lens ranking.

Risks (as proposed): Perceived as incremental (an occlusion-classification head plus depth correction) unless gains on public RGB-D benchmarks are large and the analysis shows when sensor depth beats RGB-only metric foundation models. Sim-to-real gap for D435 noise around hands; BEHAVE/InterCap use Kinect, not RealSense, so the noise model must generalise across sensors.

### novelty skeptic: score 3.5, refuted=True

Search limitation, stated first: the session's WebSearch budget was used up (200 of 200) before this lens ran, and arxiv.org, export.arxiv.org and semanticscholar.org are blocked by the egress proxy. So no new search result could be obtained. Every prior work below comes from the model's own knowledge or from the candidate's upstream list, and all are marked verified=false. The instruction is to default to refuted=true under uncertainty, and the uncertainty here points against novelty rather than for it.

Why the delta looks too small for CVPR/ICCV:
(1) The ingredients are each established. Per-keypoint or per-vertex visibility and occlusion classification is published: Learning Visibility for Robust Dense Human Body Estimation (ECCV 2022) predicts per-vertex x/y/z visibility, and PARE (ICCV 2021) does occlusion-aware part attention. Visibility-conditioned human-object tracking is published: VisTracker (CVPR 2023), and CHORE (ECCV 2022) on BEHAVE. Amodal depth behind occluders is published: Amodal Depth Anything / amodal depth estimation in the wild (2024-2025). Implicit RGB-D fusion trained on synthetic occlusions is published: AnyHand (2026), and Pyramid Deep Fusion for two hands (2023). Kinematic correction of occluded joint depth is published: Kwok, Koenig and Hu, AKC (2026).
(2) The proposed combination (visibility class + depth residual + kinematic solve) is a standard modular recipe. Reviewers will see it as engineering on top of those works, especially since BEHAVE and InterCap already fit SMPL to Kinect depth with person/object segmentation, which implicitly rejects object-owned depth. The ownership label {body, object, scene, invalid} is equivalent to instance segmentation of the depth patch at the keypoint. SAM 2 masks plus a segmentation-aware depth median (a baseline already listed) recover most of it. The remaining contribution is then the amodal offset for joints whose surface is not visible. That is closer to amodal depth / occluded-joint regression, which RGB-only metric HMR (PromptHMR 2025, Multi-HMR 2024, SAM 3D Body 2025) already does implicitly.
(3) Benchmark risk. Public RGB-D interaction sets with body ground truth (BEHAVE, InterCap) are Kinect-based, and their ground truth was itself fitted from that depth, which biases the evaluation. Hands-under-object RGB-D (HO-Cap, DexYCB) is already saturated by RGB methods.
(4) The thesis-specific failures are real but narrow and come from one subject and one desk: the desk-hip co-shift, and the depth-front rule flagging 889 of 898 frames. They show that a naive heuristic fails, not that a learned module is a top-venue contribution.

Novelty for CVPR/ICCV: about 3-4/10. A 3DV, WACV or RA-L/ICRA paper is plausible.

Prior work found:
- Learning Visibility for Robust Dense Human Body Estimation (ECCV 2022); overlap: Predicts per-vertex visibility along x, y and depth (occluded vs visible) to make body estimation robust to occlusion. This is the same 'which parts are actually observed' signal the ownership head would learn, at dense vertex level, though RGB-only.; verified=False
- VisTracker: Visibility-aware Human-Object Interaction Tracking from Single RGB Camera (CVPR 2023); overlap: Explicitly estimates object visibility under human occlusion and uses it to condition 3D human/object tracking on BEHAVE/InterCap, the exact datasets proposed for evaluation.; verified=False
- CHORE: Contact, Human and Object REconstruction from a single RGB image (ECCV 2022); overlap: Joint human-object reconstruction on BEHAVE with occlusion-aware fitting, a baseline family the idea must beat on the same benchmark.; verified=False
- BEHAVE: Dataset and Method for Tracking Human Object Interactions (CVPR 2022); overlap: Fits SMPL and the object to Kinect RGB-D point clouds with person/object segmentation, which already separates body-owned from object-owned depth. Its ground truth was derived from the same sensor depth.; verified=False
- AnyHand: A Large-Scale Synthetic Dataset for RGB(-D) Hand Pose Estimation (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.25726; overlap: Synthetic RGB-D with occlusions and a lightweight depth-fusion module for hands. It covers the hand half of the idea with implicit rather than explicit ownership. Listed as verified upstream; not re-checked in this session.; verified=False
- Seeing Through Occlusion: Deterministic Arm Kinematic Correction for Robot Teleoperation (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19240; overlap: Corrects occluded arm joint depth with bone-length constraints, the kinematic-solve half of the idea. Listed as verified upstream; not re-checked in this session.; verified=False
- PromptHMR: Promptable Human Mesh Recovery (CVPR 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06397; overlap: Metric world placement of the body from RGB with depth/scale prompts. It is the strong RGB-only baseline the idea must beat to justify sensor depth.; verified=False
- Amodal Depth Anything / Amodal Depth Estimation in the Wild (arXiv 2024 / ICCV 2025 (venue not confirmed)); overlap: Predicts depth of occluded regions given an amodal mask, which is the 'amodal depth offset' component, for generic objects.; verified=False
- Pyramid Deep Fusion Network for Two-Hand Reconstruction from RGB-D Images (arXiv 2023) https://arxiv.org/pdf/2307.06038; overlap: RGB-D fusion for interacting hands, where mutual occlusion makes the depth belong to the other hand. Listed as verified upstream.; verified=False
- Occlusion-Aware 3D Hand-Object Pose Estimation with Masked AutoEncoders (IEEE TMM 2026 (per upstream snippet)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10816; overlap: Occlusion-aware hand-object pose. Covers the explicit occlusion-reasoning claim for hands. Listed as verified upstream.; verified=False
- PARE: Part Attention Regressor for 3D Human Body Estimation (ICCV 2021); overlap: Occlusion-aware per-part attention, an earlier form of reasoning about which body parts are reliably observed.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Do not sell an ownership head. Recast the work as an analysis plus a benchmark: 'When does sensor depth help metric human and hand pose in contact-rich desk manipulation?' It would have three parts.
(a) A small, properly registered RGB-D benchmark. Use RealSense, several subjects, and an independent ground truth: a second calibrated camera or IMU/mocap, not depth-fitted. Include desk and held-object occlusion labels, so it is not circular the way BEHAVE/InterCap ground truth is.
(b) A controlled comparison: naive depth sampling vs segmentation-median vs AKC vs RGB-only metric HMR (PromptHMR, SAM 3D Body, WiLoR + Depth Anything) vs implicit fusion (AnyHand-style). The comparison is broken down by occlusion type, including the hip co-shift.
(c) A calibrated per-joint trust/abstention output, evaluated as selective prediction (risk-coverage). This is the thesis's 'honesty flags' made learnable, and plain visibility papers do not evaluate it.
Target 3DV, WACV, or RA-L/ICRA (teleoperation and digital-twin reliability) rather than CVPR. Alternatively, merge it into a broader 'learned measurement trust for kinematic tracking' paper where ownership is one input feature.

### feasibility skeptic: score 5.5, refuted=False

Feasibility verdict: buildable with the stated lab, but the plan as written has three weak points. (1) The real-data ground truth does not match the claimed failure modes. (2) The training-data source is the hardest part, not a few days of rendering. (3) A strong, cheap baseline is missing and may absorb most of the gain. None of these needs resources the lab cannot get, so the idea is not refuted. It needs the narrower scope in suggested_reframe.

What is feasible. The model is small: a per-keypoint classifier plus a depth-offset regressor on depth/RGB patches trains easily on one RTX 3080. The detector (RTMPose, under 11 ms p99) and the quaternion kinematic core exist in v3. An AKC re-implementation exists in eval/akc_comparison (eval/README.md lines 64-69). Synthetic ownership labels are exact by construction, because a renderer with instance IDs gives per-pixel owner and amodal depth. Public RGB-D sets with registered body/object meshes (BEHAVE, InterCap) let you derive pseudo-labels for ownership and amodal depth on real data: render the GT SMPL and object meshes into the camera and compare them with sensor depth. This is the strongest feasibility argument, because it gives real-image supervision and evaluation without motion capture.

Problems a skeptic would raise:
(a) The motivating evidence is weaker than presented. V3_FINDINGS lines 69-77 say the depth-front rule flagged the right wrist on 889/898 frames "because the subject reaches forward throughout". That is a badly specified heuristic, not proof that ownership is hard. In the same run, shoulders, elbows and hips got zero hits. In-repo evidence for hip/desk ownership failure is limited to the thesis's co-shift observation on one subject. There are no labels and no frequency count.
(b) The two headline failures come from the author's own desk-and-cube setup: hips hidden by the desk, and wrist depth read off the held cube. BEHAVE/InterCap mostly show standing or sitting people with chairs, boxes and large objects, not seated desk work with the pelvis hidden. The torso co-shift fix would be tested only on synthetic data and on the author's one-subject recordings, which have no hip ground truth. The own-data evaluation is therefore anecdotal: a handful of manually labelled wrists plus tape-measured wrist-to-cube distances.
(c) Evaluation circularity: BEHAVE/InterCap SMPL fits were made partly from the same Kinect depth. Methods that use depth are scored against GT built from that depth, and reviewers will discount the gains. You need a held-out-view protocol: fit GT from the other views, evaluate the single view.
(d) Missing strong baseline: SAM 2 person mask plus robust SMPL/MANO fitting to the masked point cloud, with a visibility-aware data term. This is classical RGB-D model fitting. It already rejects cube/desk pixels through segmentation, and it may close most of the gap that the learned ownership head targets. Without it, the comparison set (naive sampling, median-in-mask, AKC, RGB-only plus monocular metric depth) is a strawman for CVPR.
(e) The synthetic pipeline is underestimated. The Unity project holds one rig (Unity/Assets/Models/female-character-rig-unity.fbx). "Randomised subject shape, clothing" means importing SMPL-X bodies, clothing assets, pose sources (AMASS) and object/hand-grasp placement. That is 2-3 months of engineering, not part of month 1. Learning a D435 noise model from 28 one-subject recordings is plausible but unvalidated. Transfer to Kinect (BEHAVE/InterCap) and to D415 (hand sets) is unproven.
(f) The 4-month timeline is optimistic. Synthetic data, pseudo-label derivation, a held-out-view protocol, kinematic integration, four baselines and ablations need 8-12 months for one student. That fits the 12-18 month window, but leaves no slack for a second iteration after a reject.
(g) Data download needs user approval (BEHAVE/InterCap raw RGB-D is hundreds of GB). It is free and obtainable, so it is not a blocker.

Minimum viable experiment (4-6 weeks, no new rendering). Take 3-5 BEHAVE sequences, one Kinect view, and a few DexYCB or HO-3D sequences (RealSense RGB-D with MANO GT; sensor details from memory, unverified). Derive per-keypoint ownership and amodal-depth pseudo-labels by rendering the GT meshes against sensor depth. Report:
(i) how often naive keypoint depth belongs to the object or scene, and the MPJPE it causes per joint;
(ii) whether a small head trained on half the sequences predicts ownership (AUROC) on the rest;
(iii) whether ownership-weighted lifting beats naive sampling, median-in-mask, and masked point-cloud SMPL/MANO fitting on per-joint world MPJPE under held-out-view GT.
If (iii) shows no clear win over masked fitting, stop: the idea is not a paper. If it wins, add synthetic pretraining and the kinematic solve.

Overall: no missing resources and real GT is obtainable, so it is not refuted on feasibility. The engineering risk of the full Unity pipeline and the risk that a segmentation-plus-fitting baseline erases the gain are both substantial.

Prior work found:
- AnyHand: A Large-Scale Synthetic Dataset for RGB(-D) Hand Pose Estimation (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.25726; overlap: Synthetic RGB-D with occlusions plus a light depth-fusion module for hands; covers the hand half of the idea with implicit fusion. Not re-checked this session (search budget exhausted); listed as verified by the idea generator.; verified=False
- Seeing Through Occlusion: Deterministic Arm Kinematic Correction for Robot Teleoperation (AKC) (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19240; overlap: Deterministic bone-length correction of occluded arm depth; the repo's eval/README.md cites it and holds a re-implementation that serves as a ready baseline. No web search confirmed it this session.; verified=False
- PromptHMR: Promptable Human Mesh Recovery (CVPR 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06397; overlap: RGB-only body recovery with metric placement; the RGB-only baseline. Not re-checked this session.; verified=False
- Pyramid Deep Fusion Network for Two-Hand Reconstruction from RGB-D Images (arXiv 2023) https://arxiv.org/pdf/2307.06038; overlap: RGB-D fusion for two hands without explicit occlusion ownership. Not re-checked this session.; verified=False
- BEHAVE: Dataset and Method for Tracking Human Object Interactions (CVPR 2022); overlap: Multi-view Kinect RGB-D with registered SMPL and object meshes; the main source of real ownership pseudo-labels, but its GT is fit partly from the same depth (circularity), and it has little desk-occluded-hip content.; verified=False
- DexYCB: A Benchmark for Capturing Hand Grasping of Objects (CVPR 2021); overlap: Multi-view RealSense RGB-D with MANO and object GT (sensor details from memory); a closer sensor match than Kinect for the hand-on-object ownership case.; verified=False
- BEDLAM: A Synthetic Dataset of Bodies Exhibiting Detailed Lifelike Animated Motion (CVPR 2023); overlap: Existing large synthetic human renders with ground truth (depth and masks believed available); could replace most of the custom Unity body-rendering effort.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Narrow to a measurement-first paper: "When does sensor depth help RGB-D pose under hand-object and scene occlusion?" Step 1: derive per-keypoint depth-ownership and amodal-depth pseudo-labels on public RGB-D interaction sets (BEHAVE/InterCap one-view, DexYCB/HO-3D) by rendering the GT meshes against sensor depth. Use a held-out-view GT protocol to avoid circularity. Step 2: benchmark naive sampling, median-in-mask, masked point-cloud SMPL/MANO fitting, AKC, and RGB-only metric methods, broken down by ownership class. Step 3: add a small learned ownership-plus-offset head (pretrained on existing synthetic data such as BEDLAM or AnyHand rather than a new Unity pipeline) and a bone-length kinematic solve. Use the author's D435 desk recordings only as a qualitative case study of the desk-hip co-shift failure. Gate the project on the 4-6 week minimum viable experiment beating masked point-cloud fitting. Target 3DV/WACV first, and CVPR only if the gain over the masked-fitting baseline is large.

### venue skeptic: score 3.5, refuted=True

Read as an area chair for CVPR or ICCV 2027, this would most likely be rejected as incremental. The likely review is "an occlusion classifier plus a depth-offset regressor in front of a kinematic solver". Six issues drive that verdict.

1. The headline claim is narrow. "Naive per-keypoint depth sampling fails when an occluder owns the pixel" is well known. Since SMPLify-D-style fitting, RGB-D body fitting has used robust depth terms (one-sided or visibility-masked point-to-surface terms with Geman-McClure losses) for exactly this problem. The project's own evidence that the obvious rule fails (889 of 898 wrist frames flagged, a desk-hip co-shift) is a single-subject anecdote from a D435 desk scene. It is not a gap that the field recognises.

2. The baselines are the wrong ones. The stated set is naive sampling, median-in-mask, the AKC rule, RGB-only methods given metric scale by Depth Anything, and AnyHand-style implicit fusion. Reviewers on BEHAVE and InterCap will instead ask for:
   - the RGB-D fitting baselines that ship with those datasets;
   - object-occlusion-aware human-object interaction (HOI) reconstruction (CHORE, VisTracker, HOLD-style methods);
   - visibility-aware HMR (VisDB, OCHMR-style methods);
   - SMPL fitting on a depth point cloud with a robust loss.
   Beating naive sampling and a deterministic teleoperation heuristic (AKC, an arXiv teleop paper) is not convincing at CVPR.

3. The evaluation depends on the wrong data. BEHAVE and InterCap are Kinect-based and use moderate occlusion. Their ground truth comes from multi-view Kinect depth fitting. Desk-occluded hips, the most distinctive failure here, barely occur in those datasets. The project's own data is one subject, one room, about 28 recordings and no motion capture, so it cannot carry a top-venue claim.

4. The method's novelty is thin. The parts are:
   - per-pixel instance-ID supervision from synthetic renders, which is standard;
   - heteroscedastic offsets, which are standard;
   - a bone-length-constrained chain solve, which is standard;
   - a seated-pose prior for the root.
   The amodal-depth framing overlaps the growing amodal depth and amodal completion literature (2024-2025). That overlap is from memory, not verified here.

5. There is a bigger threat: monocular metric foundation models (Depth Anything, PromptHMR-style placement, the WiLoR/HaMeR family for hands) keep reducing how much sensor depth matters. A paper that only patches sensor-depth sampling, without settling when sensor depth still wins, will be judged niche.

6. Hands are already partly covered by AnyHand's synthetic RGB-D fusion, so a hand-only story is not new either.

Credible outcomes: a 3DV or WACV paper with a solid synthetic benchmark and a real-data analysis, or an IROS or RA-L paper framed for teleoperation or digital twins (the deterministic AKC prior work sits there). An unremarkable CVPR or ICCV submission would most likely be rejected.

Caveat: the web search budget was exhausted in this session. The prior work below that is not taken from the candidate's own verified list is cited from memory and marked verified=false.

Prior work found:
- AnyHand: A Large-Scale Synthetic Dataset for RGB(-D) Hand Pose Estimation (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.25726; overlap: Synthetic RGB-D with occlusions plus a light depth-fusion module for hands. This covers the hand half of the idea, with implicit rather than explicit ownership.; verified=True
- Seeing Through Occlusion: Deterministic Arm Kinematic Correction for Robot Teleoperation (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19240; overlap: Deterministic correction of occluded arm joint depth using bone lengths. It is the main baseline, but it is a teleoperation heuristic, not a top-venue vision baseline.; verified=True
- PromptHMR: Promptable Human Mesh Recovery (CVPR 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06397; overlap: RGB-only human placement with a depth prompt. It represents the monocular metric alternative that weakens the case for sensor depth.; verified=True
- Pyramid Deep Fusion Network for Two-Hand Reconstruction from RGB-D Images (arXiv 2023) https://arxiv.org/pdf/2307.06038; overlap: RGB-D fusion for interacting hands without explicit occlusion ownership.; verified=True
- Occlusion-Aware 3D Hand-Object Pose Estimation with Masked AutoEncoders (IEEE TMM 2026 (per search snippet)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10816; overlap: Explicit occlusion handling for hand-object pose; it overlaps the occlusion-reasoning claim.; verified=True
- BEHAVE: Dataset and Method for Tracking Human Object Interactions (CVPR 2022); overlap: The proposed evaluation dataset. It ships Kinect RGB-D fitting baselines that already handle object occlusion through depth fitting.; verified=False
- Visibility Aware Human-Object Interaction Tracking from Single RGB Camera (VisTracker) (CVPR 2023); overlap: Explicit visibility reasoning for humans occluded by objects on BEHAVE and InterCap. Reviewers would expect it as a baseline.; verified=False
- Learning Visibility for Robust Dense Human Body Estimation (VisDB) (ECCV 2022); overlap: Per-vertex and per-joint visibility prediction for occluded body estimation. This is conceptually close to the ownership head.; verified=False
- SMPLify-X style RGB-D fitting with robust depth terms (e.g., PROX) (ICCV 2019); overlap: Classical RGB-D body fitting with robust depth and scene contact terms, which already reduces occluder-depth contamination.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Recommended reframe: drop "per-keypoint ownership head" as the headline and make the paper a question about when sensor depth beats monocular metric models, with a benchmark plus a method.

1. Headline. Ask: "When does consumer sensor depth still beat monocular metric foundation models for body and hand pose under interaction occlusion, and how do you use it without being fooled?"

2. Main contribution. Build a controlled synthetic-plus-real RGB-D occlusion benchmark from the Unity rig, with exact pixel ownership, amodal depth and realistic D435 and Kinect noise. Pair it with a systematic study across BEHAVE, InterCap and HO-Cap comparing:
   - sensor-depth pipelines;
   - Depth Anything with PromptHMR, WiLoR or HaMeR;
   - RGB-D fitting baselines;
   - visibility-aware HOI methods.

3. Method. Present the ownership and amodal-depth module as the fix that turns sensor depth from a liability into an advantage. Show that it plugs into existing HMR or HOI fitting as a weighted, ownership-gated depth term, not only into the thesis's own chain.

4. Venue. Target 3DV 2027 or the CVPR or NeurIPS Datasets and Benchmarks track. If the system and teleoperation angle stays central (it builds on the AKC teleop work and the digital-twin use case), RA-L or IROS fits better than CVPR or ICCV.

5. Extra evidence needed to reach CVPR or ICCV:
   - a multi-subject real dataset with motion-capture or multi-view ground truth that covers desk-occluded hips;
   - large gains over the visibility-aware and RGB-D fitting baselines, not only over naive sampling and AKC.

## I03: Calibrated honesty: provenance- and age-conditional conformal error intervals, abstention, stale-state detection and source arbitration for causal kinematic tracking

Direction: Distribution-free uncertainty quantification and selective prediction for sequential, mode-switching 3D pose trackers on manifolds, where several noisy sources (body detector, hand model, object pose, kinematic prior) compete and carried state goes stale under occlusion.

Research question: Can the thesis's categorical honesty labels (measured / held / constrained) be replaced by per-joint-group swing-twist error intervals whose finite-sample coverage holds conditionally on the provenance state, the time since last measurement and an observability score, and stays valid on natural occlusions under temporal dependence (adaptive conformal inference), within the 30 fps budget; and does using these intervals to (a) decide measured / estimated / abstain, (b) arbitrate between detector, object-conditioned and kinematic-forecast sources, and (c) detect stale carried state (grip offsets, frozen twists) give a better risk-coverage curve, lower error and earlier, fewer-false-alarm stale-state detection than detector confidence, hand-set gates and the thesis's fixed rules?

Why novel (as proposed): CUPS (ICML 2025) and CHAMP (ICLR 2025) conformalize 3D human pose estimators on RGB benchmarks without interaction occlusions, competing sources or online streaming coverage. Conformalized Kalman Filters (arXiv 2609.27506, 2026) give sample-wise and trajectory-wise guarantees but for a single-mode filter in Euclidean state. Thumm et al. (arXiv 2604.15221, 2026) give conformal sets for human pose and motion prediction for HRC assuming visible keypoints and no carried state. Adaptive Geodesic Conformal Prediction (arXiv 2605.00233, 2026) shows marginal CP covers only about 60 percent of the hardest quartile on SE(3). CCNet (ICML 2024) calibrates 2D keypoint confidence; Hand Visibility Detector (arXiv 2608.11574, 2026) predicts per-keypoint visibility, not 3D error; Meta Pose (arXiv 2608.03516) detects rigid-object pose failures; ComPose (arXiv 2605.23523) learns a trust weighting without calibrated intervals; EvaPose (arXiv 2602.23618) uses visibility labels without coverage guarantees; the CVPR 2023 conformal keypoint work targets objects. The delta: coverage conditioned on a discrete provenance mode whose error grows with hold age and on chain observability; swing-twist geodesic scores; a 'measured' label allowed only when the interval is below a declared tolerance; uncertainty used as the arbitration signal inside the kinematic solve; a stale-state sequential test; and an honesty-scored evaluation (false_measured, coverage under occlusion). Honest caveat: Mondrian / localized / adaptive conformal machinery exists; the contribution is the mode-and-age conditional design, the manifold score, stale-state detection, and the demonstration that marginal coverage hides failure in held states.

Method (as proposed): Base estimator: v3 S2 strategy plus tangent-space Kalman, or the fixed-lag factor graph of I01; per joint group it gives a marginal covariance. State: shoulder swing on S2, twist on S1, elbow flexion on an interval, wrist in R3, torso in SE(3). Features per joint group per frame: provenance mode, frames since last measurement (age), observability (smallest information eigenvalue along that group's DOF), detector score history and heatmap entropy, bone-length residual, depth-jump and prediction-relative depth residual (measured minus predicted from carried state), depth-ownership outputs (I02), object pose residual, hand-object distance and grasp mode, grip age, forearm angle to the optical axis, covariance trace. Model: a small causal GRU / 1D conv (under 1 ms) or MLP / quantile regressor predicting a difficulty scale or nonconformity per source (detector, object-conditioned, kinematic forecast). Nonconformity: geodesic swing angle and wrapped twist error divided by the learned scale, or Mahalanobis error under the factor-graph covariance. Calibration: Mondrian bins over (mode, age bin, observability bin) with localized weighting; adaptive conformal inference (online miscoverage update) for drift; a trajectory-wise max-over-horizon variant for whole occlusion gaps. Decision: measured / inferred / abstain by thresholding calibrated risk, with the swing-twist chain kept as the feasibility layer; arbitration by inverse interval width inside the kinematic solve; a sequential test on scores of carried quantities when partial evidence returns triggers a state reset. Calibration and test labels: v3 S-type synthetic masking of clean real windows (truth = unmasked measurement) plus new grip-shift episodes, Unity renders with exact truth and D435-like noise, BEHAVE/InterCap/HO-Cap single view with multi-view truth, and own recordings with a cheap reference (forearm IMU or ArUco cuff). Unity draws interval width instead of binary red frozen joints.

Evaluation (as proposed): Metrics: empirical coverage at 80/90/95 percent overall, per mode (measured; held age 1-5, 6-15, 16+ frames; constrained), per occluder class and in the worst age/observability decile; mean interval width (deg, cm); trajectory-wise gap coverage; AURC / risk-coverage; false_measured redefined as 'labelled measured while truth outside tolerance', false_inferred; stale-state detection delay and false alarms; MPJPE after arbitration; added latency; a downstream measure (fraction of frames a digital-twin or teleoperation consumer can safely use). Baselines: thesis rule flags and gates, detector confidence thresholds, Gaussian Kalman covariance intervals, conformalized Kalman (2609.27506), marginal split CP, CHAMP-style multi-hypothesis sets from a lifter, CUPS-style deep uncertainty without online adaptation, MC-dropout / ensembles, ComPose-style learned weighting, a Meta-Pose-style logistic failure classifier, a Hand-Visibility-Detector-style head. Ablations: Euclidean vs geodesic score; drop age / observability / object / kinematic-residual / prediction-relative depth features; static vs adaptive conformal; synthetic-only vs mixed calibration.

Target venues: ICCV 2027, CVPR 2027, NeurIPS 2027 (if a theorem on age-indexed coverage under a drift bound is added), ICRA 2027 / IEEE RA-L, CoRL 2027

Builds on the thesis: Honesty labels and the Unity rule 'apply numbers, never invent them' are the motivation; the provenance labels are exactly the Mondrian partition; v3 already defines false_measured and teleport budgets; v3 S-type synthetic masking gives truth for held and constrained states without mocap; swing-twist kinematics gives the manifold; the v3 causal chain and tangent Kalman give base estimator and covariance; the 12 cm causal-offline divergence, elbow placed on the hand with high confidence, co-shifting hips, and RTMPose never abstaining (V3 D-017) are the motivating failures; the thesis's future-work item 3 is this layer made rigorous.

Feasibility (as proposed): Compute-light (small temporal model, CPU-feasible inference). No new hardware strictly required for synthetic and public-data parts; for real truth on own recordings a 9-axis forearm IMU (about USD 30-100) or an ArUco cuff plus 3-6 subjects. Needs BEHAVE/InterCap/HO-Cap downloads (approval over 1 GB), Unity renders with a depth-noise model (2-3 weeks), a few hundred manual labels on natural windows. Timeline: month 1 calibration harness on S-types; months 2-3 learned difficulty, ACI and arbitration; month 4 public-data transfer; month 5 stale-state and cuff/IMU experiments; month 6 writing. Can stand alone or serve as the uncertainty section of I01.

Risks (as proposed): Judged incremental ('conformal applied to pose tracking') given CUPS, CHAMP, conformalized Kalman filters and the 2026 conformal-HRC paper; ML venues will want a theoretical piece. Exchangeability is violated in streams and between synthetic calibration occlusions and natural occlusions (D-017 found weak labels anti-correlate with truth), so adaptive conformal gives only long-run guarantees and coverage may fail on real data; report honestly. Held-state intervals may become uselessly wide after a few frames. One-subject lab data looks thin; gains over well-tuned gates may be modest on slow desk tasks.

### novelty skeptic: score 3.5, refuted=True

Process note: the WebSearch budget for this session ran out before any query returned results. All four queries failed with "search budget exhausted": conformal 3D human pose tracking with occlusion and online coverage; conformal hand-object pose with abstention; Mondrian state-conditional coverage by time since last measurement; geodesic conformal on SO(3). So none of the works below was confirmed by a search during this run, and all are marked verified=false. The assessment rests on the idea's own prior-work list and on works I know up to mid-2026.

Verdict: the methodological delta is too small for a top CV or ML venue. Each component already exists in published work:
(1) Coverage conditioned on provenance mode x age bin x observability bin is group-conditional or Mondrian conformal. Gibbs, Cherian and Candes (Conformal Prediction with Conditional Guarantees, JRSSB 2025, arXiv 2305.12616) already give exact coverage over any finite family of groups, and over function classes of covariates such as hold age treated as a continuous covariate. "Coverage conditional on mode and age" is therefore a direct instantiation, not a new method. Localized conformal (Guan, Biometrika 2023) covers the observability weighting.
(2) Online validity under temporal dependence is Adaptive Conformal Inference (Gibbs and Candes, NeurIPS 2021) and Conformal PID Control (Angelopoulos, Candes and Tibshirani, NeurIPS 2023). Trajectory-wise or max-over-horizon coverage is in conformal time-series forecasting (Stankeviciute et al., NeurIPS 2021) and in the robotics conformal planning line: Lindemann et al., RA-L 2023; Dixit et al., L4DC 2023, adaptive conformal for dynamic agents.
