(3) A filter-covariance-based conformal state estimator is the Conformalized Kalman Filter (arXiv 2609.27506), which the idea itself cites.
(4) Conformal sets for 3D human pose estimators are CUPS (ICML 2025) and CHAMP (ICLR 2025); for object pose, the conformal keypoint work (Yang and Pavone, CVPR 2023); for vision-based HRC pose and motion, Thumm et al. (arXiv 2604.15221).
(5) Geodesic and manifold scores on SE(3) are Adaptive Geodesic Conformal Prediction (arXiv 2605.00233), which already makes the "marginal coverage hides the hardest quartile" point the idea wants to claim.
(6) "Stale-state detection by a sequential test on nonconformity scores" is conformal test martingales and conformal change-point detection (Vovk et al., Testing exchangeability online, ICML 2003; Volkhonskiy et al., conformal change detection, 2017; Vovk's conformal test martingale papers 2021).
(7) Abstain or label "measured" only when the interval is below a tolerance is selective prediction or conformal risk control (Angelopoulos et al., ICLR 2024). Source arbitration by inverse interval width is standard inverse-variance fusion with a learned scale, close to ComPose-style trust weighting (arXiv 2605.23523).

What remains new is application-level: provenance labels (measured / held / constrained) from a swing-twist kinematic tracker used as the Mondrian partition in occluded bimanual handling, plus an honesty-scored evaluation. A CVPR or ICCV reviewer would call this "Mondrian plus ACI applied to a home-built tracker". For NeurIPS, no theorem is proposed beyond what the conditional-guarantee and ACI papers already give.

The empirical base is also weak for a top venue. There is one subject and one desk setup; calibration uses synthetic masking that D-017 showed anti-correlates with truth on natural occlusions; and public HOI truth (BEHAVE, InterCap, HO-Cap) is not yet downloaded. Exchangeability between synthetic-masked calibration and natural occlusion is the core assumption and is precisely the one most likely to fail. The idea would then report a coverage failure, which is a finding, not a method.

A realistic venue as written is RA-L/ICRA or IROS as a systems-plus-UQ paper, not CVPR, ICCV or NeurIPS.

Prior work found:
- Conformal Prediction with Conditional Guarantees (Gibbs, Cherian, Candes) (JRSSB 2025 (arXiv 2023)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.12616; overlap: Exact group-conditional coverage over arbitrary finite groups and covariate function classes; mode x age-bin x observability-bin coverage is a direct special case, and hold age can be a continuous covariate.; verified=False
- Adaptive Conformal Inference Under Distribution Shift (Gibbs, Candes) (NeurIPS 2021) https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.00170; overlap: Online miscoverage update giving long-run coverage under drift; exactly the streaming mechanism proposed.; verified=False
- Conformal PID Control for Time Series Prediction (Angelopoulos, Candes, Tibshirani) (NeurIPS 2023) https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.16895; overlap: Improved online conformal for dependent sequences, with scorecasting; it is a stronger baseline than plain ACI and subsumes the 'adaptive conformal for drift' component.; verified=False
- Conformalized Kalman Filters for State Estimation with Trustworthy Confidence Regions (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27506; overlap: Conformal calibration of filter covariance with sample-wise and trajectory-wise guarantees; matches the 'Mahalanobis under factor-graph covariance' score and the trajectory-wise gap variant.; verified=False
- CUPS: Improving Human Pose-Shape Estimators with Conformalized Deep Uncertainty (ICML 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10431; overlap: Conformalized learned uncertainty for 3D human pose and shape; the learned difficulty scale divided into the nonconformity score is the same recipe.; verified=False
- CHAMP: Conformalized 3D Human Multi-Hypothesis Pose Estimators (ICLR 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.06141; overlap: Conformal prediction sets for 3D human pose.; verified=False
- Vision-Based Safe Human-Robot Collaboration with Uncertainty Guarantees (Thumm et al.) (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15221; overlap: Conformal sets for human pose and motion in an HRC streaming setting; the application story (safe downstream consumer) is already taken.; verified=False
- Adaptive Geodesic Conformal Prediction for Egocentric Camera Pose Estimation (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00233; overlap: Geodesic nonconformity on SE(3) with adaptive (difficulty-conditioned) calibration; it already shows marginal CP under-covers hard cases, which pre-empts the 'swing-twist geodesic score' and 'marginal hides failures' claims.; verified=False
- Object Pose Estimation with Statistical Guarantees: Conformal Keypoint Detection and Geometric Uncertainty Propagation (Yang, Pavone) (CVPR 2023) https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.12246; overlap: Conformal keypoints propagated into calibrated pose uncertainty for objects.; verified=False
- Safe Planning in Dynamic Environments using Conformal Prediction (Lindemann et al.) (IEEE RA-L 2023) https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.10254; overlap: Trajectory-wise or multi-step conformal regions for tracked agents, used by a downstream safety consumer.; verified=False
- Adaptive Conformal Prediction for Motion Planning among Dynamic Agents (Dixit et al.) (L4DC 2023) https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.00278; overlap: Online adaptive conformal regions for predicted agent states in a closed loop.; verified=False
- Conformal Risk Control (Angelopoulos, Bates, Fisch, Lei, Schuster) (ICLR 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.02814; overlap: Calibrated thresholds for abstain or accept decisions controlling an arbitrary loss; covers the measured / inferred / abstain decision layer.; verified=False
- Conformal test martingales / conformal change-point detection (Vovk et al.; Volkhonskiy et al.) (ICML 2003 / COPA 2017 / 2021 follow-ups); overlap: Sequential tests on conformal p-values for change detection; the proposed stale-state sequential test is an instance.; verified=False
- ComPose: When to Trust Hands for Object Pose Tracking (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23523; overlap: Learned trust weighting between hand-derived and object-derived pose sources; close to the source-arbitration component.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Drop the claim of a new conformal method. Reposition the idea in one of two ways.

(a) Empirical and benchmark paper for a CV venue. Title it something like "Marginal coverage lies under occlusion: an age-stratified audit of uncertainty in hand-object and body trackers". Run the audit on public multi-view HOI data with exact truth (HO-Cap, ARCTIC, DexYCB, HOT3D, BEHAVE). Take several off-the-shelf trackers (body, hand and object) and show that CUPS / CHAMP / conformalized-KF / ACI intervals that are marginally valid under-cover badly in held and occluded states, as a function of occlusion age and observability. Then show that a cheap fix closes most of the gap: group-conditional calibration in the style of Gibbs-Cherian-Candes, with age and observability as covariates, plus Conformal PID. The release (protocol, occlusion-age labels, false_measured metric) is the contribution. The thesis tracker is one row, not the subject.

(b) Theory-plus-robotics paper (RA-L / ICRA / CoRL). Prove an age-indexed coverage bound for carried state. Under a bounded-drift or Lipschitz motion assumption, an interval calibrated at age k inflates to a provable interval at age k+j, giving finite-sample coverage for whole occlusion gaps without calibration data at every age. Pair it with a downstream consumer, such as a teleoperation or robot handover that uses only frames certified as safe, and measure task-level safety or throughput gains over the thesis's fixed gates and over conformalized KF. Both versions need multi-subject data with real truth (ArUco cuff or IMU, at least 5 subjects) rather than synthetic masking alone. Calibration must be shown to transfer to natural occlusions, given the D-017 anti-correlation.

### feasibility skeptic: score 4, refuted=True

Feasibility verdict: the compute side is fine. The ground truth and the data the full claim needs are not, so as written the idea is refuted. A smaller version is feasible (see the reframe).

1. The truth for the core claim does not exist and the plan does not credibly create it. The headline claim is finite-sample coverage of swing-twist intervals, conditional on provenance mode and hold age, on natural occlusions. On real data the only references are: (a) v3 S-type synthetic masking, where "truth" is the withheld detector output itself. Thesis Ch. 9 and Table 9.1 document detector errors such as landmarks on the wrong body part with high confidence, elbow-on-hand failures, depth from the wrong surface, and a rig wrist 0.83-2.34 cm from labels. So intervals calibrated this way cover the error relative to the detector, not relative to the anatomy. (b) Manual wrist labels. These are a single annotator clicking a bracelet or watch on two natural-occlusion windows of one recording. The thesis itself says the label sets are so small that one frame can move a statistic. Twist and shoulder swing have no reference at all: the thesis has no wrist orientation and says the twist of a hanging arm is unobservable. The proposed fixes do not close the gap. An ArUco cuff seen by the same single D435 disappears exactly when the arm is occluded, which is when truth is needed. A 9-axis IMU gives forearm orientation only, not position, and needs IMU-to-bone and IMU-to-world calibration; the magnetometer is unreliable next to a desk and a workstation. That is a sub-project the plan does not budget for. A second calibrated camera is the obvious truth source, but it is not listed.

2. The conformal story undercuts itself on this data. Calibration comes from synthetic masks of clean windows (the arm is actually visible, so motion and depth are typical) or from Unity renders. Test is on natural occlusions (hand behind the cube, arm along the optical axis, desk-depth contamination). That is exactly the shift the thesis and V3 D-017 already document: the weak occlusion labels anti-correlate with truth, wrists are flagged on 889 of 898 frames, and flagged samples score higher. Adaptive conformal inference gives only long-run guarantees under drift, and it needs streaming labels that a single-view system never receives during an occlusion. So the 'valid under natural occlusion' claim cannot be demonstrated, only asserted.

3. Sample size per Mondrian cell is too small. The partition is (3 modes x 3-4 age bins x observability bins) x joint groups x 3 sources. At 30 fps, frames inside a gap are strongly dependent, so the effective unit is the occlusion event. One subject and 28 bags of a slow desk task yield tens of natural gaps, not the hundreds of independent events per cell needed to calibrate 95 percent intervals (at least 19 per cell just to be non-trivial) and then show held-out coverage. The "worst age/observability decile" evaluation will be dominated by noise.

4. The partition variable is itself unreliable. The provenance mode comes from the gates that V3 could not fit (D-017 thresholds are null; RTMPose never abstains). So the 'measured' cell contains the confident wrong detections that matter most, and nothing labels them.

5. The public-data transfer is under-scoped engineering. BEHAVE, InterCap and HO-Cap have no ArUco object branch. The object-conditioned source therefore needs either ground-truth object pose (an oracle that inflates arbitration gains) or a new object tracker such as FoundationPose. The torso, gravity and world frames come from desk and wall markers that do not exist there. Their SMPL or MANO pseudo-truth carries cm-level fitting error. Porting the pipeline is months of work, the downloads exceed 1 GB, and those datasets cover other subjects, cameras and tasks. Unity D435-like depth renders in 2-3 weeks are optimistic, and the sim-to-real gap for MediaPipe/RTMPose on rendered avatars is unknown.

6. Breadth versus a 12-18 month single student. There are ten baselines (CHAMP needs a 2D-to-3D lifter, CUPS a pose-shape network, plus ensembles, MC dropout, a conformalized KF re-implementation, and ComPose-style and Meta-Pose-style heads), five ablation families, stale-state sequential tests and a downstream teleoperation measure, on top of new data collection. Engineering dominates the research.

7. The novelty margin is thin for CV and ML venues: Mondrian, localized and adaptive CP applied to a tracker. The idea's own risk section says an ML venue would need a theorem, which no part of the plan produces.

What is feasible and valuable: position-only (wrist, elbow) age-conditional intervals and source arbitration, with truth from synthetic masking at scale plus a small second-camera validation. The stale-grip test can be evaluated with injected grip shifts, where truth is exact by construction.

Minimum viable experiment (8-10 weeks, existing hardware plus one borrowed or USD 300-400 second D435):
(i) Run S-type masking over every clean window in all 28 bags, with gap lengths of 1-60 frames, to get thousands of gap events. For hold-last, S2 + tangent Kalman and object-conditioned wrist placement, compute wrist and elbow position errors against the withheld measurement. Split calibration and test by recording.
(ii) Show three things: marginal split CP undercovers held states at age 16+ while age-binned Mondrian CP holds 90 percent; arbitration by inverse interval width beats the fixed thesis rule in median wrist error; a CUSUM on prediction-relative residuals detects injected grip-offset shifts of 2-5 cm with stated delay and false-alarm rate.
(iii) Validate on natural occlusions with a second calibrated D435 at about 90 deg as independent wrist and elbow truth, for 3-5 subjects and 30+ natural gaps.

If (ii) holds and (iii) shows coverage within a few points, the result is an honest RA-L/ICRA paper. If (iii) fails, report the failure as the finding: calibration from synthetic masks does not transfer to natural occlusion.

Prior-work note: the web search budget was exhausted in this session, so none of the listed works could be re-verified here. All are marked verified=false although upstream marked them verified.

Prior work found:
- CUPS: Improving Human Pose-Shape Estimators with Conformalized Deep Uncertainty (ICML 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10431; overlap: Conformalized 3D human pose-shape uncertainty on RGB benchmarks. It has no streaming, provenance or hold-age conditioning, but it occupies 'conformal + 3D human pose'.; verified=False
- CHAMP: Conformalized 3D Human Multi-Hypothesis Pose Estimators (ICLR 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.06141; overlap: Conformal sets over 3D pose hypotheses. As a proposed baseline it needs a 2D-to-3D lifter the thesis does not have.; verified=False
- Conformalized Kalman Filters for State Estimation with Trustworthy Confidence Regions (arXiv 2026 (2609.27506)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27506; overlap: Sample-wise and trajectory-wise conformal regions for a filter. This is the closest method-level overlap with the tangent-Kalman base estimator; it leaves only the mode-and-age partition and the manifold score as delta.; verified=False
- Vision-Based Safe Human-Robot Collaboration with Uncertainty Guarantees (arXiv 2026 (2604.15221)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15221; overlap: Conformal sets for human pose and motion prediction in HRC; it overlaps the downstream-safety framing.; verified=False
- Adaptive Geodesic Conformal Prediction for Egocentric Camera Pose Estimation (arXiv 2026 (2605.00233)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00233; overlap: Geodesic and adaptive conformal on SE(3), which undercuts the 'manifold score' novelty. It already shows marginal CP failing on hard cases.; verified=False
- ComPose: When to Trust Hands for Object Pose Tracking (arXiv 2026 (2605.23523)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23523; overlap: Learned trust weighting between hand and object cues, which overlaps the source-arbitration component.; verified=False
- Detecting Pose Estimation Failures via Keypoint Self-Consistency (Meta Pose) (arXiv 2026 (2608.03516)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03516; overlap: Pose failure detection; a natural baseline for the measured/abstain decision.; verified=False
- Object Pose Estimation with Statistical Guarantees: Conformal Keypoint Detection and Geometric Uncertainty Propagation (CVPR 2023) https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.12246; overlap: Conformal keypoints propagated to pose uncertainty, applied to objects.; verified=False
- On the Calibration of Human Pose Estimation (CCNet) (ICML 2024) https://proceedings.mlr.press/v235/gu24a.html; overlap: Calibration of keypoint confidence in 2D, with no coverage guarantee and no temporal component.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Narrow the idea to 'Age-conditional conformal intervals, arbitration and stale-state detection for occlusion-gap infill in a single-view RGB-D arm tracker', with position-only targets (wrist and elbow in cm), not swing-twist angles.

The goal is to show three things. First, marginal conformal coverage hides undercoverage in long held states, while hold-age-binned Mondrian CP with ACI keeps 90 percent coverage. Second, inverse-width arbitration among hold-last, the S2 + Kalman forecast and object-conditioned placement lowers median wrist error relative to the thesis's fixed rule. Third, a CUSUM on prediction-relative residuals detects injected stale-grip shifts with a measured delay and false-alarm rate.

Calibration data: large-scale S-type masking across all 28 bags plus injected grip-shift episodes, where truth is exact by construction. Real-world validation: a second calibrated D435 as independent wrist and elbow truth for 3-5 subjects and 30 or more natural gaps. This replaces the self-occluding ArUco cuff and the IMU, which cannot give position.

Drop BEHAVE, InterCap and HO-Cap and the Unity depth renders, keep 3-4 baselines (fixed gates, detector confidence, Kalman covariance, marginal split CP), and target IEEE RA-L/ICRA. Report the synthetic-to-natural coverage gap honestly as a finding. Only if that gap is small and a coverage bound for age-indexed drift can be proved should a CV or ML venue be considered.

### venue skeptic: score 3.5, refuted=True

Area-chair view, best-fitting venue judged to be IEEE RA-L / ICRA, not CVPR/ICCV/NeurIPS. Note: the web search budget for this session was exhausted (search calls returned no results), so I could not re-check recent acceptances; the prior-work list below is taken from the candidate and marked verified=false by me under the stated rule, although CUPS (ICML 2025) and CHAMP (ICLR 2025) match my own knowledge.

1. Headline claim is diffuse. The idea bundles five contributions (provenance/age-conditional conformal intervals, abstention, source arbitration, stale-state sequential test, honesty-scored benchmark). A reviewer cannot state the one-sentence result. Each piece taken alone is a known tool: Mondrian/class-conditional CP, localized CP, adaptive conformal inference (Gibbs and Candes) for drift, selective prediction with risk-coverage curves, CUSUM-style change detection, inverse-variance fusion. The candidate itself concedes "the contribution is the mode-and-age conditional design". At CVPR/ICCV that reads as "conformal applied to a custom tracker", the exact framing CUPS, CHAMP and the 2026 conformalized-Kalman and conformal-HRC preprints already occupy. The one genuinely interesting observation (marginal coverage hides failure in held/aged states) is a negative finding that is close to the known conditional-coverage critique already shown on SE(3) by the adaptive geodesic CP preprint.

2. The base problem is niche in its current form. The state (4 angles per arm, swing-twist, wrist point without orientation, ArUco-cube object, one D435, slow desk task) is a bespoke representation used by nobody else; vision reviewers will ask why not SMPL-X/MANO trackers (WHAM, HaMeR-family, FoundationPose for objects) and standard HOI benchmarks. Without a tracker-agnostic plug-in result on public data, the method is a calibration layer for the author's own pipeline.

3. Evaluation is the weakest point. (a) Calibration truth for held/constrained states comes from synthetic masking of clean windows, but the thesis's own V3 D-017 found weak labels anti-correlate with truth on natural occlusions, and the thesis showed natural occlusions behave differently (stale grip offset, arm along the optical axis). Exchangeability between calibration and test is therefore violated in exactly the regime the paper is about; ACI gives only long-run average coverage, which is precisely the marginal guarantee the paper criticizes. (b) Real-data truth is one subject with a planned IMU/ArUco cuff; reviewers will call 3-6 subjects on a slow desk task thin. (c) Public datasets (BEHAVE, InterCap, HO-Cap) have the right truth, but then the bespoke swing-twist state must be fitted to them, adding work and diluting the link to the thesis. (d) Most baselines (CUPS, CHAMP, conformalized Kalman, ComPose, Meta-Pose classifiers) are not drop-in for this state space and would need reimplementation; reviewers will doubt their tuning. (e) Expected gains over well-tuned gates on slow desk motion may be small, and held-state intervals may blow up within a few frames, giving a trivially-correct but useless result.

4. Venue fit. CVPR/ICCV: likely reject as incremental and niche. NeurIPS: only with a real theorem (e.g. age-indexed coverage under a bounded-drift or mixing assumption, or a coverage guarantee for the stale-state test), which the plan lists as optional; without it, reject. ICRA / RA-L: plausible if framed as a safety-relevant perception certificate consumed by a downstream controller or teleoperation/digital-twin filter, with a measured downstream benefit; this is where the 2026 conformal-HRC work also sits, so the delta must be the occlusion/carried-state regime. CoRL needs a robot-in-the-loop result the author does not have.

5. Feasibility for the author is acceptable (CPU-light, reuses v3 S-types and tangent Kalman), which keeps the score above the floor, but feasibility does not fix the novelty and evaluation problems for a top venue.

Overall: as written, expected outcome at CVPR/ICCV/NeurIPS is rejection; at RA-L/ICRA a sharpened version has a fair chance. Default refuted=true.

- CHAMP: Conformalized 3D Human Multi-Hypothesis Pose Estimators (ICLR 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.06141; overlap: Conformal prediction sets for 3D human pose with multi-hypothesis lifters; the natural set-valued baseline.; verified=False
- Conformalized Kalman Filters for State Estimation with Trustworthy Confidence Regions (arXiv 2026 (2609.27506)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27506; overlap: Sample-wise and trajectory-wise conformal guarantees for a filter's state; removes the 'conformal for sequential state estimation' novelty, leaving mode/age conditioning and manifold score as the delta.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Collapse to one headline claim and one venue. Recommended: IEEE RA-L (with ICRA presentation option). Title shape: "Coverage decays with occlusion age: age-conditional conformal certificates for occluded human-object tracking". Headline: marginal (and conformalized-Kalman) intervals that look valid overall under-cover by a large, measured margin in held/inferred states after k frames of occlusion; an age- and observability-conditional conformal layer restores per-age-bin coverage at bounded width, and a downstream consumer (a safety distance filter or teleoperation gating) using it gets fewer unsafe accepts at equal availability. Make it tracker-agnostic: wrap two or three public base trackers (a body/hand mesh tracker and an object pose tracker) on public multi-view HOI datasets with real occluders (HO-Cap, ARCTIC, DexYCB, BEHAVE) where truth exists in occluded frames, so calibration truth is not synthetic masking; use the author's D435 rig only as a live demo and a cross-domain transfer case. Drop source arbitration and the bespoke swing-twist state from the headline (keep geodesic scores as an ablation). Keep the stale-state test only if it has a stated false-alarm guarantee. For NeurIPS instead, the paper must lead with a theorem (coverage per occlusion-age bin under a bounded-drift or beta-mixing assumption, with a width lower bound showing when abstention is forced) and use pose tracking only as the experiment.

## I04: Integrity monitoring for single-view articulated human tracking: per-joint protection levels and fault detection that confidently-wrong landmarks cannot fool

Direction: Reliability and integrity of learned perception: fault detection, exclusion and guaranteed error bounds (protection levels) for keypoint pipelines, carried from rigid-body / GNSS settings to articulated human-object tracking.

Research question: Given one RGB-D view, a keypoint detector that never abstains (RTMPose places every occluded joint with a high score) and a few rigid scene references (desk plane, gravity marker, a held object), can a causal tracker emit per joint and per frame a protection level that upper-bounds the true 3D joint error at a prescribed integrity risk (e.g. 1e-3), and flag hazardously misleading frames, including correlated faults (both hips shifted together by desk depth, elbow placed on the hand) that pass every body-internal consistency test?

Why novel (as proposed): 'Protection Levels for Vision-Based Pose Estimation' (arXiv 2608.10023, 2026) computes RAIM-style protection levels for a rigid 6-DoF pose from neural keypoints; Meta Pose (arXiv 2608.03516, 2026) trains a logistic regression on self-consistency features for rigid 6D object pose; CCNet (ICML 2024) recalibrates 2D keypoint confidence but cannot see depth-lifting faults; correct-and-certify (RSS 2023) certifies rigid objects. None handles an articulated chain where faults are correlated across landmarks and detection redundancy must come partly from external scene references. Delta: (1) a fault-detectability analysis for articulated chains: which fault directions lie in the null space of body-internal invariants (common-mode hip shift is undetectable from bone lengths, line angles and width ratios, the thesis's observation) and which scene invariants (desk-plane pelvis height, gravity-aligned trunk prior, object-wrist coupling) restore detectability; (2) a learned fault detector whose miss rate is bounded by conformal risk control; (3) per-joint protection levels that include the undetected-fault case, propagated through the swing-twist chain. If the rigid-body paper already covers correlated multi-fault cases, the articulated, temporal and scene-reference parts remain new.

Method (as proposed): Inputs per frame: RTMPose-l 2D keypoints and heatmap statistics, aligned D435 depth, desk-world frame and gravity from ArUco markers, cube 6-DoF pose and grip state. Measurement model z = h(theta, b) + f over the swing-twist chain (torso frame + 4 angles per arm), with f a sparse/structured fault vector. Step 1 (analysis): linearize h and the invariant set (the thesis's six detectors plus new scene invariants) around operating poses; compute the parity space; enumerate fault modes from the thesis failure catalogue (wrong-surface depth on one landmark, common-mode hip depth shift, elbow-on-hand swap, stale grip offset); report detectability and minimal detectable bias per mode. Step 2 (learned detector): a small causal GRU / 1D conv (under 1 ms) over parity residuals, prediction-relative depth residuals (missing per D-017), heatmap entropy, detector score, landmark history and object pose; output P(fault) per landmark group. Labels from Unity renders of the avatar with desk/cube occluders, the real D435 noise model and RTMPose run on renders, and from BEHAVE/InterCap single-view frames with multi-view pseudo-GT. Step 3 (guarantee): threshold by conformal risk control to bound the missed-fault rate; per-joint protection level = max(conformally calibrated fault-free bound, worst undetected fault of minimal detectable size propagated through the chain Jacobian). Step 4 (exclusion): on detection, remove the group and fall back to held/constrained recovery, which inherits the protection level.

Evaluation (as proposed): Data: BEHAVE (4-Kinect RGB-D, carried boxes) and InterCap (6-Kinect) as single-view input with multi-view pseudo-GT; a Unity synthetic set with exact GT; R4-R7 with manual wrist labels and natural failure windows. Metrics: missed-detection rate at fixed false-alarm rate per fault mode, integrity risk (fraction of frames with error > protection level), tightness (median PL / median error), availability (fraction of frames with PL under an alert limit such as 5 cm), v3 false_measured and teleports. Baselines: score thresholding, CCNet-style recalibrated confidence, the thesis's six fixed-threshold detectors, Meta Pose features with logistic regression, Mahalanobis innovation gating in the tangent Kalman (D-038), MC-dropout / ensembles. Ablations: body-only vs + scene invariants (expected: common-mode hip fault detectable only with desk/gravity terms), learned vs analytic detector, with/without conformal threshold.

Target venues: CVPR 2027, ICCV 2027, RSS 2027, IEEE T-RO

Builds on the thesis: A taxonomy of silent faults with real examples (elbow on hand, co-shifting hips, wrong-surface depth, stale grip), six geometric detectors with clean-statistic thresholds, measured/held/constrained labels, metric RGB-D lifting and fiducial scene references in one world frame; v3 adds RTMPose (never abstains), false_measured and teleport metrics, synthetic occlusion scenarios and a 30 fps causal chain with headroom for a small model.

Feasibility (as proposed): Hardware sufficient (D435, RTX 3080). Need BEHAVE and InterCap (over 1 GB, approval), a Unity rendering pipeline for labelled synthetic data (avatar and scene exist), 3-5 new subjects with deliberate occlusions, optionally a second RGB-D camera for pseudo-GT. Timeline: months 1-2 detectability analysis and taxonomy on synthetic data; months 3-4 learned detector and conformal threshold; month 5 BEHAVE/InterCap; month 6 own recordings and writing. Nearest works are 2026 preprints, so submit soon.

Risks (as proposed): May be seen as RAIM transplanted from aviation with a small dataset; BEHAVE/InterCap pseudo-GT error (a few cm) may be of the same order as the faults being bounded; protection levels may be too loose (low availability). Mitigate by reporting availability honestly and showing that only scene invariants detect common-mode faults.

### novelty skeptic: score 4, refuted=True

Search caveat: this session's web-search budget was already used up (200 of 200), so all four queries I tried came back empty. I could not confirm or rule out any 2025-2026 work by search, and every entry below is marked verified=false. The task's default rule (refute when uncertain) applies. On substance, each of the idea's three claimed contributions already has a published or well-known counterpart:
(1) Fault-free bound. Yang and Pavone (CVPR 2023) already do conformal keypoint detection plus geometric propagation of the keypoint uncertainty sets to a pose error bound with a coverage guarantee. Their case is rigid, but the propagation step is the same Jacobian or worst-case pushforward this idea proposes through the swing-twist chain.
(2) Fault detection, parity space, minimal detectable bias, protection levels that cover the undetected-fault case, and correlated or common-mode faults. These are textbook ARAIM (advanced RAIM): its multiple-hypothesis solution separation explicitly handles simultaneous and correlated faults across measurements. Gupta and Gao (Stanford NAVLab, NAVIGATION 2021) already carried data-driven protection levels to camera-based localization. The idea itself cites arXiv 2608.10023 (2026) as the RAIM-for-neural-keypoints transfer. Meta Pose (arXiv 2608.03516) covers learned self-consistency failure detection, and C-3PO / correct-and-certify (Talak, Carlone et al., RSS 2022-2023, T-RO) cover certification of keypoint-based pose.
(3) Conformal risk control to bound the miss rate is a direct use of Angelopoulos et al. (ICLR 2024).
What remains new is narrow:
(a) The setting moves to an articulated human chain.
(b) The detectability observation that a common-mode hip depth shift lies in the null space of body-internal invariants and only becomes detectable through scene invariants (desk plane, gravity). In integrity-monitoring terms this is a short observability argument, not a method contribution.
(c) Temporal causality.
A CVPR/ICCV reviewer is likely to read this as RAIM plus conformal prediction applied to one more estimator, which is the risk the idea already admits. On top of that, the empirical claim is hard to support. Certifying an integrity risk of 1e-3 needs thousands of labelled fault-mode samples and ground truth well below the protection level. BEHAVE/InterCap pseudo-GT error is a few cm, about the same size as the faults and bounds being certified, so the headline integrity risk cannot be validated on real data. Synthetic Unity data only partly fixes this, because the fault distribution then reflects the simulator, not RTMPose on real occlusions. There are also probable unverified overlaps in 2024-2026 human-pose uncertainty work: conformal or calibrated uncertainty for 3D human pose and mesh recovery, multi-hypothesis HMR, and pose-estimation failure detection. These would further erode the fault-free-bound and learned-detector parts at a vision venue.

Prior work found:
- Object Pose Estimation with Statistical Guarantees: Conformal Keypoint Detection and Geometric Uncertainty Propagation (CVPR 2023) https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.12246; overlap: Conformal keypoint uncertainty sets propagated through geometry to give a pose error bound with a coverage guarantee. This is the same as Step 3's fault-free bound; it is rigid only.; verified=False
- Protection Levels for Vision-Based Pose Estimation (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10023; overlap: RAIM-style protection levels and fault detection for pose from neural keypoints. This covers the core integrity framing; it is rigid 6-DoF. Listed by the idea's author; not re-checked here because the search budget was exhausted.; verified=False
- Detecting Pose Estimation Failures via Keypoint Self-Consistency (Meta Pose) (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03516; overlap: Learned failure detector over keypoint self-consistency features. This is the same as Step 2's learned detector, for rigid objects. Listed by the idea's author; not re-checked here.; verified=False
- Data-Driven Protection Levels for Camera and 3D Map-based Safe Urban Localization (Gupta and Gao) (NAVIGATION (ION) 2021); overlap: Carries protection levels and integrity risk from GNSS to learned vision-based localization; prior art for the overall transfer.; verified=False
- Advanced RAIM (ARAIM) multiple-hypothesis solution separation, e.g. Blanch et al., IEEE TAES 2015 (IEEE TAES 2015 (classic)); overlap: Protection levels that explicitly cover simultaneous and correlated multi-fault hypotheses, plus parity-space detectability and minimal detectable bias. This is Steps 1 and 3's analytic machinery.; verified=False
- C-3PO / A Correct-and-Certify Approach to Self-Supervise Object Pose Estimators via Ensemble Self-Training (Talak, Shi, Carlone et al.) (RSS 2022 / RSS 2023 / T-RO 2023) https://www.roboticsproceedings.org/rss19/p076.pdf; overlap: Observable-correctness certificates for keypoint-based pose, which flag confidently wrong keypoint outputs; rigid.; verified=False
- On the Calibration of Human Pose Estimation (CCNet) (ICML 2024) https://proceedings.mlr.press/v235/gu24a.html; overlap: Recalibrated human keypoint confidence; a baseline for the confidently-wrong-landmark problem, but 2D only.; verified=False
- Conformal Risk Control (Angelopoulos et al.) (ICLR 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.02814; overlap: The exact tool proposed for bounding the missed-fault rate; used off the shelf.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Drop the claim of a general integrity framework for learned human pose; that part is RAIM plus conformal prediction and not new. Reposition as a robotics-safety paper for RSS/T-RO/ICRA, not CVPR, built on one sharp result:
- The core result is a detectability theorem and an experiment for articulated single-view human tracking. It proves which structured fault modes (common-mode pelvis depth shift, elbow-on-hand swap, wrong-surface depth) are undetectable from any body-internal invariant, and how few scene references (a support plane, gravity, a held tracked object) make each one detectable.
- Report the minimal detectable bias as a function of scene geometry.
- Use it to issue certified human-to-robot separation bounds for speed-and-separation monitoring (ISO/TS 15066). There an over-confident wrist or elbow is a safety hazard, and an availability-vs-integrity trade-off is the accepted metric.
- Keep the conformal per-joint bounds as the fault-free baseline, citing Yang and Pavone (CVPR 2023), rather than as a contribution.
- Validate integrity risk only where ground truth is tighter than the bound: Unity synthetic data with exact ground truth, plus a borrowed mocap or a second calibrated RGB-D camera. Do not rely on BEHAVE pseudo-GT.
- Before committing, re-run the literature search once the search budget allows: arXiv 2608.10023 (does it already cover correlated multi-fault cases?), 'conformal 3D human pose', and 'integrity monitoring human tracking HRI'.

### feasibility skeptic: score 4, refuted=True

Feasibility verdict: the analytic part is feasible and cheap, but the headline claim cannot be shown with the resources the idea names. The headline claim is a per-joint protection level that bounds true 3D joint error at an integrity risk of 1e-3 on real data.

(1) There is no ground truth for the faults that matter. The faults the idea targets are a common-mode hip shift behind the desk, a wrong-surface depth reading on an occluded joint, an elbow placed on the hand, and a stale grip offset. They occur exactly where the single camera cannot see. The lab has no motion capture. It has one subject, small sets of manual 2D wrist labels, and no labels for hips hidden by the desk. Manual 2D labels plus depth give no 3D truth for occluded joints.

(2) The proposed external data does not fit the method. BEHAVE and InterCap pseudo-GT is a multi-view SMPL fit with errors of a few cm. The idea's own risk section concedes this, and that error is about the size of the fault-free protection level being validated, so tightness and integrity risk cannot be measured on those sets. These datasets also lack the scene invariants the method depends on: no ArUco desk world frame, no gravity marker, and no fiducial object pose (object pose would need its own estimator, which has its own faults). Their subjects are mostly standing or full-body, not seated with a desk hiding the hips. The common-mode hip fault, the central detectability example, is therefore largely absent from them. They were captured with Azure Kinect, not a D435, so the depth-fault statistics differ. Downloading them over 1 GB also needs approval.

(3) The conformal guarantee needs calibration data exchangeable with the test data. If calibration comes mainly from Unity renders of the avatar with RTMPose run on them, the guarantee does not carry over to real D435 footage, because RTMPose failure modes on a game-engine avatar are not the real failure distribution. Real calibration data with true fault labels is the thing the lab cannot produce.

(4) An integrity risk of 1e-3 cannot be validated statistically. At 30 fps the frames are strongly autocorrelated, so a few thousand frames from one subject contain far fewer independent fault events than a 1e-3 tail claim needs. Only the synthetic set could reach that sample size, which runs back into point (3).

(5) Engineering risk dominates. A Unity pipeline that renders photoreal enough to provoke realistic RTMPose errors, plus labelled occluder scenes, plus a learned GRU detector, plus Jacobian propagation, plus exclusion and fallback, is a lot of infrastructure for one student. The v3 synthetic S-type scenarios mask landmarks on real recordings; they are not renders, so the rendering pipeline does not exist yet.

(6) v3 has already failed at a simpler task. D-017 found the depth-front weak labels anti-correlate with occlusion and left the occlusion gate unfitted. That is evidence that labelling occlusion faults on this task is hard.

What is feasible now: Step 1, the parity-space and detectability analysis of the swing-twist chain with and without the desk-plane, gravity and object-wrist invariants, is pure analysis on existing code. It directly formalizes a real thesis observation: co-shifting hips fool every body-internal detector. Fault injection into the real R4-R7 landmark and depth streams gives exact labels for injected faults, so missed-detection versus false-alarm curves and conformal miss-rate control can be shown honestly, for injected faults only.

Minimum viable experiment (about 3-4 months):
(a) Linearize the chain and the invariant set, and compute the minimal detectable bias for each catalogued fault mode. Do this body-only and body plus scene. Show the common-mode hip shift is undetectable without the scene terms and detectable with them.
(b) Inject the catalogued faults, with sizes swept from 1 to 20 cm, into held-out real recordings. Compare the thesis's six fixed-threshold detectors, score thresholding, Mahalanobis innovation gating in the tangent Kalman (D-038), and the analytic parity test against a small learned detector. Use a conformal threshold to bound the missed-detection rate on recordings not used for calibration.
(c) Run a small real check with a second D435 (about 300-400 USD; the idea lists it as optional, but it is required). Triangulate joints visible in the second view and confirm that the protection levels cover natural errors, reporting availability honestly.

Without (c), the protection-level claim is untested on natural faults, and a CVPR or T-RO reviewer would say so.

Literature verification: the web-search budget for this session was exhausted before any query ran, so none of the cited works could be re-verified here. All are marked verified=false in this review. The idea's own record marks them as verified by an earlier stage. From prior knowledge, CCNet (ICML 2024), Conformal Risk Control (ICLR 2024) and correct-and-certify (RSS 2023) are real. The two August 2026 arXiv preprints could not be checked at all.

Prior work found:
- Protection Levels for Vision-Based Pose Estimation (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10023; overlap: RAIM-style protection levels for rigid 6-DoF pose from neural keypoints. Closest prior work; it already covers the rigid-body version of the fault-detection and protection-level machinery.; verified=False
- Detecting Pose Estimation Failures via Keypoint Self-Consistency (Meta Pose) (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03516; overlap: Learned failure detection from self-consistency features for rigid 6D object pose; covers the learned-detector part for rigid objects.; verified=False
- On the Calibration of Human Pose Estimation (CCNet) (ICML 2024) https://proceedings.mlr.press/v235/gu24a.html; overlap: Recalibrates 2D keypoint confidence; a natural baseline, but it cannot see depth-lifting faults.; verified=False
- A Correct-and-Certify Approach to Self-Supervise Object Pose Estimators via Ensemble Self-Training (RSS 2023) https://www.roboticsproceedings.org/rss19/p076.pdf; overlap: Certifies keypoint-based rigid object pose; the idea's certification precedent.; verified=False
- Conformal Risk Control (ICLR 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.02814; overlap: The statistical tool for bounding the missed-fault rate. Its guarantee needs calibration data exchangeable with the test data, which synthetic-to-real calibration breaks.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Shrink the idea to 'fault detectability of single-view articulated tracking with scene references': a parity-space analysis plus a fault-injection study with conformal miss-rate control, validated on real recordings by injection and on a small two-D435 set for natural faults. Concrete steps: (1) Derive the parity space of the swing-twist chain and show which fault modes from the thesis catalogue are undetectable from body-internal invariants (common-mode hip depth shift, elbow-on-hand) and how adding the desk-plane, gravity and object-wrist invariants restores detectability, with minimal detectable bias numbers. (2) Inject the catalogued faults into the existing real R4-R7 streams and new recordings of the same desk task, so the fault labels are exact. Compare the six thesis detectors, innovation gating, the parity test and a small learned detector. Use conformal risk control to bound the miss rate on held-out recordings. (3) Add a second D435 (about 350 USD) and record 2-3 subjects with deliberate desk and cube occlusions. Use triangulated joints from the second view as held-out truth for natural faults, and report coverage and availability of a fault-free plus minimal-detectable-bias protection level, without claiming a 1e-3 integrity risk. Drop BEHAVE/InterCap and the photoreal Unity rendering pipeline from the critical path. Target RA-L/ICRA/IROS first, and extend to a T-RO paper only if the natural-fault coverage holds.

### venue skeptic: score 3.5, refuted=True

Note: I could not run any web searches in this session (the search budget was used up), so I have not checked what CVPR/ICCV/RSS accepted recently. The prior-work rows marked verified=false come from memory.

Verdict as area chair. CVPR/ICCV (the stated first choice) would likely reject this. RA-L with ICRA presentation would be a borderline-to-weak accept, and T-RO is out of reach with this evidence.

1. The headline is diluted. The proposal makes three claims at once: (a) a parity-space detectability analysis, (b) a learned fault detector whose miss rate is bounded by conformal risk control, and (c) per-joint protection levels propagated through a swing-twist chain. Each is a known tool: RAIM parity space, conformal risk control (ICLR 2024), and Jacobian error propagation. Reviewers will call it a transplant of RAIM plus conformal prediction from aviation and rigid pose to an articulated chain. The proposal names this risk itself and offers no strong counter. The one non-obvious-sounding insight is that common-mode hip shifts are invisible to body-internal invariants and only scene references (desk plane, gravity) make them detectable. A CVPR reviewer will find that obvious: a translation that preserves bone lengths and angles leaves internal invariants unchanged by construction. Showing that an external reference fixes it reads as a short lemma, not a contribution.

2. The core guarantee cannot be shown with the planned evaluation. An integrity risk of 1e-3 needs on the order of 10^4 or more independent, exchangeable test frames per fault mode. Frames at 30 fps are strongly autocorrelated, so independent samples per recording are few, and there is one subject (3-5 new ones planned). Conformal risk control assumes calibration and test data are exchangeable. That breaks under temporal correlation and under the synthetic-to-real (Unity) and BEHAVE/InterCap-to-own-lab shifts, which is exactly where the hazardous faults live. Reviewers will ask for coverage under shift and will not get it.

3. The ground truth is no better than the quantity being bounded. BEHAVE and InterCap pseudo-GT comes from multi-Kinect SMPL fits, with errors of a few cm. The thesis's faults (wrist 2-6 cm, hip shifts) and a 5 cm alert limit are the same size. Measured integrity risk and tightness would mostly reflect pseudo-GT noise. The only exact GT is synthetic Unity renders, and synthetic-only guarantees will not convince CVPR. A mocap system is needed but not available.

4. The problem is niche for a vision venue. Protection levels are a safety-certification concept (GNSS/aviation, AV localization). CV reviewers care about MPJPE and calibrated uncertainty. A desk-scale upper-body tracker for digital-twin playback has no safety-critical consumer, so integrity risk at 1e-3 is not motivated. The natural audience is robotics or navigation-integrity work: human-robot collaboration safety, RA-L/ICRA/IROS, or ION/NAVIGATION-style venues.

5. The baselines are incomplete for the venue. The proposal omits:
   - probabilistic 3D human pose and mesh methods that already output per-joint uncertainty (ProHMR, ICCV 2021, normalizing flows; diffusion-based multi-hypothesis HPE);
   - conformal keypoint and pose methods for rigid objects (Yang and Pavone, RA-L 2023);
   - data-driven protection levels for camera-based localization (Gupta and Gao, NAVIGATION 2021).
   The two closest 2026 items it cites are preprints with arXiv IDs (2608.xxxxx) I cannot confirm here. If they are real and recent, the delta over them is "articulated plus scene references," which is incremental.

6. Feasibility risk: there is one subject, the BEHAVE/InterCap downloads still need approval, a Unity label pipeline still has to be built, and the timeline is 6 months. Any shortfall cuts directly into the evaluation, which is already the weak point.

What could work: a narrower RA-L/ICRA paper with a clear claim. For example, "scene-referenced fault detection for single-view human tracking during object handling: body-internal checks miss correlated faults (shown empirically on synthetic data and real failure windows), and desk, gravity and object references catch them." Report missed-detection at a fixed false-alarm rate against the six thesis detectors, CCNet-style confidence, ensembles and innovation gating. Protection levels would be reported with empirical coverage at 1e-1 to 1e-2, not 1e-3.

Prior work found:
- Protection Levels for Vision-Based Pose Estimation (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10023; overlap: RAIM-style protection levels for a rigid 6-DoF pose from neural keypoints; this is the closest work, and the proposal's delta over it is the articulated chain plus scene references.; verified=False
- Detecting Pose Estimation Failures via Keypoint Self-Consistency (Meta Pose) (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03516; overlap: A learned failure detector over self-consistency features for rigid 6D pose; directly overlaps Step 2.; verified=False
- Conformal Keypoint Detection and Pose Estimation (Yang and Pavone) (RA-L 2023); overlap: Conformal prediction on keypoints, propagated to pose uncertainty sets with guarantees, for rigid objects. A missing baseline whose framing is very close to Step 3.; verified=False
- Data-Driven Protection Levels for Camera and 3D Map-based Safe Urban Localization (Gupta and Gao) (NAVIGATION (ION) 2021); overlap: Learned protection levels for vision-based localization; prior art for learning-based protection levels outside GNSS.; verified=False
- On the Calibration of Human Pose Estimation (CCNet) (ICML 2024) https://proceedings.mlr.press/v235/gu24a.html; overlap: Recalibrates 2D keypoint confidence; a baseline, but it cannot see depth-lifting faults.; verified=False
- Probabilistic Modeling for Human Mesh Recovery (ProHMR) (ICCV 2021); overlap: Per-joint uncertainty for 3D human pose via normalizing flows; a standard probabilistic HPE baseline that the proposal omits.; verified=False
- Conformal Risk Control (ICLR 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.02814; overlap: The off-the-shelf guarantee tool; its exchangeability assumption is violated by correlated video frames and by domain shift.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Retarget to RA-L with ICRA/IROS presentation (or a human-robot collaboration venue) with one headline claim: "Body-internal consistency checks are provably blind to common-mode faults in single-view human tracking during object handling; scene references (desk plane, gravity, held-object coupling) make them detectable." Shape of the paper:
- Support the claim with a short detectability lemma and with missed-detection rates at a fixed false-alarm rate, measured on exact-GT synthetic renders and on labelled real failure windows.
- Compare against score thresholding, CCNet-style confidence, ensembles, innovation gating and conformal keypoint sets (Yang and Pavone).
- Keep protection levels secondary, report their empirical coverage at realistic risk levels (1e-1 to 1e-2), and use a blocked or temporal conformal scheme.
- Collect at least a few minutes of mocap or multi-camera GT across several subjects before targeting any vision venue.
- For CVPR, the paper would need to generalize beyond this rig: a general per-joint integrity layer for any monocular 3D HPE, with a large multi-dataset evaluation of coverage under shift.

## I05: Object-as-sensor: object-conditioned, kinematics-constrained generative infilling of an occluded holding arm, guided by metric-depth free space, with a cross-dataset benchmark of how much the object tells you

Direction: Generative motion priors used for estimation (not synthesis) under long occlusion: multi-hypothesis completion of hidden limbs conditioned on the measured 6-DoF object trajectory and torso, constrained by an explicit swing-twist chain and negative evidence from metric depth, with calibrated sample spread.

Research question: When a single RGB-D camera loses the holding arm for one to several seconds but still measures the held object's 6-DoF pose, does a short-window arm-motion prior in swing-twist joint space, conditioned on the object trajectory and torso, sampled inside the kinematic feasible set and guided at inference by depth free-space, grip and bone/joint-limit constraints, cut occluded wrist and elbow error by at least 2x relative to the thesis's closed-form recovery (time-local grip offset plus two-link IK), hold-last and video-diffusion hand recovery on natural occlusions, while its sample spread gives calibrated uncertainty? How much error does the object pose remove as a function of grip type, grip age, occlusion duration and arm-to-optical-axis angle?

Why novel (as proposed): OMOMO (SIGGRAPH Asia 2023) generates full-body motion from clean object trajectories with no camera or partial body observations, no depth and no calibration claim. RoHM (CVPR 2024) infills occluded motion from noisy RGB-D without object conditioning. EgoAllo (CVPR 2025) and BoDiffusion condition on device poses, not a hand-held object with a non-rigid grip. CondMDI (SIGGRAPH 2024) and OmniControl (ICLR 2024) constrain joints in generation, not estimation. ScoreHMR (CVPR 2024) does score-guided fitting to 2D keypoints without objects or depth. ScoreHOI (ICCV 2025) uses score-guided diffusion with physical constraints from single images, not long temporal occlusion. DreamHand / ACE-Ego-Hand (arXiv 2608.20308) conditions video diffusion on pixels, not on a metric object trajectory or arm chain. VisTracker (CVPR 2023) infills the object from the human, the reverse direction. BG-HOP (CVPR 2025 workshop) is a static bimanual grasp prior; RePHO (CVPR 2026) is offline RL-in-simulation. GenHOI (arXiv 2603.19013) and the occlusion-aware MAE are single-image and hand-centric. No benchmark measures object-conditioned infilling of arm joints under physically consistent single-view visibility. Delta: (a) the measured object as a partial, noisy sensor of the hand with an uncertain latent grip offset; (b) negative evidence from metric-depth free space; (c) feasibility by construction through the explicit chain with closed-form projection, so the generative part concentrates on elbow swivel and twist, the directions the thesis identified as unobservable; (d) honesty labels (measured / constrained / generated) turned into calibrated quantities; (e) a z-buffer-visibility benchmark built from OakInk2 and ParaHome with a real metric-RGB-D split. If a 2025-2026 paper already conditions a diffusion estimator on object pose plus RGB-D free space, novelty collapses to (c)-(e); re-search CVPR 2026 and ICCV 2025.

Method (as proposed): Representation: window T = 32-64 frames at 30 fps; per frame torso pose and per arm the swing-twist parameters (shoulder swing, twist on S1, elbow flexion) plus wrist rotation and wrist point, with elbow swivel about the shoulder-wrist axis as the explicit free parameter of two-link IK; differentiable exact FK from the v3 unit-quaternion core. Conditioning: object 6-DoF trajectory in the desk frame (with validity mask; past and short future lag), torso, grasp mode, measured arm frames with honesty flags and landmark confidences, last visible arm state and WiLoR hand orientation before the occlusion. Model: a small (2-5M parameter) transformer trained with flow matching or DDPM; inpainting-style masking driven by honesty flags (measured frames clamped RePaint/CondMDI style, held/constrained frames noised). Test-time guidance at each step: (1) object-grip term ||FK_wrist - T_obj * g|| with latent grip offset g sampled from a prior centred on the thesis episode estimate with covariance from the measured scatter (7.0 / 2.7 / 6.3 cm); (2) depth free-space term penalising max(0, z_meas - z_pred - margin) for points sampled along upper-arm and forearm capsules projected into the D435 depth; (3) bone-length and joint-limit barriers with closed-form projection; (4) consistency with visible landmarks. 8-16 DDIM/flow steps, K = 8-16 samples; sample spread becomes the honesty label ('generated, sigma') rendered in Unity. Training data: SMPL-X plus object sequences from OakInk2, ARCTIC, GRAB, FullBodyManip, HO-Cap converted to the swing-twist parameterization, plus Unity-synthesised arm motions; single-view occlusion by z-buffer rendering from D435-like viewpoints above a virtual desk; detector-noise model fitted from lab recordings (v3 RTMPose-vs-v1 offsets, bone p95). Benchmark construction: K virtual RGB-D cameras around a desk-front pose on OakInk2 and ParaHome, per-joint z-buffer visibility, depth-from-wrong-surface masks, natural-duration windows; methods compared: thesis explicit recovery, improved explicit variant with grip-offset ageing and a learned swivel prior, the learned infiller, object-free baselines; every method must output uncertainty.

Evaluation (as proposed): (1) Public with real truth: held-out ARCTIC, OakInk2, ParaHome and HO-Cap subjects with re-rendered single-view occlusion; wrist and elbow error vs gap length (0.3 / 1 / 3 / 5 s) and by grip type, grip age, arm-axis angle; 'object information gain' (error with vs without object); cross-dataset (train OakInk2, test ParaHome and reverse). (2) Lab R4-R7: synthetic masks on clean windows (thesis bar: 1.7 cm median vs 21 cm hold-last) and natural windows (5.2 cm vs 11.4-17.6 cm on one, failure on the other) against manual wrist labels. (3) New recordings with a witness side camera and an ArUco wrist band visible only to it, giving wrist position and orientation truth for hidden frames. Baselines: hold-last; thesis object-conditioned recovery with frozen offset; v3 S2 + tangent Kalman; siMLPe on 8 landmarks; RoHM with the same observations; an OMOMO-style object-only generator; DreamHand-style RGB recovery where code exists. Metrics: median / p95 cm, minADE/minFDE over K, interval coverage and ECE, NLL, joint-limit and free-space violation rates, teleport rate under D-033 budgets, false_measured, reacquisition jump, latency p99. Ablations: no object conditioning, no free-space term, no constraint projection, fixed vs latent grip offset, deterministic vs generative, with vs without hand orientation, number of samples, guidance weight, public-only vs plus lab synthetic-occlusion data, object-pose noise level, z-buffer vs random masks.

Target venues: ICCV 2027, CVPR 2027, NeurIPS 2027, 3DV 2027, SIGGRAPH Asia 2027 (if the honesty-aware digital-twin rendering is strong), ICRA 2027 (if framed for teleoperation)

Builds on the thesis: Object-conditioned recovery and two-link IK are the deterministic special case, baseline and source of the grip-offset prior; the analysis that twist of a hanging arm and along-axis elbow depth are unobservable defines what must be generated; honesty flags become the inpainting mask and calibration target; the differentiable quaternion core keeps samples feasible; metric RGB-D supplies the free-space term RGB methods cannot use; v3 S-type scenarios and teleport budgets supply masks and grading; the v3 forecasting survey lists the learned baselines; R4-R7, manual wrist labels and the Unity twin cover evaluation and demonstration.

Feasibility (as proposed): RTX 3080 trains a 2-5M-parameter prior over a ~20-dimensional per-frame state in hours. Need: registration for ARCTIC, OakInk2, GRAB, FullBodyManip, ParaHome (SMPL-X licence academic; downloads over 1 GB need approval; release scripts, not data); a witness camera (borrow or buy a second D435, about USD 300-450); printed wrist-band markers; ethics approval and 6-10 additional subjects. Timeline: months 1-2 dataset conversion, retargeting and occlusion rendering; months 3-4 model, guidance and real-time variant; month 5 witness-camera recordings; months 6-7 experiments; month 8 writing. Realistic first target ICCV 2027 or NeurIPS 2027; CVPR 2027 is too tight.

Risks (as proposed): Reviewers may see 'OMOMO plus RoHM with a depth term' or call the gain obvious; for slow desk tasks the object may determine the hand so strongly that a generative model barely beats IK plus a swivel prior, so long, fast occlusions where swivel and twist matter are needed. Witness-camera truth is cm-level; the SMPL-X joint centre vs detector landmark definitional offset (D-018 kind) may dominate; retargeting public mocap onto a reduced arm chain may introduce bias; calibration may hold on synthetic masks and fail on real occlusions; virtual-camera occlusion may not match real RGB-D artefacts; a learned model may dominate the explicit one, turning the thesis method into a baseline.

### novelty skeptic: score 4, refuted=True

Search caveat: this session's web-search budget was used up (200 of 200 calls) before any search could run. None of the four required queries ran, and no new paper was confirmed this session. The judgement below therefore rests on the 14 works already listed and verified in the candidate, plus works I know of from before my knowledge cutoff. Those extra works are marked verified=false. With no fresh evidence, the brief says to default to refuted=true.

Why the delta is too small for a top vision venue in the current framing. Each named piece of the idea already has a published precedent:
(1) Generating human motion from the object's motion. OMOMO (SIGGRAPH Asia 2023) builds full-body motion from object trajectories. CHOIS (ECCV 2024) adds object waypoints and contact and collision guidance at inference time. Guidance from an object-grip term is therefore not new.
(2) Diffusion infilling of occluded motion from noisy RGB-D. RoHM (CVPR 2024) does this, and GLAMR (CVPR 2022) did occlusion infilling before it.
(3) Inference-time score guidance with physical or contact constraints during estimation (not synthesis). ScoreHMR (CVPR 2024) and ScoreHOI (ICCV 2025) do this. CondMDI and OmniControl clamp or guide chosen joints.
(4) Estimating an unobserved arm or hand from sparse signals, with the elbow swivel handled explicitly. MANIKIN (ECCV 2024) is a neural inverse-kinematics solver that predicts the swivel angle on top of an analytic arm chain. EgoPoser (ECCV 2024) and HMD-NeMo (ICCV 2023) estimate hands that are out of view or unobserved, with uncertainty-style handling. EgoAllo (CVPR 2025) adds guidance-based diffusion estimation.
(5) Forecasting body motion conditioned on objects. HOIMotion (IEEE TVCG / VR 2024) uses 3D object boxes. InterDiff (ICCV 2023) does HOI forecasting with physics-informed correction.

So "object as a sensor, kinematic chain, diffusion guidance" is a recombination. A reviewer is likely to call it "OMOMO/CHOIS + RoHM + MANIKIN". The parts that remain plausibly new are:
- (b) negative evidence from metric-depth free space, applied as a guidance term on limb capsules;
- (d) calibrated sample spread used as honesty labels;
- (e) a benchmark with z-buffer visibility and an "object information gain" analysis.
(b) is a modest guidance term. Physics, collision or penetration guidance (CHOIS, ScoreHOI) is close in spirit, so reviewers may see it as incremental.

The candidate itself names a further risk. In slow desk tasks the object almost determines the wrist: the thesis's closed-form recovery already reaches 1.7 cm median on synthetic masks. A 2x gain then depends on long, fast occlusions where swivel and twist matter, and such windows are scarce in the lab data. The evaluation also has weak spots. Witness-camera truth is only at cm level, and there is a definitional offset between SMPL-X joints and detector landmarks. Both could hide the effect.

Novelty that probably survives: a cross-dataset, visibility-faithful benchmark that measures how much the held object reveals about the hidden arm (by grip type, grip age, gap length and arm-to-axis angle), with calibrated uncertainty. As a method paper at CVPR, ICCV or NeurIPS, I judge the novelty insufficient unless the unrun searches turn up nothing closer. Even then the method delta stays incremental.

Prior work found:
- Object Motion Guided Human Motion Synthesis (OMOMO) (SIGGRAPH Asia 2023 (ACM TOG)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16237; overlap: Generates full-body and hand-joint motion conditioned on the object's 6-DoF trajectory and enforces hand-object contact. This is the core 'the object tells you the arm' idea, without partial observations or depth.; verified=True
- Controllable Human-Object Interaction Synthesis (CHOIS) (ECCV 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.03913; overlap: Diffusion conditioned on object geometry and waypoints, with inference-time guidance terms for hand-object contact and collision. This matches the proposed object-grip and constraint guidance mechanism almost exactly, in synthesis rather than estimation.; verified=False
- RoHM: Robust Human Motion Reconstruction via Diffusion (CVPR 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.08570; overlap: Diffusion-based infilling of occluded body motion from noisy RGB(-D) observations, with an inpainting-style mask. Only the object conditioning and the free-space term are missing.; verified=True
- MANIKIN: Biomechanically Accurate Neural Inverse Kinematics for Human Motion Estimation (ECCV 2024); overlap: Explicit analytic arm chain with a learned swivel-angle prediction from sparse signals, guaranteeing feasibility by construction. This overlaps delta (c): generate only the swivel and twist on an explicit chain.; verified=False
- EgoPoser: Robust Real-Time Egocentric Pose Estimation from Sparse and Intermittent Observations Everywhere (ECCV 2024); overlap: Estimates the body when hand signals drop out of the field of view, using intermittent observation masks. This is the same 'hand lost for seconds' problem, without an object.; verified=False
- HMD-NeMo: Online 3D Avatar Motion Generation from Sparse Observations (ICCV 2023); overlap: Handles hands that are only partially visible, with uncertainty-aware generation of the unobserved arm. This overlaps deltas (d) and (c).; verified=False
- Estimating Body and Hand Motion in an Ego-sensed World (EgoAllo) (CVPR 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03665; overlap: A diffusion prior used for estimation, with guidance-based optimisation against partial observations (hand detections) and samples kept as multiple hypotheses.; verified=True
- ScoreHMR: Score-Guided Diffusion for 3D Human Recovery (CVPR 2024) https://github.com/statho/ScoreHMR; overlap: Score guidance used for estimation rather than synthesis. Generic version of the proposed test-time guidance.; verified=True
- ScoreHOI: Physically Plausible Reconstruction of Human-Object Interaction via Score-Guided Diffusion (ICCV 2025) https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/ICCV2025/html/Li_ScoreHOI_Physically_Plausible_Reconstruction_of_Human-Object_Interaction_via_Score-Guided_Diffusion_ICCV_2025_paper.html; overlap: Score-guided HOI reconstruction with contact and penetration constraints. Close in spirit to the free-space and grip guidance, but single-image rather than temporal.; verified=True
- HOIMotion: Forecasting Human Motion During Human-Object Interactions Using Egocentric 3D Object Bounding Boxes (IEEE TVCG (IEEE VR) 2024); overlap: Predicts human body motion conditioned on object boxes over time. The object-to-body inference direction is already established.; verified=False
- InterDiff: Generating 3D Human-Object Interactions with Physics-Informed Diffusion (ICCV 2023); overlap: HOI motion forecasting with physics-based correction in a contact frame relative to the object. Similar to modelling a latent grip offset in the object frame.; verified=False
- Flexible Motion In-betweening with Diffusion Models (CondMDI) (SIGGRAPH 2024) https://github.com/setarehc/diffusion-motion-inbetweening; overlap: Masked-frame clamping and inpainting of motion. Same as the proposed honesty-flag-driven mask mechanism.; verified=True
- GLAMR: Global Occlusion-Aware Human Mesh Recovery with Dynamic Cameras (CVPR 2022); overlap: Generative infilling of motion for occluded or out-of-frame people, with a multi-hypothesis motion infiller. Older precedent for occlusion infilling in estimation.; verified=False
- DreamHand / ACE-Ego-Hand (arXiv 2026 (2608.20308)) https://arxiv.org/html/2608.20308v1; overlap: Recovers occluded hand motion with generative video priors. Conditions on pixels, not on a metric object trajectory.; verified=True

Suggested reframe: Lead with the measurement question and the benchmark, and treat the method as secondary. Proposed title: "How much does the held object tell you about the hidden arm? A visibility-faithful benchmark and calibrated estimators for occluded-limb recovery in hand-object manipulation." Contributions:
(1) A z-buffer, single-view RGB-D occlusion protocol on OakInk2, ParaHome, ARCTIC and HO-Cap, with natural-duration gaps and depth-from-wrong-surface masks.
(2) An "object information gain" analysis that breaks down how much the object reduces wrist and elbow error, by grip type, grip age, gap length and arm-to-optical-axis angle. Report the per-axis split, showing that swivel, twist and along-axis depth stay unobservable even with the object.
(3) A calibration-first protocol: coverage, ECE and NLL for every method, including explicit IK with a latent grip offset, MANIKIN-style learned swivel, RoHM, and an OMOMO/CHOIS-style object-conditioned diffusion model with depth free-space guidance. Report free-space violation rates alongside.
Present the free-space term and the explicit-chain projection as ablated design choices, not as the headline. Target 3DV, or the NeurIPS Datasets and Benchmarks track, before CVPR or ICCV.

An alternative angle with a weaker literature footprint: frame the work for robotics, recovering occluded arm trajectories in human demonstration videos for imitation learning or teleoperation retargeting (ICRA or CoRL). There, calibrated uncertainty can be used downstream, for example by down-weighting generated frames during policy learning.

Before committing to either framing, re-run the four searches that could not run here. Check that no CVPR 2026, ICCV 2025 or ECCV 2026 paper already conditions a diffusion estimator on the object pose plus RGB-D free space. Specific queries: CHOIS/OMOMO follow-ups used for estimation; HOI tracking with a diffusion prior under occlusion; MANIKIN plus object.

### feasibility skeptic: score 4, refuted=True

Verdict: the core model is feasible on the stated hardware, but the package as written is not. Its acceptance test (2x on occluded wrist AND elbow, measured on real natural occlusions, with calibrated spread) cannot be checked with the ground truth the plan can get. Its scope (five-dataset conversion, a cross-dataset benchmark, guided diffusion, a real-time Unity variant, witness-camera recordings with new subjects) is too much engineering for one student in 12-18 months.

What is feasible: (1) Compute. A 2-5M-parameter flow or DDPM prior over a roughly 20-D per-frame arm state trains in hours on an RTX 3080. (2) Training data. GRAB, FullBodyManip/OMOMO, ARCTIC and OakInk2 provide SMPL-X bodies with 6-DoF object trajectories under academic licences. Z-buffer occlusion from virtual desk-front cameras is a straightforward rendering job. (3) Baselines. The thesis recovery (grip offset plus two-link IK), hold-last and the v3 S2/Kalman code already exist, so the deterministic special case is ready.

Where it breaks:
(a) The lab ground truth cannot resolve the claimed effect. In the thesis natural-occlusion test (Table 7.9), the object-assisted wrist was 5.2 cm median on n=4 right-wrist frames and 13.1 cm on n=16 left-wrist frames. The manual label itself sits 2.1-4.5 cm (median) from the landmark on clean frames (Table 7.8), and click and depth uncertainty were never quantified. A 2x gain on the right wrist (to about 2.6 cm) is inside label noise. The left-wrist window is one stale-offset event. The synthetic-mask numbers (Table 7.7: 1.2-2.8 cm) are deviations from the model's own unmasked reconstruction, not from truth, and they leave almost no room for a 2x gain. R4-R7 cannot carry the claim.
(b) No elbow truth anywhere in the real evaluation. The witness camera plus wrist band gives only wrist position and orientation. Elbow swivel and twist are exactly where the generative part is supposed to help, so the headline elbow claim is untestable on real data unless an upper-arm or elbow marker is added and calibrated per subject.
(c) The 'real metric-RGB-D split' of the benchmark is not specified. OakInk2, ParaHome, GRAB and FullBodyManip give no single-view D435-like depth with real sensor artefacts, so on public data the free-space term only ever sees rendered depth. The datasets that would test it (BEHAVE and InterCap: Kinect RGB-D with SMPL(-X) and object pseudo-ground-truth) are not in the plan. Their truth is multi-view pseudo-GT, accurate to about 1-2 cm.
(d) The effect size may be small. In slow desk tasks, once the grip offset is known at occlusion onset (and with mocap truth it is known almost exactly), wrist error is set by grip-offset drift and the elbow by one swivel DoF. A deterministic IK plus a learned swivel regressor may capture most of the gain. The 2x bar against a strong explicit baseline is then unlikely except on long, fast gaps, which desk datasets contain few of.
(e) Retargeting risk. Mapping SMPL-X joint centres onto the thesis's 4-DoF effective-length chain adds a definitional offset of the D-018 kind (landmark versus joint centre, several cm). Real-data errors could be dominated by that offset, not by the method.
(f) Resource gaps the idea names but does not budget. Ethics approval and 6-10 new subjects are needed, a second D435 must be borrowed or bought, and cross-camera extrinsic calibration has to be built and validated. Each is feasible, but together with (c) and (e) the engineering dominates the research.
(g) Literature (not checked in this session). The web-search budget was exhausted, so no 2025-2026 re-check for an existing object-plus-depth-conditioned diffusion estimator was possible. The prior-work list is carried over unverified.

Minimum viable experiment (6-8 weeks, no new recordings):
- Data: train on GRAB plus FullBodyManip, test on held-out OakInk2 subjects. Use the native SMPL-X arm (shoulder, elbow, wrist) with a closed-form bone-length and joint-limit projection, not the thesis 4-DoF chain.
- Masks: single-camera z-buffer visibility from desk-front virtual viewpoints. Keep only windows where the holding arm is hidden for 0.5-5 s while the object stays visible.
- Methods compared:
  - M1: hold-last.
  - M2: thesis recovery (frozen grip offset, minimum-change swivel).
  - M3: M2 plus a deterministic learned swivel and offset-ageing regressor.
  - M4: an object-conditioned flow or diffusion infiller with grip and limit guidance, K=16.
- Metrics: wrist and elbow median and p95 versus gap length, minADE, interval coverage and ECE, and the object-information gain (M4 with versus without object input).
- Kill criterion: M4 must beat M3 by at least 1.5x on elbow error for gaps of 1 s or more, with coverage within 5 points of nominal. If it does not, the generative framing is not justified.

Stage 2 (real depth, no new subjects): run the same models on BEHAVE and InterCap single-Kinect input, measuring against their multi-view pseudo-ground-truth, with and without the free-space term.

Stage 3: a small lab witness-camera set (3-5 subjects, wrist and upper-arm ArUco bands, with the per-subject offset calibrated on mutually visible frames) as a sanity check only.

Prior work found:
- Object Motion Guided Human Motion Synthesis (OMOMO) (SIGGRAPH Asia 2023 (ACM TOG)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16237; overlap: Generates full-body motion from object trajectory; the FullBodyManip data it releases is the natural training set; an object-only generator baseline.; verified=False
- RoHM: Robust Human Motion Reconstruction via Diffusion (CVPR 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.08570; overlap: Diffusion infilling of occluded motion from noisy RGB-D observations, no object conditioning; the main object-free baseline and the reason reviewers may call the delta incremental.; verified=False
- Flexible Motion In-betweening with Diffusion Models (CondMDI) (SIGGRAPH 2024) https://github.com/setarehc/diffusion-motion-inbetweening; overlap: Masked/clamped conditioning scheme the method reuses for measured frames.; verified=False
- ScoreHMR: Score-Guided Diffusion for 3D Human Recovery (CVPR 2024) https://github.com/statho/ScoreHMR; overlap: Test-time score guidance for estimation; the guidance mechanism is not new.; verified=False
- ScoreHOI: Physically Plausible Reconstruction of Human-Object Interaction via Score-Guided Diffusion (ICCV 2025) https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/ICCV2025/html/Li_ScoreHOI_Physically_Plausible_Reconstruction_of_Human-Object_Interaction_via_Score-Guided_Diffusion_ICCV_2025_paper.html; overlap: Score-guided HOI reconstruction with physical constraints, single-image; closest in mechanism.; verified=False
- BEHAVE: Dataset and Method for Tracking Human Object Interactions (CVPR 2022); overlap: Real Kinect RGB-D with SMPL and object pseudo-ground-truth; the missing real-depth evaluation set that makes the free-space term testable without new subjects.; verified=False
- InterCap: Joint Markerless 3D Tracking of Humans and Objects in Interaction (GCPR 2022 / IJCV 2024); overlap: Multi-Kinect RGB-D with SMPL-X and object poses; second real-depth evaluation set.; verified=False
- OAKINK2: A Dataset of Bimanual Hands-Object Manipulation in Complex Task Completion (CVPR 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.19417; overlap: Proposed test set with body and object truth; no D435-like single-view real depth.; verified=False
- Visibility Aware Human-Object Interaction Tracking from Single RGB Camera (VisTracker) (CVPR 2023) https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.16479; overlap: Infills the occluded object from the human (reverse direction); evaluated on BEHAVE/InterCap, which shows those sets support occlusion-infilling evaluation.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Recast it as one estimation paper, not a benchmark plus a system: 'How much does a visible held object determine a hidden arm? Object-conditioned, constraint-guided probabilistic arm infilling under single-view occlusion.'
