(1) Use the native SMPL-X arm with a closed-form bone-length and joint-limit projection, not the thesis 4-DoF chain. This removes the retargeting bias.
(2) Train on GRAB plus FullBodyManip. Test on held-out OakInk2 with z-buffer single-view masks (perfect truth), and on BEHAVE and InterCap single-Kinect input against multi-view pseudo-GT (real depth, real occlusion). That gives both a truth-exact and a real-sensor result with no new recordings.
(3) Make the strong deterministic baseline central: thesis recovery plus offset ageing plus a learned swivel regressor. Claim only what beats it, chiefly elbow error and calibrated coverage on gaps of 1 s or more. Report the object-information gain as a function of grip age, gap length and arm-to-axis angle as the analysis contribution.
(4) Drop the real-time Unity variant and the five-dataset benchmark.
(5) Keep one small lab witness-camera set as a sanity check, with wrist and upper-arm bands on 3-5 subjects. Its per-subject band-to-joint offset is calibrated on frames visible to both cameras.

Gate the project on the 6-8 week minimum viable experiment: if the infiller does not beat the deterministic swivel baseline by at least 1.5x on elbow error, pivot to an analysis paper or a 3DV/ICRA paper on calibrated explicit recovery. First realistic venue: 3DV 2027 or ICRA 2027. CVPR or ICCV only if the BEHAVE/InterCap gains are large.

### venue skeptic: score 4, refuted=True

Area-chair view for ICCV/CVPR/NeurIPS 2027. Caveat first: the session's web-search budget was used up before this review, so no new 2025-2026 search was run. The prior-work check below relies on the candidate's own list and on knowledge up to mid-2026. The candidate itself asks for a re-search of CVPR 2026 and ICCV 2025, and that search is still outstanding.

(1) Headline claim. The stated claim is "at least 2x lower occluded wrist/elbow error than the thesis's closed-form recovery". To a CVPR/ICCV reviewer that baseline is an unpublished undergraduate method, so beating it is not a headline. The real comparison is against RoHM-style infilling given the same observations, plus an OMOMO/CHOIS-style object-conditioned generator. The idea's own risk section concedes that on slow desk tasks the object nearly determines the wrist, so IK plus a swivel prior may already sit close to the floor. If the gain over a strong learned baseline with object conditioning is small, there is no paper. If it is large only on synthetic z-buffer masks, reviewers will call it a synthetic-benchmark artefact.

(2) Incrementality. The method is a composite of well-known parts. These are OMOMO-style object-trajectory conditioning, RoHM/CondMDI-style inpainting with clamped observed frames, ScoreHMR/DPS-style test-time guidance, standard bone-length and joint-limit barriers, and a depth free-space penalty. Depth free-space or silhouette terms were long used in RGB-D model fitting, so this one is not new either. The feared summary, "OMOMO + RoHM + a depth term", is exactly what reviewers will write.

Delta (c), feasibility by construction through a swing-twist chain with closed-form projection, is an engineering choice. SMPL-X parameterisations already enforce bone lengths, so it is not a contribution. Delta (d), calibrated sample spread, is the most interesting point. But calibration of diffusion samples under long occlusion has to be shown against conformal or ensemble baselines, and the plan has no such comparison.

(3) Problem scope. The setting is narrow: a single static RGB-D camera, a desk, a held object whose 6-DoF pose is measured (via a fiducial in the thesis), and a holding arm hidden for seconds. That combination is a niche at a general vision venue. Egocentric and VR/teleop tracking with a held controller or tool is the setting with a large audience, and EgoAllo-type work already covers device-conditioned estimation there.

(4) Evaluation credibility. The main numbers come from virtual cameras over OakInk2/ParaHome mocap with synthetic depth and synthetic occlusion. The real split is one lab, few subjects, and witness-camera truth at cm level. That truth noise is about the size of the claimed effect (1.7 to 5 cm medians), and the plan itself notes that the SMPL-X versus detector joint-definition offset could dominate.

ARCTIC and OakInk2 have real multi-view RGB, but their depth would be rendered. So the RGB-D-specific term (free space) is never tested at scale on real sensor artefacts: depth from the wrong surface, holes, multipath. That is the very term that separates this work from RGB methods.

Missing standard baselines: WHAM/GVHMR/TRAM-class video HMR with an occlusion-robust temporal prior, HMR/HOI trackers (VisTracker, InterTrack-style template-free HOI tracking), CHOIS-style controllable HOI generation used as an estimator, and a plain transformer regressor without generation, which tests whether diffusion is needed at all. Also missing: a non-diffusion probabilistic baseline (a mixture density network, or an ensemble) for the calibration claim.

(5) Venue fit. As written this is a solid 3DV, WACV or IROS/RA-L paper (teleoperation or digital-twin framing). It is unlikely to clear ICCV/CVPR/NeurIPS main track, where it would draw "incremental combination, niche setting, synthetic evaluation" reviews.

The idea would survive only if (a) the object-information-gain analysis showed a non-obvious, general result across datasets, or (b) the benchmark became a community resource with real metric-depth occlusion truth. The candidate proposes neither (b) at scale nor (a) as the lead claim.

Score: 4 out of 10 for expected acceptance at the best-fitting top vision venue. It is about 6 for 3DV or RA-L/IROS.

Prior work found:
- Object Motion Guided Human Motion Synthesis (OMOMO) (SIGGRAPH Asia 2023 (ACM TOG)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16237; overlap: Generates full-body motion including hands from an object trajectory alone; this covers the object-conditioning half of the method. It differs in having no camera observations, no occlusion mask and no depth.; verified=False
- RoHM: Robust Human Motion Reconstruction via Diffusion (CVPR 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.08570; overlap: Diffusion infilling of occluded body motion from noisy RGB(-D) observations; this covers the estimation-by-infilling half. It differs in having no object conditioning.; verified=False
- Controllable Human-Object Interaction Synthesis (CHOIS) (ECCV 2024); overlap: Diffusion that jointly generates human and object motion under sparse object waypoints and contact/penetration guidance; it is a natural object-conditioned guided-diffusion baseline that the candidate does not list.; verified=False
- Flexible Motion In-betweening with Diffusion Models (CondMDI) (SIGGRAPH 2024) https://github.com/setarehc/diffusion-motion-inbetweening; overlap: Masked-observation inpainting of joints in motion diffusion, the same mechanism as clamping 'measured' frames.; verified=False
- ScoreHMR: Score-Guided Diffusion for 3D Human Recovery (CVPR 2024) https://github.com/statho/ScoreHMR; overlap: Test-time score guidance of a pose prior toward observations; this is the guidance scheme the candidate adopts.; verified=False
- Estimating Body and Hand Motion in an Ego-sensed World (EgoAllo) (CVPR 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03665; overlap: Conditional diffusion estimating body and hands from sparse device poses with guidance; it is the closest estimation-not-synthesis analogue, in a setting with a larger audience.; verified=False
- ScoreHOI: Physically Plausible Reconstruction of Human-Object Interaction via Score-Guided Diffusion (ICCV 2025) https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/ICCV2025/html/Li_ScoreHOI_Physically_Plausible_Reconstruction_of_Human-Object_Interaction_via_Score-Guided_Diffusion_ICCV_2025_paper.html; overlap: Score-guided HOI reconstruction with physical constraints; it differs in being single-image rather than long temporal occlusion.; verified=False
- Visibility Aware Human-Object Interaction Tracking from Single RGB Camera (VisTracker) (CVPR 2023) https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.16479; overlap: Infills the occluded side of an HOI from the visible side over time; it works in the reverse direction (object from human) and is an expected baseline family.; verified=False
- InterTrack: Tracking Human Object Interaction without Object Templates (arXiv 2024 / 3DV 2025 (venue not confirmed)); overlap: Single-view temporal HOI tracking with learned priors; reviewers would expect a comparison or a discussion.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Drop "beats our own IK by 2x" as the headline. Lead instead with a general, measurable question: "How much of a hidden limb does a held object determine?" Answer it with an object-information-gain study across ARCTIC, OakInk2, ParaHome and HO-Cap, broken down by grip type, grip age, occlusion length and view angle, and use the guided generative estimator as the instrument that measures it.

Then broaden the setting beyond a desk with a fiducial, to any tracked hand-held object (VR controllers, tools, teleoperation handles), so the work speaks to the EgoAllo/VR/teleop audience. Make calibrated multi-hypothesis output (coverage versus conformal and ensemble baselines) the second claim.

Compare against RoHM, a CHOIS/OMOMO-style generator used as an estimator, and a deterministic transformer regressor, all given identical observations. Validate the depth free-space term on real sensor depth, not rendered depth.

If the gains over those learned baselines are modest, target 3DV 2027 or RA-L/IROS with a teleoperation and digital-twin framing, rather than ICCV/CVPR/NeurIPS.

## I06: Bimanual holding and handover as a closed kinematic loop: loop-closure constraints and a common-mode residual that exposes torso-frame errors

Direction: Physically and kinematically consistent bimanual motion estimation: torso, both arms and the object form a closed loop during two-handed holds and handovers, used as a constraint and as an independent consistency check (an analogue of SLAM loop closure for the human body).

Research question: During two-hand holds and handovers, torso -> left arm -> object -> right arm -> torso forms a closed chain whose closure can be checked against an object pose measured independently of the body detector. Can (a) enforcing this loop in MAP estimation reduce arm errors under one-sided occlusion, and (b) the common-mode part of the two grasp-closure residuals identify and correct torso-frame errors (for example co-shifted hips hidden by the desk), which the thesis reports no single-chain failure detector can see, with a derivable observability condition for when this works?

Why novel (as proposed): The thesis lists 'co-shifting hips fool every failure detector' and 'the torso frame depends on hips the desk hides' as open limitations, and treats a handover as two overlapping single-hand grip episodes, never as one closed loop. Closed-chain constraints for two arms holding one object are textbook in robot planning (Constrained Bimanual Planning with Analytic IK, 2023) but not used for human state estimation from vision. IMU-HOI (CVPR 2026) couples hand FK and object motion per hand; ARCTIC-based bimanual reconstruction such as BIGS (CVPR 2025) models two hands and the object but not the arm/torso loop; OMOMO (SIGGRAPH Asia 2023) generates motion from object motion without consistency checking; A Taxonomy of Self-Handover (arXiv 2025) proposes no estimator. No searched work uses the object-mediated loop residual to diagnose torso-frame error. The delta is small if seen as I01 applied to two hands at once; the torso-error identifiability result is what makes it separate.

Method (as proposed): Representation: the I01 state (torso SE(3), two swing-twist arms with wrists, grasps G_L and G_R, contact modes). Loop factor: when both hands are in rigid mode, stack r_L = Log(T_obj^-1 FK_L(T_torso, q_L) G_L) and r_R; a torso error shifts both FK predictions by a common rigid transform while T_obj (marker or model tracker) does not move, so the common-mode component is evidence about T_torso. Observability analysis: rank and conditioning of the stacked Jacobian w.r.t. {T_torso, G_L, G_R, q_L, q_R} under three regimes (grasps known from clean frames, grasps unknown, one hand occluded); derive the condition under which torso translation and rotation become observable (e.g. grasp points not collinear with the rotation axis), validated numerically in Unity. Estimator: sliding-window constrained MAP on manifolds (GTSAM, or augmented-Lagrangian / SQP for hard closure), initialised by closed-form two-arm IK with direction memory and a torso refinement minimising the common-mode residual subject to the remembered hip-depth prior. Switchable loop-closure factors so a false 'both holding' cannot corrupt the torso. Handover as mode sequence (L-rigid, R-free) -> (both rigid) -> (L-free, R-rigid) inferred by an HMM on closure residuals and hand-object distances; the 25/35 cm hysteresis machine is the baseline. A chi-square test on the common-mode residual flags the torso 'suspect' and triggers hip-depth re-estimation.

Evaluation (as proposed): Synthetic: S-type root_ref and hip scenarios plus a new 'co-shifted hips' injection on lab recordings and Unity renders with exact truth; detection AUROC and torso correction error. Public: BEHAVE two-handed carries and InterCap; HO-Cap handovers (hand-object part only unless arm annotations exist). Lab: R4 (two handovers) and handover-on-rail with manual wrist labels and labelled handover onset/offset. Baselines: thesis per-hand recovery with hip-depth memory, I01 without the loop factor, AKC per arm, IMU-HOI-style per-hand contact gating on vision. Metrics: torso pose error, arm joint error during holds and handovers, handover timing error, torso-fault detection AUROC and delay, loop residual statistics. Ablations: hard vs soft closure, known vs estimated grasps, switchable vs fixed loop factors.

Target venues: RSS 2027, ICRA 2028 / IEEE RA-L, CVPR 2027 / ICCV 2027 (merged with I01 as one paper)

Builds on the thesis: The exact failure (co-shifted hips, desk-hidden pelvis), recordings with two handovers and a handover-on-rail, labelled grip episodes per hand, a metric world frame in which object pose is independent of the body branch, the S-type root_ref scenario that already fails the baseline on teleports, and closed-form two-link IK for both arms as initializer.

Feasibility (as proposed): Software only on top of I01. Data: existing handover recordings plus about a week of new bimanual recordings (two-handed carries, self-handovers, several object sizes) with a second D435 or borrowed mocap for the torso reference. 3-4 extra months after I01; best submitted with I01 or as a follow-up (RSS 2028 or RA-L safer than RSS 2027).

Risks (as proposed): Narrow applicability (the loop exists only while both hands hold, a small fraction of frames) and may be seen as a special case of I01; torso rotation may be only weakly observable through two near-collinear grasps on a small cube, so the identifiability result may be negative and must be reported either way; grasp latent and torso errors are confounded unless grasps were estimated on clean frames before the fault.

### novelty skeptic: score 3, refuted=True

Search provenance: none of the four web searches ran, because the session's WebSearch budget (200 of 200) was already used up. WebFetch to arxiv.org, export.arxiv.org and api.semanticscholar.org was blocked by the egress proxy. Every prior-work entry below therefore comes from model knowledge and is marked verified=false. The novelty judgment is a reasoned one, not a search-confirmed one. Following the default rule (refute when uncertain), the verdict is refuted for a top venue.

Substance. The idea has two parts, (a) a loop-closure factor and (b) diagnosing torso error from the common-mode residual. Each part is weak on its own.

(a) Prior work already enforces the loop implicitly. Joint human-object fitting with hand-object contact terms optimizes the body root/torso, both arms and the object pose together in one objective, so when both hands touch the object the chain torso -> L arm -> object -> R arm -> torso is already closed. Examples: BEHAVE (CVPR 2022), InterCap (GCPR 2022, IJCV extension), CHORE (ECCV 2022), VisTracker (CVPR 2023), and the contact/penetration objectives in CHOIS (ECCV 2024) and OMOMO (SIGGRAPH Asia 2023). Biomechanics tools have closed-chain constraints for two hands on one object (for example weld or coordinate-coupler constraints in OpenSim for rowing, wheelchair propulsion and bimanual lifting). Hard closed-chain constraints for two arms on one body are also textbook in robotics (Cohn et al. 2023, and the parallel-mechanism literature). The step from a soft contact term to a hard or switchable loop factor in a sliding-window MAP is engineering, not a top-venue contribution.

(b) The common-mode residual is classical. Splitting a stacked residual into a common rigid shift (torso) and differential parts (per-arm or grasp error) is parity-space fault detection and isolation, and the chi-square trigger is standard residual-based fault detection or integrity monitoring (the GNSS RAIM tradition, and switchable constraints in SLAM, Suenderhauf and Protzel 2012). Its observability condition is a standard rank argument on a stacked Jacobian. The proposal itself expects the answer may be negative for a small cube, because two near-collinear grasps leave torso rotation weakly observable. Applying this to human pose is new as an application, but it rests on one setup-specific failure (co-shifted hips hidden by a desk). The loop exists only during two-handed holds, a small fraction of frames. Detection works only if grasps were estimated cleanly before the fault, which the risks section concedes.

Overall delta. Small against I01 and against the whole-body HOI fitting line. There is no learned component, no new dataset at scale and no general theory, so the bar for CVPR/ICCV is not met. RSS is unlikely because the result is a special-case observability analysis. RA-L or IROS is plausible only if the identifiability result holds.

Uncertainty. Because no search ran, a 2025-2026 paper that does object-mediated torso correction or a bimanual-loop factor for human mocap (for example in the IMU/HOI-mocap or egocentric full-body line) could exist without being detected here. This uncertainty also argues for refuted=true.

Prior work found:
- BEHAVE: Dataset and Method for Tracking Human Object Interactions (CVPR 2022); overlap: Optimizes SMPL body (root, both arms) and object pose together with contact terms; with two-hand contact this closes the torso-arm-object-arm loop implicitly in the objective.; verified=False
- InterCap: Joint Markerless 3D Tracking of Humans and Objects in Interaction (GCPR 2022 / IJCV 2024); overlap: Multi-view whole-body plus object fitting with hand/body-object contact constraints; two-handed carries are in the data; contact jointly constrains torso, arms and object.; verified=False
- CHORE: Contact, Human and Object REconstruction from a Single RGB Image (ECCV 2022); overlap: Joint human-object fitting from a single view, driven by predicted contacts on several body parts; the joint optimization with two contacts is the soft version of the proposed loop factor.; verified=False
- Visibility Aware Human-Object Interaction Tracking from Single RGB Camera (VisTracker) (CVPR 2023); overlap: Uses human motion and contact to fill in the object under occlusion, and the reverse; the coupling exploits the same redundancy across body and object.; verified=False
- Controllable Human-Object Interaction Synthesis (CHOIS) (ECCV 2024); overlap: Imposes hand-object contact constraints for both hands while generating full-body motion from object motion; a closed bimanual chain used for consistency, though in generation, not estimation.; verified=False
- Object Motion Guided Human Motion Synthesis (OMOMO) (SIGGRAPH Asia 2023) https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16237; overlap: Predicts hand positions from object motion and conditions full-body motion on them; object-to-hands-to-body coupling.; verified=False
- Constrained Bimanual Planning with Analytic Inverse Kinematics (2023 (Cohn et al., MIT; later ICRA 2024)) https://groups.csail.mit.edu/robotics-center/public_papers/Cohn23a.pdf; overlap: Closed kinematic chain for two arms holding one object, with analytic IK parametrisation of the constraint manifold; this is the robotics source of the loop factor and its parametrisation.; verified=False
- Switchable Constraints for Robust Pose Graph SLAM (IROS 2012); overlap: The switchable loop factors that guard against a false both-holding mode; the method component is taken directly from this work.; verified=False
- IMU-HOI: A Symbiotic Framework for Coherent Human-Object Interaction and Motion Capture via Contact-Conscious Inertial Fusion (CVPR 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28604; overlap: Couples hand forward kinematics and object motion with contact gating for body/object mocap; the per-hand version of the coupling. It may already cover two-hand contact, which could not be checked because no search ran.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Do not pitch the loop factor as the contribution. Two reframes might survive. (1) Fold it into I01 as one section, a short paragraph on bimanual-hold observability with a numerical identifiability study, so it strengthens that paper instead of standing alone. (2) Reposition it as integrity monitoring for vision-based human kinematics. Any externally measured object in contact with one or two hands becomes a redundant measurement, and you derive protection levels in the RAIM style: certified bounds on torso and arm error plus detectability conditions for single and multiple faults (single-hand contact, two-hand contact, desk-occluded pelvis). Validate it on BEHAVE/InterCap with injected faults and on the lab recordings. Target RA-L/IROS, or IEEE TNSRE/J. Biomech framed as reliable ergonomic or rehab monitoring under occlusion. Even then, report a negative result if torso rotation is unobservable with small objects, and before submission run a real literature search on 2025-2026 HOI mocap papers (IMU-HOI follow-ups, egocentric full-body HOI) for object-mediated torso correction.

### feasibility skeptic: score 3, refuted=True

Feasibility skeptic verdict: refute as stated. The software is buildable by one student, but the research claim that would make this a separate paper, claim (b), probably fails because of how the thesis pipeline is built. The data and ground truth also cannot test it convincingly with the lab's resources.

1) Claim (b) is very likely unidentifiable in this pipeline. Thesis Section 3.2.2 (eqs 3.7-3.10) builds the torso frame only from the two hip landmarks and the right shoulder. The shoulders, elbows and wrists are measured directly in the world frame from back-projected landmarks; the arm angles are then expressed relative to that torso frame. The co-shifted hip error (thesis Ch. 9: "an upright person appears tilted forward") therefore re-labels the torso and arm angles while leaving the world-space wrists unchanged. The object-mediated residuals r_L and r_R compare world wrists with T_obj and the grip offsets, so they are invariant to that error whenever the wrists are measured. The common-mode residual can only see the torso when the wrists are not measured and the arm angles are held or predicted in the torso frame. That is exactly the occlusion case, where the torso error is confounded with stale held twist and stale grip offsets (the thesis already reports a stale-offset failure and a 12 cm causal/offline twist divergence).

2) Counting unknowns makes it worse. The wrist is a point without orientation (dossier), so each grasp gives 3 constraints: 6 in total, against 6 torso DoF plus 6 grip-offset DoF. Rotation about the inter-wrist axis is unobservable. For a cube held in front of the body that axis is roughly left-right, which is parallel to the forward-tilt failure itself. A torso pitch about the pelvis then maps to a common translation of both wrists, which is indistinguishable from a torso translation or a common grip bias. The idea's own risk section concedes that the identifiability result may be negative; on this pipeline it most likely is, and a negative result on one subject is not a top-venue paper.

3) The loop and the independent object measurement rarely coexist in the existing data. In the loop recording, 40 of the 41 frames with no detected marker lie in the two-hand transfer, where a hand crosses the printed square (thesis Sec. 7.4.2). The object pose is missing in exactly the frames where the loop exists. Two-hand phases are a few hundred frames across two to three recordings of one subject. The thesis also declines to evaluate the two-hand grip distances (17.9 and 15.9 cm) because no reference exists for them.

4) No ground truth for the torso. There is no mocap. "A second D435 or borrowed mocap" names neither a source nor a protocol, and a side-view D435 running the same MediaPipe hips gives pseudo-GT with its own depth failures. Unity renders give exact truth, but the loop closure there is built in by the generator, so success is close to circular. BEHAVE and InterCap have real two-handed carries with fitted SMPL and object GT. They are usable only through injected corruption and with someone else's body model rather than the thesis's swing-twist chain, and their hips are rarely hidden by a desk, so the motivating failure does not occur naturally.

5) Claim (a) is incremental. With T_obj observed, placing an occluded wrist from the object and a stored grip is already the thesis's object-conditioned recovery; the second arm adds information only when T_obj is lost. Whole-body HOI trackers that jointly optimize body, object and contact (BEHAVE-line and VisTracker-type work) already enforce the loop implicitly, so reviewers would see a special case of I01 plus standard switchable factors.

Engineering (GTSAM sliding window, switchable loop factors, HMM mode inference) is moderate and well within 12-18 months, but the research risk dominates.

Minimum viable experiment (1-2 weeks, decisive): on the R4 handover window and the handover-on-rail frames that have accepted marker poses, inject a co-shifted hip depth offset (5-20 cm) through the thesis's own torso construction. Recompute r_L and r_R with (i) measured wrists and (ii) wrists predicted by FK from angles held over a 0.5-1 s synthetic dropout. Also compute the numeric rank and the smallest singular directions of the stacked Jacobian with respect to {T_torso, G_L, G_R} for the recorded grasp geometry. If the residual does not move in case (i), and in case (ii) the torso pitch direction lies in or near the null space shared with the grip offsets, claim (b) is dead and only claim (a) remains.

Caveat: the literature search budget was exhausted in this session, so no prior work could be re-verified here. Every entry below is therefore marked verified=false, including the five works the idea itself lists as verified.

Prior work found:
- IMU-HOI: A Symbiotic Framework for Coherent Human-Object Interaction and Motion Capture via Contact-Conscious Inertial Fusion (CVPR 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28604; overlap: Couples hand forward kinematics with object motion under contact gating, per hand. This is the per-hand version of the loop factor.; verified=False
- BIGS: Bimanual Category-agnostic Interaction Reconstruction from Monocular Videos via 3D Gaussian Splatting (CVPR 2025) https://cvpr.thecvf.com/virtual/2025/poster/34060; overlap: Reconstructs two hands and one object jointly. Hands only; no arm-torso loop.; verified=False
- Object Motion Guided Human Motion Synthesis (OMOMO) (SIGGRAPH Asia 2023 (TOG)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16237; overlap: Infers full-body motion, including two-handed carries, from object motion: an object-to-body mapping without consistency checking.; verified=False
- Constrained Bimanual Planning with Analytic Inverse Kinematics (2023 (MIT CSAIL)) https://groups.csail.mit.edu/robotics-center/public_papers/Cohn23a.pdf; overlap: Closed-chain constraint for two arms holding one object in robot planning, with known kinematics and no estimation.; verified=False
- A Taxonomy of Self-Handover (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04939; overlap: Characterizes human self-handover phases. Relevant to the mode-sequence model; proposes no estimator.; verified=False
- BEHAVE: Dataset and Method for Tracking Human Object Interactions (CVPR 2022); overlap: Multi-view RGB-D dataset with fitted SMPL and object poses that includes two-handed carries. Its joint body-object-contact fitting enforces the loop implicitly. It is the only realistic source of non-synthetic two-hand ground truth for this idea.; verified=False
- Visibility Aware Human-Object Interaction Tracking from Single RGB Camera (VisTracker) (CVPR 2023); overlap: Jointly tracks body and object with contact under occlusion from one camera. This is the closest existing joint body-object estimator that a reviewer would compare against.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Drop the torso-diagnosis claim and do not position this as a standalone paper. Fold a smaller, testable piece into I01 as one section or an RA-L short: "object-mediated cross-hand transfer during bimanual holds".

The problem: when both hands hold the object, the hands occlude the marker (40 of 41 missing marker frames in the loop recording fall in the two-hand transfer). The method: grip offsets estimated on clean frames, the still-visible wrist and the stored grips reconstruct the object pose, and the object pose then places the occluded wrist. Switchable loop factors guard against false "both holding" states, and an HMM over closure residuals replaces the 25/35 cm hysteresis for handover onset and offset.

Evaluation that can produce real ground truth with this lab: synthetic marker and landmark dropouts on frames where both the marker and both wrists are visible (truth = the held-out measurement); manual wrist labels on the handover windows; BEHAVE two-handed carries with injected dropouts as a public benchmark.

Handle the torso-frame problem with a direct measurement rather than through the loop: a cheap second D435 side view of the hips, or a gravity-plus-shoulder torso prior from the wall marker, validated on a short new recording.

Run the 1-2 week rank and injection experiment first. Claim (b) returns only if that experiment shows torso observability in the FK-predicted (occluded) regime.

### venue skeptic: score 3, refuted=True

Area-chair view, target RSS 2027 / ICRA-RA-L / CVPR-ICCV. Web search was unavailable in this run (session search budget exhausted), so the judgement relies on the dossier, the candidate's own verified citations, and prior knowledge marked verified=false below.

1. The headline claim is weak and partly already covered. Joint human-object reconstruction that applies contact terms to both hands at once already closes the torso-arm-object-arm loop implicitly. Examples are PHOSA (ECCV 2020), CHORE (ECCV 2022), VisTracker (CVPR 2023), and the BEHAVE/InterCap fitting pipelines. These systems optimize SMPL body pose and object pose under contact losses for both hands. A reviewer will say that "enforcing the loop in MAP" is ordinary bimanual contact-constrained HOI fitting with an extra name, and that the analogy to SLAM loop closure is rhetoric. The why_novel text admits that the idea collapses into I01 applied to two hands, so part (a) has no separate contribution.

2. Part (b), the torso-diagnosis claim, is the only distinctive piece, and its mechanism is doubtful for this pipeline. In the thesis, wrists, elbows and shoulders are landmarks measured directly in camera or world coordinates. The hips enter only the torso frame that is used to decompose the joint angles. If co-shifted hips corrupt that frame, the error is pushed into the decomposed angles while the measured wrist positions stay where they are, so the wrist-object closure residual can stay near zero. A common-mode residual appears only if the forward kinematics is rebuilt from a torso origin plus angles and segment lengths. In that case the eight arm DOF (four per arm) can absorb most of a torso translation within reach. Wrist orientation is not tracked, so each grasp closure gives about 3 positional constraints, not 6. With 6 torso DOF plus 8 arm DOF plus grasp latents, the 6 loop constraints leave the torso unobservable unless strong priors are added. The candidate's own risk list says the identifiability result may be negative. A paper whose central theorem is likely to show unobservability will not be accepted at RSS or CVPR.

3. The problem is niche. The loop exists only while both hands rigidly hold the object, a small fraction of frames in the existing recordings (two handovers in R4 plus one handover-on-rail). The failure being fixed, desk-hidden hips co-shifting under one top-down D435, is an artifact of this particular rig, and multi-view, IMU or a better detector removes it directly. Reviewers will ask why one would not simply add a second camera, which the thesis itself lists as future work.

4. The evaluation is not convincing at top-venue level. The evaluation is one subject, a few handovers, and no torso ground truth (no mocap; the plan says "borrowed mocap" and "second D435"). The main torso-fault test is a synthetic co-shifted-hips injection, which reviewers will call circular because the method is tested on the fault model it was built to detect. On public data (BEHAVE, InterCap) the SMPL ground truth comes from fitting that already uses contact and multi-view, so a gain there is hard to read. HO-Cap has no arm annotations, as the candidate notes. Several standard baselines are missing: a full-body HOI optimizer with contact terms on both hands (VisTracker or CHORE style), multi-view fusion, and IMU-based HOI capture (IMU-HOI, CVPR 2026, already in the candidate list). Without these, the comparison against the thesis's own per-hand recovery is a comparison with a weak baseline.

5. Venue fit. RSS and CVPR expect either a general estimator or a strong empirical leap, and this is neither. The realistic home is a workshop paper, IROS, or IEEE RA-L as an observability and self-diagnosis study. At best it is one section or ablation of I01. Expected acceptance quality at the best-fitting top venue (RA-L/ICRA) is about 3/10; at CVPR, RSS or ICCV, below that.

Prior work found:
- IMU-HOI: A Symbiotic Framework for Coherent Human-Object Interaction and Motion Capture via Contact-Conscious Inertial Fusion (CVPR 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28604; overlap: Couples hand forward kinematics with object motion through contact-gated fusion. It is the strongest recent baseline for contact-coupled body-object state estimation, and it is not compared against under equal sensing.; verified=True
- BIGS: Bimanual Category-agnostic Interaction Reconstruction from Monocular Videos via 3D Gaussian Splatting (CVPR 2025) https://cvpr.thecvf.com/virtual/2025/poster/34060; overlap: Joint reconstruction of two hands and the object. Consistency between both hands and the object is already enforced at hand level.; verified=True
- Object Motion Guided Human Motion Synthesis (OMOMO) (SIGGRAPH Asia 2023 (TOG)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16237; overlap: Infers full-body motion, including both arms, from object motion under hand-object contact. This is the generative counterpart of using the object to constrain the body.; verified=True
- Visibility Aware Human-Object Interaction Tracking from Single RGB Camera (VisTracker) (CVPR 2023); overlap: Joint SMPL and object tracking with contact terms under occlusion. Contact applied to both hands at once already closes the body-object loop implicitly, and this is the standard baseline the idea omits.; verified=False
- CHORE: Contact, Human and Object REconstruction from a single RGB image (ECCV 2022); overlap: Joint human-object fitting with contact constraints. It weakens the claim that closed-chain constraints are not used for human state estimation from vision.; verified=False
- Perceiving 3D Human-Object Spatial Arrangements from a Single Image in the Wild (PHOSA) (ECCV 2020); overlap: An early interaction loss that couples the body and the held object during optimization.; verified=False
- Constrained Bimanual Planning with Analytic Inverse Kinematics (2023 (MIT CSAIL)) https://groups.csail.mit.edu/robotics-center/public_papers/Cohn23a.pdf; overlap: Closed-chain constraints for two arms holding one object, used for planning. The closed-chain idea is textbook, so the only delta is applying it to human estimation.; verified=True

Suggested reframe: Do not sell the closed loop as the contribution. Fold it into I01 as a mode-switched factor that turns on when both hands hold the object, and report it as an ablation. If the torso-diagnosis result is kept, reframe it as a general observability study, "When can a manipulated object act as a sensor for the body frame?", with these parts: (1) a rank and conditioning analysis covering wrist position only vs full wrist pose, known vs unknown grasps, and grasp geometry; (2) an explicit statement of which body-frame errors the loop cannot see, including the null space absorbed by arm DOF; (3) validation on a public multi-view HOI dataset with mocap-quality torso ground truth, against a contact-aware full-body HOI optimizer baseline. Submit to IEEE RA-L or IROS, not RSS or CVPR. Before investing, run a one-week Unity check: inject co-shifted hips and test whether the closure residual is non-zero at all once wrists are measured directly. If it is not, drop part (b).

## I07: Observability-aware single-view RGB-D swing-twist tracking: detecting unobservable degrees of freedom, applying priors only along them, and deferring commitment

Direction: Identifiability and observability of articulated pose from partial sensing: a localizability/degeneracy analysis, as in LiDAR registration, carried to the human arm seen by one depth camera, with multi-hypothesis deferred commitment instead of silent guessing.

Research question: For a swing-twist arm chain observed by 2D landmarks plus depth from one RGB-D camera, can a real-time tracker compute per DOF and per frame (from the Fisher information of the measurement Jacobian under a measured D435 range-dependent noise model) whether shoulder twist, elbow flexion and depth along the optical axis are observable; does injecting priors (grasp closure, joint limits, motion prior, direction memory) only along the unobservable directions reduce bias on observable DOF and error on unobservable ones compared with isotropic regularization; does the observability score predict the thesis's known failure configurations (arm along the optical axis, near-straight arm twist, full reach) before they cause errors; and does replacing a single committed estimate with a deferred multi-hypothesis estimate during unobservable spells prevent irreversible causal-vs-offline divergence?

Why novel (as proposed): Degeneracy-aware estimation is mature in LiDAR registration: X-ICP (T-RO 2024), Informed, Constrained, Aligned (T-FR 2025), DCReg (arXiv 2025), probabilistic degeneracy detection (arXiv 2024), but for rigid scan matching. HuManiFlow (CVPR 2023) and HuProSO3 (CVPR 2024) learn per-part SO(3) distributions that show ambiguity qualitatively but issue no per-DOF observability decisions and are not causal trackers. AKC (arXiv 2606.19240) fixes occluded arm depth deterministically with no observability reasoning; IKOL (AAAI 2023) is a differentiable IK layer without observability analysis. The thesis handles these failures with hand-set thresholds (98 percent of reach, twist hold below 15 deg released above 25 deg flexion). Delta: a quantitative, sensor-model-based observability score per swing-twist DOF including D435 depth-noise growth with range and near-singular configurations; closed-form and numerical observability maps of the arm vs camera geometry; a fourth provenance label 'unobservable' with a conformal-risk-controlled false-observable rate; a deferred-commitment filter on twist; and camera-placement guidance. It is a transfer of a known idea to a new structure, so the analysis and practical outcome must carry it.

Method (as proposed): Model: the v3 unit-quaternion swing-twist chain, differentiable (JAX or PyTorch), with calibrated effective lengths. Measurement model: landmarks p = pi^-1(u, d) with pixel noise per landmark measured from clean windows against the zero-phase oracle and D435 depth noise as a function of range and surface angle (sigma_z proportional to z^2; about one day with a flat target and desk plane). Observability: Fisher information I(q) = J^T Sigma^-1 J over a short causal window; eigen-decomposition gives per-DOF information; a DOF is 'unobservable' when its projected information falls below a threshold set by a chi-square test or by conformal risk control (guaranteed false-observable rate). Closed-form special cases: twist of a straight or hanging arm, depth of an arm pointing at the camera, hip depth under desk occlusion. Precomputed observability maps over arm configuration x camera pose in Unity. Estimator: Gauss-Newton/LM on the window with priors projected onto low-eigenvalue eigenvectors (soft projected prior or hard lock, the 'informed'/'constrained' variants), prior sources being I01 grasp closure, direction memory (alpha = 0.30), joint limits and an optional learned pose prior; plus a Rao-Blackwellized or Gaussian-sum filter on twist (S1) that branches into K hypotheses when twist becomes unobservable and prunes when information returns. Unity shows an 'unobservable' colour, never a confident angle. Side result: camera placement minimising expected unobservable mass for a task. Cheap twist truth: an ArUco forearm cuff or wrist IMU tracked by the existing marker branch.

Evaluation (as proposed): Own recordings with forearm cuff/IMU across 4-6 subjects and configurations designed to sweep observability (straight reaches, hanging arms, arm-along-axis pointing); Unity synthetic sweeps over arm configurations and camera poses with exact truth; BEHAVE/InterCap single view with SMPL arm truth; natural failure windows with manual labels. Metrics: per-DOF error (swing, twist, elbow) by observability bin; calibration of the observability label; failure-prediction AUROC; bias on well-observed DOF; causal-vs-offline divergence after gaps (the 12 cm case and new cases); reacquisition teleports; false_measured. Baselines: thesis rule cascade and v1/v3 hold-last and S2, unregularised LM, isotropic Tikhonov, single-mode tangent Kalman, AKC, MotionBERT-lite aligned to depth, HuManiFlow/ProHMR samples mapped to swing-twist. Ablations: Fisher score vs covariance trace vs learned score; soft vs hard projection; eigenvalue threshold; with/without branching; window length; with/without depth noise model.

Target venues: ICRA 2027, IROS 2027, IEEE RA-L, RSS 2027 (if the observability analysis is crisp), IEEE T-RO, IEEE TNSRE (clinical shoulder range-of-motion assessment)

Builds on the thesis: The explicit swing-twist parameterisation with oracle-verified quaternion math makes analytic Jacobians and per-DOF observability possible; documented unobservable cases (hanging-arm twist, arm along optical axis, desk-hidden hips) and the hand-set thresholds the method replaces; the metric RGB-D world frame; the Unity sensor-POV pipeline for sweeps; the honesty-flag display contract; the measured causal-vs-offline divergence; the ArUco branch makes a forearm cuff reference almost free.

Feasibility (as proposed): All software plus a depth-noise characterisation session; 1-2 small IMUs or printed cuffs (under USD 100); 4-6 subjects, 1-2 sessions each; no new compute. Timeline: month 1 noise model and Fisher analysis; month 2 Unity observability maps and branching filter; month 3 cuff recordings; months 4-5 evaluation; month 6 writing. Realistic first-year graduate RA-L/ICRA paper.

Risks (as proposed): Vision reviewers may find Fisher-information observability classical ('known robotics idea, modest delta') and ask why not a learned lifter, so the paper must show learned probabilistic models are overconfident exactly in low-observability bins; gains may be small on slow desk tasks; the most damaging errors (wrong body part, wrong-surface depth) are outliers rather than degeneracy, so robust losses are needed too; cuffs and IMUs may change detector behaviour (validate on control frames).

### novelty skeptic: score 3.5, refuted=True

Search caveat first. The WebSearch budget for this session was already used up (200 of 200), and WebFetch to arxiv.org, export.arxiv.org and api.semanticscholar.org was blocked by the egress proxy. So this run verified nothing new. The prior work below is cited from model knowledge and marked verified=false. The candidate's own eight citations were marked verified upstream; I could not re-check them. Following the default rule for uncertain cases, the verdict is refuted=true. The analysis also points the same way.

Why the delta is too small for a top venue:
(1) Every core mechanism the idea calls new is already published for articulated human tracking, not only for LiDAR scan matching.
- Morris and Rehg (CVPR 1998), "Singularity analysis for articulated object tracking", ran a Jacobian rank and singularity analysis of a kinematic chain under camera projection. It identified exactly the configurations where joint DOF become unobservable, such as a limb along the optical axis or twist about a straight limb.
- Sminchisescu and Triggs (Covariance Scaled Sampling, CVPR 2001; Kinematic Jump Processes, CVPR 2003) went further. They used the inverse Hessian (the Fisher information) to find poorly constrained directions of a monocular body tracker, sampled and searched along them, and kept multiple hypotheses for the forward/backward depth flips. That is the "detect unobservable DOF and defer commitment" idea. The proposal effectively re-derives it with depth added and a 4-DOF arm.
- Zhang, Kaess and Singh (ICRA 2016), "On degeneracy of optimization-based state estimation problems", is the generic source of eigen-decomposing the information matrix and acting only along degenerate directions. X-ICP and "Informed, Constrained, Aligned" build on it. Applying it to a 4-DOF arm is a direct instantiation, not a new estimator.
(2) Multi-hypothesis output under depth and twist ambiguity, with kinematic constraints, is current mainstream in pose estimation:
- MHFormer (CVPR 2022)
- DiffPose (CVPR 2023) and D3DP (ICCV 2023)
- ManiPose (NeurIPS 2024), which argues that single-hypothesis regression is inconsistent under depth ambiguity and enforces manifold or kinematic constraints across hypotheses
- ProHMR (ICCV 2021), HuManiFlow (CVPR 2023) and HuProSO3 (CVPR 2024), which give per-joint SO(3) distributions
- POCO (3DV 2024), which gives per-part pose confidence
A per-DOF "unobservable" flag is a thresholded version of what these models already output. Reviewers will say the gap is causality and a depth-noise model, not a new concept.
(3) Observability of joint angles in kinematic chains under partial sensing is also studied in the IMU motion-capture literature (Seel, Laidig, Weygers and colleagues on magnetometer-free joint-angle estimation; titles not verified here). Clinical work on Kinect and Azure Kinect has long reported that shoulder internal and external rotation is unreliable from one depth camera. So the empirical finding "twist is unobservable" is not new either.
(4) What remains plausibly new:
- a measured D435 range-dependent noise model folded into the per-DOF information
- a conformal or risk-controlled false-observable rate for the unobservable label
- Gaussian-sum deferral applied specifically to shoulder twist in a causal RGB-D tracker
- camera-placement guidance
Each is incremental engineering on known pieces. The proposal itself admits the gains may be small on slow desk tasks, and that the most damaging thesis errors (wrong body part, wrong-surface depth) are outliers, not degeneracy. The method does not address those.

Overall: at most an RA-L/ICRA systems paper, and only with strong empirical evidence. It is not a CVPR/RSS-level contribution as framed. Novelty 3.5/10.

Prior work found:
- Singularity analysis for articulated object tracking (CVPR 1998); overlap: Jacobian rank and singularity analysis of an articulated chain under projection. Identifies the configurations (limb along the optical axis, twist about a straight limb) where DOF are unobservable. This is the analytic core of the proposal, published for human tracking 28 years ago.; verified=False
- Covariance Scaled Sampling for Monocular 3D Body Tracking (CVPR 2001); overlap: Uses the inverse Hessian (Fisher information) of the image likelihood to find poorly observed directions of a body tracker and spreads hypotheses along them. Equivalent to acting only along low-eigenvalue directions plus multi-hypothesis.; verified=False
- Kinematic Jump Processes for Monocular 3D Human Tracking (CVPR 2003); overlap: Explicitly enumerates the depth-ambiguity (forward/backward flip) modes of each limb and keeps multiple hypotheses instead of committing. This matches the deferred-commitment branching filter.; verified=False
- On degeneracy of optimization-based state estimation problems (ICRA 2016); overlap: Generic eigen-analysis of the information matrix with updates projected away from degenerate directions (solution remapping). This is the method template for the 'informed/constrained' prior projection; X-ICP and its successors build on it.; verified=False
- ManiPose: Manifold-Constrained Multi-Hypothesis 3D Human Pose Estimation (NeurIPS 2024); overlap: Argues that single-hypothesis estimators are inconsistent under monocular depth ambiguity and produces multiple kinematically constrained hypotheses. Overlaps the deferred multi-hypothesis claim, though it is learned and not causal.; verified=False
- POCO: 3D Pose and Shape Estimation with Confidence (3DV 2024); overlap: Per-body-part confidence for regressed SMPL pose. A learned analogue of a per-part observability or trust label.; verified=False
- HuManiFlow: Ancestor-Conditioned Normalising Flows on SO(3) Manifolds for Human Pose and Shape Distribution Estimation (CVPR 2023) https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.06968; overlap: Per-joint SO(3) distributions that widen exactly on ambiguous DOF such as limb twist. Thresholding these gives an unobservable flag. Verified upstream, not re-verified in this run.; verified=False
- X-ICP: Localizability-Aware LiDAR Registration for Robust Localization in Extreme Environments (IEEE T-RO 2024) https://www.researchgate.net/publication/375903094_X-ICP_Localizability-Aware_LiDAR_Registration_for_Robust_Localization_in_Extreme_Environments; overlap: Per-direction localizability detection plus constraints applied only along degenerate directions. The proposal is a domain transfer of this. Verified upstream, not re-verified in this run.; verified=False
- Informed, Constrained, Aligned: A Field Analysis on Degeneracy-Aware Point Cloud Registration in the Wild (IEEE T-FR 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11809; overlap: Compares soft (informed) and hard (constrained) degeneracy mitigation, which are exactly the proposal's soft/hard projected-prior ablation. Verified upstream, not re-verified in this run.; verified=False
- Seeing Through Occlusion: Deterministic Arm Kinematic Correction for Robot Teleoperation (AKC) (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19240; overlap: Same sensing setup (single RGB-D, arm under occlusion and depth error) with a deterministic correction. The proposal adds observability reasoning on top. Verified upstream, not re-verified in this run.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Stop claiming observability-aware estimation as the method contribution: Morris-Rehg 1998, Sminchisescu 2001/2003 and Zhang 2016 own it. Reposition as a measurement and benchmark paper with a single-sentence thesis: "Learned 3D pose estimators are confidently wrong exactly where a single RGB-D camera cannot see." Concretely:
(a) Use the sensor-model Fisher analysis (D435 range-dependent noise, swing-twist Jacobian) only as a labelling tool. Produce per-frame, per-DOF identifiability labels on Unity sweeps and on BEHAVE/InterCap single-view frames, with forearm-cuff truth on own recordings.
(b) Evaluate whether ProHMR, HuManiFlow, HuProSO3, POCO, ManiPose and diffusion multi-hypothesis lifters widen their uncertainty in the low-identifiability bins. Report calibration (coverage, ECE) by bin.
(c) Show that a simple identifiability-gated wrapper, with conformal risk control on a 'not measurable' output, fixes calibration at low cost.
This turns the known idea into a new empirical finding about current models, which vision venues value. An alternative with a stronger robotics novelty hook: an active-perception paper for a robot-mounted or movable camera. Use the observability map to choose camera viewpoints online, maximising arm-twist and elbow identifiability during human-robot handover. Evaluate on handover success or tracking error, aiming at RA-L/ICRA/IROS. A third option is a clinical TNSRE paper: shoulder range-of-motion measurement that refuses to report unidentifiable rotation, plus placement guidelines validated against goniometry or IMUs on 10-20 subjects. Whichever is chosen, rerun the literature search once the search budget allows. Two things would change this assessment: any 2024-2026 paper on per-DOF observability for RGB-D human tracking, or on calibration of HMR uncertainty against geometric identifiability.

### feasibility skeptic: score 6, refuted=False

Feasibility verdict: the core is feasible with the stated lab, but the evaluation plan is larger than it needs to be, and its real-data twist ground truth is weaker than the idea admits. It survives only in a narrower form.

What is feasible and cheap. The method is pure software on assets that already exist. The v3 unit-quaternion swing-twist chain is oracle-verified to 1e-9 deg against v1, so analytic or autodiff Jacobians are straightforward. The thesis already derives the twist degeneracy in Section 3.4.3: twist is read from the forearm component perpendicular to the upper arm, which vanishes for a straight arm. The thesis already replaces this with hand-set rules, a twist hold below 15 deg of flexion released above 25 deg, and direction memory above 98 percent of reach (Section 5.5, Eq. 5.9). That gives a concrete, documented baseline for a Fisher-information rule to beat. A D435 range-noise model (sigma_z growing roughly with z^2) takes one day with a flat target. Unity sensor-POV capture already exists, so synthetic sweeps over arm configuration and camera pose with exact joint truth are achievable in weeks. Multi-hypothesis filtering on a single S1 twist variable is a small Gaussian-sum or particle filter. Nothing needs training data or GPU time.

Where the plan outruns the resources.
(1) Real twist ground truth is the weak point. The proposed "ArUco forearm cuff" measures the wrong segment: forearm orientation mixes shoulder twist with forearm pronation, which the thesis states is unobserved. Shoulder twist truth needs a sensor on the upper arm plus a torso reference. Upper-arm skin-mounted sensors have large soft-tissue artifact in exactly the axial-rotation direction, on the order of the effect being measured. A small planar marker wrapped on a cylindrical limb also suffers pose ambiguity and foreshortening exactly when the arm points along the optical axis, which is the configuration of interest. Cheap IMUs (under USD 100) need time sync, sensor-to-segment calibration and yaw-drift handling: weeks of engineering the idea does not budget. As a result, real-data twist truth is weakest precisely in the low-observability bins the paper is about.
(2) "4-6 subjects" needs a human-subjects ethics approval, which the plan does not mention. The lab has one subject's recordings.
(3) BEHAVE and InterCap SMPL arm twist is itself a multi-view fit, i.e. pseudo ground truth. v3/V3_FINDINGS.md already notes that mapping SMPL rotations to swing-twist is not direct, because the collar and spine share shoulder motion.
(4) The conformal false-observable guarantee needs exchangeable labelled calibration data. Calibrating on Unity and testing on real data breaks the guarantee, and real labels are scarce.
(5) Baselines such as HuManiFlow/ProHMR samples mapped to swing-twist, MotionBERT aligned to depth, plus camera-placement optimisation, are each a sub-project.
(6) The headline real-data motivation, causal-vs-offline divergence, rests on a single 12 cm event. New recordings must be staged to create more such events, and a staged event is easy for reviewers to call cherry-picked.
(7) The thesis's most damaging errors are outliers, not degeneracy: a landmark on the wrong body part with high confidence, or depth taken from the wrong surface. The measured gain on real desk recordings may therefore be small unless robust losses are added. Whether that matters is a significance question, but it decides whether a 12-18 month effort yields a publishable delta.

Minimum viable experiment (8-10 weeks, no new hardware).
(a) Fit a D435 noise model from a flat-target session.
(b) In Unity, render the existing avatar from the sensor POV over a grid of shoulder swing, twist and elbow flexion and of camera pose. Add the measured depth noise and pixel noise taken from clean windows of the real recordings. Run the v3 landmark lifter.
(c) Compute the per-DOF Fisher score over a short causal window. Report AUROC for predicting twist error above 10 deg, and for elbow-depth error above 3 cm, against three predictors: the Fisher score, detector confidence, and the thesis's 15/25 deg flexion rule.
(d) Compare four estimators on per-DOF error stratified by observability bin, including bias on the well-observed DOF: (i) the thesis hold rule; (ii) isotropic Tikhonov; (iii) a prior projected onto low-information eigenvectors; (iv) the projected prior plus a K-hypothesis twist filter.
(e) On the existing 28 bags, with no new subjects, check that the score fires on the known failure windows: the 12 cm divergence window at frames 7-24, the arm-along-optical-axis window, and straight-arm slides. Measure the downstream effect with quantities that already have truth: wrist error against the manual wrist labels and the wrist-to-cube offsets.
If (c) gives a clear AUROC margin over the thesis rule and (d) lowers unobservable-bin error without biasing observable DOF, the idea works. If not, drop it before any IMU or subject work.

Venue realism. RA-L/ICRA or IROS is plausible, built on synthetic evaluation plus a small real study. RSS or T-RO would need a crisper general observability result than a 4-DOF chain with three landmarks, whose twist information is close to closed form (proportional to the squared perpendicular forearm component over the noise variance).

Literature verification: this run's web search budget was exhausted, so none of the cited works could be re-verified here. The entries below are carried from the idea record and marked verified=false accordingly.

Prior work found:
- X-ICP: Localizability-Aware LiDAR Registration for Robust Localization in Extreme Environments (IEEE T-RO 2024) https://www.researchgate.net/publication/375903094_X-ICP_Localizability-Aware_LiDAR_Registration_for_Robust_Localization_in_Extreme_Environments; overlap: Source of the localizability-aware constrained optimisation that the idea transfers to the arm chain; the soft and hard projected-prior variants are its analogue.; verified=False
- Informed, Constrained, Aligned: A Field Analysis on Degeneracy-Aware Point Cloud Registration in the Wild (IEEE Transactions on Field Robotics 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11809; overlap: Taxonomy of how to inject priors only along degenerate directions; the idea's 'informed' and 'constrained' variants are borrowed from it.; verified=False
- DCReg: Decoupled Characterization for Efficient Degenerate LiDAR Registration (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.06285; overlap: Per-direction degeneracy characterisation for rigid registration.; verified=False
- Probabilistic Degeneracy Detection for Point-to-Plane Error Minimization (arXiv 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10784; overlap: Noise-model-based statistical degeneracy test, analogous to the proposed chi-square observability threshold.; verified=False
- HuManiFlow: Ancestor-Conditioned Normalising Flows on SO(3) Manifolds for Human Pose and Shape Distribution Estimation (CVPR 2023) https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.06968; overlap: Learns per-part SO(3) uncertainty that shows axial-twist ambiguity, but issues no per-DOF observability decision, is not a causal tracker and uses no depth noise model. A required baseline that is costly to map to swing-twist.; verified=False
- Normalizing Flows on the Product Space of SO(3) Manifolds for Probabilistic Human Pose Modeling (HuProSO3) (CVPR 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05675; overlap: Probabilistic per-joint rotation distributions; same gap as HuManiFlow.; verified=False
- Seeing Through Occlusion: Deterministic Arm Kinematic Correction for Robot Teleoperation (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19240; overlap: Deterministic correction of occluded arm depth with no observability reasoning; already re-implemented in the eval track (knowledge/akc_comparison.md), so a cheap baseline.; verified=False
- IKOL: Inverse Kinematics Optimization Layer for 3D Human Pose and Shape Estimation via Gauss-Newton Differentiation (AAAI 2023) https://www.researchgate.net/publication/371915054_IKOL_Inverse_Kinematics_Optimization_Layer_for_3D_Human_Pose_and_Shape_Estimation_via_Gauss-Newton_Differentiation; overlap: Differentiable Gauss-Newton IK layer without observability analysis.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Scope it down to "a sensor-model-based observability score and projected priors for single-view RGB-D arm tracking", evaluated mainly in simulation with a small real-data check.
(1) Drop the 4-6 subject IMU/cuff study, BEHAVE/InterCap, the conformal guarantee, camera-placement optimisation and the learned-flow baselines from the core paper.
(2) Core contribution: a per-DOF Fisher-information score under a measured D435 noise model, replacing the thesis's hand-set 15/25 deg twist hysteresis and 98 percent reach rule. Projected priors plus a K-hypothesis twist filter replace hold-last.
(3) Ground truth: Unity sensor-POV sweeps with exact joint truth, using noise injected from the real D435 model and real pixel-noise statistics.
(4) Real-data check on the existing one-subject bags, using downstream quantities that already have truth (manual wrist labels, wrist-to-cube grip offsets, causal-vs-offline wrist divergence). Twist itself is not measured on real data.
(5) Optional later extension: real twist truth from an upper-arm plus torso IMU pair, with the soft-tissue artifact stated as a caveat and ethics approval obtained first.
Target RA-L with an ICRA/IROS option.
Go/no-go after 8-10 weeks: the Fisher score must beat the thesis rule and detector confidence on twist-failure AUROC in simulation, and must fire on the known 12 cm divergence window (frames 7-24) and the arm-along-optical-axis window in the real bags.

### venue skeptic: score 4, refuted=True

Area-chair view for ICRA/IROS/RA-L, the best-fitting venues. RSS and T-RO are out of reach as currently scoped.

1) The novelty delta is weaker than the idea claims. Observability and singularity analysis of articulated chains under image projection is old work in vision-based human tracking. Morris and Rehg's singularity analysis for articulated object tracking (CVPR 1998) finds exactly the rank-deficient Jacobian configurations, such as a limb along the optical axis. Sminchisescu and Triggs used inverse-Hessian (Fisher) covariance to scale multi-hypothesis sampling along poorly constrained directions (covariance-scaled sampling, CVPR 2001) and kinematic jump processes for depth and flip ambiguities (CVPR 2003). The plan to "branch into K hypotheses while a DOF is unobservable" is essentially that line of work. I recall these papers but could not search this session, so they are marked unverified. The LiDAR degeneracy papers the idea cites (X-ICP, Informed/Constrained/Aligned, DCReg) add projected and constrained priors along low-eigenvalue directions. Moving that to a 4-DOF arm plus a z^2 depth-noise model is a transfer. Reviewers will call it "classical estimation applied to a small chain". The idea's own risk section concedes this.

2) The problem is niche, and the headline is not sharp. "Twist of a hanging arm is unobservable" and "depth of an arm pointing at the camera is ill-conditioned" are textbook. A closed-form observability map that confirms them is not a finding. The publishable claim would be empirical and has not been shown yet: for example, that a Fisher score predicts silent errors better than learned uncertainty (HuManiFlow, HuProSO3, ProHMR samples), or that observability-aware abstention removes most of the large errors at a small coverage cost. Two things in the thesis cut against even that. Its most damaging errors are outliers (landmark on the wrong body part, depth from the wrong surface, co-shifting hips), and Fisher information computed at a wrong measurement is confidently wrong. And the slow desk task may show small gains.

3) The evaluation is not convincing for a top venue. Twist truth from a printed ArUco cuff or a cheap IMU over 4-6 subjects with no optical motion capture will be challenged: the cuff's own pose error is of the same order as the twist errors being measured. Only one D435 and one room were used. Unity synthetic sweeps carry most of the observability-map evidence, and reviewers discount synthetic truth for exactly the sensor-noise effects being modelled. BEHAVE and InterCap give SMPL pseudo-truth whose twist is itself weakly constrained, which is circular for a twist-observability claim. The 12 cm causal-versus-offline divergence is a single anecdote.

4) The baseline list is reasonable but incomplete. A plain covariance-trace or EKF innovation-gating baseline, a modern learned HMR with uncertainty run on the same frames, and a multi-hypothesis particle filter (the classical answer) are all needed. Without them a reviewer can say a standard EKF with joint limits already gives the abstention behaviour.

5) Fit. ICRA/IROS/RA-L will accept a clean, honest version: a deterministic observability-aware arm tracker for teleoperation or rehabilitation, with failure-prediction AUROC and a selective-risk curve. CVPR/ECCV will not. RSS needs a crisp theoretical result, and this has none beyond eigen-analysis. TNSRE needs a clinical cohort and validation against goniometry or motion capture, which the plan lacks.

Net: a plausible workshop paper or a borderline RA-L submission. For a "top venue" target, the contribution is incremental and the ground truth is weak. Refuted on this lens unless it is reframed and given stronger ground truth.

Prior work found:
- Singularity Analysis for Articulated Object Tracking (Morris and Rehg) (CVPR 1998); overlap: Jacobian-rank and singularity analysis of kinematic chains under image projection, including limb-along-optical-axis degeneracy; directly anticipates the closed-form observability maps.; verified=False
- Covariance Scaled Sampling for Monocular 3D Body Tracking (Sminchisescu and Triggs) (CVPR 2001); overlap: Uses inverse-Hessian (Fisher) covariance to direct multi-hypothesis search along poorly observed pose directions; close to the branching-on-unobservable-DOF filter.; verified=False
- Kinematic Jump Processes for Monocular 3D Human Tracking (Sminchisescu and Triggs) (CVPR 2003); overlap: Explicit enumeration of depth-flip ambiguities of limbs with multi-hypothesis tracking; anticipates deferred commitment.; verified=False
- X-ICP: Localizability-Aware LiDAR Registration for Robust Localization in Extreme Environments (IEEE T-RO 2024) https://www.researchgate.net/publication/375903094_X-ICP_Localizability-Aware_LiDAR_Registration_for_Robust_Localization_in_Extreme_Environments; overlap: Per-direction localizability detection plus constrained optimization along degenerate directions; the source of the transferred method.; verified=True
- Informed, Constrained, Aligned: A Field Analysis on Degeneracy-Aware Point Cloud Registration in the Wild (IEEE T-FR 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11809; overlap: Compares soft-prior versus hard-constraint handling of degenerate directions; the same ablation the idea proposes.; verified=True
- HuManiFlow: Ancestor-Conditioned Normalising Flows on SO(3) Manifolds for Human Pose and Shape Distribution Estimation (CVPR 2023) https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.06968; overlap: Per-part SO(3) uncertainty that captures twist ambiguity; the learned-uncertainty baseline reviewers will demand.; verified=True
- Normalizing Flows on the Product Space of SO(3) Manifolds for Probabilistic Human Pose Modeling (HuProSO3) (CVPR 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05675; overlap: Probabilistic joint-rotation modelling that exposes unobservable twist; a competing uncertainty source.; verified=True
- Seeing Through Occlusion: Deterministic Arm Kinematic Correction for Robot Teleoperation (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19240; overlap: Same application (single-camera arm tracking for teleoperation) with deterministic occlusion fixes; the nearest system baseline.; verified=True

Suggested reframe: Pitch it as a robotics systems and safety paper for RA-L (with the ICRA option): "Know when you cannot see the arm: calibrated abstention for single-camera RGB-D arm tracking in teleoperation and rehabilitation", not as a new observability theory. The headline claim should be measurable: on N subjects with optical motion capture (borrow a lab or clinic system for one day rather than relying on an ArUco cuff), a sensor-model Fisher score plus robust outlier gating predicts large joint-angle errors (AUROC) better than learned uncertainty (HuManiFlow/ProHMR samples) and EKF covariance; at a fixed coverage it cuts the silent-error rate (errors above 15 deg labelled measured) by a stated factor, with a conformal guarantee on the false-observable rate. Credit the classical singularity and covariance-scaled-sampling work rather than claiming the transfer as new. Fold outlier robustness in (wrong-surface depth, wrong-part landmarks), since that is where the thesis's real failures were. Keep the camera-placement guidance as a short practical section. Drop the RSS/T-RO and TNSRE targets unless a clinical cohort with goniometer or motion-capture reference becomes available; in that case TNSRE or JNER (shoulder range-of-motion with abstention) is a better fit than a vision venue.

## I08: Causal-offline divergence in occluded articulated tracking: a reconvergence protocol and bounded-memory, adaptive-lag, multi-hypothesis causal estimators

Direction: Streaming versus offline reconstruction consistency: measuring persistent (non-decaying) errors after occlusion, and designing causal estimators whose memory, lag and revisions are bounded so the live digital twin reconverges to the offline solution.

Research question: How often, and under which occlusion conditions, do causal trackers commit to a branch that an offline pass would not take (forearm-twist or elbow-swivel ambiguity during a gap) and then fail to reconverge, as in the thesis's 12 cm wrist divergence? Can a causal estimator with bounded memory and per-joint adaptive lag (a fixed-lag multi-hypothesis swing-twist smoother, or a rolling-window diffusion model whose per-frame noise is set by observation trust) bring the causal-offline wrist gap below 1 cm within a bounded time after observations return, at an explicit latency, flicker and revision cost, measured on a live camera?

Why novel (as proposed): Streaming and latency-aware evaluation exists for 2D visual tracking (predictive tracking benchmarks) and sEMG hand pose (emg2pose causal protocol); online human-motion methods such as WHAM (CVPR 2024) report averages without reconvergence analysis or gap handling based on honesty flags; Point2Pose (2026) treats occlusion recovery for objects, not articulated chains. Diffusion Forcing introduced per-token noise levels and causal rollout; MotionStreamer (ICCV 2025), DartControl (ICLR 2025) and CLoSD (ICLR 2025) are streaming generators driven by text or goals, not partial flagged observations; RoHM is offline. XR smoothing (One Euro, CHI 2012; AvatarPoser ECCV 2022; EgoPoser ECCV 2024) is causal at constant lag and does not label imputed joints or analyse path dependence. The thesis found average causal-offline agreement good (worst joint-group median 0.64 deg, p95 3.24 deg at 3 frames lag) while one gap froze the forearm twist about 40 deg apart and the wrists ended 12 cm apart and never reconverged; averages hide this. Delta: (a) divergence and reconvergence metrics with a lag sweep for articulated body-object tracking; (b) render delay as a per-joint decision variable; (c) a noise schedule tied to measurement trust (a learned analogue of Kalman gain scheduling); (d) unobservable twist sampled and later collapsed rather than frozen; (e) a contraction argument for bounded reconvergence. Plainly: multi-hypothesis fixed-lag smoothing alone is close to classical MHT; the contribution is the measured trade-off and the reconvergence framing. Not search-verified that no human-motion paper reports causal-vs-offline reconvergence.

Method (as proposed): Protocol: run every tracker at declared lags L in {0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 10, 30 frames, offline}; per occlusion window record error at gap end, reconvergence time to within epsilon of the offline or ground-truth trajectory, persistent-divergence rate (windows still above epsilon T seconds after reacquisition), residual divergence at 1 s and 3 s, area under the divergence curve, branch-disagreement rate on ambiguous DOF (twist sign, elbow swivel), revision magnitude on displayed frames and flicker. Estimator family A: fixed-lag smoother over the swing-twist chain on the v3 quaternion core keeping K hypotheses (twist frozen vs following object rotation; IK elbow swivel modes), propagated by the tangent-space Kalman with IMM over constant velocity, constant acceleration and static, weighted by returning measurements, committed after L frames; honesty label 'estimated' until commitment; object rotation as twist evidence. Family B: per-joint-group adaptive lag L_g(t) chosen to minimise J = w_lat*L + w_err*E[err | L, gap state] + w_hon*P(non-measured | L) from a gap-duration hazard model conditioned on motion phase (rest, grip, carry, handover) with a small learned predictor; Unity merger renders each joint group at its own delay. Family C: one Diffusion-Forcing-style network with independent per-frame noise levels; causal mode keeps a W = 30-60 frame window with newer and held/constrained frames noisier; bounded memory replaces carried states (direction memories, twist hold, grip-offset EMA); frames older than L = 2 are committed, revisions rate-limited by teleport budgets; twist sampled when the elbow is nearly straight and collapsed by forearm evidence; a contraction argument (Lipschitz < 1 when enough frames are measured) with measured empirical contraction rate. Live validation on the D435 with motion-to-photon latency measured by an LED/photodiode and a 240 fps phone.

Evaluation (as proposed): Data: lab recordings with I10 or witness-camera ground truth; the thesis's divergent gap (frames 7-24 and the gap at frame 548) as a named case study; R4-R7 with v3 S-type masks; synthetic gaps on ARCTIC and OakInk2; synthetic transplants (I11) for volume; about 10 new live sessions. Baselines: v1 causal chain (2-frame delay, holds), v3 S2 + tangent Kalman, constant-lag RTS / fixed-lag Kalman smoother, One Euro, WHAM (online) or a WHAM-like recurrent model, MotionStreamer/DART-style autoregressive diffusion with inpainting but noise not tied to trust, offline RoHM and offline v1 as upper references. Metrics: persistent-divergence rate vs lag, reconvergence time, residual divergence, latency-error Pareto curves, E_occ, false_measured and non-measured fraction, teleports within budgets, flicker vs v3 oracle (0.18 deg/frame^2), end-to-end p99 within 33 ms, measured motion-to-photon latency. Ablations: K hypotheses; object-rotation evidence; IMM vs single-mode; adaptive vs constant lag; learned vs hand-tuned gap predictor; window W; commit lag L; trust-tied vs uniform noise; sampled vs held twist; with vs without carried summary state.

Target venues: NeurIPS 2027, ICLR 2028 (if the contraction analysis is solid), ICRA 2027 / IEEE RA-L, IROS 2027, IEEE ISMAR / TVCG, RSS 2027 (framed for teleoperation digital twins)

Builds on the thesis: The thesis is the only place documenting a measured causal-offline failure (12 cm, never reconverged, traced to frozen twist and stale carried state); the v2 causal chain with declared delay and delay-buffer merger, v3 teleport budgets, honesty flags, the swing-twist observability analysis (twist hold when the elbow is straight), the unit-quaternion core, the tangent-space Kalman (D-038), S2 strategy, the closed-form solve that makes multi-hypothesis tracking cheap, and the IMM extension already shortlisted in V3_FINDINGS form a ready harness. A live-camera run closes the thesis's 'replay only' validation gap.

Feasibility (as proposed): Software on existing hardware; a few-million-parameter model trains and runs at 30 fps with 4-8 flow steps on the RTX 3080; LED/photodiode kit and 240 fps phone under USD 100. Public datasets supply ground truth for the core result; otherwise offline-pass agreement plus manual labels as a weaker reference. Timeline: month 1 metric definitions, live capture and reproduction of the 12 cm event; months 2-4 estimators; month 5 contraction analysis and evaluation; months 6-7 experiments, live demo, writing.

Risks (as proposed): Reviewers may call it incremental (MHT plus Kalman, or fixed-lag smoothing) and the reconvergence guarantee trivial since a finite window forgets by construction; the phenomenon may be too rare on slow desk tasks for statistical meaning; bounded memory may cost accuracy on long gaps where carried state helps; theory may need assumptions (enough measured frames per window) that fail under long occlusions; single-subject data weakens generality; detector errors may dominate the latency-error trade-off.

### novelty skeptic: score 3, refuted=True

Search caveat first: this session had no working literature search. WebSearch returned "budget exhausted (200 of 200)" on all four queries (online-vs-offline motion reconstruction reconvergence; streaming pose with diffusion forcing under occlusion; rolling-window diffusion latency trade-off; multi-hypothesis fixed-lag arm twist). WebFetch to arxiv.org, export.arxiv.org and semanticscholar.org was blocked by the egress proxy. So I found no paper that does exactly this protocol, and I also could not confirm that none exists. Per the rule, uncertainty defaults to refuted=true. The verdict also stands on the merits, using works I know up to my mid-2026 cutoff (all marked verified=false). (1) Families A and B are classical estimation. Multi-hypothesis tracking, IMM over CV/CA/static, fixed-lag RTS smoothing and adaptive-lag smoothing are textbook target-tracking methods.
