The idea admits family A is "close to classical MHT". Choosing lag per joint group by minimising a latency/error/honesty cost is a scheduling heuristic, not a new estimator. (2) Family C is mostly prior work. Diffusion Forcing (NeurIPS 2024) already uses independent per-token noise, treats noise level as uncertainty, and runs causal rollout. Rolling Diffusion Models (ICML 2024) already add more noise to newer frames inside a sliding window. Autoregressive diffusion for real-time body reconstruction from partial, arbitrary sparse inputs exists in DiffusionPoser (CVPR 2024). Diffusion-based sparse or intermittent tracking also exists (AGRoL CVPR 2023, BoDiffusion ICCV 2023, HMD-Poser CVPR 2024, EgoPoser ECCV 2024). RoHM (CVPR 2024) already conditions denoising on per-frame occlusion masks, though offline. Tying noise to "observation trust" is a small step from these, and the "learned Kalman gain scheduling" analogy is an interpretation, not a mechanism. (3) The contraction argument is close to trivial: a finite window with enough measured frames forgets the initial condition by construction. The idea's own risk list predicts this objection, and the assumption fails exactly in the long-occlusion regime that motivates the paper. (4) The empirical motivation is thin. It rests on one 12 cm divergence event in one subject's slow desk task. Persistent divergence may be too rare there to give a statistically meaningful "rate", and synthetic gaps on ARCTIC/OakInk2 would be an artificial stand-in. (5) What survives is the evaluation lens: reconvergence time, persistent-divergence rate versus declared lag, and revision/flicker cost for articulated body-object tracking. Online human-motion papers (WHAM, EgoPoser, HMD-Poser) do report averaged online numbers. I could not verify, either way, whether any paper reports reconvergence of causal versus offline trajectories after occlusion. Predictive and latency-aware evaluation exists for 2D tracking (Predictive Visual Tracking 2021), and emg2pose (NeurIPS 2024 D&B) evaluates causally. On its own, this protocol is a workshop, dataset-track or RA-L/ISMAR systems contribution, not a NeurIPS/ICLR methods paper. Novelty 3/10 as a methods paper for the venues listed; perhaps 5/10 as a benchmark/protocol contribution if a search later confirms the gap.

Prior work found:
- Diffusion Forcing: Next-token Prediction Meets Full-Sequence Diffusion (NeurIPS 2024) https://github.com/buoyancy99/diffusion-forcing; overlap: Independent per-token noise levels, noise as an uncertainty proxy, causal rollout with a bounded window; this is the core of estimator family C, minus the link from noise level to measurement-trust flags.; verified=False
- Rolling Diffusion Models (ICML 2024); overlap: A sliding-window diffusion in which newer frames get more noise and older frames are committed; this is the exact 'rolling window, newer frames noisier, commit after L' mechanism in family C.; verified=False
- DiffusionPoser: Real-time Human Motion Reconstruction From Arbitrary Sparse Sensors Using Autoregressive Diffusion (CVPR 2024); overlap: Real-time autoregressive diffusion that reconstructs full-body motion from partial or missing sensor inputs via inpainting; this is a causal estimator from partial observations, the setting of family C.; verified=False
- RoHM: Robust Human Motion Reconstruction via Diffusion (CVPR 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.08570; overlap: Diffusion denoising conditioned on per-frame occlusion masks and noisy observations; this is the trust-aware conditioning idea, offline.; verified=False
- EgoPoser: Robust Real-Time Egocentric Pose Estimation from Sparse and Intermittent Observations Everywhere (ECCV 2024); overlap: Causal real-time pose estimation that explicitly handles intermittent observations (tracking loss of the hands); it covers the occlusion-gap handling in a causal setting without the reconvergence metric.; verified=False
- HMD-Poser: On-Device Real-time Human Motion Tracking from Scalable Sparse Observations (CVPR 2024); overlap: Online, on-device body tracking from partial and variable observations with a recurrent state; it reports causal accuracy at real-time latency.; verified=False
- WHAM: Reconstructing World-grounded Humans with Accurate 3D Motion (CVPR 2024) https://github.com/yohanshin/WHAM; overlap: An online/causal recurrent human motion estimator with offline-comparable accuracy; it is the natural baseline, and its existence weakens the claim that causal articulated estimation is under-studied.; verified=False
- emg2pose: A Large and Diverse Benchmark for Surface Electromyographic Hand Pose Estimation (NeurIPS 2024 Datasets and Benchmarks) https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.02725; overlap: Defines a causal evaluation protocol for articulated hand pose; this is precedent for the protocol framing.; verified=False
- Predictive Visual Tracking: A New Benchmark and Baseline Approach (arXiv 2021) https://arxiv.org/abs/2103.04508; overlap: A latency-aware streaming evaluation of trackers; this is precedent for latency-error trade-off benchmarking.; verified=False
- AGRoL: Avatars Grow Legs - Generating Smooth Human Motion from Sparse Tracking Inputs with Diffusion Model (CVPR 2023); overlap: Diffusion-based full-body motion from sparse inputs, run online in a sliding-window mode with smoothness metrics (jitter); this overlaps the flicker/revision evaluation.; verified=False
- MotionStreamer: Streaming Motion Generation via Diffusion-based Autoregressive Model in Causal Latent Space (ICCV 2025) https://github.com/zju3dv/MotionStreamer; overlap: A causal streaming diffusion motion model with bounded latent memory; the architecture overlaps, though it is conditioned on text, not on flagged observations.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Drop the methods-paper claim; family C in particular is largely Diffusion Forcing plus Rolling Diffusion plus DiffusionPoser. Recast it as a benchmark and protocol paper, "Path dependence in streaming articulated hand-object tracking", aimed at the NeurIPS Datasets and Benchmarks track or IEEE RA-L. (a) Define reconvergence time, persistent-divergence rate versus declared lag, residual divergence at 1 s and 3 s, and revision/flicker cost. (b) Apply them to public hand-object and egocentric datasets with real ground truth (ARCTIC, HOT3D, OakInk2, DexYCB), using real occlusions rather than synthetic masks. (c) Run released online trackers (WHAM online mode, EgoPoser, HMD-Poser, DiffusionPoser, a FoundationPose-style object tracker) against their offline counterparts. The goal is to show that averaged metrics hide non-reconverging branches on ambiguous DOF (forearm twist, elbow swivel). Provide one simple baseline, a trust-scheduled rolling-window smoother, and present it as a baseline rather than the contribution. Present the thesis's 12 cm event only as the motivating anecdote, and add a live D435 motion-to-photon measurement as a systems appendix. Before committing, run the literature check this session could not run: search for "online vs offline consistency" or "reconvergence" in human or hand pose tracking from 2024 to 2026, since the reframe depends on that gap actually being open.

### feasibility skeptic: score 3, refuted=True

Feasibility verdict: refuted as written, but a much smaller version is feasible. Five problems.

(1) The motivating evidence is thinner than the idea claims. Thesis Section 8.3 (thesis_v9.txt, lines 4415-4430) traces the 12.0 cm wrist gap at frame 18 to the offline failure mask and the live gates firing on different frames over frames 7-24. The two paths then froze shoulder twist about 40 deg apart "until the elbow bent far enough inside that window to release the twist hold". So this was an n=1 mismatch between two hand-written gating policies, and the twist hold did eventually release. It was not a measured intrinsic causal-estimation failure, and it is not literally "never reconverged". The second window (frame 548) differed by up to 24.1 deg in elbow flexion, yet the wrists nearly coincided at frame 595: it reconverged. On the slow desk task, the rate of persistent divergence may be close to zero. A rate-versus-lag curve would then have no statistical content.

(2) Ground truth. There is no motion capture. The "causal-offline gap" metric compares the pipeline with itself, and the thesis itself says neither path is a reference. Driving that gap below 1 cm proves nothing about correctness, because a causal estimator can match a wrong offline answer. Real accuracy needs ARCTIC or OakInk2 with synthetic gaps. That makes the lab hardware, the D435 live sessions and the 28 single-subject bags nearly irrelevant to the core claim. The 1 cm target is also below the measured noise floor of the lab system: the kinematic wrist is 0.83-2.34 cm from manual labels.

(3) Engineering scope dominates the research. The plan has three estimator families: MHT plus IMM fixed-lag smoothing; per-joint adaptive lag with a learned hazard model and per-joint render delay in Unity; and a Diffusion-Forcing-style rolling-window network with trust-tied noise. On top of that it adds a contraction proof, a live LED/photodiode motion-to-photon rig and about 10 new live sessions. That is two or three projects for one student in 12-18 months. The v2 live-camera path was never validated in the thesis, so the live part starts from scratch.

(4) Novelty risk compounds the feasibility risk. Family A is classical MHT or IMM fixed-lag smoothing. The bounded-reconvergence argument is close to trivial for a finite window. Family C needs a trained motion prior for the upper body plus an object. ARCTIC and OakInk2 can supply that data in SMPL-X form, but the thesis rig has only 4 DOF per arm with no wrist orientation, so a retargeting layer is needed. That is more engineering again.

(5) The phenomenon (twist ambiguity near a straight elbow) is real and well posed. Public mocap datasets with synthetic occlusions can test it cheaply. That is the feasible core.

Minimum viable experiment (about 2-3 months):
- Data: ARCTIC and/or OakInk2 sequences with mocap ground truth, reduced to the thesis's swing-twist arm chain (shoulder swing x2, twist, elbow flexion, wrist point).
- Occlusions: synthetic gaps of 0.3-3 s, inserted preferentially when the elbow is nearly straight and the object is rotating.
- Lag sweep: the v3 tangent-space Kalman and S2 at L = 0, 2, 5, 10, 30 and offline RTS.
- Metrics, all against ground truth: persistent-divergence rate (error above 3 cm at 1 s after reacquisition), reconvergence time and residual error.
- Then a K = 2-4 twist-hypothesis fixed-lag smoother that uses object rotation as twist evidence.
- Success criteria: (a) persistent divergence occurs in a non-trivial fraction of gaps (more than about 5%) at small lag; and (b) the multi-hypothesis smoother cuts that rate by half or more at L of 5 frames or less, without raising median error.
- If (a) fails, drop the direction.
- Add the lab recordings only as a qualitative case study, with the gating-mismatch cause stated honestly.

Literature note: the web search budget was exhausted in this session, so no prior work could be re-verified here. All entries below are marked verified=false for that reason, although the upstream idea record lists several of them as search-verified.

Prior work found:
- emg2pose: A Large and Diverse Benchmark for Surface Electromyographic Hand Pose Estimation (NeurIPS 2024 Datasets and Benchmarks) https://arxiv.org/html/2412.02725v1; overlap: Causal, streaming evaluation protocol for hand pose; latency-aware benchmarking exists, though without a reconvergence analysis.; verified=False
- RoHM: Robust Human Motion Reconstruction via Diffusion (CVPR 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.08570; overlap: Offline diffusion-based reconstruction of motion under occlusion; the offline upper reference.; verified=False
- WHAM: Reconstructing World-grounded Humans with Accurate 3D Motion (CVPR 2024) https://github.com/yohanshin/WHAM; overlap: Online recurrent human-motion estimator; reports average accuracy with no reconvergence metrics.; verified=False
- Diffusion Forcing: Next-token Prediction Meets Full-Sequence Diffusion (NeurIPS 2024) https://github.com/buoyancy99/diffusion-forcing; overlap: Per-token noise levels and causal rollout; Family C is essentially an application of this to tracking with flagged observations.; verified=False
- MotionStreamer: Streaming Motion Generation via Diffusion-based Autoregressive Model in Causal Latent Space (ICCV 2025) https://github.com/zju3dv/MotionStreamer; overlap: Streaming autoregressive motion diffusion; conditioned on text, not on partial observations.; verified=False
- DartControl: A Diffusion-Based Autoregressive Motion Model for Real-Time Text-Driven Motion Control (ICLR 2025) https://github.com/zkf1997/DART; overlap: Real-time autoregressive diffusion motion model; a candidate baseline for Family C.; verified=False
- Point2Pose: Occlusion-Recovering 6D Pose Tracking and 3D Reconstruction for Multiple Unknown Objects Via 2D Point Trackers (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/html/2604.10415; overlap: Occlusion recovery in tracking, for rigid objects rather than articulated chains.; verified=False
- EgoPoser: Robust Real-Time Egocentric Pose Estimation from Sparse and Intermittent Observations Everywhere (ECCV 2024); overlap: Real-time body pose from intermittent observations; closest XR-side work on causal estimation under observation gaps.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Narrow the idea to one paper: "Persistent divergence after occlusion in causal articulated hand-object tracking: a reconvergence benchmark and a multi-hypothesis fixed-lag baseline". Target RA-L/ICRA or IROS.

- Ground truth: use ARCTIC and OakInk2 mocap with synthetic gaps placed at observability-poor configurations (near-straight elbow, object rotating). Do not use the causal-versus-offline gap of the lab pipeline as ground truth.
- Lag sweep: report the persistent-divergence rate, reconvergence time and residual error against ground truth across lags 0-30 frames and offline, for constant-velocity Kalman, IMM, the v3 S2 strategy, One Euro and an online learned baseline.
- Method: contribute only Family A, K twist and swivel hypotheses with object rotation as evidence and commitment after L frames, and its latency-versus-persistent-divergence Pareto curve.
- Lab data: use the lab recordings only as a qualitative case study. State honestly that the thesis event came from mismatched gating between the offline and live paths.
- Deferred: drop per-joint adaptive render lag, the trust-tied diffusion network, the contraction proof and the LED/photodiode live-latency rig from the first paper. Keep them as follow-up work, and only if the benchmark shows persistent divergence is common enough to matter.

### venue skeptic: score 3, refuted=True

Area-chair view. (1) No clear headline claim. The idea bundles a metric protocol, three estimator families (MHT fixed-lag smoother, per-joint adaptive lag, trust-scheduled rolling diffusion), a contraction argument and a live latency rig. A NeurIPS/ICLR reviewer will not find one claim that holds the paper together. An ICRA/RA-L reviewer will see a large systems paper on one subject and one camera. (2) The motivating phenomenon is self-inflicted and seen once. The thesis text (around line 4420 of thesis_v9.txt) puts the 12 cm wrist gap on the thesis's own hand-crafted carried state: twist hold while the elbow is straight, direction memories and the grip-offset EMA, plus live gates and the offline mask firing on different frames. It is a single event in one recording. A reviewer will say: remove the hold heuristic and the problem disappears; this is a bug in a bespoke pipeline, not a property of causal articulated tracking. Nothing shows that published online methods (WHAM, AvatarPoser/EgoPoser-type XR trackers, streaming diffusion) suffer persistent non-reconverging divergence. Without that, the problem looks niche. (3) Novelty is weak on the method side, and the idea admits it. Multi-hypothesis fixed-lag smoothing with IMM is textbook MHT/IMM. Adaptive lag chosen by a cost function is classical decision-theoretic smoothing. Per-frame noise levels with causal rollout are Diffusion Forcing and Rolling Diffusion. Tying the noise to observation confidence is a natural, small step (diffusion inpainting with confidence masks is common). The bounded-reconvergence argument is close to trivial: a finite-window estimator with no carried state forgets by construction. The risks section already says this. (4) The evaluation would not convince. Ground truth is weak (no mocap; offline pass as reference, which is circular when offline-causal agreement is the metric). There is one subject and a slow desk task, so persistent divergence may be too rare to give statistically meaningful rates. Synthetic gaps on ARCTIC/OakInk2 test imputation rather than the branch-commitment failure, unless ambiguous-twist conditions are built in. The baselines (One Euro, RTS, v1/v3 chains) are mostly the author's own or classical. Strong learned online baselines on standard benchmarks are thin. (5) Venue fit. NeurIPS/ICLR: wrong venue, with no learning-theoretic or broad ML contribution. RSS: too niche. ICRA/IROS/RA-L or ISMAR/IEEE VR is plausible only for a narrowed systems paper with a live demo and a measured latency-error trade-off, and even there acceptance depends on showing the problem in other people's methods. Expected quality at the best-fit venue (RA-L/ICRA or ISMAR) is borderline-reject as proposed. Literature check limitation: web search was exhausted in this session (budget reached), so I could not check whether 2025-2026 work already reports causal-vs-offline reconvergence for human motion. I relied on the idea's own verified citations and the thesis text.

Prior work found:
- Diffusion Forcing: Next-token Prediction Meets Full-Sequence Diffusion (NeurIPS 2024) https://github.com/buoyancy99/diffusion-forcing; overlap: Per-token independent noise levels and causal rollout; the trust-scheduled variant (family C) is a small step from this.; verified=False
- Rolling Diffusion Models (ICML 2024 (from memory)); overlap: Sliding-window diffusion with noise that rises toward newer frames; this is the core of the rolling-window causal estimator.; verified=False
- WHAM: Reconstructing World-grounded Humans with Accurate 3D Motion (CVPR 2024) https://github.com/yohanshin/WHAM; overlap: Online recurrent human motion estimator; would be the main learned causal baseline, and the place the divergence phenomenon must be shown to exist.; verified=False
- RoHM: Robust Human Motion Reconstruction via Diffusion (CVPR 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.08570; overlap: Offline diffusion-based motion reconstruction under occlusion and noise; offline upper reference.; verified=False
- emg2pose: A Large and Diverse Benchmark for Surface Electromyographic Hand Pose Estimation (NeurIPS 2024 Datasets and Benchmarks) https://arxiv.org/html/2412.02725v1; overlap: Causal-evaluation protocol precedent for pose estimation; weakens the novelty claim of a causal protocol in general.; verified=False
- EgoPoser: Robust Real-Time Egocentric Pose Estimation from Sparse and Intermittent Observations Everywhere (ECCV 2024); overlap: Causal tracking under intermittent observations (hands leaving the field of view) in XR; the closest application-level competitor for gap handling.; verified=False
- MotionStreamer: Streaming Motion Generation via Diffusion-based Autoregressive Model in Causal Latent Space (ICCV 2025) https://github.com/zju3dv/MotionStreamer; overlap: Streaming diffusion motion model; the architecture is close to family C.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Shrink to one claim that can be tested on other people's methods: 'Causal articulated trackers commit to unobservable-DOF branches (forearm twist, elbow swivel) during occlusion, and a measurable fraction never reconverge to the offline or ground-truth solution; mean-error benchmarks hide this.' First show it exists. Run published online methods (WHAM or similar, EgoPoser/AvatarPoser-type XR trackers, a streaming diffusion model) on public datasets with ground truth (ARCTIC, OakInk2, EMDB or similar) under controlled synthetic gaps placed at elbow-straight or ambiguous configurations. Report persistent-divergence rate, reconvergence time and residual divergence against a lag sweep. Use the thesis event only as a motivating anecdote. Then give one simple, principled fix: keep twist and swivel hypotheses alive for a bounded lag and collapse them on evidence, with object rotation as twist evidence. Report the latency/revision/flicker trade-off on a live camera with measured motion-to-photon latency. Drop the contraction theorem and the adaptive-lag cost model unless they become nontrivial. Target IEEE RA-L (with ICRA/IROS option) or ISMAR/IEEE VR (TVCG track) for the teleoperation or XR digital-twin angle, not NeurIPS/ICLR. If the phenomenon cannot be shown in published methods, drop it as a paper and fold the metrics into a broader benchmark or systems paper as one evaluation axis.

## I09: Learning when not to trust a confident detector: per-landmark measurement trust inside a kinematic estimator, trained on mined real detector failures and a synthetic RGB-D desk twin, validated against motion capture

Direction: Hybrid learned-plus-model-based estimation: a small temporal model predicts per-landmark error, measurement covariance and outlier probability (the thesis's third future-work item), while an explicit kinematic chain and object-conditioned factors enforce feasibility.

Research question: Can a small causal temporal model, given landmark history, detector scores, depth-consistency residuals, the swing-twist kinematic residual and the measured object pose, detect confident-but-wrong landmarks (wrong body part, depth from the desk or object) with substantially higher AUROC than detector confidence, which never abstains and anti-correlates with occlusion (V3 D-017: flagged wrists median score 0.818 vs 0.789); and when its calibrated covariances weight a Kalman / factor-graph estimator with object-conditioned wrist and IK elbow factors, does it reduce false_measured and wrist/elbow error under hand-object occlusion against motion-capture ground truth, transfer across subjects without real labels, and improve the executability of retargeted robot actions?

Why novel (as proposed): Learned covariances and gains are known: LSTM-KF (ICCV 2017), KalmanNet (arXiv 2021 / IEEE TSP 2022) on generic systems without structured outliers or occlusion semantics. VisTracker (CVPR 2023) predicts visibility as a model output from RGB, not as a validator of a separate detector with depth residuals; POCO (3DV 2024) predicts per-frame mesh confidence without object or depth context; EvaPose (arXiv 2602.23618) learns visibility from 3M real labelled egocentric frames; occlusion-aware amodal completion for HOI (ACM MM 2025) reconstructs rather than validates. Single-camera robot-learning pipelines (DemoBot 2026, Human2Sim2Robot 2025, OKAMI CoRL 2024) do not report capture accuracy against mocap during occlusion or estimate when their measurements are untrustworthy. Delta: (a) the target is detector error under hand-object occlusion, not visibility; (b) labels are real detector failures mined from multi-view mocap datasets (ARCTIC, TACO) run through the system's own detector, complemented by Unity renders of the actual capture setup with D435 artefacts and self-supervised masking of clean real joints; (c) trust drives an explicit kinematic chain and is evaluated by downstream honesty and by a mocap-referenced fidelity study (Cornell bimanual handover dataset) tied to retargeted action quality. Plainly the weakest delta among the estimation topics; strongest at robotics venues or as a component of I01/I03.

Method (as proposed): Label sources: (a) mining: run the v3 detector stack (RTMPose-l plus YOLOX on ONNX Runtime) on every allocentric view of ARCTIC (8 views) and TACO (12 allocentric views plus egocentric RGB-D), project mocap SMPL-X joints into each view, and label each landmark-frame with pixel and (where depth exists) 3D error, giving hundreds of thousands of real confident-but-wrong cases; (b) Unity renders from the calibrated D435 pose with randomised avatars, desk/cube occluders, lighting and a D435 depth model (range noise, edge flying pixels, invalid holes filled by background, reproducing hip-on-desk), with RTMPose run on the renders so detector errors are real; (c) self-supervision on real recordings by masking clean joints (v3 S-type) and penalising filter error; (d) optional cross-detector disagreement (MediaPipe vs RTMPose-l vs RTMPose-x) as weak labels. Features per landmark per frame: score, 2D velocity/acceleration over 16 frames, residual to the swing-twist chain prediction, bone-length deviation, prediction-relative depth residual, depth-patch statistics, distance and visibility relation to the object box and marker, grip state and grip-offset age, arm-axis angle to the optical axis, current honesty flags and factor-graph residuals. Model: causal temporal conv or GRU (under 0.5M parameters, under 1 ms) outputting P(error > tau) and a heteroscedastic scale; focal loss plus Gaussian NLL; post-hoc temperature or conformal calibration on held-out subjects. Integration: the scale becomes R_t in the v3 tangent-space Kalman extended with an object-conditioned wrist factor (wrist = object pose x grip offset, age-dependent noise) and IK elbow factor, or the landmark-factor weights and switchable-constraint priors in the I01 factor graph; optional end-to-end fine-tuning through Theseus; honesty flags derived from posterior covariance. Domain adaptation to the D435 lab with existing manual wrist labels plus about 2,000 new landmark-frames. Downstream: retarget to a simulated dual-arm robot and compare action error against actions retargeted from mocap.

Evaluation (as proposed): Detection: AUROC, AUPRC and ECE for landmark-error detection on held-out ARCTIC/TACO subjects and objects and on lab recordings; NLL and coverage of predicted variance. Mocap-referenced fidelity: Cornell bimanual handover dataset (120 Hz upper-body bones, object 6-DoF, two 30 Hz RGB-D streams; single stream as input; extrinsics checked or solved from the object trajectory) and HOH as a markerless second test; lab R4-R7 with manual labels and the rail-line reference; BEHAVE/InterCap single view; Unity held-out avatars. Baselines: detector score; thesis bone and depth-jump gates and remembered hip depth; v3 D-017 rules; isolation-forest anomaly detector; KalmanNet/LSTM-KF-style learned filter without occlusion features or kinematics; v1 offline chain; v3 S2 + tangent Kalman; hold-last; MotionBERT-lite lifter; siMLPe infill. Downstream: false_measured, wrist and elbow error (median, p95) split by occlusion state, teleports, p99 latency within 33 ms (v3 grade harness), retargeted action error and simulated replay success. Ablations: remove depth residual / object features / kinematic residual / depth-patch features; history length; mined real failures vs synthetic noise vs Unity renders vs self-supervision; with vs without the kinematic factor graph; no object factor.

Target venues: ICRA 2027 / IEEE RA-L, IROS 2027, CVPR 2027 workshop then WACV 2028, ECCV 2028 (with downstream results), IEEE T-RO (if extended with a full data-engine study)

Builds on the thesis: The thesis and v3 identified the failure mode (high-confidence wrong landmarks, desk depth stealing hip or wrist, co-shifting hips) and documented that obvious weak labels fail (D-017); the explicit kinematic chain gives an informative residual feature black-box models lack; the Unity rig with frame-exact sensor-POV capture check, failure catalogue (Table 9.1), V3 GPU detector stack, S-type scenarios, object-conditioned recovery and tangent Kalman, and the v3 grade harness (false_measured, teleports) are all reused.

Feasibility (as proposed): RTMPose over ARCTIC and TACO views is a few GPU-days on the RTX 3080; storage of the order of 1-2 TB for raw multi-view video, a subset of views keeps it under 1 TB (approval needed); Unity renderer and depth model 3-4 weeks; Cornell dataset download (size unchecked). No purchase for the core paper; downstream robot replay can stay in simulation. Timeline: months 1-2 mining, rendering and Cornell conversion; month 3 model; month 4 integration and lab fine-tuning; months 5-6 experiments and writing. Fastest of the learned-trust variants to a first paper.

Risks (as proposed): Reads as an engineering filter improvement with modest novelty at CVPR-tier venues (learned covariances are known); ICRA/RA-L is more realistic unless the mined-failure dataset becomes a contribution. Newer detectors may shrink the error class; transfer from mocap studio views and Unity renders to the D435 desk view may be weak; reviewers will ask for more than one lab subject; Cornell RGB-D views may not be placed like a desk camera and its objects may carry no fiducial, so object conditioning must use the mocap object pose; gains may concentrate in a small fraction of occluded frames.

### novelty skeptic: score 3, refuted=True

Search budget for the session was exhausted (all four WebSearch calls returned "web search budget used, 200 of 200"), so this verdict rests on prior work recalled from memory and on the candidate's own verified list. Following the default-to-refuted rule under uncertainty, and on the substance, the delta is too small for a top vision venue. (1) The core mechanism is established: a learned network outputs a heteroscedastic measurement covariance and/or outlier weight that feeds a Kalman or factor-graph estimator over a known kinematic or dynamic model. Examples are Backprop KF (NeurIPS 2016), differentiable filters with learned heteroscedastic noise (Kloss et al., Autonomous Robots 2021), TLIO (RA-L 2020; learned measurement plus covariance in an EKF), AI-IMU (IEEE T-IV 2020; learned EKF noise), KalmanNet (IEEE TSP 2022) and LSTM-KF (ICCV 2017). Swapping the measurement source from IMU or VO to RTMPose landmarks is a domain transfer, not a new method. (2) Per-keypoint error and uncertainty prediction for 2D pose detectors already exists: RLE (ICCV 2021) predicts a per-joint scale, PoseFix (CVPR 2019) detects and corrects any detector's erroneous keypoints without depending on the model, and POCO (3DV 2024) and VisTracker (CVPR 2023) predict confidence and visibility. The proposed classifier, P(error > tau) plus a scale trained with focal loss and Gaussian NLL, is a straightforward combination of these. (3) The application overlaps with occlusion-robust teleoperation arm correction (Seeing Through Occlusion, arXiv 2606.19240, 2026; deterministic kinematic correction of occluded arms for teleop) and with motion priors that handle occlusion (HuMoR, ICCV 2021). (4) The new elements left are the feature set (depth-consistency residual, swing-twist kinematic residual, object-box relation, grip-offset age) and mining real detector failures from ARCTIC/TACO. Feature engineering is not a CVPR-level contribution. Mining failures by projecting mocap ground truth into views is standard practice for building error-prediction training sets. The idea itself admits it is the weakest delta. (5) The evaluation also depends on a single lab subject and a D435 desk view that differs from the mocap-studio and Unity training domains. Reviewers will likely see transfer gains confined to a small fraction of occluded frames as an engineering filter improvement. The idea is plausible for RA-L/ICRA as a systems paper but not novel enough for CVPR/ECCV/NeurIPS in its current framing.

Prior work found:
- Backprop KF: Learning Discriminative Deterministic State Estimators (NeurIPS 2016); overlap: Learns image-conditioned observation noise covariance end-to-end inside a Kalman filter; same core mechanism of a network-predicted R_t.; verified=False
- How to Train Your Differentiable Filter (Autonomous Robots 2021); overlap: Systematic study of learned heteroscedastic observation and process noise in differentiable EKF/UKF/particle filters; covers the learned-covariance-plus-model-based-filter design and end-to-end fine-tuning.; verified=False
- TLIO: Tight Learned Inertial Odometry (IEEE RA-L 2020); overlap: Network outputs a measurement plus its covariance, fused in an EKF with chi-square outlier rejection; the template for learned trust inside a model-based estimator, published at the proposed target venue.; verified=False
- AI-IMU Dead-Reckoning (IEEE T-IV 2020); overlap: Learned adaptive measurement covariance for a kinematic EKF; same learned R_t idea.; verified=False
- KalmanNet: Neural Network Aided Kalman Filtering for Partially Known Dynamics (IEEE TSP 2022) https://github.com/KalmanNet/KalmanNet_TSP; overlap: Learned Kalman gain with known model structure; the proposal names it as a baseline, and it covers the learned-plus-model-based framing.; verified=False
- Long Short-Term Memory Kalman Filters: Recurrent Neural Estimators for Pose Regularization (ICCV 2017); overlap: An LSTM predicts Kalman noise terms for human and object pose sequences, the same domain (pose regularization) as the proposal.; verified=False
- Human Pose Regression with Residual Log-likelihood Estimation (RLE) (ICCV 2021); overlap: Predicts a per-keypoint heteroscedastic scale trained with likelihood, the same per-landmark error-scale output, though without a filter.; verified=False
- PoseFix: Model-agnostic General Human Pose Refinement Network (CVPR 2019); overlap: Learns to identify and correct erroneous keypoints from an arbitrary detector using that detector's error statistics, close to validating a separate detector's confident-but-wrong outputs.; verified=False
- POCO: 3D Pose and Shape Estimation with Confidence (3DV 2024); overlap: Per-sample confidence for body estimates, used to flag unreliable outputs.; verified=False
- Visibility Aware Human-Object Interaction Tracking from Single RGB Camera (VisTracker) (CVPR 2023) https://github.com/xiexh20/VisTracker; overlap: Occlusion and visibility reasoning for human-object interaction tracking from a single view, with a temporal model that fills occluded frames.; verified=False
- HuMoR: 3D Human Motion Model for Robust Pose Estimation (ICCV 2021); overlap: Learned motion prior in an optimization that is robust to occlusion and noisy RGB-D observations; a model-based fitting baseline that already discounts bad observations.; verified=False
- Seeing Through Occlusion: Deterministic Arm Kinematic Correction for Robot Teleoperation (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19240; overlap: Same application: correcting occluded arm kinematics from a single camera for robot teleoperation. Removes much of the robotics-venue delta.; verified=False
- Egocentric Visibility-Aware Human Pose Estimation (EvaPose / Eva-3M) (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.23618; overlap: Large-scale learned per-joint visibility and reliability from real labelled frames.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Move the contribution away from learned covariance in a filter, which is known, to a measurable claim that existing benchmarks cannot test. (a) Release a benchmark of confident detector failures under hand-object occlusion: real failures of current detectors (RTMPose, ViTPose, Sapiens, MediaPipe) mined from ARCTIC, TACO and the Cornell handover mocap. Label taxonomies such as wrong body part, depth stolen by the desk or object, and left/right swap. Use protocols that hold out subjects and objects. Then show that detector confidence and existing uncertainty heads (RLE, POCO-style) fail there. (b) Give distribution-free guarantees: use conformal prediction on the trust score to give coverage-guaranteed abstention per landmark, and show that the guarantee carries through to a bound on retargeted robot-action error or a safety-stop rate in a dual-arm teleop/retargeting loop, measured against mocap. That makes this an RA-L/ICRA paper on certified honest capture for robot teleoperation rather than a filter tweak, and it separates the work from Seeing Through Occlusion (2026), which is deterministic and gives no guarantee. Otherwise fold it as a component into the stronger I01/I03 factor-graph idea instead of submitting it alone.

### feasibility skeptic: score 4, refuted=True

Feasibility verdict: the core question can be tested in a small lab, but not the idea as written. It carries four label sources (ARCTIC/TACO mining, a Unity D435 twin, self-supervision, cross-detector disagreement), five evaluation datasets, a factor graph with optional Theseus end-to-end training, and a simulated robot retargeting study. Its motivating evidence is also misread. Engineering risk outweighs the research contribution for one student over 12-18 months. Specific problems:

(1) The motivating evidence is misread. V3 D-017 (v3/V3_DECISIONS.md) does not show that detector confidence anti-correlates with occlusion. It shows that the plan's torso-relative depth-front weak label is invalid. That label flagged the right wrist on 889 of 898 frames because the subject reaches forward all the time. The 0.818 vs 0.789 score gap therefore compares 'reaching forward' with 'not', not occluded with visible. D-017's own Review note says the pinned bag may contain too few true occlusion frames to fit wrist thresholds at all. The repository has no measured count or base rate of confident-but-wrong landmarks on the D435 desk view, only the qualitative failure catalogue (thesis Table 9.1). The premise that confident-but-wrong cases are common enough to matter is therefore unmeasured. If they are rare (the risk section already admits gains may sit in a small fraction of occluded frames), AUROC gains will not move median wrist or elbow error.

(2) The mined labels lack the distinctive features. ARCTIC is, from memory, RGB-only multi-view with SMPL-X, so it supplies no depth. Depth-consistency residuals and depth-patch statistics are the features that target the thesis's actual failure (desk depth stealing hip or wrist), so they get no real training signal from the main mined source. TACO's annotations are, from memory, MANO hands plus object poses, not full-body arms. It would give wrist labels only, with no elbow or shoulder labels, and its RGB-D is egocentric, not a desk-mounted third-person view. That leaves the Unity D435 twin as the only source of the depth failure modes. A faithful stereo-IR artefact model (flying pixels, holes filled from the background, hip-on-desk) is a research problem in itself, and 3-4 weeks is optimistic.

(3) Label-definition noise. As V3 D-018 measured, the COCO wrist is not the anatomical joint: twist spread is about 6.3-6.6 deg, with offsets up to 9 deg on shoulder swing. Projected SMPL-X joints carry a definitional offset of a few cm against RTMPose keypoints, comparable to any sensible error threshold tau. Per-joint offset regression is needed before the labels mean anything, and the brief does not plan for it.

(4) Mocap ground truth depends on one dataset whose suitability is unconfirmed. The Cornell bimanual handover dataset is the only mocap-plus-RGB-D source named. Its RGB-D streams, sensor placement and mocap-to-camera extrinsics were not confirmed here, and the idea itself proposes to 'solve extrinsics from the object trajectory', which is a fragile step. The task is standing two-person handover, not a seated desk task. HOH reference is from multi-view point clouds, not mocap. On the lab side the only ground truth remains one subject's manual wrist labels and the rail line, so the claimed D435-lab improvement can only be checked weakly. That is acceptable as a transfer demonstration, not as a headline result.

(5) Scope creep. Robot retargeting with simulated replay success, Theseus end-to-end fine-tuning, an I01 factor graph and five learned-infill baselines are each a separate project. None is needed to answer the research question.

(6) Venue realism. Learned measurement covariance is established (LSTM-KF, KalmanNet), so the contribution reads as an occlusion-aware learned R_t for a kinematic filter. ICRA or RA-L is plausible with a clean cross-dataset study. CVPR-tier is not, unless the mined-failure benchmark becomes the contribution, which needs storage (about 1 TB, approval needed) and multi-dataset engineering.

What is feasible: the RTX 3080 handles RTMPose inference over tens of thousands of frames and training a model under 0.5M parameters. The v3 tangent Kalman, S-type masking, false_measured metric and grade harness already exist, so integrating learned R_t is a few weeks of work.

Minimum viable experiment, 3-4 months:
- Data: take BEHAVE and/or InterCap, which (from memory) are multi-subject RGB-D with body fits and object 6-DoF poses. These give real depth and real desk- or table-like occluders, unlike ARCTIC.
- Labels: run the v3 RTMPose + YOLOX stack on single RGB-D views, back-project with depth, and label per-landmark 3D error against the fitted body joints. Fit per-joint definitional offsets on clean frames first, and set tau above the pseudo-ground-truth noise (e.g. 6-8 cm).
- Model: train a causal GRU on score, 16-frame kinematics, swing-twist residual, prediction-relative depth residual and object-box relation.
- Detection test: on held-out subjects and objects, report AUROC, AUPRC and ECE against detector score, against the thesis bone and depth-jump gates, and against an isolation forest.
- Filter test: feed the calibrated variance as R_t into the existing v3 tangent Kalman with the object-conditioned wrist factor, and report wrist and elbow error split by occlusion state, plus false_measured.
- Mocap test: if and only if the Cornell RGB-D-to-mocap alignment is confirmed, use it as the mocap-referenced test set.
- Lab transfer: about 2,000 manually labelled landmark-frames on a new recording with deliberate occlusions (this also unblocks D-017).

Success criterion: AUROC above detector score by at least 0.1 on held-out subjects, with the occluded-frame wrist p95 error and false_measured both reduced relative to v3 S2 + Kalman and hold-last.

Failure signal: if confident-but-wrong landmarks are under a few percent of occluded frames, or depth features add nothing over score plus kinematic residual, stop.

Carried over from the candidate's list; no search was run in this session (search budget exhausted).; verified=False
- Long Short-Term Memory Kalman Filters: Recurrent Neural Estimators for Pose Regularization (ICCV 2017); overlap: Recurrent prediction of Kalman noise covariances for pose; direct precedent for learned R_t.; verified=False
- Visibility Aware Human-Object Interaction Tracking from Single RGB Camera (VisTracker) (CVPR 2023) https://github.com/xiexh20/VisTracker; overlap: Single-view human-object tracking with predicted visibility; trained and evaluated on BEHAVE/InterCap, the datasets proposed here as the feasible training source.; verified=False
- BEHAVE: Dataset and Method for Tracking Human Object Interactions (CVPR 2022); overlap: Multi-subject Kinect RGB-D with body fits and object poses (from memory); the most practical source of real depth-failure labels for this idea.; verified=False
- InterCap: Joint Markerless 3D Tracking of Humans and Objects in Interaction (GCPR 2022 / IJCV 2024 (from memory)); overlap: Multi-view Azure Kinect RGB-D with SMPL-X and object poses (from memory); a second RGB-D source with pseudo ground truth.; verified=False

- TACO: Benchmarking Generalizable Bimanual Tool-ACtion-Object Understanding (CVPR 2024) https://github.com/leolyliu/TACO-Instructions; overlap: Proposed mining source; from memory, hand (MANO) and object annotations, not full-body arm joints, so elbow and shoulder labels are missing.; verified=False
- Dataset of bimanual human-to-human object handovers (Data in Brief 2023) https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S2352340923003967; overlap: The only mocap-referenced test set named; whether its RGB-D streams align with the mocap was not confirmed in this session.; verified=False
- Seeing Through Occlusion: Deterministic Arm Kinematic Correction for Robot Teleoperation (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19240; overlap: Deterministic kinematic correction of occluded arm landmarks; a learned-trust method must beat it (re-implemented in the thesis eval track).; verified=False
- Egocentric Visibility-Aware Human Pose Estimation (EvaPose) (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.23618; overlap: Learns per-joint visibility from large labelled data; overlaps the 'predict when a landmark is unreliable' contribution.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Narrow the work to 'Occlusion-aware learned measurement trust for single-view RGB-D arm tracking during hand-object interaction', aimed at ICRA or RA-L. Drop ARCTIC/TACO mining, the Unity D435 depth twin, Theseus end-to-end training, the I01 factor graph and robot retargeting from the core paper.

- Training data: real single-view RGB-D with body and object ground truth from BEHAVE and InterCap, run through the v3 RTMPose stack. Remove the COCO-vs-joint offsets per joint (D-018-style) and set tau above the pseudo-ground-truth noise.
- Model: a causal GRU under 0.5M parameters that predicts P(error > tau) and a variance from score, kinematic residual, prediction-relative depth residual and object relation.
- Calibration: conformal calibration on held-out subjects.
- Integration: plug the calibrated variance into the existing v3 tangent Kalman with the object-conditioned wrist factor.
- Evaluation: held-out BEHAVE/InterCap subjects; the Cornell handover mocap set only after its camera-to-mocap alignment is confirmed; one new lab recording with deliberate occlusions plus about 2,000 manual labels, which also resolves D-017.
- Gate: first measure the base rate of confident-but-wrong landmarks under occlusion. If it is under a few percent, pivot.
- Later work: Unity renders and mined multi-view failures come back only as a follow-up data-engine study.

### venue skeptic: score 4, refuted=True

Area-chair view, best-fitting venue ICRA 2027 / RA-L. (Web search budget was exhausted in this session, so the venue check relies on the idea's own verified citations plus memory; memory-only items are marked unverified.)

1. No single headline claim. The idea bundles five contributions: a per-landmark error detector, calibrated covariances, object-conditioned wrist and IK elbow factors in a Kalman or factor-graph estimator, a mined-failure data engine (ARCTIC/TACO plus Unity D435 twin), and a downstream retargeting study. A reviewer cannot tell what the paper is about. The research question chains four conditional claims (higher AUROC, then lower false_measured and error, then cross-subject transfer, then better robot actions). Each of these needs its own evaluation, and any one of them failing weakens the whole story.

2. The core novelty reads as incremental. Learned measurement noise and learned gains for Kalman filters are standard: KalmanNet, LSTM-KF, backprop-KF (Haarnoja et al., NeurIPS 2016), and differentiable factor-graph smoothers (Yi et al., IROS 2021). Learned keypoint and mesh confidence also exists (POCO, VisTracker visibility, EvaPose visibility at 3M-frame scale). "Predict P(error > tau) from residuals and features, then use it as R_t" is exactly what an ICRA reviewer expects as an engineering step. The stated delta (the target is error rather than visibility, and the labels are mined failures) is real but thin. The idea's own text concedes it is "the weakest delta among the estimation topics". At CVPR/ECCV this would be rejected as an incremental filter improvement. At ICRA/RA-L it is borderline unless the system evidence is strong.

3. The evaluation is the weakest point. The thesis has one subject, three recordings, small manual label sets and no mocap. The proposal leans on the Cornell handover dataset and HOH for mocap-referenced fidelity, but whether either has a desk-like single RGB-D view, and whether its extrinsics can be recovered, is unchecked. TACO and ARCTIC are studio multi-view setups whose occlusion statistics differ from a D435 desk view. The lab domain stays one person, and reviewers will ask for more than one. The key failure mode (wrong body part with high confidence, desk depth stealing the hip) may be specific to MediaPipe and the D435 geometry. Newer detectors (RTMPose-x, ViTPose, whole-body SAM-based models) may shrink this error class, and the proposal has no plan to show the problem persists across detectors. Gains likely concentrate in a small fraction of occluded frames, so aggregate improvements may not reach significance.

4. The baselines are adequate but the comparison targets drift. The idea lists many baselines, which is good, but it omits the strongest natural ones. These are a learned temporal 3D pose model that already handles occlusion end to end (WHAM / TRAM / GVHMR-style video HMR, from memory) and a robust-kernel factor graph with switchable constraints and no learned trust. If a robust M-estimator or a stronger video HMR model closes most of the gap, the learned-trust contribution evaporates.

5. Scope and cost relative to payoff. 1-2 TB of multi-view mining, a 3-4 week Unity depth model and a Cornell conversion, all for a component paper. The downstream retargeting result in simulation is unlikely to be persuasive at CoRL/RSS without real-robot execution.

Expected outcome: weak reject at CVPR/ECCV/WACV; borderline at ICRA/RA-L, decided by evaluation breadth. It fits better as a component of a larger system paper (I01/I03) or as a RA-L paper with a sharpened single claim.

Prior work found:
- KalmanNet: Neural Network Aided Kalman Filtering for Partially Known Dynamics (IEEE TSP 2022) https://github.com/KalmanNet/KalmanNet_TSP; overlap: Learned Kalman gain or covariance on a model-based filter; the core mechanism of the proposal; verified=True
- Long Short-Term Memory Kalman Filters: Recurrent Neural Estimators for Pose Regularization (ICCV 2017); overlap: Learned temporal noise and gain for human pose filtering; verified=False
- Backprop KF: Learning Discriminative Deterministic State Estimators (NeurIPS 2016); overlap: End-to-end learned observation noise inside a Kalman filter; verified=False
- Differentiable Factor Graph Optimization for Learning Smoothers (IROS 2021); overlap: Learned per-factor noise models in factor-graph smoothing; covers the Theseus fine-tuning angle; verified=False
- Visibility Aware Human-Object Interaction Tracking from Single RGB Camera (VisTracker) (CVPR 2023) https://github.com/xiexh20/VisTracker; overlap: Occlusion-aware human-object tracking with predicted visibility; verified=True
- POCO: 3D Pose and Shape Estimation with Confidence (3DV 2024); overlap: Per-frame learned pose confidence; verified=False
- Egocentric Visibility-Aware Human Pose Estimation (EvaPose) (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.23618; overlap: Learned per-joint visibility at large real-data scale; weakens the 'mined labels' delta; verified=True
- Seeing Through Occlusion: Deterministic Arm Kinematic Correction for Robot Teleoperation (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19240; overlap: Kinematic correction of occluded arm landmarks for teleoperation; a direct model-based competitor; verified=True
- Occlusion-Aware Temporally Consistent Amodal Completion for 3D Human-Object Interaction Reconstruction (ACM MM 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.08137; overlap: Handles hand-object occlusion in HOI reconstruction; verified=True

Suggested reframe: Narrow it to one falsifiable headline and make the evidence the contribution. Option A (RA-L / ICRA): "Detector confidence is not a trust signal under hand-object occlusion: a mocap-referenced study and a kinematics-aware trust model for single-RGB-D demonstration capture." The paper would (1) quantify on Cornell/HOH/TACO, across three or more detectors (MediaPipe, RTMPose, ViTPose or a video HMR), how often confident-but-wrong landmarks occur and how much they cost downstream; (2) show that one feature, the residual to an explicit kinematic and object-conditioned prediction, separates them far better than score. A small calibrated model plus switchable-constraint factors closes most of the gap. Baselines must include a robust-kernel factor graph without learning and a modern video HMR model. Drop the Unity depth twin and robot retargeting unless the first two results hold. Option B: fold it in as the measurement-trust module of the I01/I03 system paper rather than a standalone submission. CVPR/ECCV is not a realistic target for this idea alone.

## I10: Does the tracker know when it is wrong? An honesty-scored, occlusion-labelled single-view RGB-D benchmark for upper-body and hand-object tracking during bimanual manipulation and handover

Direction: Benchmarks and evaluation protocols for human and human-object tracking that score honesty (failure signalling, abstention, calibration), causality and recovery after occlusion, not only offline accuracy.

Research question: Under realistic single-view RGB-D occlusion during object manipulation and hand-to-hand handover, which current body, hand and object trackers (RTMPose/ViTPose + depth, MediaPipe, MotionBERT, WiLoR, HaMeR, PromptHMR, WHAM, RoHM, FoundationPose, SAM 2, VisTracker, HOLD-type optimisation, the thesis v1 and v3 pipelines) correctly signal their own failures, how much accuracy do they lose on handover and long-occlusion segments when restricted to causal operation, and does ranking by honesty metrics (3D Average-Jaccard-style score penalising false 'measured', false_measured rate, AURC, conditional interval coverage, reacquisition teleports, reconvergence) differ from ranking by MPJPE, broken down by occlusion duration and cause?

Why novel (as proposed): Existing bimanual and hand-object datasets score position from expensive multi-view or mocap rigs: ARCTIC (CVPR 2023), OakInk2 (CVPR 2024), GigaHands (CVPR 2025), HOT3D (CVPR 2025, egocentric), TACO, ParaHome, HOGraspNet. HO-Cap (NeurIPS 2025 D&B) already shows 8 RealSense cameras plus a HoloLens can replace mocap and includes hand-to-hand handovers, but has no arm/torso kinematics, single-view occlusion protocol or abstention scoring. HOH (NeurIPS 2023 D&B) covers human-to-human handovers. TAP-Vid and TAPVid-3D score position and visibility jointly for points, not articulated chains or held objects. Occlusion benchmarks for human pose are synthetic and score error only (arXiv 2504.10350 BlendMimic3D, VOccl3D ICCV 2025). CCNet (ICML 2024) evaluates keypoint calibration on COCO images; RadProPoser (arXiv 2508.03578) reports radar per-joint calibration but not occlusion-conditioned honesty; BEHAVE and VisTracker score HOI accuracy only; RoHM, HuMoR and GeneOH evaluate accuracy or plausibility under masks. No benchmark scores silent (confidently wrong) failures, provenance claims, or the temporal cost of re-acquisition. Delta: the protocol (causal track with declared lag, per-frame occlusion labels by depth test and occluder class, 3D-AJ honesty metric, duration/cause splits, reacquisition-snap and reconvergence metrics) rather than scale. As a dataset it cannot compete on size; value must come from a measured rank reversal.

Method (as proposed): Two data layers sharing one protocol. Layer A (public, no new capture): re-render ARCTIC, OakInk2, TACO (and HOT3D where the licence allows) from virtual desk-facing RGB-D viewpoints with z-buffer per-joint visibility and depth-occluder identity; use BEHAVE and InterCap single views with visibility from other views and depth; add a Unity occlusion suite from v3 S-type scenarios (wrist_short, wrist_long, elbow_long, root_ref, hip, pose_loss) plus stressors (arm along optical axis, hanging-arm twist, desk-occluded hips, handover) run through the real detector; release scripts only. Layer B (new capture): the D435 test view is the only stream a tracker sees; 2-3 extra RealSense cameras (hardware sync) used only for ground truth, all registered to the desk ArUco world frame with wall-marker gravity; body truth from ArUco/AprilTag cuffs on upper arm, forearm, wrist plus sternum and pelvis plates triangulated across auxiliary views and checked against multi-view triangulated RTMPose-l; object truth from marked cube and printed props on a registered rail / pegboard route and a detent turntable, with a marker-inpainted variant; occlusion labels automatically by depth test (test-view depth more than tau in front of the true joint depth), occluder class from SAM 2 masks (desk, object, other arm, own body, out of frame), 'depth from wrong surface' flags, grasp mode and handover timestamps; 10-15 subjects, 2 sessions, tasks: loop, rail, handover-on-rail, lift-carry-place, hand-to-hand handover, reach behind object, crossing arms. Protocol: every tracker emits per joint group a 3D estimate and a state in {measured, estimated, abstain} (adapters convert confidences to scalar uncertainty, interval or provenance); offline and causal tracks with declared lag. Metrics: 3D-AJ at 2, 5 and 10 cm with 'measured' on occluded joints as false positive; E_occ by duration bins (1-5, 6-15, 16-45, more than 45 frames) and occluder class; false_measured / false-confident rate; risk-coverage and AURC; ECE and conditional coverage by visibility class; reacquisition snap vs clean-motion teleport budgets (D-033); reconvergence time and causal-offline divergence; stale-state detection latency; object point-to-path, segment length and turntable orientation error. Code, datasheet and leaderboard reuse V3 bench/grade.py.

Evaluation (as proposed): Run all listed trackers frozen plus the thesis pipelines and I01-I05 methods as reference entries. Headline: Kendall tau between MPJPE and honesty rankings and rank reversals within duration bins and occlusion classes; effect of adding object information; single-view vs multi-view upper bound to quantify what one view can recover (Chapter 9 claims made quantitative). Ground-truth validation: cuff residuals vs tape and rail, cuffs vs triangulated keypoints on clean frames, one borrowed optical-mocap session (report own GT error as EMDB does, 2.3 cm), label error budgets, manual clicks on a subset. Realism check of Layer A against the D435 recordings (visibility statistics, gap lengths). Ablations of the benchmark: sensitivity of rankings to tau and pseudo-GT noise, occlusion-label sensitivity, multi-view vs synthetic label agreement, cuffs vs no cuffs on clean-frame 2D error, subject/session robustness; optional small user study showing honesty metrics predict perceived trustworthiness.

Target venues: NeurIPS 2027 Datasets and Benchmarks, CVPR 2027 (if paired with a strong method), 3DV 2027, IJCV (extended)

Builds on the thesis: World-frame calibration with desk and wall markers, ArUco object tracking, swing-twist kinematics, honesty flags, the bag-file archive pipeline, Unity sensor-POV frame-exact capture check, v3 grade.py, false_measured, teleport budgets from clean motion, S-type windows, RTMPose on ONNX (p99 under 13 ms), the causal replay harness, and the documented failure catalogue (Table 9.1) give the taxonomy and metric code; the thesis finding that synthetic and natural occlusion disagree and windows rank recovery methods differently motivates the duration- and cause-split; the 28 bags are a pilot split.

Feasibility (as proposed): Layer A is mostly data engineering (pyrender / nvdiffrast on the RTX 3080, adapters for baselines, 4-8 weeks), needs dataset downloads and licence review. Layer B needs 2-3 extra RealSense cameras (about USD 300-450 each), sync cable, cuffs, rail, pegboard, turntable (about USD 1000-1500 total), possibly a powered hub or second PC, ethics approval for 10-15 subjects (2-3 months, in parallel with rig building), storage for several TB. Timeline: months 1-2 rig and GT validation; months 3-5 capture; months 5-7 labelling, baselines and mocap cross-check; months 8-9 paper and release. NeurIPS D&B 2027 (around May-June 2027) is realistic; Layer A alone about 6 months.

Risks (as proposed): Too small and lab-specific next to HO-Cap, GigaHands, HOT3D and EgoDex; reviewers may say the analysis could be run on HO-Cap single views; the protocol must change model rankings or it is a workshop paper; if rankings do not change the central claim fails. Cuff GT error (1-2 cm) and BEHAVE/InterCap pseudo-GT noise may be comparable to method differences; cuffs may change detector behaviour; HO-Cap already occupies 'cheap multi-RealSense ground truth', so do not lead with cost; licence restrictions may limit release to scripts.

### novelty skeptic: score 3.5, refuted=True

Search caveat: the session's web-search budget was used up (200 of 200 calls), so none of my four queries ran. The papers below come from the candidate's own verified list or from memory, and the memory ones are marked verified=false. Judged on that evidence, the novelty claim rests on combining existing pieces rather than on a new primitive.

(1) The headline metric is not new. TAPVid-3D (NeurIPS 2024 D&B) already defines a 3D Average Jaccard that scores 3D position and predicted visibility together, with depth-scaled thresholds. Predicting "visible" on an occluded point already counts as a false positive there, which is the 'measured on occluded joints = false positive' rule proposed here. The delta is moving it from points to articulated joint groups plus a three-state provenance label (measured / estimated / abstain). A reviewer will see that as a relabelling of TAP-Vid occlusion accuracy.

(2) Scoring a tracker for reporting that its target is lost is established in single-object tracking. The VOT long-term challenges (VOT-LT 2018 to 2022, ongoing in VOTS 2023/2024) score tracking precision and recall with target absence and re-detection after disappearance. TAP-Vid already scores visibility, BOP visibility-aware scoring, and the TAPVid-3D and BOP re-detection protocols cover similar ground. The proposed reacquisition and reconvergence metrics mirror the VOT-LT re-detection analysis.

(3) Calibration and selective-prediction metrics for pose already exist. CCNet (ICML 2024) evaluates keypoint confidence calibration. Probabilistic HMR work (ProHMR ICCV 2021, HuManiFlow CVPR 2023, ScoreHMR CVPR 2024) reports uncertainty-error correlation. Learning Visibility for Robust Dense Human Body Estimation (ECCV 2022) predicts per-vertex visibility. AURC and ECE are standard. Combining them is engineering unless it produces a surprising result.

(4) Occlusion benchmarks for human pose with duration and cause analysis are crowded: VOccl3D (ICCV 2025), BlendMimic3D (arXiv 2504.10350), 3DPW-OCC, OCHuman, and the PoseBench robustness benchmark. Their cue is error only, but adding a visibility head is a small step.

(5) On data, HO-Cap (NeurIPS 2025 D&B) already uses multi-RealSense ground truth with hand-to-hand handovers. Its single views can be taken as test views, with occlusion labels from depth tests against the multi-view ground truth. That is exactly the "reviewers will say run it on HO-Cap" risk the idea names, and it removes most of the reason for Layer B. GigaHands, HOT3D, ARCTIC and OakInk2 dwarf a 10-15 subject capture in one lab.

(6) What is still unclaimed, as far as I can tell without a live search: a protocol that holds upper-body (arm/torso) kinematic chains and held objects to account for provenance claims under causal operation with a declared lag, plus a measured rank reversal between MPJPE and honesty rankings. That is a real gap, but narrow. It is publishable at a top datasets-and-benchmarks track only if the rank reversal is large and shown on public data. Otherwise it is a workshop or 3DV paper.

The candidate's own risk list admits the central claim fails if rankings do not change. Nothing is known yet about whether they do, so the default verdict is refuted.

Prior work found:
- TAPVid-3D: A Benchmark for Tracking Any Point in 3D (NeurIPS 2024 Datasets and Benchmarks) https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05921; overlap: Defines 3D-AJ that jointly scores 3D position and occlusion/visibility prediction (claiming visible on occluded = penalised); the proposed 3D-AJ honesty metric is this metric applied to joints.; verified=True
- TAP-Vid: A Benchmark for Tracking Any Point in a Video (NeurIPS 2022 Datasets and Benchmarks) https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.03726; overlap: Origin of Average Jaccard and occlusion accuracy: jointly scoring position and the tracker's own visibility claim.; verified=True
- HO-Cap: A Capture System and Dataset for 3D Reconstruction and Pose Tracking of Hand-Object Interaction (NeurIPS 2025 Datasets and Benchmarks) https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06843; overlap: Multi-RealSense ground truth including hand-to-hand handovers; single views can serve as test views with depth-test occlusion labels, largely subsuming Layer B for hands and objects (lacks arm/torso).; verified=True
- VOT long-term tracking challenge (VOT-LT, precision/recall with target absence and re-detection) (VOT Challenge, ECCV/ICCV workshops 2018-2022; VOTS 2023/2024); overlap: Scores trackers on correctly reporting target absence and on re-detection after disappearance, the object-tracking precedent for honesty and reacquisition metrics.; verified=False
- On the Calibration of Human Pose Estimation (CCNet) (ICML 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.17105; overlap: Calibration evaluation of keypoint confidences; ECE-style honesty scoring for pose already exists.; verified=True
- HuManiFlow: Ancestor-Conditioned Normalising Flows on SO(3) Manifolds for Human Pose and Shape Distribution Estimation (CVPR 2023); overlap: Evaluates per-joint uncertainty quality and its correlation with error and visibility for 3D human pose.; verified=False
- Score-Guided Diffusion for 3D Human Recovery (ScoreHMR) (CVPR 2024); overlap: Probabilistic body recovery with uncertainty under occlusion; a candidate baseline that already reports uncertainty behaviour.; verified=False
- Learning Visibility for Robust Dense Human Body Estimation (ECCV 2022); overlap: Predicts per-joint/vertex visibility (truncation, occlusion) jointly with pose, the measured-vs-occluded provenance head for bodies.; verified=False
- VOccl3D: A Video Benchmark Dataset for 3D Human Pose and Shape Estimation under Real Occlusions (ICCV 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.06757; overlap: Occlusion benchmark for video human pose with occlusion analysis; error only, but occupies the occlusion-benchmark slot.; verified=True
- Benchmarking 3D Human Pose Estimation Models under Occlusions (BlendMimic3D) (arXiv 2025 (2504.10350)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10350; overlap: Occlusion-type and duration breakdown of 3D pose models; overlaps the duration/cause split.; verified=True
- HOT3D: Hand and Object Tracking in 3D from Egocentric Multi-View Videos (CVPR 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.19167; overlap: Large-scale hand-object tracking ground truth; any small lab dataset is compared against it.; verified=True
- GigaHands: A Massive Annotated Dataset of Bimanual Hand Activities (CVPR 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.04244; overlap: Bimanual multi-view dataset at scale; single views could be re-scored with occlusion labels.; verified=True
- ARCTIC: A Dataset for Dexterous Bimanual Hand-Object Manipulation (CVPR 2023) https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2023/html/Fan_ARCTIC_A_Dataset_for_Dexterous_Bimanual_Hand-Object_Manipulation_CVPR_2023_paper.html; overlap: Mocap ground truth for bimanual hand-object; supports Layer A re-rendering, so the protocol rather than the data is the only contribution.; verified=True
- HOH: Markerless Multimodal Human-Object-Human Handover Dataset (NeurIPS 2023 Datasets and Benchmarks) https://arxiv.org/html/2310.00723v5; overlap: Handover-specific multi-view RGB-D data; handover as a stress condition is not new.; verified=True

Suggested reframe: Drop the new-capture dataset as the main contribution and lead with a falsifiable empirical claim. For example: "Under single-view causal operation, MPJPE rankings of body, hand and object trackers reverse when provenance claims are scored." Show it first, cheaply, on public data: HO-Cap and GigaHands single views with depth-test occlusion labels from their multi-view ground truth, ARCTIC/OakInk2 re-rendered from desk-facing views, and the thesis v3 Unity S-type suite.

Explicitly cite and extend TAPVid-3D 3D-AJ and the VOT-LT absence scoring to articulated chains with a three-state provenance output (measured / estimated / abstain). Add the two pieces neither covers: causal-vs-offline divergence with a declared lag, and the reacquisition-snap / reconvergence cost. Use the thesis's 28 bags and a small cuff-instrumented upper-body set only as the arm/torso split that HO-Cap lacks.

Then pair the benchmark with a method so it can target CVPR/ICCV rather than only a datasets track. Candidate method: object-conditioned recovery that outputs calibrated provenance (for example, a conformal interval per joint group). It would win on the honesty metrics while staying competitive on MPJPE.

Before investing in rig hardware, run a two-week Layer A pilot measuring Kendall tau between the MPJPE and 3D-AJ rankings. If tau is above about 0.8, abandon the benchmark framing.

### feasibility skeptic: score 4, refuted=True

Feasibility verdict for the full idea (Layer A plus Layer B) under the small-lab assumptions: refuted. A reduced version that uses existing data only is feasible and is described in suggested_reframe.

Note on literature: the web search budget for this session was used up before I could run any search, so I re-verified nothing. Every prior_work entry below is copied from the candidate's own list and marked verified=false by rule. It is not marked false because I have evidence against it.

1. Layer B is a second project hidden inside the first, and engineering dominates it. It needs all of the following, none of which the lab has today:
- 2 to 3 extra hardware-synchronised RealSense cameras, plus a powered hub or a second PC.
- Cuffs and sternum/pelvis plates.
- A registered rail, a pegboard and a detent turntable.
- Ethics approval for 10 to 15 subjects (2 to 3 months).
- Several TB of storage.
- A borrowed optical mocap session. The idea never says where it would come from, and the lab has no mocap.
The budget figures are plausible. The schedule is not: rig building and ground-truth validation (months 1-2), capture of 10-15 subjects in 2 sessions (months 3-5), then labelling, about 15 baseline adapters and the mocap cross-check (months 5-7). For one graduate student who is also writing the thesis code, that is optimistic by a factor of about 2. The thesis itself reports how fragile single-subject capture already is: an unmeasured wall-marker angle, a route never registered into the world frame, and stale grip offsets.

2. The ground truth is not convincing at the precision the claim needs.
- Cuff markers give segment poses, not joint centres. Elbow and shoulder centres need per-subject offset calibration, and the shoulder in particular has no rigid landmark.
- The idea itself puts cuff error at 1 to 2 cm. Its 3D-AJ thresholds are 2, 5 and 10 cm, so at the 2 cm threshold ground-truth noise is the same size as the metric.
- Occlusion labels come from "test-view depth more than tau in front of the true joint depth". They inherit the joint-depth error, plus D435 depth noise along the line of sight, which the thesis itself identifies as the failure mode (arm along the optical axis, depth from the wrong surface).
- Cuffs change the appearance of the arm. The claim is about how off-the-shelf detectors behave under occlusion, so this contaminates exactly what is being measured. The idea admits it and offers only a clean-frame 2D check.

3. The central claim is fragile even if the data is perfect.
- Most of the listed trackers (WHAM, PromptHMR, HaMeR, WiLoR, MotionBERT, FoundationPose) emit no calibrated per-joint confidence and no abstain state. Their {measured, estimated, abstain} labels would be produced by adapters written by the benchmark authors.
- So any "rank reversal" between MPJPE and honesty can be manufactured, or erased, by adapter threshold choices. Reviewers will say exactly this.
- If the honest entries are mostly the authors' own pipelines (v1, v3, I01-I05), the benchmark looks self-serving.
- If the reversal does not appear, or is explained by trackers that simply always say "measured", the idea's own risk statement says the paper falls to workshop level. There is no pilot evidence yet that a reversal exists: one subject, 28 bags, and synthetic versus natural occlusion results that disagree.

4. Layer A is more feasible but under-specified.
- ARCTIC, OakInk2 and TACO are RGB-only or multi-view RGB with fitted meshes. Re-rendering them "from virtual desk-facing RGB-D viewpoints" gives untextured or poorly textured humans, and real detectors such as RTMPose or MediaPipe will not behave realistically on those renders.
- The realistic route is to use the real single views that already exist, with real RGB-D: BEHAVE and InterCap (Kinect RGB-D, multi-view, SMPL plus object ground truth) and HO-Cap (multi-RealSense, hand plus object ground truth, handovers). Visibility labels then come from the ground-truth mesh z-buffer compared with the sensor depth.
- That is also exactly what reviewers will say could be done instead of a new capture. It removes most of the novelty claim for Layer B.

Minimum viable experiment (about 3 to 4 months, existing hardware, no new subjects):
- Take single test views from BEHAVE and InterCap (body plus object) and HO-Cap (hands plus object, including hand-to-hand handovers).
- Compute per-joint visibility and occluder class automatically from a ground-truth mesh z-buffer against the observed depth, then bin occlusion duration.
- Run 6 to 8 frozen trackers, both causal and offline: RTMPose plus depth, MediaPipe, WHAM, HaMeR or WiLoR, FoundationPose, plus the thesis v3 pipeline.
- Score with threshold-free honesty metrics only: risk-coverage/AURC over each tracker's native confidence, conditional coverage by visibility class, and reacquisition-snap and reconvergence time. Keep thresholded 3D-AJ as secondary and report its sensitivity to the thresholds.
- Success criterion: a statistically supported rank reversal in at least one duration or occluder bin that holds under bootstrap and threshold sweeps.
- If it fails, stop before buying hardware.
- Use the thesis 28 bags only as a qualitative realism check of gap-length statistics, not as ground truth.

Score rationale: 4/10. Layer A alone, restricted to existing real RGB-D data, is about 7/10 feasible. The two-layer plan with new multi-subject capture and cuff ground truth is about 2-3/10 for the stated lab, and the central claim has no pilot evidence. When uncertain the default is refuted, so the full idea is refuted.

Prior work found:
- HO-Cap: A Capture System and Dataset for 3D Reconstruction and Pose Tracking of Hand-Object Interaction (NeurIPS 2025 Datasets and Benchmarks) https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06843; overlap: Multi-RealSense ground truth with hand-to-hand handovers. Its single RealSense views are the obvious substitute for Layer B and can serve as the existing-data test set.; verified=False
- BEHAVE: Dataset and Method for Tracking Human Object Interactions (CVPR 2022) https://arxiv.org/abs/2204.06950; overlap: Multi-view Kinect RGB-D with SMPL and object ground truth. A single view plus visibility from the mesh z-buffer gives body-object honesty labels without new capture.; verified=False
- ARCTIC: A Dataset for Dexterous Bimanual Hand-Object Manipulation (CVPR 2023) https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2023/html/Fan_ARCTIC_A_Dataset_for_Dexterous_Bimanual_Hand-Object_Manipulation_CVPR_2023_paper.html; overlap: Bimanual ground truth with body and object, but RGB only. Re-rendering it to RGB-D gives unrealistic detector inputs.; verified=False
- TAPVid-3D: A Benchmark for Tracking Any Point in 3D (NeurIPS 2024 Datasets and Benchmarks) https://arxiv.org/html/2407.05921v1; overlap: Already scores 3D position and visibility jointly (3D Average Jaccard), which the proposed 3D-AJ honesty metric borrows.; verified=False
- VOccl3D: A Video Benchmark Dataset for 3D Human Pose and Shape Estimation under Real Occlusions (ICCV 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.06757; overlap: Occlusion benchmark for human pose that scores accuracy only. It is the closest benchmark framing the idea competes with.; verified=False
- On the Calibration of Human Pose Estimation (CCNet) (ICML 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.17105; overlap: Evaluates keypoint confidence calibration on images. It shows calibration metrics for pose already exist, though not occlusion-conditioned or temporal.; verified=False
- HOT3D: Hand and Object Tracking in 3D from Egocentric Multi-View Videos (CVPR 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.19167; overlap: Large hand-object tracking benchmark with mocap ground truth. A new small capture cannot compete with it on scale.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Drop Layer B (the new capture, cuffs, ethics and mocap). Reframe the project as an evaluation-protocol paper on existing real RGB-D multi-view datasets: "Silent failures under occlusion: occlusion-duration- and cause-conditioned honesty evaluation of single-view body, hand and object trackers on BEHAVE, InterCap and HO-Cap."

Method:
- Visibility and occluder class come from a ground-truth mesh z-buffer against the sensor depth.
- Score with threshold-free risk-coverage/AURC and conditional coverage on each tracker's native confidences, plus reacquisition-snap and reconvergence metrics in causal and offline modes.
- Report sensitivity of every thresholded metric (3D-AJ, false_measured) to the threshold choice.
- Evaluate the thesis v3 pipeline as one entry only, not as the headline.

Gate: run a 6-week pilot on one dataset first. Continue only if it shows a rank reversal that survives a bootstrap and a threshold sweep.

Targets: 3DV, WACV or the NeurIPS D&B track. Buy hardware for a small held-out desk-handover split only if the pilot succeeds.

### venue skeptic: score 3.5, refuted=True

Area-chair verdict for NeurIPS D&B 2027 / CVPR 2027 / 3DV 2027: reject as currently framed. I could not run new web searches because the session search budget was exhausted. This judgment therefore rests on the prior work listed in the idea, the dossier, and my own knowledge of the field.

(1) The headline claim depends on a result nobody has measured yet. The paper only works if honesty-based rankings differ from MPJPE rankings (low Kendall tau, rank reversals inside occlusion bins). The risks section admits this. If the rankings stay correlated, which is likely because stronger trackers usually also fail less silently, nothing is left except a small lab dataset. A benchmark proposal whose single selling point is a hoped-for rank reversal is a bet, not a contribution. An AC would ask for a pilot showing the reversal before 9 months of capture.

(2) The protocol measures the adapters, not the trackers. Almost none of the listed systems (WHAM, PromptHMR, MotionBERT, HaMeR, WiLoR, FoundationPose, SAM 2, RoHM) emits a {measured, estimated, abstain} state or a calibrated 3D interval. The benchmark's adapters would supply those states by thresholding heatmap scores, visibility logits or mask IoU. So false_measured, 3D-AJ and AURC rankings would largely reflect adapter design choices, and reviewers will say so immediately.
