A fair version needs a principled, tracker-agnostic way to produce confidence (for example, post-hoc selective prediction fitted on a held-out split, as in the selective-classification literature) plus a sensitivity analysis showing the rankings survive adapter choices. That analysis is a paper in itself.

(3) Layer B is redundant next to existing data, and reviewers will say "run it on HO-Cap / ARCTIC / DexYCB single views". ARCTIC (9 views), HO-Cap (8 RealSense views plus HoloLens, with handovers), DexYCB (8 RealSense views), OakInk2, GigaHands (dozens of views) and BEHAVE/InterCap already provide real multi-view RGB(-D) with mesh ground truth. Hold-one-view-out evaluation with z-buffer visibility from the GT meshes gives real images, per-joint occlusion labels, occluder identity and handovers, at far larger scale than 10-15 subjects, and with no new capture or ethics process. Layer A as written (re-rendering meshes from virtual viewpoints) is weaker than that: rendered untextured or retextured meshes put RGB detectors far outside their training distribution, so the measured failures would partly be synthetic-domain artifacts. The one thing Layer B uniquely adds is arm/torso kinematics during desk manipulation from a single desk-facing D435. That is a niche the AC will see as lab-specific.

(4) The ground truth may be too coarse for the differences being measured. Cuff markers at 1-2 cm error, BEHAVE/InterCap pseudo-GT noise, and a 2 cm threshold in 3D-AJ are all of the same order. Cuffs also change the image that RGB detectors see. The proposal acknowledges this but does not solve it, and one borrowed mocap session is thin validation for a benchmark paper.

(5) Too broad, and the baselines do not share a task. Body, hand and object trackers, RGB-only and RGB-D, offline and causal, monocular HMR next to a depth back-projection pipeline: these do not solve the same problem. Pooling them into one leaderboard invites "apples to oranges" reviews. The comparison set also misses standard references a CV AC would expect for uncertainty in pose: probabilistic HMR (e.g. ProHMR, ICCV 2021), MC-dropout or ensemble baselines, and conformal prediction applied to pose (not verified this session).

(6) Fit to novelty. Scoring honesty and recovery after occlusion is a reasonable gap; TAP-Vid already established joint position-plus-visibility scoring for points, and CCNet established keypoint calibration. Carrying that idea over to articulated 3D chains under occlusion is incremental unless it comes with either a large-scale reusable resource or a method that wins on the new metric. As proposed, it has neither. Best realistic outcomes: a CVPR/ECCV workshop, BMVC or WACV paper, or 3DV only if the rank-reversal result is strong. NeurIPS D&B is reachable only after the reframe below.

What is good: the metric design itself (3D-AJ that penalises a false 'measured' label, duration and occluder-class splits, reacquisition-teleport and reconvergence metrics, causal track with declared lag) is concrete, builds directly on working thesis code (grade.py, false_measured, teleport budgets), and addresses a real gap for robotics and teleoperation users who need to know when the tracker is wrong.

Prior work found:
- HO-Cap: A Capture System and Dataset for 3D Reconstruction and Pose Tracking of Hand-Object Interaction (NeurIPS 2025 Datasets and Benchmarks) https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06843; overlap: Multi-RealSense ground truth with handovers. Its real per-view images could host a hold-one-view-out honesty protocol, which makes the proposed new capture largely redundant.; verified=False
- ARCTIC: A Dataset for Dexterous Bimanual Hand-Object Manipulation (CVPR 2023) https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2023/html/Fan_ARCTIC_A_Dataset_for_Dexterous_Bimanual_Hand-Object_Manipulation_CVPR_2023_paper.html; overlap: Nine real views with SMPL-X body, MANO hands and articulated-object meshes. Allows single-view evaluation with z-buffer occlusion labels and no rendering domain gap.; verified=False
- DexYCB: A Benchmark for Capturing Hand Grasping of Objects (CVPR 2021); overlap: Eight RealSense views with hand and object ground truth: the standard multi-RealSense single-view hand-object benchmark reviewers will cite.; verified=False
- TAP-Vid / TAPVid-3D (NeurIPS 2022 / NeurIPS 2024 Datasets and Benchmarks) https://arxiv.org/html/2407.05921v1; overlap: Already scores position and occlusion prediction jointly through Average Jaccard. The proposed 3D-AJ carries this over to joints, which reviewers will see as incremental.; verified=False
- On the Calibration of Human Pose Estimation (CCNet) (ICML 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.17105; overlap: Keypoint confidence calibration; establishes calibration evaluation for pose, though in 2D and image-level.; verified=False
- VOccl3D: A Video Benchmark Dataset for 3D Human Pose and Shape Estimation under Real Occlusions (ICCV 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.06757; overlap: Occlusion-focused 3D human benchmark; scores error only, which is the gap the idea targets.; verified=False
- HOT3D: Hand and Object Tracking in 3D from Egocentric Multi-View Videos (CVPR 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.19167; overlap: Large-scale hand-object tracking benchmark with mocap ground truth; sets the scale bar a new lab dataset must clear.; verified=False
- GigaHands: A Massive Annotated Dataset of Bimanual Hand Activities (CVPR 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.04244; overlap: Dense multi-view bimanual capture usable for hold-one-view-out occlusion evaluation.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Turn this from a dataset into a protocol plus a method. Title direction: "Selective 3D hand-body-object tracking: a hold-one-view-out failure-awareness protocol and a learned trust model". (a) Build the benchmark entirely on existing real multi-view datasets (ARCTIC, HO-Cap, DexYCB, OakInk2, GigaHands, BEHAVE): take one real camera as the test view, use the full rig's meshes as ground truth, and compute per-joint visibility and occluder class by z-buffer. This gives real images, scale, handovers and no ethics delay. Release scripts only. (b) Make confidence fair across trackers: fit post-hoc selective-prediction or conformal heads per tracker on a calibration split, so the rankings reflect the trackers rather than hand-made adapters, and report how sensitive the rankings are to that choice. (c) Add the method half, taken from the thesis future work: a small learned trust model over landmark history, detector confidence, kinematic consistency and object pose, which decides measure / estimate-from-object / abstain and enforces kinematic feasibility. Show that it cuts silent failures (false_measured, AURC) at equal coverage across several base trackers. (d) Run a 2-week pilot first, on ARCTIC or HO-Cap with 4-5 trackers. Go ahead only if the honesty and MPJPE rankings actually diverge (Kendall tau clearly below 1, with reversals in long-occlusion bins). If they do not diverge, drop the benchmark claim and publish the trust model as the contribution. Keep the D435 desk recordings and Unity S-type suite as a small realism and causal-operation split, not as the headline dataset. Target CVPR/ECCV main track (method plus protocol) or NeurIPS D&B; fall back to 3DV or a robotics venue (ICRA/IROS) framed around safe teleoperation and digital twins.

## I11: Is synthetic occlusion a valid proxy? Measuring whether landmark masking, depth-consistent pixel occlusion and natural occlusion rank recovery methods the same way

Direction: Benchmark methodology: the validity of synthetic stress tests for occlusion robustness in pose and object tracking.

Research question: On the same real RGB-D sequences with metric ground truth, how well do three protocols agree in ranking occlusion-recovery methods: (i) masking solver inputs at landmark level (the thesis and V3 S-type scenarios), (ii) depth-consistent pixel-level occluder transplant into RGB and depth, and (iii) natural occlusion? Which properties of an occlusion (duration, occluder type, motion during the gap, stale grip state) explain the disagreement?

Why novel (as proposed): Occlusion robustness is routinely reported with synthetic protocols: detector-behaviour keypoint noise (arXiv 2504.10350, BlendMimic3D), temporary-occlusion corruption (Human3.6M-C and HumanEva-I-C, CVPRW 2024), mask corruptions (PoseBench, arXiv 2406.14367), rendered occluders (VOccl3D, ICCV 2025), occlusion measurement for interaction (ICMI Companion 2025). None checks on real data with metric ground truth that synthetic rankings predict natural-occlusion rankings; the 2504.10350 summary notes interpolation working on synthetic patterns struggles with realistic durations, which hints at the problem without measuring it. The thesis is a documented case: on synthetic masking, object-conditioned recovery tracked the reference to 1.7 cm median vs 21 cm hold-last; on natural occlusion it helped on one window (5.2 cm vs 11.4-17.6 cm) and not on another (left wrist 13.1 cm vs 12.3 cm for the plain solve) because of a stale grip offset and poor along-axis depth. Delta: a quantitative validity study plus a depth-consistent RGB-D occluder-transplant generator that keeps clean ground truth.

Method (as proposed): Data: sequences with metric ground truth (lab recordings plus the I10 capture with auxiliary views; a minimal version with 2 extra RealSense cameras and wrist cuffs). P1 landmark masking: delete or perturb solver inputs over V3 S-type windows (wrist_short, wrist_long, elbow_long, root_ref, hip, pose_loss) with 2504.10350-style detector noise. P2 depth-consistent occluder transplant: take an occluder layer (other forearm, cube, sheet, desk-edge proxy) as RGB, depth and SAM 2 mask from another frame or session and composite with a z-buffer rule (replace only where the occluder is nearer), with shading/colour matching and optional video-diffusion harmonisation; trajectories, duration and occluder class sampled to match measured natural statistics; the detector runs on edited pixels so confidently-wrong landmarks and wrong-surface depth appear naturally. P3 natural occlusion: real windows labelled by the I10 depth test. Methods ranked: hold-last, direction memory, object-conditioned recovery, S2 decayed velocity, tangent-space Kalman and IMM, siMLPe on 8 landmarks, MotionBERT-lite infill, RoHM. Analysis: Kendall tau and Spearman agreement per duration bin and occluder class; mixed-effects regression of each method's synthetic-minus-natural error gap on duration, wrist excursion, grip age, arm-axis angle; calibration of P2 sampling so its rankings match P3 on held-out sessions.

Evaluation (as proposed): Primary: rank agreement (Kendall tau with bootstrap CIs over windows and subjects) P1-P3 and P2-P3. Secondary: absolute error-transfer ratio, E_occ, reacquisition snap, false_measured. Public replication: P1 and P2 on HO-Cap RealSense sequences and P1 on Human3.6M-C. Ablations: P2 without z-buffer (2D paste), without duration matching, with/without diffusion harmonisation; power analysis on the number of natural windows needed for a stable ranking. Pre-register the analysis.

Target venues: CVPR 2027, NeurIPS 2027 Datasets and Benchmarks, ECCV 2026 workshops then IJCV

Builds on the thesis: The S-type landmark-masking generator, teleport budgets and graders, the recovery methods (hold-last, direction memory, object-conditioned recovery, S2, tangent Kalman), natural windows with manual wrist labels, and a documented synthetic-vs-natural disagreement that is the paper's opening observation.

Feasibility (as proposed): With the I10 rig about 4-6 months of analysis. Without it: 2 extra RealSense cameras (about USD 700), printed cuffs, the author plus 3-5 volunteers. Transplant is light; SAM 2 and optional harmonisation run offline on the 3080. Timeline: months 1-2 build and validate P2; months 2-3 natural sessions; months 4-5 all methods and statistics; month 6 write-up.

Risks (as proposed): If the ranking disagreement is small, the paper becomes a negative or confirmatory result; a top venue wants a clear effect plus a fix (P2 calibrated to match P3). Natural windows are few and correlated, so CIs may be wide. P2 may look like engineering unless the matching-to-natural-statistics step is principled.

### novelty skeptic: score 3.5, refuted=True

Search limitation: the session's web-search budget (200 calls) was already used up. All four queries I issued ("synthetic occlusion vs real occlusion benchmark ranking correlation pose estimation", "do synthetic corruption benchmarks predict real-world robustness rank correlation 2024 2025", "depth-consistent occluder copy-paste augmentation RGB-D object pose tracking occlusion benchmark", "real occlusion vs synthetic occlusion hand-object pose estimation evaluation 2025 benchmark") returned no results. So I could not confirm or rule out a 2023-2026 paper that runs exactly this study. Under the default-to-refute rule, that uncertainty counts against the idea. Every prior-work item below is therefore marked verified=false, including the five the idea lists: an earlier agent reportedly verified them, but no search in this session showed them.

Assessment from what is known.
(1) The core scientific question, whether synthetic corruption robustness predicts robustness under natural shift and whether it ranks methods the same way, is not new as a concept. Taori et al. (NeurIPS 2020) showed that robustness to synthetic shift largely fails to transfer to natural distribution shift. Hendrycks et al. (The Many Faces of Robustness, ICCV 2021) showed that methods help on some synthetic or rendition shifts and not others. Kar et al. (3D Common Corruptions, CVPR 2022) already argued for geometry- and depth-consistent corruptions as more realistic than 2D ones, which is the stated rationale for P2. In occlusion for pose specifically, Sarandi et al. (2018) pasted synthetic occluders for 3D human pose. Real-occlusion benchmarks such as 3DPW-OCC, 3DOH50K, OCHuman and VOccl3D (ICCV 2025) exist. So does 2504.10350, whose own summary already notes that methods doing well on synthetic patterns struggle with realistic durations. The idea's novelty therefore reduces to applying a known meta-question to one niche: temporal recovery of upper-body landmarks during bimanual object handling, ranked on metric ground truth.
(2) The proposed P2 generator (z-buffer composite of an occluder layer plus segmentation masks, with optional harmonisation) is close to standard depth-aware copy-paste augmentation. It is an engineering delta, not a method contribution.
(3) The evidence base is weak for a top venue. The motivating observation is n=2 natural windows from one subject. The natural windows will be few and correlated, so the confidence intervals on Kendall tau will be wide. The ranked methods include thesis-specific baselines (hold-last, direction memory, S2) that reviewers will not see as the community's methods.
(4) If agreement turns out high, the paper is a confirmatory or negative result. If it turns out low, it echoes Taori 2020 in a narrower domain.
Net: possibly publishable as a workshop or IJCV-style analysis paper, but the delta is too small for CVPR or NeurIPS D&B in its current form. Novelty score 3.5.

Prior work found:
- Measuring Robustness to Natural Distribution Shifts in Image Classification (Taori et al.) (NeurIPS 2020) https://arxiv.org/abs/2007.00644; overlap: Core meta-question: does robustness under synthetic corruption predict robustness, and model rankings, under natural shift. Finds that it largely does not. The idea's main claim is this question asked for occlusion in pose and object tracking. Pre-2023, but it sets the conceptual bar.; verified=False
- The Many Faces of Robustness: A Critical Analysis of Out-of-Distribution Generalization (Hendrycks et al.) (ICCV 2021) https://arxiv.org/abs/2006.16241; overlap: Compares synthetic and real distribution shifts and finds that method rankings are inconsistent across shift types. This is the same 'synthetic stress tests are not a valid proxy' thesis.; verified=False
- 3D Common Corruptions and Data Augmentation (Kar et al.) (CVPR 2022) https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.01441; overlap: Argues for geometry- and depth-consistent corruptions as more realistic than 2D ones, and builds them from depth. This is the rationale and mechanism behind the idea's P2 depth-consistent transplant.; verified=False
- Benchmarking 3D Human Pose Estimation Models under Occlusions (arXiv 2025 (2504.10350)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10350; overlap: Synthetic occlusion protocols with detector-behaviour keypoint noise for 3D pose. Reportedly notes that interpolation methods tuned on synthetic patterns degrade under realistic durations, which is the idea's hypothesis stated qualitatively.; verified=False
- VOccl3D: A Video Benchmark Dataset for 3D Human Pose and Shape Estimation under Real Occlusions (ICCV 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.06757; overlap: Explicitly motivated by the gap between synthetic and real occlusion in existing benchmarks. Provides more realistic occlusion video with ground truth, which is the 'more realistic occlusion data' half of the idea.; verified=False
- Improving the Robustness of 3D Human Pose Estimation: A Benchmark and Learning from Noisy Input (Human3.6M-C, HumanEva-I-C) (CVPR Workshops 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.06797; overlap: Temporal-occlusion corruption benchmark for 3D pose, the P1-style synthetic protocol the idea proposes to validate.; verified=False
- PoseBench: Benchmarking the Robustness of Pose Estimation Models under Corruptions (arXiv 2024 (2406.14367)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14367; overlap: A synthetic corruption suite including masking for pose. Same family of protocols, with no natural-occlusion validation.; verified=False
- How Robust is 3D Human Pose Estimation to Occlusion? (Sarandi et al.) (IROS Workshops 2018) https://arxiv.org/abs/1808.09316; overlap: Pasting synthetic occluders as a stress test and augmentation for 3D pose. This is the 2D-paste baseline of P2.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Drop the custom capture and the thesis-specific baselines. Run the validity study at scale on public multi-view hand-object datasets with metric ground truth (for example DexYCB, HO-Cap, ARCTIC, OakInk2). The key point: in one camera view, occlusion is natural, while the other views and the mocap or annotation pipeline supply clean ground truth. That gives hundreds of natural occlusion windows across many subjects and objects, for free. Define per-view natural occlusion events from the multi-view ground truth (visibility via rendered depth). Then generate matched P1 (landmark masking) and P2 (depth-consistent occluder transplant) events on the same frames. Rank 8-12 recognised methods: temporal 3D pose and hand-object methods, motion infilling (MotionBERT, RoHM, diffusion infill) and model-based trackers such as FoundationPose and BundleSDF for the object. The contributions would be: (a) a quantitative proxy-validity metric, with bootstrap confidence intervals, for occlusion stress tests in hand-object interaction; (b) the finding of which occlusion attributes (duration, occluder class, motion during the gap) cause ranking flips; (c) a calibrated synthetic generator, released as a benchmark, whose rankings match natural ones on held-out datasets. Target NeurIPS Datasets and Benchmarks. Keep the thesis's stale-grip-offset case only as a motivating example, not as the evidence base. Before committing, run a fresh literature search, which this check could not do, for 2024-2026 work on synthetic-vs-real occlusion rank agreement in hand-object or 6D pose benchmarks (BOP-related analyses in particular).

### feasibility skeptic: score 3, refuted=True

Feasibility verdict for a small lab (one D435, one RTX 3080, one subject's data, 12-18 months): the idea as written does not hold up. Its main measurement depends on ground truth the lab does not have, and the effect it wants to detect is smaller than the noise of the ground truth it can afford.

1) No metric ground truth during natural occlusion. The idea assumes "real RGB-D sequences with metric ground truth", but none exist in the assets. The thesis natural-occlusion evaluation (thesis Sec. 7.4.2, Tables 7.8/7.9) is 16 left-wrist and 4 right-wrist frames from one recording, clicked by a single annotator on a bracelet or watch. On clean frames that label sits 2.1-4.5 cm (median) and up to 5.5 cm from the wrist measurement. Frames where the feature was hidden were excluded, and click and depth uncertainty were never quantified. The synthetic numbers are not metric truth either: they are errors relative to the unmasked model reconstruction (Tables 7.6/7.7), and V3 E_occ is in degrees relative to a clean run. The "I10 rig with auxiliary views" belongs to a different candidate idea, and nobody has obtained it. The fallback (2 extra D435s plus wrist cuffs) still needs:
- hardware sync between the cameras;
- multi-camera extrinsic calibration;
- triangulated RTMPose wrists, with an expected error of roughly 1-3 cm.
In a desk task the hand that the desk or cube hides from the main view is often also hard to see from the side views.

2) The effect is smaller than the reference noise. On synthetic windows the recovery methods differ by 0.4-2.5 cm (Table 7.7: e.g. 0.4 vs 0.7 vs 1.2 cm median in the low-motion window). With a reference noise floor of 1-5 cm, Kendall tau between protocols will be low simply because the ground truth is noisy, not because the protocols disagree. The design has no noise ceiling (agreement of the reference with itself) to separate these two causes. The headline "1.7 cm vs 21 cm" gap compares only hold-last with object-conditioned recovery in one regime, not a ranking of 8 methods.

3) Statistical power. To rank 8 methods within duration bins and occluder classes, with mixed-effects regression on 4 covariates, you need many independent natural windows from several subjects. One subject plus 3-5 volunteers in one room gives a few dozen correlated windows. The risk section admits the CIs may be wide, but the plan has no fix for it beyond a power analysis.

4) Engineering outweighs the research question.
- P2 (depth-consistent occluder transplant on D435 depth) has to handle holes, flying pixels at occluder edges, the IR-pattern texture, and the cube marker being occluded consistently.
- Diffusion harmonisation of the RGB breaks consistency between RGB and depth.
- Validating P2 needs P3 ground truth, so P2 cannot rescue problem 1.
- The method zoo adds its own work: siMLPe on 8 landmarks, a MotionBERT-lite infill and RoHM (a body-model-based method) each need retraining or adaptation to this landmark set and a desk-seated domain, and no matching training data exists.

5) Novelty and venue risk. Whether synthetic corruptions predict robustness to natural shifts has a known precedent in classification (Taori et al., "Measuring Robustness to Natural Distribution Shifts in Image Classification", NeurIPS 2020, not verified in this session), where synthetic robustness largely did not transfer. So a confirmatory or negative finding here is expected rather than surprising, and the paper would need a strong fix (calibrated P2) to reach a top venue. NeurIPS D&B also expects a public, multi-subject dataset, which this lab cannot produce at scale.

6) The opening observation is weak. The documented synthetic-vs-natural disagreement rests on 4 and 16 hand-labelled frames from one recording, with selection bias. That is an anecdote, not a pilot effect size.

Minimum viable experiment that would show the idea works (about 2-3 months, no new hardware):
- Data: a public multi-view RGB-D hand-object dataset with 3D ground truth, as the idea already plans for replication. HO-Cap (RealSense multi-view, bimanual, hand and object pose) is the likely choice; DexYCB is the fallback. Use one view as the input camera and the dataset ground truth as metric truth.
- Natural windows: label occlusion of the input-view wrist automatically by testing ground-truth joint depth against the sensor depth (no manual clicks).
- P1 windows: duration-matched masks on visible segments.
- Methods: 5 cheap ones (hold-last, constant velocity, tangent Kalman, object-conditioned recovery from ground-truth and from estimated object pose, plus one small learned infill).
- Outputs: Kendall tau between P1 and natural rankings with window-level bootstrap, and a reference-noise ceiling from cross-view triangulation.
- Success criterion: tau clearly below the ceiling, and the gap explained by one measurable variable (for example duration or grip age).
Only if that holds is P2 and the lab capture worth building.

Prior-work verification: no web search returned results in this session (the search budget was exhausted), so every cited work is marked verified=false. The candidate's five closest works were reported as verified by an earlier stage of the pipeline.

Prior work found:
- Measuring Robustness to Natural Distribution Shifts in Image Classification (NeurIPS 2020); overlap: Already asks the core methodological question: whether robustness to synthetic corruptions predicts robustness under natural shifts. It found largely not, which pre-empts the 'nobody checked' novelty claim at the general level.; verified=False
- Benchmarking 3D Human Pose Estimation Models under Occlusions (arXiv 2025 (2504.10350)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10350; overlap: Synthetic, detector-like keypoint occlusion protocol. Notes that interpolation fails at realistic durations. This is the closest P1 analogue.; verified=False
- VOccl3D: A Video Benchmark Dataset for 3D Human Pose and Shape Estimation under Real Occlusions (ICCV 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.06757; overlap: Rendered realistic occluders with clean ground truth. Overlaps the P2 occluder-transplant idea.; verified=False
- Improving the Robustness of 3D Human Pose Estimation: A Benchmark and Learning from Noisy Input (CVPR Workshops 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.06797; overlap: Temporary-occlusion corruption benchmarks (Human3.6M-C, HumanEva-I-C) that the P1 replication would reuse.; verified=False
- HO-Cap: A Capture System and Dataset for 3D Reconstruction and Pose Tracking of Hand-Object Interaction (arXiv 2024); overlap: Multi-view RealSense RGB-D with hand and object pose ground truth, including bimanual handling. It could supply the natural-occlusion ground truth the lab lacks, which makes the custom capture unnecessary for a first study.; verified=False
- PoseBench: Benchmarking the Robustness of Pose Estimation Models under Corruptions (arXiv 2024 (2406.14367)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14367; overlap: Synthetic corruption protocols for pose estimation, but no check that they agree with natural occlusion.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Reframe as a public-data validity study first, with the lab capture as a small case study rather than the backbone.

Step 1 (3 months, existing hardware):
- On HO-Cap (fallback DexYCB), take a single input view as the observation and the multi-view ground truth as metric truth.
- Detect natural wrist-occlusion windows automatically with a depth test against ground truth.
- Generate duration-matched P1 masks on visible segments.
- Rank 5 cheap recovery methods: hold-last, constant velocity, tangent-space Kalman, object-conditioned recovery from ground-truth and from estimated object pose, and one small learned infill.
- Report Kendall tau with bootstrap CIs, plus a reference-noise ceiling.
- Regress the synthetic-minus-natural gap on duration, wrist excursion during the gap, and grip-offset age.
This targets a workshop paper (e.g. an ECCV or CVPR robustness or hand-object workshop).

Step 2 (only if tau is clearly below the ceiling and one variable explains the gap):
- Add a depth-consistent occluder transplant (z-buffer compositing, no diffusion) on the same public data.
- Fit its sampling of duration and motion to the measured natural statistics, and show it closes the ranking gap on held-out sequences.
- Use the thesis loop recording only as a qualitative real-desk example.
Drop the custom multi-camera capture, the RoHM/MotionBERT/siMLPe zoo, and NeurIPS D&B as a target unless step 1 gives a large effect.

### venue skeptic: score 3, refuted=True

Area-chair view, CVPR 2027 / NeurIPS D&B 2027. (1) The headline question has been asked before at a more general level. Image-classification robustness already showed that synthetic-corruption robustness does not predict robustness to natural shifts (Taori et al., NeurIPS 2020, and the effective-robustness work that followed), so reviewers will read "synthetic occlusion is not a valid proxy" as a domain port of a known result. It is not a new phenomenon. (2) The evidence base is far too thin for a benchmark-validity claim. The motivating disagreement comes from two natural-occlusion windows on one subject. The proposed capture (3-5 volunteers, one room, one slow desk task, a single D435 plus 2 auxiliary cameras) would give tens of correlated natural windows, so Kendall tau with bootstrap CIs over about 8 methods will almost certainly have intervals spanning most of [-1, 1]. The idea's own risk list concedes this. A D&B track rejects a validity study whose main statistic is not significant. (3) The ranked methods are mostly the author's own baselines: hold-last, direction memory, object-conditioned recovery, S2, tangent Kalman. Only siMLPe, MotionBERT-lite and RoHM are community methods. A rank-agreement study is persuasive only when the methods are the ones the field actually ranks, such as HaMeR or WiLoR for hands, FoundationPose or BundleSDF for objects, and WHAM, TRAM or RoHM for bodies, run on standard data. (4) Landmark-level masking of an 8-landmark upper-body kinematic solver is niche. CVPR reviewers will see a MediaPipe-plus-IK pipeline rather than a protocol that is standard in the field. (5) The constructive part, the P2 depth-consistent occluder transplant with a z-buffer rule, is close to copy-paste and occlusion augmentation work already used for hand-object and pose data, and to rendered-occluder benchmarks such as VOccl3D (ICCV 2025). Without a principled fitting step it reads as engineering. The "calibrate P2 to match P3 rankings" fix would be tuned on very few held-out natural windows, which invites overfitting critiques. (6) The public-replication plan (HO-Cap, Human3.6M-C) is the strongest part, but it is secondary in the plan. Expected outcome: clear reject at CVPR or NeurIPS D&B. A borderline paper at most at a workshop, 3DV, WACV or BMVC, or IEEE RA-L if reframed around tracking. The headline claim is unclear: it is either "proxies disagree" (known in general and underpowered here) or "our generator fixes it" (not established). Literature search was unavailable in this run (search budget exhausted), so prior work beyond the idea's own verified list is cited from memory and marked unverified.

Prior work found:
- Measuring Robustness to Natural Distribution Shifts in Image Classification (NeurIPS 2020); overlap: Establishes, at scale, that robustness to synthetic corruptions does not predict robustness to natural shifts; the proposed study is a domain-specific instance of this validity question; verified=False
- Benchmarking 3D Human Pose Estimation Models under Occlusions (arXiv 2025 (2504.10350)); overlap: Synthetic detector-behaviour occlusion protocol; notes that interpolation fails on realistic durations; verified=True
- VOccl3D: A Video Benchmark Dataset for 3D Human Pose and Shape Estimation under Real Occlusions (ICCV 2025); overlap: Rendered realistic occluders with clean ground truth, close to the P2 generator; verified=True
- PoseBench: Benchmarking the Robustness of Pose Estimation Models under Corruptions (arXiv 2024 (2406.14367)); overlap: Synthetic corruption benchmark for pose, including occlusion-like masks; verified=True
- Improving the Robustness of 3D Human Pose Estimation: A Benchmark and Learning from Noisy Input (CVPR Workshops 2024); overlap: Temporary-occlusion corruption protocol (Human3.6M-C); verified=True

Suggested reframe: Drop the custom capture and the author's own recovery baselines as the core. Reframe as a public-data validity study for hand-object and body tracking under occlusion. Use existing multi-view RGB-D datasets in which natural occlusion in the primary view can be labelled from the other views and ground truth is metric (DexYCB, HO-Cap, ARCTIC, HOT3D, and possibly 3DPW-OCC or VOccl3D on the body side). Rank 8-12 widely used published methods (for example HaMeR, WiLoR, FoundationPose, BundleSDF, WHAM, RoHM) under (i) keypoint masking, (ii) 2D paste occlusion and (iii) a depth-consistent occluder transplant, against natural occlusion on the same frames. Headline claim: a quantified ranking-inversion rate, with significant CIs, plus the occlusion properties that predict it (duration, occluder depth gap, motion during the gap). Offer the transplant generator as a drop-in protocol whose rankings match natural ones on held-out datasets, not held-out windows. Target the NeurIPS Datasets and Benchmarks track only if it covers several datasets and many methods. Otherwise target 3DV, WACV or BMVC. The thesis's two-window case becomes one motivating figure, not the evidence.

## I12: Provenance-aware human-to-robot data: per-joint honesty labels as observability weights in bimanual retargeting and as loss masks in policy learning from single-camera RGB-D demonstrations

Direction: Data engines for robot learning from human video that know which frames and which action dimensions are trustworthy, from retargeting through policy training (data quality at frame and joint level, not only episode level).

Research question: When bimanual robot policies are trained on actions retargeted from single-view RGB-D human demonstrations, does carrying the capture pipeline's per-frame, per-joint-group provenance (measured / held / constrained / object-conditioned) and posterior variance (a) into retargeting, weighting each human DOF by its observability, filling the rest from robot-side objectives and enforcing a closed chain through the held object during two-hand holds, and (b) into training, as an action-dimension mask, weight or conditioning input, raise kinematic feasibility, replay success and simulated / real task success compared with copy-all-angles or end-effector-only retargeting, uniform training, detector-confidence filtering and episode-level curation (DemInf, CUPID, SCIZOR), and how does the benefit scale with the occlusion rate of the demonstrations?

Why novel (as proposed): Demonstration curation scores whole demonstrations or state-action pairs from the learning side: DemInf (RSS 2025) mutual information, CUPID (CoRL 2025) influence functions, SCIZOR (2025) self-supervised outlier removal, all built for teleoperated data where actions are never unobserved. Human-video pipelines (Phantom CoRL 2025, Humanoid Policy ~ Human Policy CoRL 2025, What Matters When Cotraining on Everyday Human Videos 2026) show hand-pose quality governs transfer but treat estimates as equally valid or filter by thresholds. Object-aware retargeting (OKAMI CoRL 2024; OmniRetarget 2025; ObjRetarget 2026; WARP 2026; HL-IK 2025) assumes clean human pose and does not weight DOF by single-camera observability or treat a two-hand hold as a closed chain whose object pose is the most reliable measurement. The delta is a sensor-side, per-joint observability signal that already exists in this thesis and objectives that use it, plus a measured counter-argument to confidence filtering: RTMPose never abstains and occluded wrists receive higher detector scores (median 0.818 vs 0.789, V3 D-017). Retargeting closest-work risk is high; no searched paper propagates per-joint observability masks from human capture into BC loss.

Method (as proposed): Capture: third-person D435 demonstrations of desk bimanual tasks (pick, carry, self-handover, two-hand lift) through the thesis pipeline, giving torso pose, swing-twist angles, wrist points, metric object 6-DoF, grip episodes and per-joint-group provenance with geometric variance (object-conditioned wrist from marker noise and grip-offset age; IK elbow from along-axis depth). Retargeting: per frame min_q sum_g w_g(t) d_g(phi_g(q), phi_g^human(t))^2 + lambda_obj ||FK_L(q) G_L - T_obj||^2 + lambda_obj ||FK_R(q) G_R - T_obj||^2 (second term only in dual-hold) + manipulability, joint-limit margin, self-collision and SO(3) smoothness terms; phi_g are swing-twist quantities mapped through a scaled shoulder-elbow-wrist representation; w_g(t) from provenance and variance; output carries provenance per robot joint; QP or Gauss-Newton (Pinocchio or PyRoki, unverified). Actions: object-centric and wrist-centric end-effector targets (gripper pose = object pose x grip offset during grasp; pre-grasp orientation from an object-relative approach frame), gripper open/close from grip episodes, optional elbow swivel. Training: diffusion policy or ACT-style chunked policy with (a) hard masking of unobserved dimensions in the loss, (b) inverse-variance weighting of constrained dimensions, (c) provenance-conditioned variant. Corruption model fitted from the 28 recordings and S-type scenarios (outage durations, stale-grip drift, wrong-surface depth spikes, hip co-shift) applied to clean simulated expert data with ground-truth provenance, swept 0-40 percent corrupted frames. Real: 50-100 demonstrations per task retargeted to two low-cost arms.

Evaluation (as proposed): Simulation: RoboTwin 2.0 bimanual tasks (Handover Block, pick-and-place, stack) with fitted corruption; a humanoid upper-body model for generality; success over 100 seeds, action error on clean data, rate of reproduced artefacts (teleports, frozen twists). Real: two SO-101-class arms, 3 tasks x 20 trials; tasks where arm posture matters (clutter, reaching around the object, two-hand holds). Retargeting metrics: kinematic feasibility rate, object-relative gripper error, self-collision count, open-loop replay success, elbow swivel deviation on measured frames. Baselines: copy-all-angles, end-effector-only IK (OKAMI style), SEW representation (WARP style), interaction-mesh retargeting; uniform BC; drop episodes with any non-measured frame; detector-score filtering (expected to fail per D-017); DemInf- and CUPID-style curation; hold-last-filled demos. Ablations: no observability weights, no closed chain; mask vs weight vs conditioning; frame vs joint-group granularity; corruption-rate sweep; with vs without object-conditioned recovery.

Target venues: CoRL 2027, RSS 2027, IEEE RA-L with ICRA/IROS presentation, Humanoids

Builds on the thesis: Honesty flags per joint group, frame-exact and validated on v3 S-type scenarios (false_measured 0.000); object-conditioned recovery and closed-form elbow IK supply plausible but uncertain values with modellable uncertainty; the swing-twist model with singular-branch handling (oracle identity to 1e-9 deg); the metric world frame for object-centric actions; the documented depth-to-elbow failure along the optical axis; the 28 recordings and scenario tooling give a measured failure model; the detector-confidence anti-correlation is a ready motivating result.

Feasibility (as proposed): Have D435, RTX 3080 (enough for small diffusion policy / ACT and RoboTwin 2.0 data generation; RoboTwin on a 3080 not verified), pipeline, recordings, Unity. Must acquire a bimanual robot: two LeRobot SO-100/SO-101 class arms (a few hundred USD each, price not verified) or a borrowed ALOHA-class rig. A simulation-only version needs no purchase. Timeline: months 1-2 corruption model, retargeting solver and simulation sweep (publishable signal already); months 3-4 robot assembly and data collection; months 5-6 real experiments and writing. ICRA 2027 deadline has likely passed; RSS 2027, RA-L/IROS 2027, CoRL 2027 are realistic.

Risks (as proposed): Loss masking by confidence may be called obvious and retargeting incremental over OKAMI, OmniRetarget and ObjRetarget; the effect may be small at realistic corruption rates or invisible if tasks tolerate elbow posture; reviewers want real hardware, diverse objects and more than one demonstrator; the thesis wrist has no orientation and no fingers, limiting tasks to parallel grippers; the ArUco marker will be questioned (show a markerless tracker secondarily).

### novelty skeptic: score 3.5, refuted=True

Tool caveat: this session had already used up its web-search budget (200 of 200), and the egress proxy blocks arxiv.org and api.semanticscholar.org. So this verdict rests on prior knowledge (cutoff June 2026) plus the closest-work list already given in the candidate. Every citation below is marked verified=false because I saw no search result for it in this session. I could not run the four required queries. Per the brief's default, uncertainty counts against the idea.

Why the delta looks too small for CoRL or RSS:

(1) Mechanism (b), masking or weighting action dimensions in the behaviour-cloning loss, is standard machinery. Cross-embodiment generalist policies (Octo, RDT-1B, CrossFormer, pi0 with padded unified action spaces) already mask action dimensions that are missing or invalid in the loss. Heteroscedastic or inverse-variance weighting of regression targets is textbook. Older imperfect-demonstration work (2IWIL/IC-GAIL, ICML 2019) already weights demonstrations by confidence. Changing where the mask comes from (sensor provenance instead of embodiment padding) is an engineering choice, and reviewers will call it obvious. The candidate's own risk list says the same.

(2) Mechanism (a), observability-weighted inverse kinematics during retargeting, is also standard. In human pose fitting the data term has long been weighted by per-keypoint confidence (SMPLify, ECCV 2016, and its successors). Object-aware retargeting from a single video is covered by OKAMI (CoRL 2024), OmniRetarget (2025), ObjRetarget (2026) and WARP (2026). A dual-arm IK that closes the chain through a grasped object is classical in cooperative manipulation (decades of closed-chain dual-arm control). The only new part is the weight source, provenance labels instead of detector scores. Its main empirical support is one measured anti-correlation of RTMPose scores (0.818 vs 0.789 median) from one subject and one rig. That is a motivating observation, not a contribution.

(3) Data-side curation is crowded. DemInf (RSS 2025), CUPID (CoRL 2025) and SCIZOR (2025) work at episode or sample level. Human-video co-training papers (Phantom, CoRL 2025; HAT/PH2D, CoRL 2025; EgoMimic, ICRA 2025; EgoVLA and Being-H0, 2025; What Matters When Cotraining on Everyday Human Videos, 2026) already study pose-quality effects. Large human-hand datasets such as EgoDex (2025) reportedly ship per-joint tracking confidence; I did not verify this here. If they do, a confidence-weighted or confidence-masked baseline sits next to existing data. The claim that no paper propagates per-joint observability masks from capture into the BC loss could not be checked in this session. Even if it holds, the gap is narrow, and someone could fill it as a side ablation in a larger human-video paper.

(4) The evaluation leans on a synthetic corruption model fitted to 28 single-subject recordings, injected into clean simulated expert data. Reviewers will say the corruption model decides the outcome. Also, in RoboTwin-style tasks with parallel grippers and a wrist that has no orientation, elbow posture rarely matters, so the effect may disappear. The core asset, honesty flags, comes from a marker-based single-subject rig, and that does not scale to the human-video regime where the method would matter.

What is genuinely original is the system-level combination: provenance labels validated frame-exactly (false_measured 0.000), object-conditioned recovery with modellable variance, and the counter-evidence against detector confidence. That supports a solid RA-L, Humanoids or workshop paper, but not a top-venue novelty claim as currently framed.

Prior work found:
- CUPID: Curating Data your Robot Loves with Influence Functions (CoRL 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.19121; overlap: Curates demonstration data at sample or episode level by its effect on policy performance. Covers the data-quality-for-BC framing; it does not use per-joint sensor provenance.; verified=False
- SCIZOR: A Self-Supervised Approach to Data Curation for Large-Scale Imitation Learning (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22626; overlap: Removes low-quality state-action pairs at a finer granularity than episodes. It is a direct baseline for frame-level filtering.; verified=False
- DemInf: demonstration quality estimation via mutual information (RSS 2025); overlap: Scores demonstration quality from the learning side. It is the episode-level curation baseline the idea names.; verified=False
- Octo: An Open-Source Generalist Robot Policy (RSS 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.12213; overlap: Masks missing or invalid action and observation dimensions in training across heterogeneous datasets. This is the same loss-masking mechanism as the idea's variant (a).; verified=False
- RDT-1B: a Diffusion Foundation Model for Bimanual Manipulation (ICLR 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.07864; overlap: Unified bimanual action space with padding and masks for unavailable dimensions in the diffusion loss. Same masking machinery, applied to bimanual policies.; verified=False
- SMPLify: Keep it SMPL (and successors) (ECCV 2016); overlap: Weights each keypoint residual by detector confidence when fitting a body model. This is the standard confidence-weighted IK that observability-weighted retargeting extends.; verified=False
- OKAMI: Teaching Humanoid Robots Manipulation Skills through Single Video Imitation (CoRL 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.11792; overlap: Object-aware retargeting from a single RGB-D human video to a bimanual humanoid. It is the closest retargeting baseline; it does not weight DOFs by observability.; verified=False
- OmniRetarget: Interaction-Preserving Data Generation for Humanoid Whole-Body Loco-Manipulation and Scene Interaction (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.26633; overlap: Retargeting that preserves interactions (interaction mesh) with object and scene constraints, close to the closed-chain-through-object term.; verified=False
- ObjRetarget: An Object-Aware Motion Retargeting Framework with Anthropomorphic Arm Constraints and Polyhedral Hand Modeling (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03828; overlap: Object-aware arm retargeting with anthropomorphic constraints. Overlaps the retargeting half of the idea.; verified=False
- WARP: Whole-Body Retargeting for Learning from Offline Human Demonstrations (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29940; overlap: Shoulder-elbow-wrist (SEW) style retargeting of offline human demonstrations for policy learning. Overlaps the scaled SEW representation in the method.; verified=False
- Phantom: Training Robots Without Robots Using Only Human Videos (CoRL 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.00779; overlap: Turns human video into robot training data. Shows that hand-pose quality governs transfer, and filters or edits frames rather than weighting per joint.; verified=False
- Humanoid Policy ~ Human Policy (PH2D, HAT) (CoRL 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13441; overlap: Co-trains bimanual humanoid policies on retargeted human data. Human pose estimates are treated as equally valid.; verified=False
- EgoMimic: Scaling Imitation Learning via Egocentric Video (ICRA 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.24221; overlap: Co-trains with human hand data and handles noisy hand tracking through normalization and alignment. A closely related human-video bimanual pipeline.; verified=False
- EgoDex: Learning Dexterous Manipulation from Large-Scale Egocentric Video (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11709; overlap: Large human-manipulation dataset that reportedly ships per-joint tracking confidence. Weighting or masking by that confidence is an obvious existing baseline (confidence field not verified here).; verified=False
- What Matters When Cotraining Robot Manipulation Policies on Everyday Human Videos? (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06627; overlap: Empirical study of which human-video data properties matter for co-training. Any study of per-joint data quality would sit inside or next to it.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Drop "loss masking plus weighted IK" as the headline contribution, because reviewers will see it as standard machinery. Reposition as an empirical, falsifiable claim about human-video data: "Detector confidence is not observability: calibrated capture-side provenance predicts retargeting and policy failure from single-view human demonstrations, and detector scores do not." Concretely:

(1) Build a small benchmark of human bimanual demonstrations with ground-truth arm and wrist state. Use the thesis rig plus a second synchronized view or a cheap IMU as reference, with several subjects. Release per-frame, per-joint observability labels with it.

(2) Train a learned observability or abstention estimator. This is the thesis's own future-work item (3). It predicts per-joint error from markerless cues alone (landmark history, depth consistency, object proximity, view geometry), and its calibration is checked against the reference.

(3) Show that its uncertainty, applied to existing large human datasets (EgoDex, HOT3D, or the PH2D/HAT data), improves downstream co-training. Compare against detector-confidence weighting and against the CUPID, SCIZOR and DemInf curation baselines, all on the same policies.

Taken together, the contribution is a calibrated per-joint trust signal that transfers beyond the marker rig, plus a measured failure of detector confidence. That is harder to dismiss than a new loss mask. Keep closed-chain dual-hold retargeting as a secondary component evaluated on two-hand-hold tasks only. If hardware is limited, target RA-L or Humanoids for the system version and CoRL only with the multi-subject benchmark and the cross-dataset result.

### feasibility skeptic: score 3, refuted=True

Feasibility verdict: refuted as written. A smaller simulation-first version is feasible. Web search was not available for this pass because the search budget was used up. The judgement below rests on the dossier, the repository decision logs and the prior work the idea already cites.

(1) The planned real hardware cannot show the joint-level effect. The plan names two SO-100/SO-101-class arms. Each has 5 arm DOF plus a gripper, so it is not redundant: the end-effector target fixes the arm posture, and the elbow, swivel and twist have nothing left to choose. Per-joint observability weights for retargeting, elbow swivel deviation and arm-posture tasks only matter on redundant arms (7-DoF pairs, ALOHA-class arms with a 6-DoF wrist, humanoids). The idea does not say how it would get such arms. A borrowed ALOHA is mentioned but not arranged. The real-robot part therefore reduces to end-effector-only retargeting, which is the OKAMI-style baseline the idea wants to beat.

(2) The motivating evidence is misread. V3 D-017 (v3/V3_DECISIONS.md, line 420 onward) does not show that occluded wrists get higher detector scores. It shows that wrist samples flagged by a depth-front weak-label rule score higher (median 0.818 vs 0.789), and the same entry rejects that rule as invalid: it fired on 889 of 898 right-wrist frames because the subject reaches forward the whole time. So the numbers say nothing reliable about occlusion versus confidence, and the occlusion-gate thresholds are still unfitted. The counter-argument to confidence filtering is therefore not yet measured.

(3) The honesty flags are less validated than the idea assumes. false_measured 0.000 was measured on synthetic S-type scenarios where the landmarks were masked on purpose, so the pipeline knows what is missing by construction. The failure the thesis itself documents on real data is a landmark placed on the wrong body part with high confidence, or depth taken from the wrong surface. Those frames are labelled measured, so the flags do not catch them. A loss mask built from these flags misses exactly the hard errors, and the idea has no ground truth for how often that happens on natural occlusions (no motion capture, no labelled occlusion set, D-017 open).

(4) The "measured failure model" mostly does not exist yet. The 28 recordings come from one subject with no occlusion ground truth. Outage durations and S-type windows were designed (D-019), not measured. Stale-grip drift and hip co-shift each have one or two documented instances. Fitting a corruption model to sweep 0-40 percent is guesswork until labels exist.

(5) The simulation core is close to tautological. If you corrupt clean simulated expert actions and then give the policy the true corruption mask, masking will help almost by construction. Reviewers at CoRL or RSS will call that obvious, and the idea's own risk list says so. The non-obvious question is how much benefit survives imperfect provenance (missed flags, false flags) and correlated multi-frame corruption. The method as written does not foreground that question.

(6) The engineering load outweighs the research. One student in 12-18 months would need to build: a new task set captured with markers; a bimanual retargeting solver with closed-chain, collision and manipulability terms (Pinocchio or PyRoki integration not verified); RoboTwin 2.0 setup (not verified on an RTX 3080); a corruption model; diffusion or ACT training with three variants; robot assembly; real trials. The thesis wrist has no orientation and no fingers, so gripper orientation must be invented from an object-relative approach frame, which adds unvalidated provenance. The ArUco cube on every object limits which tasks are possible.

(7) Novelty pressure. The idea's own verified list (OKAMI, OmniRetarget, ObjRetarget, WARP, HL-IK) covers object-aware and human-like retargeting. CUPID and SCIZOR cover curation. Per-dimension loss masking for missing labels is standard practice in pose and trajectory learning. What remains new is narrow: sensor-side provenance fed into behaviour cloning.

Minimum viable experiment (about 3-4 months, simulation only, no purchase):
- Take clean scripted bimanual expert demonstrations from an existing simulator (RoboTwin 2.0 if it runs on the RTX 3080, otherwise an ALOHA sim task such as transfer-cube), on a redundant arm model.
- Inject structured, temporally correlated corruption into actions or proprioception: outages that hold the last value, slow drift (the stale grip offset), and depth spikes. Pair it with a provenance channel whose flag accuracy can be degraded on purpose, including measured-but-wrong frames with no flag.
- Train ACT or a small diffusion policy under five regimes: uniform; drop corrupted frames; drop whole episodes; per-dimension mask; inverse-variance weight.
- Report success over 100 seeds against corruption rate (0-40 percent) and against flag recall and precision.
- The idea is supported if per-dimension masking beats frame dropping and episode dropping at realistic corruption rates, and still helps at flag recall around 0.7. Otherwise it is falsified cheaply.
- Optional real check: replay retargeted end-effector trajectories open-loop from 20 thesis-pipeline demonstrations on one borrowed arm.

Prior work found:
- CUPID: Curating Data your Robot Loves with Influence Functions (CoRL 2025) https://arxiv.org/html/2506.19121; overlap: Learning-side curation at demonstration and state-action level; an episode-level baseline the idea must beat; it does not use sensor provenance.; verified=False
- SCIZOR: A Self-Supervised Approach to Data Curation for Large-Scale Imitation Learning (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.22626; overlap: Removes suboptimal or outlier state-action pairs, i.e. curation finer than whole episodes; partly overlaps frame-level filtering.; verified=False
- Phantom: Training Robots Without Robots Using Only Human Videos (CoRL 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.00779; overlap: Human-video-to-robot pipeline with end-effector actions; shows the end-effector-only route works without joint-level observability.; verified=False
- OKAMI: Teaching Humanoid Robots Manipulation Skills through Single Video Imitation (CoRL 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.11792; overlap: Object-aware retargeting from a single video; the end-effector-centric baseline that a non-redundant SO-101 setup collapses to.; verified=False
- WARP: Whole-Body Retargeting for Learning from Offline Human Demonstrations (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/html/2606.29940v1; overlap: Retargeting from offline human demonstrations with a shoulder-elbow-wrist representation; overlaps the retargeting half of the idea.; verified=False
- What Matters When Cotraining Robot Manipulation Policies on Everyday Human Videos? (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06627; overlap: Studies how hand-pose quality affects transfer from human video; the closest empirical framing of the data-quality question.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Recast it as a simulation-first study: "Learning from partially observed demonstrations: when does per-dimension provenance beat frame or episode curation in bimanual behaviour cloning, and how robust is it to imperfect flags?"

- Drop the real SO-101 experiments and the custom bimanual retargeting solver from the core contribution.
- Use clean simulated expert data on a redundant arm model.
- Add structured corruption with a flag channel whose recall and precision are controlled, including measured-but-wrong frames with no flag.
- Compare mask, weight and conditioning against frame dropping, episode dropping and CUPID-style curation.
- Use the thesis pipeline only for a secondary qualitative demonstration (provenance-labelled human captures replayed open-loop), not as the source of the corruption statistics.
- If the simulation shows a clear, flag-robust gain, then add a small real study on borrowed 6/7-DoF arms with 20 trials, and fix the D-017 claim with a manually labelled occlusion set before citing it.

Venue for the simulation-only version: an RA-L or ICRA workshop first. A full CoRL or RSS paper needs the real-hardware follow-up.

### venue skeptic: score 3, refuted=True

Area-chair verdict for CoRL/RSS 2027: likely reject in its current form. Five reasons.

1. No single headline claim. The idea bundles two contributions: (a) retargeting that weights each DOF by observability and adds a closed chain through the held object, and (b) behaviour-cloning losses that mask or weight action dimensions by provenance. Each is weak alone, and together they read as a pipeline paper. Reviewers will compare (a) with OKAMI, OmniRetarget, ObjRetarget and WARP and call it incremental. They will call (b) obvious. Per-dimension loss masks for missing or padded action dimensions are already routine in cross-embodiment policies (Octo, RDT-style unified action spaces). Inverse-variance weighting is textbook heteroscedastic regression. Down-weighting noisy samples has its own imitation-learning literature (2IWIL, DWBC). The novelty argument is that "no searched paper propagates per-joint masks into the BC loss". That is an absence-of-search argument, not a contribution reviewers will value.

2. The motivating result is misstated. The idea cites V3 D-017 as showing that occluded wrists get higher detector scores (0.818 vs 0.789). D-017 (v3/V3_DECISIONS.md, line 420 on) says something different. The "flagged" set came from a depth-front weak label that the project itself REJECTED. It fires on the right wrist in 889 of 898 frames because the subject reaches forward the whole task, and the text says the labels "anti-correlate with any plausible occlusion truth". So the comparison shows that the weak label is wrong, not that confidence is high under occlusion. A reviewer who checks the claim, or a co-author who runs it on real occlusion labels, could see the central counter-argument to confidence filtering disappear. The thesis's own evidence for that argument rests on one subject and a few windows.

3. The evaluation can produce its own result. The main evidence would be simulated clean expert data with corruption injected, where the ground-truth provenance is known by construction. In that setup, masking the corrupted dimensions helping is close to a tautology. Area chairs discount this design. The real-robot part is weak against 2025-2026 norms: 3 tasks x 20 trials, one demonstrator, one room, a single third-person D435, an ArUco cube, no wrist orientation and no fingers. Recent accepted human-video papers (Phantom, HAT/PH2D, EgoMimic-line work) use many demonstrators and diverse scenes, and the 2026 cotraining study uses everyday human videos. Next to them this looks small.

4. The method does not fit the planned hardware. The per-joint provenance is richest for shoulder and elbow. On SO-100/SO-101 arms (5 DOF plus a gripper) those angles carry little information: there is no elbow-swivel redundancy, and the end-effector pose fixes the arm configuration. The thesis wrist has no orientation, so the action that matters most, gripper orientation, is exactly the one the pipeline does not measure. It would have to come from a heuristic approach frame. The observability-weighting story only bites on redundant 7-DOF arms or humanoid upper bodies, and the plan does not budget for that hardware.

5. Generality and fit. The provenance labels (measured / held / constrained / object-conditioned) belong to this one pipeline. The idea does not show they can be produced for mainstream human-video stacks (HaMeR/WiLoR hands, egocentric Aria/EgoMimic data), so the community can neither adopt nor reproduce them. Without that, the contribution is a feature of a bespoke pipeline, not a data-engine principle.

Fit: CoRL/RSS is the right community, but this version would land as a weak reject or borderline paper at best. More realistic outlets are IEEE RA-L or Humanoids, or a CoRL workshop paper as a first step. Expected acceptance quality at the best-fitting top venue is about 3/10.

Note: the web-search budget was used up in this session, so no new searches could be run. Prior-work entries not checked here are marked verified=false.

Prior work found:
- CUPID: Curating Data your Robot Loves with Influence Functions (CoRL 2025) https://arxiv.org/html/2506.19121; overlap: Learning-side data curation for imitation learning at demonstration and state-action level. A natural baseline; reviewers will say provenance weighting is a hand-crafted special case of curation.; verified=True
- SCIZOR: A Self-Supervised Approach to Data Curation for Large-Scale Imitation Learning (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.22626; overlap: Removes suboptimal or redundant state-action pairs. Same goal of frame-level data quality, without sensor-side labels.; verified=True
- DemInf: demonstration quality via mutual information (RSS 2025 (per earlier search summary; page not opened)); overlap: Episode-level quality scoring for curation, used as a baseline.; verified=False
- Phantom: Training Robots Without Robots Using Only Human Videos (CoRL 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.00779; overlap: Human-video-to-robot pipeline accepted at the target venue. Sets the bar for scale and real-robot evaluation that this idea's 3x20-trial plan does not reach.; verified=True
- Humanoid Policy ~ Human Policy (PH2D, HAT) (CoRL 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13441; overlap: Co-trains human and humanoid data in a unified action space, with many demonstrators. The data-scale norm reviewers will apply.; verified=True
- What Matters When Cotraining Robot Manipulation Policies on Everyday Human Videos? (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06627; overlap: Studies how hand-pose quality affects transfer. Overlaps the claim that estimate quality matters for downstream policies.; verified=True
- OKAMI: Teaching Humanoid Robots Manipulation Skills through Single Video Imitation (CoRL 2024) https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.11792; overlap: Object-aware retargeting from a single RGB-D video. The direct comparison for part (a).; verified=True
- OmniRetarget: Interaction-Preserving Data Generation for Humanoid Whole-Body Loco-Manipulation and Scene Interaction (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.26633; overlap: Interaction-mesh retargeting that preserves human-object contact. Makes the closed-chain-through-object term look incremental.; verified=True
- ObjRetarget: An Object-Aware Motion Retargeting Framework with Anthropomorphic Arm Constraints (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03828; overlap: Object-aware retargeting with arm constraints. Close to the retargeting contribution.; verified=True
- WARP: Whole-Body Retargeting for Learning from Offline Human Demonstrations (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/html/2606.29940v1; overlap: Shoulder-elbow-wrist representation for retargeting offline human data to policies. Overlaps the SEW-based retargeting step.; verified=True
- Octo: An Open-Source Generalist Robot Policy (action-dimension masking across embodiments) (RSS 2024); overlap: Masks padded or missing action dimensions in the training loss. Undercuts novelty of hard provenance masking.; verified=False
- Discriminator-Weighted Offline Imitation Learning from Suboptimal Demonstrations (DWBC) (ICML 2022); overlap: Sample weighting of imperfect demonstrations in BC. Classic precedent for weighting by data quality.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Make one falsifiable claim instead of a pipeline. The best option: "Detector confidence is not observability: geometric observability predicts retargeting and policy error from single-view human demonstrations, and confidence does not."

Step 1. Build the measurement properly. Record a small multi-view or mocap-referenced bimanual dataset with several demonstrators and deliberate occlusions (even 2-3 extra cameras for ground truth). Show, with real occlusion labels, how RTMPose, MediaPipe and HaMeR/WiLoR confidence relates to per-joint error. Do not reuse the D-017 numbers: that label was rejected and does not support the claim as stated.

Step 2. Define a pipeline-agnostic observability score that any human-video stack can compute: depth along the optical axis, self-occlusion against the reconstructed body, and object-conditioned consistency. Show that it predicts per-joint error and retargeting infeasibility.

Step 3. Show one downstream effect where posture matters: a redundant 7-DOF or humanoid upper body (in simulation if necessary) on two-hand holds, comparing against uniform training, confidence filtering and CUPID-style curation. Report the effect at measured, not injected, corruption rates.

If the robot study is dropped, an IEEE RA-L paper or a CVPR/ICCV workshop paper on observability-aware human capture for robot learning is the honest target. Avoid SO-101 as the main platform: its 5 DOF make elbow observability irrelevant.

## I13: Learning self-handover from single-camera human demonstrations: metric object tracks, contact-event grip re-estimation and torso-relative transfer locations for dual-arm robots

Direction: Bimanual coordination learned from human demonstrations, specifically intra-person (self) handover and role switching between arms.

Research question: Can a dual-arm robot learn where and when to transfer an object between its own grippers, and how to re-grasp it, from a handful of third-person RGB-D human demonstrations, if each demonstration is decomposed into an object-centric metric trajectory, per-hand role phases (single hold, dual hold, release) and grip offsets re-estimated at contact events, and does this beat end-effector imitation that ignores the handover structure?

Why novel (as proposed): A Taxonomy of Self-Handover (2025) characterises six human self-handover patterns from cooking video and classifies them with a VLM without robot transfer. BUDS (CoRL 2023) assigns stabilising and acting roles, not transfer between hands. YOTO (2025) and DemoBot (2026, single D455 RGB-D video) learn bimanual skills from one human video without modelling the handover event, grip transfer or transfer location relative to the body. Human-to-robot handover work concerns a human giving to a robot. RoboTwin 2.0 has a Handover Block task but scripted experts; MonoDuo (2026) uses one arm to learn bimanual policies. The delta is a demonstration representation and learning method for self-handover from cheap human capture, turning the thesis's known failure (grip offset goes stale exactly around handovers) into an explicit contact-event model.

Method (as proposed): Segmentation: from the metric object track and per-hand wrist-to-object distance (thesis grip episodes) detect phases single-hold-L, dual-hold, single-hold-R and the transfer event; re-estimate each hand's grip offset in the object frame at the start of every hold phase from frames where that hand is measured (fixing the stale-offset failure; can reuse the I01 mode inference). Representation: object trajectory in the desk frame, transfer pose in the torso frame (body-relative handover location), per-hand object-frame grasp poses, timing. Robot side: map torso frame to the robot mid-base frame; choose the robot transfer pose by optimising dual-arm reachability and manipulability near the human torso-relative pose; plan giver and receiver as a closed chain during dual-hold. Learning: (a) keypose-plus-planner where a small network predicts transfer and grasp poses from object start and goal, or (b) a chunked diffusion policy on retargeted trajectories with an auxiliary phase head; MimicGen-style replay to multiply demos (not searched).

Evaluation (as proposed): Simulation: RoboTwin 2.0 Handover Block and a custom cube-transfer task with start and goal on opposite sides so a transfer is necessary; real: two low-cost arms with the thesis cube, rail and loop tasks re-staged. Metrics: success, transfer-event success (no drop, both grippers in contact during dual-hold), object pose error at goal, number of demos needed. Baselines: end-effector replay with fixed grip offset (thesis v1 behaviour), YOTO-style diffusion policy without phase structure, scripted RoboTwin expert, single-arm pick-and-place where reachable. Ablations: stale vs re-estimated grip offset; torso-relative vs world-fixed transfer location; with/without honesty masking of occluded transfer frames.

Target venues: ICRA, IEEE RA-L, CoRL

Builds on the thesis: The loop recording already contains two self-handovers with metric object poses; the grip-episode and grip-offset machinery and its documented failure (stale offset, Chapter 5 and Table 9.1) map onto the contact-event model; the torso frame gives a body-relative transfer location egocentric pipelines lack; honesty flags mark trustworthy transfer frames.

Feasibility (as proposed): Same robot purchase as I12 (two SO-101-class arms or a borrowed ALOHA-class rig); RoboTwin 2.0 on one consumer GPU (assumption, not verified on an RTX 3080). Record 20-40 self-handover demos with 3-4 marked objects. Timeline: 1 month segmentation on existing recordings, 1-2 months simulation, 2 months real robot, 1 month writing.

Risks (as proposed): The task family may look narrow (cube-sized rigid objects, parallel grippers) and reviewers may ask why a handover is needed when one arm could reach; the human transfer location may not transfer to a robot with a very different workspace, weakening the claim that human demos matter beyond object goals.

### novelty skeptic: score 3, refuted=True

Search situation: the session's WebSearch budget was used up (200 of 200), and WebFetch to arxiv.org, export.arxiv.org and api.semanticscholar.org was blocked by the egress proxy. No new search ran in this pass, so every work below is listed from model knowledge with verified=false. The six closest works in the candidate JSON were marked verified by an earlier pass. I could not re-check them here.

Why the delta is too small for CoRL, and borderline for ICRA or RA-L:

(1) Handover between two robot arms is already a standard learned bimanual task. The ACT/ALOHA paper (RSS 2023) has the simulated "Transfer Cube" task: one arm picks up a cube and hands it to the other, and the policy learns this from demonstrations. robosuite ships a TwoArmHandover environment. DexMimicGen (ICRA 2025) multiplies bimanual demonstrations, including tasks that need a transfer between arms. RoboTwin 2.0 has Handover Block. The proposed custom cube-transfer task and its success metric are therefore toy versions of existing benchmarks. Reviewers will see "learn a handover policy" as solved by an unstructured ACT or diffusion policy with enough demonstrations.

(2) Splitting human demonstrations at contact events (grasp, hold, release) and re-estimating the hand-object grasp per segment is the core of Learning-from-Observation. Wake, Ikeuchi et al. at Microsoft built one-shot grasp-manipulation-release teaching frameworks (2021-2023) and GPT-4V task planning from human video (2023-2024). The same group wrote "A Taxonomy of Self-Handover" (2025) and states robot self-handover as its motivation. A robot follow-up from that group is the most likely scoop, and I could not check for one. Contact-segmented, object-centric bimanual imitation from human video already exists: Bi-KVIL (keypoint visual imitation of bimanual tasks with coordination categories, ICRA 2024), the KIT bimanual taxonomy and segmentation work (Krebs and Asfour, RA-L 2022), ScrewMimic (RSS 2024), YOTO (2025) and DemoBot (2026, single RGB-D video). Adding a handover phase label to these pipelines is an incremental change.

(3) Choosing where a dual-arm robot regrasps or hands over is classical planning. Examples: Vahrenkamp et al., dual-arm re-grasping (IROS 2009), and Wan and Harada, dual-arm regrasp and handover planning (2016-2019). The proposed method picks the robot transfer pose "by optimising dual-arm reachability and manipulability near the human pose". The optimiser therefore does most of the work, and the human torso-relative prior is at best a regulariser. This conflicts with the claim that human demonstrations matter. The candidate's own risk section already names this weakness.

(4) The "stale grip offset" fix (re-estimating the grip at each hold phase) solves a problem in the thesis's own tracker. It is not a robot-learning contribution, and roboticists who use marker or FoundationPose object tracking would not count it as novel.

(5) Evidence scale: one RGB-D camera, ArUco-marked cubes, 20-40 demonstrations, low-cost SO-101 arms with parallel grippers, and short rigid-object transfers. Top venues would expect many objects, dexterous or orientation-changing handovers, or a scaling result.

Net: every component (handover task, contact-event segmentation, object-centric retargeting, reachability-based transfer pose, MimicGen-style augmentation) has published precedent. Novelty rests only on combining them for self-handover from third-person capture, plus a body-relative transfer prior that the method's own optimiser undercuts.

Prior work found:
- Learning Fine-Grained Bimanual Manipulation with Low-Cost Hardware (ACT / ALOHA), simulated Transfer Cube task (RSS 2023) https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.13705; overlap: Its simulated Transfer Cube task is a learned handover between two arms. A chunked policy learns it from demonstrations with no explicit phase or handover model, the same task the idea proposes as a custom benchmark.; verified=False
- DexMimicGen: Automated Data Generation for Bimanual Dexterous Manipulation via Imitation Learning (ICRA 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.24185; overlap: Splits bimanual demonstrations into per-arm subtasks with coordination and sequential constraints and multiplies them in simulation, including tasks that need a transfer between arms. This covers the idea's MimicGen-style replay and per-arm phase structure.; verified=False
- A Taxonomy of Self-Handover (Wake et al., Microsoft) (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04939; overlap: Characterises human self-handover patterns from video and classifies them with a VLM, explicitly to motivate robot self-handover. Its group already has Learning-from-Observation robot-teaching pipelines, so a robot follow-up is the most likely scoop.; verified=False
- A Learning-from-Observation Framework: One-Shot Robot Teaching for Grasp-Manipulation-Release Household Operations (Wake, Ikeuchi et al.) (IEEE/SICE SII 2021 (and later LfO plus GPT-4V work, 2023-2024)); overlap: Splits human demonstrations at grasp and release contact events and re-estimates each segment's grasp for robot execution. This is the idea's contact-event grip re-estimation step.; verified=False
- Bi-KVIL: Keypoints-based Visual Imitation Learning of Bimanual Manipulation Tasks (ICRA 2024); overlap: Learns bimanual coordination categories and object-centric keypoint constraints from a few human demonstration videos and transfers them to a dual-arm robot. It covers the idea's role and phase-aware object-centric imitation, though it has no explicit handover event.; verified=False
- You Only Teach Once: One-Shot Bimanual Robotic Manipulation from Video Demonstrations (YOTO) (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/html/2501.14208v1; overlap: Extracts hand and object motion from a human video and turns it into bimanual keyposes and a diffusion policy with demonstration augmentation. This is the idea's learning pipeline without a handover label.; verified=False
- DemoBot: Efficient Learning of Bimanual Manipulation with Dexterous Hands From Third-Person Human Videos (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.01651; overlap: Same capture setup as the idea (a single third-person RGB-D video) and the same goal of transferring bimanual skills to a robot, with richer hands.; verified=False
- ScrewMimic: Bimanual Imitation from Human Videos with Screw Space Projection (RSS 2024); overlap: Bimanual imitation from human video through an object-relative structured representation, with a structure prior in place of end-effector replay. This is the same argument the idea makes against unstructured imitation.; verified=False
- Dual-arm regrasp / handover planning (Wan and Harada; Vahrenkamp et al., Humanoid motion planning for dual-arm manipulation and re-grasping tasks) (IROS 2009; ICRA/IROS/T-ASE 2016-2019); overlap: Chooses the handover pose and grasp pair from reachability and grasp feasibility. This is the idea's robot-side optimiser, and it makes the human torso-relative prior hard to justify.; verified=False
- Dynamic Handover: Throw and Catch with Bimanual Hands (CoRL 2023); overlap: Learned transfer of an object between a robot's own two hands (by RL). Learned intra-robot handover already appears at CoRL.; verified=False
- RoboTwin 2.0 (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.18088; overlap: Already includes the Handover Block task and scripted experts that the idea plans to use as its benchmark and baseline.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Drop "learn self-handover" as the headline. Unstructured ACT or diffusion policies already do cube transfer, and reachability planners already choose transfer poses. One of two narrower questions might survive.

(A) Handover as regrasp for reorientation. Humans hand an object across mainly to change the grasp, so that the goal orientation or a tool-use grasp becomes reachable. Learn from human demonstrations a distribution over (giver grasp, receiver grasp, transfer pose) conditioned on the object's start and goal orientation. Test it on tasks where a single arm or an unstructured policy fails: tool reorientation, insertion with a required final grasp, long objects.
