Compare against grasp-pair planning (Wan and Harada style) and against ACT or DexMimicGen policies trained on equal data. The novelty claim is that human handover choices encode task-relevant grasp-switch priors that a planner lacks. It needs a result where planner-only and unstructured baselines measurably lose.

(B) A perception and dataset paper. Release a metric single-RGB-D benchmark of human self-handovers (many objects, several subjects, contact-event and grip labels, honesty flags), with a contact-event-aware hand-object tracker that keeps object pose and grip consistent through transfers under occlusion. Evaluate against FoundationPose or HOI trackers, and show downstream that the YOTO or DemoBot retargeting failure rate drops around handovers. Target RA-L or an ICRA dataset track, not CoRL.

Before investing, check for 2025-2026 robot follow-ups from the Wake et al. Microsoft group and for DexMimicGen or RoboTwin handover-specific methods. A direct search was not possible in this pass.

### feasibility skeptic: score 3.5, refuted=True

Feasibility verdict: refuted as specified (full sim-plus-real study, two learning variants, five baselines and three ablations, aimed at ICRA/CoRL, for one student in 12-18 months), with a smaller version possibly viable. Note: the web search budget for this session was exhausted (both searches returned "budget used"), so no prior work was re-verified here; all citations below are marked verified=false even though the idea's own JSON lists them as verified.

1) The demonstration side cannot supply the representation the method needs. The dossier and thesis state that the wrist is a tracked point without orientation and that fingers are not modelled. The thesis's grip offset is a 3-D wrist-to-object translation, and the thesis says (Section 7.3, near line 4020 of thesis_v9.txt) that one scalar distance "constrains neither the direction of that offset nor its behaviour from frame to frame". So "per-hand object-frame grasp poses" (6-DoF) cannot be extracted with the existing codebase. Doing so needs a 3-D hand-pose estimator (a MANO-type model) added to the pipeline and validated, and the idea neither mentions nor budgets that. Gripper approach direction, which decides whether a parallel-jaw handover succeeds, therefore comes from a heuristic and not from the human.

2) The thesis's signature failure does not carry over to a robot benefit. The stale grip offset is a perception problem: the human wrist is inferred while it is occluded. A robot knows its own gripper pose exactly and grasps fresh every time. Re-estimating the offset at contact events only improves the derived human hand trajectories, which matter only for the end-effector-replay baseline. The ablation "stale vs re-estimated grip offset" would therefore mostly show that a strawman baseline gets better, not that the proposed method works.

3) The data does not exist yet, and the existing data is tiny. The archive holds one subject. Only the loop recording (two handovers) and the handover-on-rail recording (one transfer) contain self-handovers, i.e. about three events. Handovers are also exactly where the thesis reports occlusion of the cube and hands and the weakest recovery. 20-40 new demos with 3-4 marked objects is cheap to record, but the honesty masking will flag many transfer frames, so the transfer pose itself may be poorly observed from one D435.

4) Robot access is stated but under-specified, and the engineering risk dominates. Two SO-101-class arms are cheap. However, a 5-DoF arm has limited dexterity for mid-air transfers, and the "reachability and manipulability optimisation" plus "closed-chain dual-hold planning" are substantial engineering on hardware with weak controllers. The project would also need camera-to-robot calibration, a teleop or scripted data path for the diffusion-policy variant, and a retargeting pipeline. None of this exists in the codebase (v1/v2/v3 are human-tracking only). For a student who has never built a robot stack, this is most of the project, and the research question gets squeezed.

5) The simulation story is incoherent. RoboTwin 2.0's Handover Block uses scripted experts. Human RGB-D demos do not map into simulation without a real-to-sim object-trajectory step, and RoboTwin on an RTX 3080 is unverified (the idea itself labels this an assumption).

6) The evaluation ground truth is fine for the robot (task success is self-evident). It is weak for the central scientific claim: that a torso-relative human transfer location helps beyond object start and goal. With one subject and a robot whose workspace differs from a human's, a null result is likely, and the idea's own risks section concedes this. Reviewers will also ask why a handover is needed at all when a single arm could reach.

Minimum viable experiment (about 3-4 months, possible before buying hardware):
(a) Record about 20 self-handover demos of the marked cube with start and goal placed beyond one-arm reach. Segment phases (single-L, dual, single-R) from the existing grip-episode state machine. Validate the segmentation against manually labelled transfer frames; that ground truth is cheap.
(b) Extract object keyposes plus a torso-relative transfer point.
(c) In one simulator with a stock dual-arm model, drive a keypose-plus-motion-planner with grasps chosen by a simple object-frame grasp sampler. Compare transfer location from demos (torso-relative) against world-fixed against a midpoint heuristic, reporting success over 50+ randomised trials per condition.
If torso-relative human transfer locations do not beat the midpoint heuristic there, the full project should not proceed.

Prior work found:
- A Taxonomy of Self-Handover (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04939; overlap: Characterises human self-handover patterns from video and classifies them with a VLM; no robot transfer. It defines the phenomenon this idea would exploit.; verified=False
- DemoBot: Efficient Learning of Bimanual Manipulation with Dexterous Hands From Third-Person Human Videos (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.01651; overlap: Bimanual robot skills from a single third-person RGB-D (D455) human video. Same capture modality, so the novelty rests only on explicit handover-event modelling.; verified=False
- You Only Teach Once: Learn One-Shot Bimanual Robotic Manipulation from Video Demonstrations (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/html/2501.14208v1; overlap: One-shot bimanual imitation from human video with hand-motion extraction and demo augmentation. It is the natural strong baseline, and it already handles hand pose, which the thesis pipeline lacks.; verified=False
- Stabilize to Act: Learning to Coordinate for Bimanual Manipulation (BUDS) (CoRL 2023) https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.01087; overlap: Role assignment between the two arms (stabilising vs acting), learned from demonstrations; it covers the role-structure part of the idea.; verified=False
- RoboTwin 2.0: A Scalable Data Generator and Benchmark with Strong Domain Randomization for Robust Bimanual Robotic Manipulation (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.18088; overlap: Contains a handover task with scripted experts. It is the proposed simulation benchmark; that it runs on an RTX 3080 is not established.; verified=False
- MonoDuo: Using One Robot Arm to Learn Bimanual Policies (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29298; overlap: Low-cost route to bimanual policies. It competes with the claim that cheap human capture is the right data source.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Shrink it to a perception-to-planning paper on phase-segmented, object-centric self-handover demonstrations and drop end-to-end policy learning. Contribution 1: contact-event segmentation of single-camera RGB-D demos into hold, dual-hold and release phases, with honesty-aware confidence on the transfer frames. Validate it against manually labelled transfer events over 20-40 new recordings, possibly with a second subject. Contribution 2: a keypose-plus-planner that places the robot's transfer pose from the torso-relative human transfer location under dual-arm reachability. Test it in one simulator against world-fixed and midpoint heuristics, and only then on two SO-101 arms with the thesis cube. Leave out the diffusion-policy variant, the MimicGen augmentation and the claim of learning grasp poses (the pipeline has no hand orientation); use an object-frame grasp sampler instead. Target RA-L or an ICRA/CoRL workshop first. Gate the project on the simulation result: if human-derived transfer locations do not beat the midpoint heuristic, pivot to the segmentation and perception contribution alone.

### venue skeptic: score 3, refuted=True

An area chair at ICRA, RA-L or CoRL would most likely see this as an incremental systems paper on a narrow task. Main concerns:

(1) The headline claim is weak. Self-handover of a cube between two parallel grippers is already a standard solved benchmark. "Transfer Cube" in the ALOHA/ACT simulation suite (RSS 2023) is exactly this task, learned from about 50 scripted or teleoperated demos with high success. RoboTwin 2.0 also has a handover task. A custom "cube-transfer with start and goal on opposite sides" task re-creates an existing benchmark. Reviewers will ask why human video is needed when 50 teleop demos on a low-cost ALOHA/SO-101 rig already solve it. The proposal names no case where teleop fails and human demos win.

(2) The novel ingredient is small. "Re-estimate the grip offset at contact events" is a fix for the thesis's own tracking failure (a stale offset), not a new learning idea. To a robot-learning reviewer, phase segmentation by wrist-object distance plus keypose-plus-planner is a standard pipeline. Prior work already uses keypose or phase decomposition for bimanual imitation from human video: BUDS, YOTO, DemoBot, and keypoint-based bimanual visual imitation such as Bi-KVIL. That leaves the handover-specific parts: body-relative transfer location and grasp-switching.

(3) The torso-relative transfer location is the one distinctive scientific claim, and the proposal itself admits it may not survive retargeting to a robot with a different workspace. It is then overridden by a reachability/manipulability optimisation. If the optimiser decides the transfer pose, the human's transfer location contributes little. The ablation "torso-relative vs world-fixed" will probably show a small or null effect on a fixed-base dual-arm robot. That undercuts the paper's reason to exist.

(4) The evaluation is not credible at top-venue level. The human data comes from one subject, one D435 viewpoint and 20-40 demos of 3-4 marker-tagged cube-sized objects. ArUco markers on objects make it a lab-instrumented capture, not "cheap human capture" in the in-the-wild sense that CoRL now expects. Recent human-video papers (EgoMimic, Phantom, DemoBot, YOTO) use markerless capture and many tasks.

The baselines are also weak or strawman:
- v1 fixed-offset replay is the author's own broken system.
- "YOTO-style without phase structure" is a re-implementation.
- The strongest standard baseline is missing: ACT or Diffusion Policy trained on an equal time budget of teleop demos on the same robot.
- There is no comparison against a policy with no handover at all, using a mobile base or a wider reach, which reviewers will raise ("why not one arm").

(5) The thesis assets help less than claimed. The existing data holds only two self-handovers, the grip machinery fails exactly at handover, and there is no robot yet. The 6-month plan includes a hardware purchase and real-robot experiments on an unfamiliar platform, so a thin result is likely.

Venue fit: not CoRL (too narrow, with little learning contribution). ICRA is possible as a workshop or short systems paper. The best realistic fit is IEEE RA-L with an ICRA/IROS option, or Humanoids, and only with a broader task family. Expected acceptance quality at the best-fitting venue (RA-L/IROS) is about 3-4/10 as written; at CoRL, about 1-2/10.

Search note: the web search budget was exhausted in this run, so no new searches were possible. Prior-work entries are marked verified=false, including those the idea's generator had marked as verified.

Prior work found:
- Learning Fine-Grained Bimanual Manipulation with Low-Cost Hardware (ALOHA / ACT), includes the simulated Transfer Cube handover task (RSS 2023) https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.13705; overlap: Its standard simulation benchmark is the same bimanual cube handover task the proposal plans to build. ACT solves it from about 50 demos, so it is the obvious baseline and makes the custom task non-novel.; verified=False
- RoboTwin 2.0: A Scalable Data Generator and Benchmark with Strong Domain Randomization for Robust Bimanual Robotic Manipulation (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.18088; overlap: Already has a handover task with an expert data generator, so a simulation handover result adds little.; verified=False
- A Taxonomy of Self-Handover (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04939; overlap: Characterises human self-handover patterns from video. The proposal's human-side analysis partly overlaps it.; verified=False
- You Only Teach Once: Learn One-Shot Bimanual Robotic Manipulation from Video Demonstrations (YOTO) (arXiv 2025) https://arxiv.org/html/2501.14208v1; overlap: Learns bimanual skills from human video using keyposes and augmentation. Adding phase/handover labels to it looks incremental.; verified=False
- DemoBot: Efficient Learning of Bimanual Manipulation with Dexterous Hands From Third-Person Human Videos (arXiv 2026) https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.01651; overlap: Learns bimanual skills from third-person RGB-D human video with a RealSense camera. This is the closest pipeline, and it is markerless.; verified=False
- Stabilize to Act: Learning to Coordinate for Bimanual Manipulation (BUDS) (CoRL 2023) https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.01087; overlap: Decomposes bimanual tasks into per-arm roles. The role-phase decomposition is an established idea.; verified=False
- Bi-KVIL: Keypoints-based Visual Imitation Learning of Bimanual Manipulation Tasks (ICRA 2024 (from memory, not searched)); overlap: Object-centric, keypoint-based bimanual imitation from a few human demos. It overlaps the object-centric representation claim.; verified=False
- Dynamic Handover: Throw and Catch with Bimanual Hands (CoRL 2023 (from memory, not searched)); overlap: Robot learns transfer between its own two hands, by RL rather than human demos. It shows that intra-robot handover itself is not new.; verified=False
- EgoMimic: Scaling Imitation Learning via Egocentric Video (arXiv 2024 / ICRA 2025 (from memory, not searched)); overlap: Sets the current bar for human-video-to-bimanual-robot transfer: markerless, many demos, co-training. The proposal's marker-instrumented single-subject capture falls below it.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Drop "self-handover of a cube" as the headline, because ALOHA Transfer Cube already covers it. Reframe the work as grasp-switching events in human-video imitation: regrasp, handover, re-grip and hand-to-hand role swaps in long-horizon tasks.

Headline claim: existing human-video-to-robot pipelines (YOTO, DemoBot, Phantom-style retargeting) assume a fixed hand-object relation per segment, and they fail on tasks whose grasp changes mid-trajectory. A contact-event model that re-estimates per-hand object-frame grasps at each switch fixes this, measured on a benchmark of grasp-switching tasks.

To be credible, the paper needs the following:
(a) Markerless object pose from a foundation pose model, for example FoundationPose-class, instead of ArUco. Keep the thesis's honesty flags as a confidence gate around occluded contact events.
(b) At least 5 subjects and at least 6 tasks where transfer or regrasp is necessary: long objects, tool reorientation, and a workspace split by an obstacle.
(c) A head-to-head against ACT/Diffusion Policy trained on equal-time teleop, and against YOTO/DemoBot-style pipelines, reporting where human video plus event modelling wins: fewer robot-hours, cross-embodiment reuse.
(d) An explicit test of whether body-relative transfer location matters, with a pre-registered null-result path.

Target IEEE RA-L (with ICRA/IROS option) or Humanoids first. Aim for CoRL only if the event model generalises across many tasks and embodiments.

## I14: Self-calibrating, self-synchronizing two-to-three RGB-D camera rig from the hand-held fiducial object and the human skeleton, with online calibration-validity flags

Direction: Low-cost multi-sensor fusion for digital twins: online spatiotemporal calibration of unsynchronized consumer RGB-D cameras using scene content (rigid object plus articulated body) instead of a calibration session.

Research question: Can the extrinsics and per-camera time offsets of two or three unsynchronized consumer RGB-D cameras be estimated, and kept valid online, to an accuracy that makes multi-view hand-object reconstruction better than the best single view, using only a marker-bearing hand-held object and the subject's own skeleton (bone-length and joint-angle invariance), with no checkerboard session and no hardware sync?

Why novel (as proposed): All prior work here is from memory and unverified (search budget exhausted for this lens). Takahashi et al. (CVPR Workshops 2018) calibrate and synchronize unsynchronized RGB cameras from 2D body keypoints, RGB only (scale ambiguity), offline, no rigid object; Kalibr (IROS 2013) needs a target and IMU; OpenCap (PLOS Comput Biol 2023) calibrates two phones with a checkerboard beforehand, offline; BEHAVE (CVPR 2022) and InterCap (GCPR 2022) use 3-4 Kinects calibrated offline with boards. Delta: a joint estimator combining a 6-DoF rigid fiducial seen by several cameras, metric depth fixing scale, and a body with invariant segment lengths; sub-frame time offsets as continuous variables; an online monitor emitting a calibration-validity flag (honesty labels extended from joints to sensors). A 2024-2026 check (ICRA/IROS 2025, CVPR 2026) is still needed.

Method (as proposed): Inputs: 2-3 D435 streams at 30 fps with host-clock stamps; per-camera ArUco detections of cube, desk and wall markers (thesis marker branch with IPPE two-lobe disambiguation); RTMPose-l keypoints back-projected with metric depth. Representation: per camera extrinsic T_c in SE(3) to the desk world frame and time offset dt_c; cube trajectory as a continuous-time cubic B-spline in SE(3); body as the swing-twist chain with per-subject segment lengths. Estimator: sliding-window nonlinear least squares (Ceres or GTSAM) with residuals (1) cube corner reprojection per camera at t_frame + dt_c on the spline; (2) desk-marker reprojection (gauge); (3) depth-to-surface residual of visible cube faces; (4) cross-view body residuals after transform and time shift, Cauchy-robustified and weighted by the thesis failure flags; (5) bone-length constancy across views. Initialization: Kabsch on co-visible cube poses; time offset by cross-correlating cube speed profiles. Online: fixed-lag re-estimation on a 10-20 s window; a chi-square residual test raises 'calibration stale' (camera bumped, clock drift) in the Unity twin. The never-measured wall-marker mounting angle becomes an estimated parameter.

Evaluation (as proposed): Reference: board calibration with hardware-synced D435 pairs, then sync disabled with injected delays and clock drift; rail line (0.26 cm median) and tape waypoints. Metrics: rotation/translation error vs board calibration, time-offset error (ms), cross-camera cube reprojection error, rail-line residual of the fused cube track, wrist-to-cube agreement, fraction of thesis occlusion frames that become measured in some view, time-to-flag and false-alarm rate for deliberate camera bumps. Baselines: board calibration, Takahashi-style 2D-pose calibration, object-only Kabsch, body-only. Ablations: remove each residual family; no time-offset estimation; vary view overlap. Public: BEHAVE and InterCap multi-Kinect sequences with published calibrations.

Target venues: IEEE/RSJ IROS, IEEE RA-L (with ICRA option), IEEE ISMAR, IEEE TVCG

Builds on the thesis: Marker branch with two-lobe disambiguation and plausibility cleaning, metric back-projection with 5x5 median depth, swing-twist chain and bone-length gates, failure flags as residual weights, rail and waypoint tooling, honesty labels generalized to sensors. Also provides the rig for the multi-view variant of I01 and the auxiliary views of I10.

Feasibility (as proposed): Buy or borrow 1-2 more D435 (about USD 300-350 each) and a sync cable; the RTX 3080 handles RTMPose for three 640x480 streams with batching. Record 10-15 sessions with one or two subjects, each with a short board sequence. Timeline: month 1 rig and synced reference; months 2-3 estimator and offline evaluation; month 4 online monitor and bump/drift experiments; month 5 public benchmark and writing; IROS/RA-L in about 6 months.

Risks (as proposed): May be seen as an engineering combination of known residuals (bundle adjustment plus human-pose calibration) unless time-offset estimation and online staleness detection show clear gains over board calibration; marker corner noise at 2-3 m and small view overlap may limit cube co-visibility; novelty unverified.

### novelty skeptic: score 3, refuted=True

Search budget: WebSearch was exhausted on the first call (200/200 for the session), and arxiv, Semantic Scholar, OpenAlex and dblp are blocked by the egress proxy. Twelve GitHub repository searches were the only live evidence channel, so most paper-level checks are from memory and marked verified=false. Default-to-refute applies, and what was found points the same way.

What the evidence shows. (1) Using the human body as the calibration target is an active and crowded topic in 2024-2026. GitHub search turned up HumanCalib (flodelaplace/HumanCalib, created 2026-03). It describes itself as "a fully automated, markerless extrinsic camera calibration pipeline using human motion" with RTMPose or Metrabs, 3D lifting and real-world metric scaling. That is the same detector this idea plans to use, with the scale problem already handled. Also found: "Human Pose-Guided Joint Optimization for Camera Auto-Calibration System with Unknown Intrinsics" (ICCE 2025, Oral), a C++ reimplementation of "Auto calibration of multi-camera system for human pose estimation", and an extrinsic-calibration repo built on human skeletons from ToF cameras (mladennick/kalibrate). (2) Continuous-time B-spline spatiotemporal calibration with time offsets is mature tooling. GitHub showed HPGT (IEEE TVCG 2025, spatiotemporal calibration for XR SLAM benchmarking), EF-Calib (RA-L 2024, continuous-time spatiotemporal calibration of event and frame cameras), M2-Calibr (targetless spatiotemporal calibration of multiple IMUs, cameras and LiDARs) and multical (multiple targets, multiple sensors, spatiotemporal). That makes the "dt_c as a continuous variable on an SE(3) spline" component standard. (3) From memory (unverified): CasCalib (3DV 2024) calibrates sparse unsynchronized cameras and recovers their time offsets from human pose. That is the closest single prior and covers the body-based spatiotemporal half of the research question. Takahashi et al. (CVPRW 2018) already did joint calibration and sync from 2D keypoints. (4) Multi-camera fused ArUco-cube pose with extrinsic calibration exists as engineering code (diegotiozzo21/multicam_pose_estimation, 2026).

The remaining delta has three parts: (a) metric depth plus a rigid hand-held fiducial plus bone-length constancy in one estimator; (b) an online chi-square "calibration stale" flag; (c) evaluation on hand-object reconstruction. Part (a) is a combination of known residual families, which the idea's own risk section already admits. Part (b) is standard innovation-based fault detection (chi-square on residuals, as in VIO and sensor-fusion FDI) applied to extrinsics, which is useful but incremental. A ChArUco cube seen by several D435s is exactly what a board calibration would use, so "no calibration session" is a weak selling point when the fiducial object is a calibration target in all but name. For CVPR/ICCV/ECCV/NeurIPS the delta is too small. At most it is an IROS or RA-L systems paper, and only if the online-validity monitoring shows clear, quantified value (time-to-detect, false alarms, downstream error prevented) over periodic board recalibration.

Prior work found:
- HumanCalib: fully automated markerless extrinsic camera calibration pipeline using human motion (RTMPose/Metrabs, 3D lifting, metric scaling) - code repository (GitHub, 2026 (no paper venue shown)) https://github.com/flodelaplace/HumanCalib; overlap: Markerless multi-camera extrinsic calibration from human motion with the same RTMPose detector and metric scale recovery; covers the body-only half of the estimator and the 'no calibration session' claim.; verified=True
- Human Pose-Guided Joint Optimization for Camera Auto-Calibration System with Unknown Intrinsics (ICCE 2025 (Oral)) https://github.com/Nathan-AVG/PoseGuidedCalibration; overlap: Camera auto-calibration with human pose as the target, solved by joint optimization; goes further than the idea by also estimating intrinsics.; verified=True
- HPGT: Spatiotemporal Calibration and Ground Truth Estimation for SLAM Benchmarking (IEEE TVCG 2025) https://github.com/ylab-xrpg/xr-hpgt; overlap: Joint spatial and temporal (time-offset) calibration for XR capture rigs at a TVCG venue; the dt_c estimation component is not new.; verified=True
- EF-Calib: Spatiotemporal Calibration of Event- and Frame-Based Cameras Using Continuous-Time Trajectories (IEEE RA-L 2024) https://github.com/ShaoanWang/EF-Calib; overlap: Continuous-time trajectory (spline) formulation with per-sensor time offsets: the same estimator design as the proposed SE(3) B-spline with dt_c.; verified=True
- M2-Calibr: targetless spatiotemporal calibration framework based on multi-output Gaussian processes for multiple IMUs, cameras and LiDARs (GitHub, 2025 (venue not shown)) https://github.com/cchester25/M2-Calibr; overlap: Targetless multi-sensor spatiotemporal calibration; undercuts the 'no target' framing.; verified=True
- multicam_pose_estimation: multi-camera extrinsic calibration and robust fused ArUco-cube pose estimation (ROS) (GitHub, 2026) https://github.com/diegotiozzo21/multicam_pose_estimation; overlap: Multi-camera extrinsics plus fused ArUco-cube pose; the rigid-fiducial half of the idea is already engineering practice.; verified=True
- CasCalib: Cascaded Calibration for Motion Capture from Sparse Unsynchronized Cameras (3DV 2024); overlap: Calibrates sparse, unsynchronized cameras and recovers their time offsets from human pose alone; directly covers spatial calibration plus time sync from the body.; verified=False
- Human Pose as Calibration Pattern; 3D Human Pose Estimation with Multiple Unsynchronized and Uncalibrated Cameras (CVPR Workshops 2018); overlap: Joint extrinsic calibration and sub-frame synchronization of unsynchronized cameras from 2D body keypoints.; verified=False
- Unified Temporal and Spatial Calibration for Multi-Sensor Systems (Kalibr) (IROS 2013); overlap: Continuous-time B-spline estimation of extrinsics and time offsets; the standard backbone the method reuses.; verified=False
- BEHAVE: Dataset and Method for Tracking Human Object Interactions (CVPR 2022) https://arxiv.org/abs/2204.06950; overlap: Multi-Kinect human-object capture with offline board calibration; it is the baseline setting, not a competitor, but its public data makes the evaluation comparison straightforward for others too.; verified=True

Suggested reframe: Drop "self-calibration from object plus skeleton" as the headline: it is crowded (HumanCalib 2026, ICCE 2025 pose-guided calibration, CasCalib 3DV 2024 from memory) and its components are standard (Kalibr-style spline time offsets, HPGT TVCG 2025). The one piece that might survive is sensor-level honesty for digital-twin capture. The reframe: an online integrity monitor for low-cost multi-RGB-D hand-object capture. It detects and localizes calibration and synchronization faults (camera bump, clock drift, a marker remounted at a different angle) from the residuals of scene content. It then propagates them into per-joint and per-object measured/held/constrained labels, so the twin never renders a value built on a stale sensor. Contributions to claim and measure: (1) fault detectability and isolability analysis, i.e. which faults are observable from rigid-object versus body residuals, and with what view overlap; (2) time-to-detect and false-alarm curves versus periodic board recalibration and versus a body-only monitor in the HumanCalib/CasCalib style; (3) downstream error prevented in the hand-object reconstruction. Target IROS or RA-L, not CVPR. Before committing, re-run a literature check: the session's search budget was exhausted, so CasCalib and any 2025-2026 calibration-monitoring work (for example, online extrinsic drift detection for multi-camera rigs) remain unverified.

### feasibility skeptic: score 5.5, refuted=False

Feasibility verdict: buildable in a small lab, but the idea as stated is too large and depends on two weak links. It is not refuted because the extra resources are cheap and named (1-2 extra D435 at about USD 300-350 each plus a sync cable), and calibration ground truth is unusually easy to get for this topic. D435s support hardware inter-camera sync, so a board-calibrated, hardware-synced pair is a real reference for extrinsics and time offsets. Injected delays and drift on top of the synced streams give exact offset ground truth. A deliberately bumped camera gives exact event times for the staleness monitor. One subject is acceptable here because calibration does not depend on the subject. No learning and no labelling are needed, and the RTX 3080 is enough. Concrete feasibility problems: (1) The thesis cube carries one ArUco marker on one face (thesis text: 'the ArUco marker centre on the cube face'). Cameras with a useful baseline (60-90 deg apart, which is what helps with occlusion) will see that face at grazing angles or not at all, so cube co-visibility, the core of the initialization and of residual (1), may be close to zero. A new object with markers on several faces (or a ChArUco cube) is needed, and that changes the object the thesis pipeline was validated on. (2) The task is slow by design (the thesis gates assume slow desk motion). Time-offset observability grows with object and body speed, so sub-frame offsets on slow carries may be weakly observable. The evaluation must include deliberate fast motions or report observability honestly. (3) The body residuals are biased. A back-projected keypoint lands on the surface facing each camera, not on the joint centre, so two views of the same 'shoulder' differ by several cm along different directions. Bone-length invariance adds nothing because metric depth already fixes scale. The skeleton term may hurt more than help, which undermines half of the title. (4) Engineering load: three D435 streams on one host need separate USB3 controllers, a continuous-time SE(3) spline estimator, sliding-window online re-estimation, and Unity integration. Roughly half the 6-month plan is systems work, and a 6-month IROS/RA-L timeline is optimistic for one student; 9-12 months is realistic. (5) The headline downstream claim, multi-view hand-object reconstruction beating the best single view, has weak ground truth. The rail line and tape waypoints cover only the object, and wrist accuracy rests on a small manual label set, so the 'better reconstruction' claim will be shown mainly as coverage (more frames measured) rather than accuracy. (6) The BEHAVE/InterCap public check is feasible but only for the body term (those datasets have no fiducial objects), so it cannot test the main object+depth estimator. Novelty risk is outside this lens but bears on whether the effort pays off: skeleton-based extrinsic calibration of multi-Kinect rigs and moving-wand calibration (standard practice in mocap) are well-trodden. The publishable delta is narrow: metric object+depth calibration with continuous time offsets plus an online validity flag. The literature search could not run in this pass (search budget exhausted), so every prior-work entry is unverified except BEHAVE. Minimum viable experiment (4-6 weeks, one borrowed D435, a sync cable, a cube with markers on 3+ faces): record 5 sessions of the thesis desk task with two hardware-synced D435s about 45-70 deg apart, each preceded by a 30 s board sequence. Run an offline batch estimator (discrete poses with linear interpolation, no spline yet) using cube-corner reprojection, desk-marker gauge and the depth-to-face residual, with time offset found by cross-correlating speed profiles and then refined. Inject offsets of 0-100 ms plus a linear drift. Success criteria: extrinsic error within about 2x the board's own repeatability (order 0.3-0.5 deg, 3-5 mm), offset error under about 5 ms on sessions with real motion, and a camera bump flagged within a few seconds with no false alarms over a full session. If this fails on cube co-visibility or offset observability, the full idea fails too.

Prior work found:
- Human Pose as Calibration Pattern; 3D Human Pose Estimation with Multiple Unsynchronized and Uncalibrated Cameras (CVPR Workshops 2018); overlap: Calibrates extrinsics and temporal offsets of unsynchronized cameras from 2D human keypoints (RGB only, offline). Directly covers the body-as-calibration-pattern part of the idea.; verified=False
- Unified Temporal and Spatial Calibration for Multi-Sensor Systems (Kalibr) (IROS 2013); overlap: Continuous-time B-spline estimation of extrinsics and time offsets. The spline/time-offset machinery proposed here is standard from this line of work, but Kalibr needs a calibration target and an IMU.; verified=False
- OpenCap: Human movement dynamics from smartphone videos (PLOS Computational Biology 2023); overlap: Low-cost two-camera human capture that uses checkerboard pre-calibration. This is the baseline workflow the idea wants to replace.; verified=False
- BEHAVE: Dataset and Method for Tracking Human Object Interactions (CVPR 2022) https://arxiv.org/abs/2204.06950; overlap: Multi-Kinect human-object capture calibrated offline with boards. Proposed as a public benchmark, but it has no fiducial on the objects, so it can test only the body term.; verified=True
- InterCap: Joint Markerless 3D Tracking of Humans and Objects in Interaction (GCPR 2022); overlap: Multi-Kinect human-object capture with offline board calibration. It has the same limitation as BEHAVE as a benchmark for this idea.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Narrow the project to 'object-as-wand' metric spatiotemporal calibration with an online validity monitor for 2 unsynchronized consumer RGB-D cameras. Use a multi-face fiducial object moved during the actual task, together with depth-to-surface residuals and continuous time offsets. Treat the skeleton only as an optional, robustified prior evaluated in an ablation, not as a headline contribution. Keep two cameras, not three. Lead with the evaluation the lab can do convincingly: a hardware-synced, board-calibrated reference with injected offsets, drift and bumps, reporting extrinsic error, offset error versus motion speed (observability), time-to-flag and false-alarm rate. Present the downstream benefit as the fraction of thesis occlusion frames that become measured, with object accuracy on the rail line. Target RA-L/IROS or ISMAR as a system-and-evaluation paper on a 9-12 month plan, and run the 4-6 week minimum experiment above as a go/no-go gate before buying a third camera or building the online spline estimator.

### venue skeptic: score 3, refuted=True

Area-chair view, best-fitting venue IROS or RA-L. Web search could not be used: the session's search budget was already exhausted, and every query this lens issued was refused. So none of the novelty claims could be checked against 2024-2026 papers, and that alone argues for the default refutation. From memory (unverified), the space is crowded. Takahashi et al. (CVPRW 2018) already calibrate and synchronize cameras from human pose. Lee et al. (RA-L 2022) do extrinsic calibration from a moving person. CasCalib (3DV 2024) does cascaded calibration plus synchronization for motion capture from sparse, unsynchronized cameras using human keypoints. Sync-NeRF (AAAI 2024) and related work estimate per-camera time offsets for unsynchronized video. Multi-Kinect skeleton calibration is common in Sensors-level venues. Continuous-time spline estimation with time offsets (Kalibr, Qin and Shen IROS 2018 online temporal calibration) is textbook. The proposed delta has three parts: adding a marker-bearing rigid object, using metric depth to fix scale, and adding a chi-square staleness monitor. Reviewers will read this as standard bundle adjustment with known residual families stacked together. Two points weaken the headline further. First, a fiducial object is itself a calibration target, so the "no calibration session" claim is soft: waving the ArUco cube is close to waving a board. Second, metric depth plus ArUco makes the scale problem that motivates human-pose calibration largely trivial. No single headline claim stands out. The likely result is that accuracy matches board calibration, which is a parity result, not a gain. The downstream claim (multi-view hand-object reconstruction beats the best single view) is expected and is not specific to the calibration method. The evaluation has gaps. There is one subject, one room and the D435 only. The reference is board calibration with hardware sync, so the method can at best tie its reference. The bump/drift detection experiments are staged. BEHAVE and InterCap provide calibrations, but their Kinect clocks are already synchronized, so time-offset estimation cannot be tested on them without synthetic shifts. Named baselines that are missing: CasCalib, Lee et al. 2022, and modern learned or targetless multi-camera calibration such as COLMAP or DUSt3R/MASt3R-style pose estimation on static background frames. MASt3R-type feed-forward multi-view pose estimation is a strong 2024-2026 targetless baseline any reviewer would ask for. Venue fit: too narrow and incremental for TVCG or ISMAR main track, and at best borderline for IROS. A more realistic home is IEEE Sensors Journal, an ICRA/IROS workshop, or a section of a larger systems or dataset paper. Expected acceptance quality at IROS is about 3/10.

Prior work found:
- Human Pose as Calibration Pattern; 3D Human Pose Estimation with Multiple Unsynchronized and Uncalibrated Cameras (CVPR Workshops 2018); overlap: Joint extrinsic and temporal calibration of unsynchronized cameras from human keypoints; the core idea of using the body as the calibration pattern.; verified=False
- Extrinsic Camera Calibration From a Moving Person (IEEE RA-L 2022); overlap: Targetless multi-camera extrinsic calibration from a walking person's keypoints; same venue family as the target.; verified=False
- CasCalib: Cascaded Calibration for Motion Capture from Sparse Unsynchronized Cameras (3DV 2024); overlap: Recovers intrinsics, extrinsics and time offsets of unsynchronized cameras from human pose; directly overlaps the spatiotemporal-from-skeleton claim.; verified=False
- Sync-NeRF: Generalizing Dynamic NeRFs to Unsynchronized Videos (AAAI 2024); overlap: Estimates per-camera time offsets for unsynchronized multi-view video as continuous variables.; verified=False
- Online Temporal Calibration for Monocular Visual-Inertial Systems (IROS 2018); overlap: Online time-offset estimation inside a sliding-window estimator; the time-offset machinery is established.; verified=False
- Unified Temporal and Spatial Calibration for Multi-Sensor Systems (Kalibr) (IROS 2013); overlap: Continuous-time B-spline joint spatial and temporal calibration; the proposed estimator structure.; verified=False
- BEHAVE: Dataset and Method for Tracking Human Object Interactions (CVPR 2022) https://arxiv.org/abs/2204.06950; overlap: Multi-Kinect human-object capture with offline board calibration; the proposed public benchmark, whose clocks are already synchronized.; verified=False
- MASt3R / DUSt3R feed-forward multi-view pose estimation (ECCV 2024 / CVPR 2024); overlap: Strong targetless extrinsic baseline from static scene content, which reviewers would expect to see compared.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Do not pitch calibration as the contribution. Two options are more defensible. (a) Make the online calibration-validity monitor the headline, as a general safety layer for consumer multi-camera digital twins. That would mean detecting and localizing extrinsic and clock degradation from scene content during operation, with calibrated false-alarm and detection-delay guarantees. It would need multiple rigs, many sessions, and comparisons against CasCalib-style and MASt3R-style recalibration. Target RA-L. (b) Fold the self-calibrating rig in as the enabling component of a multi-view hand-object capture system or dataset paper, where the headline is coverage of single-view occlusion failures and the calibration is a section. In both cases, drop the "no calibration session" claim, because the ArUco cube is a target. Benchmark against CasCalib, Lee et al. RA-L 2022 and MASt3R, and test time-offset recovery on real unsynchronized hardware, not only on injected delays.

## I15: Does showing provenance change what people conclude and do? User studies of honesty-annotated avatars for remote observers, clinicians and live demonstrators

Direction: Human-centred honesty-aware rendering: how a markerless capture system should communicate which joints are measured, held or inferred, tested with three audiences (remote XR observers, rehabilitation clinicians, and demonstrators recording robot-learning data).

Research question: When a real-time digital twin fills tracking gaps with held or kinematically inferred joints, does rendering per-joint provenance and uncertainty (a) improve remote observers' accuracy and trust calibration on task-level judgments (reach extent, which hand holds the object, trunk compensation) without harming presence, (b) improve physiotherapists' and occupational therapists' rating accuracy and reduce over-trust in inferred joints during occlusion compared with unannotated replay and raw RGB video, and (c) when fed back live to a demonstrator, raise the fraction of fully measured frames and downstream policy success per minute of capture compared with no feedback and feasibility-only feedback?

Why novel (as proposed): Telepresence systems (Holoportation UIST 2016, VirtualCube IEEE VR 2022, Project Starline SIGGRAPH Asia 2021) aim for seamless realism and never expose which parts are measured; avatar-perception studies measure the tracked user's embodiment, not third-party judgments when parts are imputed. Lee et al. (CHI 2021) studied therapists with AI assessment outputs and feature explanations, not measurement provenance on a 3D reconstruction; markerless rehab with Unity replay (RePose, arXiv 2026) streams angles without marking inferred ones. ARCap (ICRA 2025) gives demonstrators AR feedback on robot feasibility (reported higher replay success than DexCap, RSS 2024), and HumanPlus (CoRL 2024) shadows from RGB pose; EasyMimic (2026) is a low-cost human-video pipeline; none feeds back sensing observability. Delta: controlled studies with ground-truth-injected tracking failures, enabled by a pipeline that already labels each joint group and by v3 S-type scenarios with known truth. Most citations here are from memory and unverified; a 2024-2026 IEEE VR / CHI / CSCW check is required.

Method (as proposed): Stimuli: lab recordings replayed through the causal chain into the Unity twin; failures injected with the v3 S-type generator (known truth) and filled by object-conditioned recovery (correct inference), hold-last (drifting) or a deliberately wrong grip offset (confidently wrong); for clinicians, 30-40 trials of reaching, transfer and handover including scripted impairment by actors (compensation, slowed movement, reduced elbow extension). Study A (observers, within-subject): C0 seamless avatar; C1 hide unmeasured segments; C2 categorical provenance colouring; C3 continuous uncertainty (translucency or ghosted cone scaled by Kalman covariance); C4 provenance timeline; tasks: detect measured vs fabricated motion, estimate reach and wrist-to-object distance, judge trunk compensation, rate confidence; repeat key conditions in an HMD (Quest 3). Study B (clinicians): raw RGB vs unannotated avatar vs honesty colouring with metric intervals vs plus abstention banner; rate compensation, elbow extension category, flag untrusted trials; two formative interface rounds first. Study C (demonstrators): causal chain (28.7 fps, p99 12-13 ms) drives the twist with a robot ghost and audio/visual cues when a hand becomes unobservable or the grip offset ages; conditions none / feasibility-only / observability / both; feedback latency ablation (live vs 1 s delayed). Preregistered mixed-effects analyses.

Evaluation (as proposed): Study A: 24-36 lay observers plus a small expert group; judgment error vs truth, d' for detecting fabricated motion, Brier score, presence/co-presence questionnaires, time; ablations of encoding, failure duration, correct vs wrong inference. Study B: 12-20 licensed PT/OT; primary outcome error on occluded-segment judgements; trust calibration separately on measured vs inferred segments; time per trial; NASA-TLX; interviews. Study C: 10-16 participants on 3 desk tasks; capture yield (fraction of measured frames per joint group, recovery events), task time, naturalness (path length, jerk), downstream policy success per minute of data (RoboTwin-style replay and low-cost arms). Report effect sizes with CIs; release stimuli and the Unity provenance shader.

Target venues: IEEE VR (TVCG journal track), IEEE ISMAR, ACM CHI, ACM CSCW, HRI, ICRA / IROS / RA-L (Study C)

Builds on the thesis: Per-joint honesty flags in the streamed packet; the Unity rule 'apply numbers, never invent them' with frozen joints in red; object-conditioned recovery as correct-but-inferred motion and hold-last as drifting motion; v3 S-type scenarios with teleport budgets and false_measured; the causal real-time chain with delay-buffer merger; the Unity avatar rig and side-by-side films; the observed rig/display discrepancy (5.5-6.8 cm) is a design issue to fix and report.

Feasibility (as proposed): Low hardware cost: no new hardware for Study A; a Quest 3 (about USD 500) or borrowed HMD; robot purchase optional for Study C if downstream stays in simulation. Ethics approval (2-3 months, minimal risk); clinician recruitment needs a clinical or teaching partner (bottleneck); Study C needs live-camera validation the thesis did not complete; fix the rig/display offset. Timeline: about 6 months for Study A (IEEE VR September cycle), 9-12 months to a CHI submission for Study B, about 5 months for Study C.

Risks (as proposed): Narrow stimulus variety (one subject, one task, one object) and small expert samples may not support claims about remote clinical assessment; results may be unsurprising (annotated condition simply wins) or null if imputation errors are not consequential; actors are not patients; Study C may be seen as an interface contribution with modest depth, feedback may make motions less natural, and the unvalidated live path may consume the schedule; avoid overclaiming clinical validity.

### novelty skeptic: score 4, refuted=True

Evidence status: I could not run a single literature search. The session's WebSearch budget was used up (200 of 200 calls), and WebFetch to arxiv.org, export.arxiv.org and api.semanticscholar.org was blocked by the egress proxy. So I found no paper that does exactly this, and I could not confirm the gap either. The task says to default to refuted=true under that uncertainty. The judgement below rests on memory, and every work listed is unverified unless the idea's own JSON already marked it verified.

Arguments for refutation or a small delta:
(1) Uncertainty visualization is a mature HCI/VIS field. Kay et al., Padilla et al., hypothetical outcome plots, and trust-calibration studies of AI confidence displays (for example Zhang, Liao and Bellamy, FAT* 2020) already show that showing uncertainty or confidence changes reliance. "Annotate imputed avatar joints and observers trust them less where they are imputed" is the expected result of applying that body of work to a new medium. Top HCI reviewers (CHI, IEEE VR) will call it an obvious hypothesis unless the study finds a non-obvious effect, such as presence costs, encoding interactions or miscalibration on correct-but-inferred motion.
(2) VR avatar research has studied incomplete or partially tracked bodies: missing limbs, hidden versus IK-driven segments, and the IK-generated legs of consumer HMD avatars. The condition "C1 hide unmeasured segments vs C0 seamless" is close to existing avatar-completeness work in IEEE VR/TVCG, although that work mostly measures self-embodiment rather than third-party judgement.
(3) Clinician-facing human-AI work exists. Lee et al. (CHI 2021) and Lee and Chew (CSCW 2023, counterfactual explanations and reliance in rehabilitation assessment) already study how clinicians rely on AI outputs and explanations. Adding provenance colouring of a 3D reconstruction is incremental within that line. The planned stimuli are also weak for a clinical claim: one subject, actors simulating impairment, 12-20 clinicians.
(4) Demonstration-capture feedback is an active area. ARCap (ICRA 2025, verified in the idea JSON) gives live AR feedback during capture. Belkhale et al. (NeurIPS 2023, Data Quality in Imitation Learning) and later demonstration-curation methods, such as DemInf (2025, from memory), address data quality after capture. UMI (RSS 2024) and DexUMI (2025) handle tracking or SLAM loss mostly by filtering. Live sensing-observability feedback is plausibly new, but "feasibility feedback vs observability feedback" is a small change to ARCap's protocol. The policy-per-minute outcome then has to carry the paper, and the live camera path is not validated.
(5) Merging three audiences (XR observers, clinicians, robot demonstrators) dilutes the work. No single venue rewards all three, each sub-study is small, and none is clearly top-venue on its own.

Arguments for some survival:
- I know of no paper that injects ground-truth tracking failures with known truth into a live markerless digital twin and measures third-party detection (d') of fabricated motion. The thesis's per-joint measured/held/constrained flags and the v3 S-type generator are a real enabling asset.

Net: the delta rests on a medium transfer (uncertainty visualization moved onto avatar joints) plus a three-way merger. I rate novelty about 4/10, unverified.

Prior work found:

- Data Quality in Imitation Learning (NeurIPS 2023); overlap: Formalizes demonstration quality and its effect on policy success; Study C's yield-to-policy-success argument sits inside this framing.; verified=False
- Universal Manipulation Interface (UMI) (RSS 2024); overlap: Handheld capture whose SLAM tracking loss is handled by filtering demonstrations; observability of capture is treated as a data-validity problem, though without live observability feedback studied as a variable.; verified=False
- A human-AI collaborative approach for clinical decision making on rehabilitation assessment (CHI 2021); overlap: Therapists using AI-derived rehabilitation assessments with explanations; measures reliance and accuracy, the same outcome family as Study B.; verified=False
- Understanding the effect of counterfactual explanations on trust and reliance on AI for human-AI collaborative clinical decision making (CSCW 2023); overlap: Trust calibration and over-reliance of clinicians on AI rehabilitation assessment; Study B reuses the outcome measures with a different explanation type (measurement provenance).; verified=False
- Effect of confidence and explanation on accuracy and trust calibration in AI-assisted decision making (FAT* 2020); overlap: Shows that showing confidence changes reliance and trust calibration; the generic hypothesis of Studies A and B.; verified=False
- DexCap: Scalable and Portable Mocap Data Collection System for Dexterous Manipulation (RSS 2024); overlap: Portable capture for robot learning; ARCap's baseline; no observability feedback.; verified=True
- HumanPlus: Humanoid Shadowing and Imitation from Humans (CoRL 2024); overlap: Markerless RGB pose drives robot data collection; no provenance or observability exposure.; verified=True

Suggested reframe: Drop the three-audience merger and keep one sharp, falsifiable study whose outcome is not obvious. The best candidate is Study A, reframed as "imputation transparency" for markerless avatars. Hypothesis: seamless kinematic gap-filling, such as object-conditioned recovery, IK or hold-last, produces confidently wrong third-party judgements, and the best encoding depends on whether the inference is correct or wrong. Use the v3 S-type generator to inject failures with known truth, and include correct-inferred, drifting and confidently-wrong fills, so the study can show that naive annotation also lowers trust in correct inferences (an over-correction cost). Report d' for detecting fabricated motion and Brier-scored trust calibration across encodings. Target IEEE VR (TVCG track) or ISMAR, and release the provenance shader and stimuli. If the robot-learning angle is kept, make it a separate RA-L/ICRA paper that measures policy success per minute with observability feedback against ARCap-style feasibility feedback. Do that only after the live camera path is validated. ### feasibility skeptic: score 4, refuted=True

Feasibility verdict as written: refuted. Three separate user studies across three communities (XR observers, licensed clinicians, robot-learning demonstrators) is more than one graduate student can do in 12-18 months. Two of the three studies need resources the idea does not say how to obtain, or rest on engineering the thesis never validated.

Study A (remote observers) is feasible and is the only part that should survive.
(1) The stimuli already exist. 28 single-subject RealSense recordings feed v3 S-type synthetic outages (six scenarios; false_measured 0.000; teleport budgets). The repo also has the S2 recovery strategy and the hold-last baseline. Ground truth is free because masking is injected on clean frames.
(2) The per-joint honesty flags already reach Unity, where frozen joints show red. Categorical colouring and a covariance-scaled ghost from the existing tangent-space Kalman filter are small rendering jobs.
(3) Lay participants and a desktop or Quest 3 setup cost little. Ethics approval for a minimal-risk study takes about 2-3 months.

Caveats on Study A:
(a) The truth is the clean pipeline output, not anatomical truth: there is no mocap, and the kinematic wrist is 0.8-2.3 cm from manual labels. Every claim must say "relative to the unmasked measurement".
(b) The 5.5-6.8 cm rig/display discrepancy has to be fixed first. Otherwise the stimulus itself carries a systematic error larger than many of the injected effects.
(c) The "detect fabricated motion" d' measure is trivial under categorical colouring, because the colour gives the answer. The real dependent variables are judgment error on reach and wrist-to-object distance, and over-trust in confidently wrong inferences. If those are not consequential, the result is unsurprising or null.
(d) One subject and one task limit generality. Recording 4-6 more people is cheap and should be planned.

Study B (clinicians) does not say how to obtain resources. 12-20 licensed PT/OT need a clinical partner the dossier does not have. Actors scripting impairment need new recordings and ethics coverage. A single-view, upper-body-only system with no wrist orientation and no fingers yields weak clinical stimuli. Claims about remote assessment validity would not survive CHI or TNSRE reviewers on this sample.

Study C (demonstrators) is dominated by engineering risk.
(1) It depends on the live-camera path, which the thesis explicitly did not validate.
(2) "Downstream policy success per minute" needs a human-to-robot retargeting and policy-training pipeline from a pose that has no wrist orientation and no fingers. That is a separate research project.
(3) No robot is available. Simulation replay alone would make the outcome measure hard to defend at ICRA or RA-L.

Overall: the bundle is overscoped, and two thirds of it lacks the resources it needs. A reduced Study A is a credible IEEE VR, ISMAR or TVCG submission within about 9-12 months.

Literature status: the web search budget was exhausted in this session, so no citation was re-verified here. All prior-work entries are marked unverified; ARCap, DexCap, HumanPlus and EasyMimic were marked verified upstream. The novelty claim needs a 2024-2026 IEEE VR / CHI / ISMAR check before committing. Uncertainty visualisation (hypothetical outcome plots and similar) and avatar-fidelity perception work are likely closer neighbours than the telepresence systems cited.

Minimum viable experiment:
- Fix the rig offset.
- Take 3 recordings, ideally from 3-5 subjects, and inject 40-60 outages of known truth with durations from 0.5 to 3 s.
- Fill each outage three ways: correct object-conditioned recovery, drifting hold-last, and a deliberately wrong grip offset.
- Render four encodings: seamless; hide-unmeasured; categorical provenance colour; Kalman-covariance ghost.
- Run 24 within-subject lay participants on a desktop. Measure reach-extent and wrist-to-object estimation error, held-hand identification, and confidence (Brier score and calibration slope), separately on measured vs inferred segments.
- Use preregistered mixed-effects models.

Success criterion: provenance encodings cut over-trust (the confidence-error gap) on confidently wrong inferences without raising error on correct inferences, and they do not drop presence below the seamless condition. Only if this holds, repeat the 2 best conditions on a Quest 3.

### venue skeptic: score 3.5, refuted=True

Area-chair view (IEEE VR/TVCG, CHI, ICRA). Search budget for this session was exhausted, so no 2024-2026 venue check could be run in this pass; citations below are from memory except those the idea already marked verified. Reasons for refuting as written: (1) No single headline claim. It is three papers (XR observers, clinicians, robot-data demonstrators) with three populations, three venue cultures and three evaluation standards. A CHI or IEEE VR committee will see the clinical and robot-learning arms as unfunded add-ons, and a robotics committee will see Study C as an interface tweak with no policy-level evidence. (2) The likely main result is predictable: if one condition shows observers which joints are fabricated, observers will detect fabricated motion better than in the seamless condition. Reviewers in uncertainty visualization (a mature field: hypothetical outcome plots, Kay/Hullman-style trust-calibration studies, Brier and d' measures) will ask what is learned beyond 'disclosure helps detection'. The interesting part, the cost to presence and co-presence and whether calibrated trust survives in an HMD, is listed but not made the hypothesis. (3) Stimulus validity is weak. One subject, one desk task, one cube, a single-camera upper-body rig with no fingers, and a known 5.5-6.8 cm rig/display offset. Imputation errors of a few centimetres on a slow desk task may simply not change task-level judgements, which makes a null result likely and hard to publish. The population that actually faces this problem at scale, VR users whose legs and torso are inferred from sparse HMD and controller signals (AvatarPoser ECCV 2022, EgoPoser, and similar full-body-from-sparse-input work), is not addressed, so the problem looks niche to an XR audience. (4) Clinician arm: no clinical partner, actors instead of patients, 12-20 therapists, and a remote-assessment claim that a single-viewpoint upper-body rig cannot support; CHI reviewers will flag overclaiming, and TNSRE reviewers will ask for patients and agreement statistics (ICC) against in-person rating. (5) Demonstrator arm: ARCap (ICRA 2025) already establishes in-capture AR feedback for data quality; the delta (observability rather than feasibility) is plausible but the downstream 'policy success per minute' requires a robot learning stack, a validated live camera path the thesis never completed, and real-robot results, which the author does not have. (6) Baselines are partly missing: no comparison to standard uncertainty encodings already used in VR avatar error studies, and no condition with a learned imputer whose errors are realistic rather than injected. Fit: Study A alone fits IEEE VR (TVCG track) or CHI; Study B belongs at CHI or a health-informatics venue only with a clinical partner; Study C belongs at a CoRL/RSS workshop or HRI late-breaking unless real-policy gains are shown. Expected acceptance at the best-fitting venue (IEEE VR conference track, Study A only, current design): low, about 3-4 of 10.

Prior work found:
- ARCap: Collecting High-quality Human Demonstrations for Robot Learning with Augmented Reality Feedback (ICRA 2025) https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.08464; overlap: Live AR feedback to demonstrators during capture to improve demonstration quality; Study C differs only in feeding back sensing observability instead of robot feasibility.; verified=True
- DexCap: Scalable and Portable Mocap Data Collection System for Dexterous Manipulation (RSS 2024); overlap: Baseline capture system for human-demonstration data that ARCap compares against; no observability feedback.; verified=True
- HumanPlus: Humanoid Shadowing and Imitation from Humans (CoRL 2024); overlap: RGB-pose-driven human-to-robot data capture; shows the robot-learning community values capture throughput, not provenance display.; verified=True
- A Human-AI Collaborative Approach for Clinical Decision Making on Rehabilitation Assessment (CHI 2021); overlap: Therapists using AI assessment outputs with explanations; closest clinical HCI precedent for Study B, but on model outputs rather than per-joint measurement provenance.; verified=False
- AvatarPoser: Articulated Full-Body Pose Tracking from Sparse Motion Sensing (ECCV 2022); overlap: Representative of the large VR full-body inference line where most of the body is imputed; the natural, larger population for an honesty-disclosure study that the idea does not target.; verified=False
- Holoportation: Virtual 3D Teleportation in Real-time (UIST 2016); overlap: Telepresence aiming for seamless realism without exposing measured versus inferred regions.; verified=False
- Project Starline: A high-fidelity telepresence system (SIGGRAPH Asia 2021 (ACM TOG)); overlap: Same seamless-realism telepresence goal; no provenance display.; verified=False

Suggested reframe: Cut to one study with one sharp, non-obvious hypothesis and move it off the thesis rig's niche. Proposed headline: 'Seamless or honest? Disclosing imputed body parts in avatar telepresence trades presence for calibrated trust, and the trade-off depends on imputation quality.' Design: a within-subject IEEE VR / TVCG study (about 30-40 participants, in an HMD, preregistered) where a remote partner's avatar has parts filled by (a) a good imputer, (b) a drifting hold-last, and (c) a confidently wrong imputer, crossed with seamless, categorical provenance, and continuous-uncertainty encodings. Use ground-truth-injected failures (the v3 S-type generator) and, critically, add a second, mainstream stimulus source: full-body-from-sparse-input VR avatars (HMD plus controllers, legs and torso inferred), so the result speaks to every consumer VR avatar, not only a single-camera desk rig. The non-obvious claims to test: disclosure lowers presence only when imputation is good (a cost with no benefit) and is net positive when imputation is wrong, which motivates adaptive disclosure keyed to the estimator's own covariance; report d', Brier, presence, and task error with effect sizes. Fix the 5.5-6.8 cm rig/display offset before any stimulus is recorded. Drop the clinician arm until a clinical partner and patients are available (then a separate CHI or TNSRE paper with ICC against in-person rating), and drop the robot-data arm unless a real policy-learning stack is available, in which case it is a separate CoRL/RA-L paper whose headline is downstream policy success per capture minute versus ARCap-style feasibility feedback.

## I16: An object-anchored, uncertainty-aware single-RGB-D instrument for standardized seated upper-limb tasks: phase segmentation, calibrated metric intervals with abstention, functional segment calibration and simulation-guided protocol design

Direction: Clinical upper-limb kinematic assessment with one consumer RGB-D camera: turning the thesis pipeline into a measurement instrument with object-clocked phase segmentation, consensus metrics with calibrated intervals and abstention, subject-specific functional calibration of joint centres and segment lengths, and a sensor-faithful digital twin that predicts where each metric can be trusted.

Research question: With one D435 and a fiducial-tagged test object, can the reach, grasp, transport and release phases of a standardized seated transfer task be segmented automatically from the object pose, and can the consensus kinematic metrics (movement time, peak hand speed, SPARC smoothness, elbow extension, trunk displacement, reach extent, workspace) be reported with calibrated intervals whose coverage holds against a reference system and which abstain when a joint group is not measured? How much bias do effective landmark-derived segment lengths introduce into these metrics, and can a 30-60 s object-guided functional calibration remove it? Can a Unity twin with a D435 noise model predict, across camera placements and impairment patterns, the real per-metric error well enough to choose the placement and layout?

Why novel (as proposed): All citations here are from memory and unverified; a 2024-2026 search for instrumented Box-and-Block, ARAT or Wolf Motor systems is required. The Second Stroke Recovery and Rehabilitation Roundtable (Kwakkel et al. 2019) defined metrics and tasks assuming lab mocap. Markerless clinical work (OpenCap 2023; video-based Parkinson rating, npj Digital Medicine 2023) gives point estimates with no per-trial uncertainty and no object tracking, so cannot timestamp grasp and release from the object. StrokeRehab (NeurIPS D&B 2022) segments primitives without metric 3D kinematics or confidence. Needham et al. (Sci Rep 2021) and Wade et al. (PeerJ 2022) show keypoints sit at wrong anatomical positions; functional joint-centre methods (SCoRE) need markers; OpenCap fits a musculoskeletal model to keypoints without object constraints. BEDLAM (CVPR 2023) trains estimators rather than certifying clinical metrics, without impaired movement, tabletop occlusion or RGB-D noise. Delta: phase events from the measured object; intervals from propagating D435 depth noise and landmark noise through the swing-twist chain followed by conformal calibration against a reference; honesty labels driving abstention; object-constrained functional calibration (rail and known desk targets) with the metric-bias analysis as a result; and a predictive sim-to-real validity model for protocol design.

Method (as proposed): Protocol: fixed seated transfer task (start pad, target pad, set distances) with a tagged cube or tagged blocks in a reduced Box-and-Block layout. Phase segmentation: hybrid rule-plus-HMM on object kinematics (grasp onset = first sustained object velocity with wrist-to-object below the grip radius; transport end = object at rest on target; release = wrist leaving grip radius). Kinematics: v1/v3 landmark branch with the swing-twist model; object-conditioned recovery fills grasp-phase wrist occlusions. Uncertainty: Monte Carlo or sigma-point propagation of range-dependent D435 depth noise, landmark pixel noise from static frames and grip-offset staleness through the chain; split-conformal calibration of each metric against the reference; a metric is reported only if the measured-frame fraction in its phase exceeds a threshold chosen on the calibration split; held/constrained frames widen intervals. Functional calibration: the subject slides the cube along the rail at three heights, places it on 6-9 known desk targets with a straight elbow, and makes small extended-elbow arm circles; nonlinear least squares over shoulder-centre offset, upper-arm and forearm lengths and wrist-to-grip offset, with residuals (i) chain-predicted wrist vs object-implied wrist, (ii) a SCoRE-like sphere constraint on the extended-elbow wrist path, (iii) landmark reprojection and depth, and anthropometric priors scaled by height. Twin: Unity scene and avatar with sensor-POV capture, a D435 depth renderer (range-dependent noise, edge dropout, grazing-angle invalid pixels), ArUco cube; healthy recordings retargeted to the rig with parametric impairments (joint range limits, reach-proportional trunk flexion, 4-6 Hz tremor, time scaling); the unchanged pipeline runs on rendered frames; a gradient-boosting surrogate from scenario descriptors to metric error optimises camera height, yaw and distance.

Evaluation (as proposed): Reference: borrowed IMU suit or upper-body IMUs, or a partner-lab mocap session (standard upper-limb marker set for a subset), two-iPhone OpenCap as a weaker reference, palpated and tape-measured anatomical landmarks by a trained assessor. Subjects: 15-20 healthy young and 15-20 healthy older adults (minimal-risk ethics), covering body sizes and both sexes, with repeat sessions; then a 10-15 person stroke or PD pilot through a clinical partner. Metrics: Bland-Altman, ICC(2,1), SEM and MDC per metric; empirical coverage of nominal 90 percent intervals; abstention rate; phase-event timing error (ms) vs reference and hand-labelled video; joint-centre (mm) and segment-length error and change in metric bias before vs after calibration; test-retest ICC of calibrated parameters; Spearman correlation between simulated and real per-metric error across 6-10 real camera/layout configurations; error reduction of the recommended placement vs naive frontal; calibration of predicted abstention. Baselines: MediaPipe or RTMPose plus depth without object (hand-only segmentation), OpenCap, stopwatch timing, effective lengths, height-only anthropometric scaling, keypoint-only model fit, thesis default placement. Ablations: no object-conditioned recovery, no honesty gating, Gaussian-only vs conformal, v1 MediaPipe vs v3 RTMPose, dropping each calibration movement, twin without depth noise / edge dropout / clothing variation.

Target venues: IEEE Transactions on Neural Systems and Rehabilitation Engineering, Journal of NeuroEngineering and Rehabilitation, Journal of Biomechanics, IEEE Transactions on Biomedical Engineering, IEEE Journal of Biomedical and Health Informatics, npj Digital Medicine (after the patient cohort)

Builds on the thesis: Object-conditioned recovery fills grasp and transport occlusions; the marker branch is a precise event clock hand-only systems lack; honesty labels make abstention and interval widening principled; the metric desk-and-gravity frame gives reach in cm without scale ambiguity; the v2 causal chain allows a bedside report; the rail fit (0.26 cm median) and tape waypoints give a geometric calibration reference; the thesis Section 6.3 comparison of effective vs measured lengths is the calibration pilot; the Unity sensor-POV twin, v3 synthetic occlusion scenarios and D435 noise characterisation underpin the simulation study.

Feasibility (as proposed): Camera, GPU, pipeline and calibration tooling exist. Need ethics approval (healthy cohort 2-3 months; clinical cohort 4-6 months via a hospital partner), a reference system (borrow IMUs or mocap, or a few hundred dollars of IMUs), a task board, rail and tagged blocks, a palpation-trained assessor or physiotherapy collaborator, and 40-60 sessions. The twin is software and can start immediately (3-4 months). Timeline: months 0-3 protocol, ethics, segmentation code and twin; months 3-6 healthy cohort, giving a TNSRE validity paper and a calibration paper (about 6-8 months); months 6-12 patient pilot for JNER or npj Digital Medicine.

Risks (as proposed): Insufficient validation: a single-site healthy-only cohort with an IMU reference (itself drifting) or palpation/tape references may be judged weak; without patients the clinical claim is motivational; a tagged object departs from normed standardized tests; trunk displacement depends on the hip-occlusion weakness unless I17 is solved first; impaired participants may not perform calibration movements; the calibration gain may be small relative to depth noise; the sim-to-real gap may break the twin's predictions and scripted impairments are not real pathology.

### novelty skeptic: score 3, refuted=True

Search limits. The web-search budget for this session was exhausted (200 of 200 calls) before any query in this review ran. PubMed, arXiv, Semantic Scholar and OpenAlex were all blocked by the egress proxy. None of the four planned queries returned results: an automated or instrumented Box-and-Block test with RGB-D, markerless stroke drinking-task kinematics, conformal or uncertainty-aware markerless joint angles, and object-based reach/grasp/transport/release segmentation. Every work listed below is therefore from memory and marked verified=false. The task rules say to default to refuted=true when no evidence either way can be found, and this verdict follows that rule.

Substantive assessment. The idea bundles four contributions. Each one has close precedent that I recall from memory.
(1) Automated standardized-test instrumentation with a depth camera has a decade of precedent: Kinect-based digital Box-and-Block tests (Hsiao et al. 2013; Ona et al. 2018-2020) and Kinect or video ARAT/WMFT scoring. Object-clocked phase events are an incremental refinement. Instrumented and smart objects (sensorized cups and blocks, IMU-based primitive segmentation such as PrimSeq, StrokeRehab) already timestamp grasp and release. A printed ArUco tag on a cube is a weaker version of the instrumented object, not a new principle.
(2) Uncertainty on markerless biomechanics is an active line. Cotton and colleagues (Shirley Ryan AbilityLab) report probabilistic or confidence-interval biomechanical reconstruction from markerless capture, including rehabilitation-population work. Adding split-conformal calibration on top is a known generic recipe. It is a minor methodological delta unless coverage under distribution shift (healthy calibration, patient deployment) is the actual contribution, and the plan does not test that with adequate power.
(3) Functional calibration of joint centres and segment lengths is classical: SCoRE/SARA functional methods, and the OpenCap/OpenSim model scaling of keypoints. Doing it with an object rail is a protocol variant. The thesis itself shows only a pilot comparison.
(4) Simulation-guided camera placement with synthetic humans and sensor noise has precedent in synthetic-data studies (BEDLAM, SMPL-based rendering of clinical scenes) and camera-placement optimisation papers.

The integration is coherent and suits TNSRE or JNER. However, its novelty is the sum of several small deltas. The validation as planned (one site, healthy-first cohort, an IMU or palpation reference, an optional patient pilot of 10-15) is the weak point clinical reviewers attack first. The patient cohort, which carries the clinical claim, is 6-12 months away and depends on a partner. It is not a top CV/ML-venue contribution. For clinical top journals (npj Digital Medicine) the novelty of the instrument is low compared with OpenCap-scale validation studies. Breadth (phases + intervals + abstention + calibration + twin) dilutes each claim, and every sub-claim is individually contestable.

Prior work found:
- The Digital Box and Block Test: automating traditional post-stroke rehabilitation assessment (Kinect) (IEEE PerCom Workshops 2013); overlap: Depth-camera automated Box-and-Block test that counts and times block transfers; prior automation of the same standardized test with a consumer depth sensor.; verified=False
